PIXEL CLICKER - GAME REFERENCE FOR AI READERS
FORMAT: plain text. Sections start with "=== NAME ===". Lines are dense facts; "->" = leads to / produces; "x" = multiplier; numbers in parentheses are code defaults (0 in an Inspector field usually means "use the coded default"). Code identifiers are kept verbatim so they can be searched (grep) in Assets/Scripts.
SCOPE: section 1 is a hand-written overview of how the game plays and fits together. Sections 2+ are GENERATED from the code (enums, block catalog, packs, devices, skills) and from every system's own notes (CLAUDE.md index, script header comments, docs/systems/*.md). Regenerate with: python3 tools/game-reference/build.py
NAMING: player-facing name "husk" = code name "old pixel" (OldPixelInfo, oldPixels, OldPixelDespawn). "Pixel type" = "tier" = "block type" (PixelClicker.PixelTier, enum PixelType). "Pure" pixels = the six basic types (White, Gray, Black, Red, Green, Blue).

=== 1. OVERVIEW ===
ENGINE: Unity 6 (also guarded for older Unity), C#, TextMeshPro, URP. Single scene. All code in Assets/Scripts (~250 scripts, ~120k lines). Almost all UI and 3D models are built at runtime in code (no prefabs). PixelClicker.Awake adds most systems as components. Both input systems supported (legacy + new Input System) via PixelInput.
GENRE: 3D incremental clicker with skills (RuneScape-like), crafting, devices / light factory building, minigames, visitors / vendors and collectibles.

CORE LOOP
- One clicker CUBE in the middle of the screen shows a BLOCK of some pixel type. Left click = hit. Normal blocks break in 1 click; TOUGH blocks need clicksToCollect hits (only the last pays; PixelHit / PixelFinalHit events).
- Breaking a block pays CURRENCY of that block's own type (each pixel type is its own currency): amountPerClick (or a random payoutMin..payoutMax) x PayoutMultiplier (Ultra x Value x wish) x clickMultiplier. Click flow: CollectInternal -> AddCurrency -> PixelCollected event -> effects -> SpawnFallingCopy (the husk) -> RollTier (next block) -> Materialize.
- Next block = weighted random (spawnWeight) among UNLOCKED, spawn-enabled types of the current rotation (PickSpawnTier). Basic types unlock by thresholds of the previous type (TierUnlockMode.PreviousTierThreshold) or by shop purchase (ShopOnly).
- Every break drops a HUSK: a small physical cube (Rigidbody) of that type that falls to the floor. Husks despawn after a lifetime (oldPixelLifetime 15 s + Standard perk), are capped in number (OldPixelCap, base 50 + Standard perk), can be grabbed and thrown with the mouse (Pixel Grabbing, PixelGrab), and are the MATERIAL of the game.
- PIXEL RULES (docs/systems/PixelRules.md): a click gives the block's VALUE (currency); the HUSK is material and never pays currency by itself (PixelClicker.husksPayCurrency = off). Consumed husks give skill XP instead (GiveHuskXp -> PixelSkills.AddHuskXp, huskXpShare 0.5 of a click's XP; pure husks train Standard, a skill's husks train that skill). The only husk -> currency path is the RECYCLER device (10 husks, 2 s shake, pays 50% of what a click on that block pays now). Holding a UTILITY block gives CHARGES (e.g. Pesticide: 3 Hornet Nest charges that auto-clear hazards).
- AUTO CLICKER (shop) clicks on a timer; auto clicks give reduced skill XP (autoXpFactor 0.25) but Standard still gets its flat 1 XP.

BLOCK HOLD (right mouse button, PixelBlockHold)
- Hold right on the cube: the block is pulled out of the rotation and floats beside the cube while a meter drains (5 s, 10 s for hold blocks); the cube rolls a new block so you keep clicking. Letting go puts it back next in line (keeps its hits). Recharge 8 s; running dry = longer cooldown. Hazards and some specials can't be held.
- STAKE: holding one of the six pure blocks for >= 0.75 s stakes it: value 1 + 0.6 x seconds held + 0.1 per click during the hold; on release a win roll (60%) decides: it later pays that multiplier or nothing (revealed with a green / red aura when it comes round).
- Meter energy: every 5 own clicks +1% meter. Meter UI is an arc beside the cursor, shown only while hovering the cube or holding.
- HOLD BLOCKS (PixelBlockRules.HoldRule): data rules for bonuses while held (Whetstone = extra rapid clicks; Pesticide = charges; XP / payout multipliers). SPECIAL blocks = rare replacements of rolled blocks (Great Oak etc.).

SKILLS (PixelSkills + partial files; window PixelSkillsWindow)
- STANDARD: the always-on skill. Every collected block = flat 1 Standard XP (standardXpPerBlock), manual or auto, any skill, no multipliers. Perks: husks last +5 s per level, floor holds +5 husks per level from level 4. Level 2 popup opens the Skills window beside a highlighted Standard card.
- GATHERING SKILL PACKS: Woodcutting, Foraging, Fishing, Mining (= Prospector mode), Farming, Electrician. Choosing a pack (Skills window only, never Toggles) replaces the basic rotation with that pack's blocks; you train that skill. Packs have a FILLER block (useless, shrinks in share with level), HAZARD blocks (Pest, Gas Pocket, Hornet Nest, Toadstool...: tough, pay nothing, penalise XP; right click to clear), SPECIAL blocks and perks per level, a skill TREE (1 point per level), XP curve baseXp x xpGrowth.
- OFFLINE PROGRESSION skill: trains from every click while the chosen pixel pack still gets 50% XP.
- XP multipliers: minigames enabled add a global multiplier; bonus XP bank doubles gains while it lasts; hazards apply a temporary penalty; Mastery = progress of all skills together.
- VALUE CAP: a pixel type's Value upgrade level can't exceed the level of the skill that owns it (pure / non-pack types -> Standard) x valueLevelsPerSkillLevel (1).

ECONOMY / SHOP (PixelShop, PixelShopUI, PixelShopDefaults)
- Shop button appears once Black is unlocked. Tabs: Pixels (base pixels, toggleable single pixels, skill packs), Upgrades (sub-tabs Features = packs/upgrades, Value = per-type value levels, Operation = consumable stat upgrades), Consumables (potion / utility / seed cards), Minigames (packs that unlock minigames).
- Packs = ShopPack entries (BuiltInPacks table adds missing ones). Testing packs are hideInShop (Fire, Nuclear, Farmer's, Prospector's, Block Hold, Oak Trees test); the Dev Tools 'Shop unlocks' widget unlocks / relocks any pack.
- VALUE upgrades: per pixel type, paid in that type, cost valueBaseCost 25 x amountPerClick x 1.15^level; payout x (1 + level x valueBonusPerLevel) x milestone multiplier; capped by skill level (above). Hazards have no Value row.
- VENDORS sell / buy for pure currency: Lumberjack (Woodcutting) and Prospector (Mining) BUY skill blocks for pure pixels at a rarity-based rate (UnitValue = 1 / (spawnWeight x amountPerClick / clicks)) x sellRate 0.8 x luck, and sell devices / potions. Other visitors: Tinkerer, Wizard, Entrepreneur (trades), Farmer; rare cats Egg and Bean.
- CUBIE (PixelGuideVendor): the guide / shopkeeper. Visit 1 comes after 50 White pixel clicks and gives a Recycler ({recycler} page token); visit 2 holds time until the shop is opened (shop nudge + shop tutorial). Page texts are Inspector lists.

CONSUMABLES (PixelConsumables)
- POTIONS: one single potion per pixel type (all non-rare types): for N seconds only that type spawns. COMBO potions (crafted): 2-type for every pair of types; 3-type only among non-standalone types (pre-built at start). Potions can be auto-priced from expected return.
- DEVICES (DeviceKind): placed in the world by right-clicking them in the Inventory: Fan, Vacuum Device, Magnet, Quantum Organizer, Storage Crate (10 slots), Sprinkler, Funnel, Wood Chipper, Recycler, Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, Bait Bucket, seeds, Prime Gauntlet... Some timed (countdown, shrink away), some permanent. Attuned devices (crafted with a potion) only affect husks of one type. Placed devices are saved (PlacedState: position, yaw, remaining, extra data).
- CARRY HANDLES (PixelCarryHandles): Storage Crate, Recycler and the Bait Bucket can be dragged by their handles (scroll / Q / E turns).
- CRAFTING (PixelCrafting): recipes, potion + pixel -> combo potion, device + potion -> attuned device.
- TESTER MACHINES (factory, not final): conveyor belts, kiln, splitter, elevator, belt sorter, trash can, tiny robot, connector.

MINIGAMES (PixelMinigame base; PixelMinigameLimits caps how many are on screen)
- Bought as packs (Minigames tab): Black Hole, Ghost, Meteor, Evil Tree, Snake, Sorting Race, Cube Dash, Red Light Green Light, Cube Evasion, Cube Blink, Breakout, Bomb Defusal, Ultra Holes. Passive: Stardust. Skill minigame: Weak Spots (Fishing catch). Some are TAKEOVER minigames (PixelMinigame.TakeoverActive pauses the normal game).
- Each enabled minigame raises the global XP multiplier.

COLLECTIBLES / RARE DROPS
- 7 Dragon Cubes (gather all -> wish), 3 God Cubes (~1 in 7 million, one of each), 6 Prime Cubes + the Wizard's Prime Gauntlet forging the Prime Cubelette. Rare drops sit at the END of the tier list; Collection Log book and a walkable gallery show them.

OTHER SYSTEMS
- PETS: one pet per block type (all except the rare collectible cubes), roam the floor, perks per type, outfits / names / Outfit Studio.
- FIRST PERSON: walk the floor, carry and stack husks, axe; out-of-bounds wall.
- CAMERA: base view, View window (V): orbital, top-down, first person, free cam, orthographic, zoom. Camera intro on start.
- WORLD LOOK: floor styles, sky styles, day / night, screen filters, bar edge styles, flat view.
- UI: Inventory (Currency groups, Consumables with Devices tab), Log (events, goals), Toggles window (after Auto Clicker), Pocket window + custom keybinds, Pixel Guide, Game Database, block tooltips, notices / tips (PixelNotice, PixelHints), status boxes, achievements, stats, title screen with music player, pause menu (settings, save / load, game guide, feedback, music player with shuffle).
- SAVES: JSON in persistentDataPath (PixelSaveGame); per-save settings sync (PlayerPrefs keys "PixelClicker.Setting.*"); autosave history with screenshots; offline progress on startup load. Inspector data upgrades: version ints (packDataVersion, looksVersion, ...) gate one-time fix-ups that RUN EVERY PLAY (play-mode changes are not saved to the scene), so removing them breaks the scene's old data.
- SHARED HELPERS: PixelCalm (InMenu / CameraBusy / ForTip), PixelPointerClaim (a world object owns the click), PixelHighlight (gold frame round any UI), PixelFind (find with version guard), PixelUIKit (UI builders), PixelFactoryKit (primitive builder), PixelBlockCatalog (one card per block type with TAGS), PixelStatusLines / PixelStatusBox, PixelWindows (one window at a time).
- DEV TOOLS (Editor / dev builds): Dev Hub (F9) with widgets, To-Do (Assets/Resources/Todo.txt), Changelog (Assets/Resources/Changelog.txt, never cleared), inspector, shapes, mockups, balance report, snapshot probe (F10 / F11).

PROJECT RULES (from CLAUDE.md)
- Every variable editable in Unity ([SerializeField] private + [Tooltip]); field initializers don't reach already-serialized components (use Awake / OnValidate fix-ups).
- Enum values are only appended. Copy the whole Assets/Scripts folder together. Compile check: tools/compile-check/check.sh.
- Save compatibility is not a concern during development. Skill packs are chosen only in the Skills window. Player-facing texts say "husk".
GENERATED: 2026-10-24 07:32 UTC

=== 2. DATA MODEL (from CLAUDE.md) ===
- PixelTier: name, colour/UIColor, PixelType enum (see PixelClicker.cs; values are only ever appended so saves stay valid), TierUnlockMode (PreviousTierThreshold / ShopOnly), spawnWeight, randomPayout + payoutMin/Max (a random whole amount per harvest, e.g. Electric 1-15; amountPerClick = the average for estimates; migrated by packDataVersion 3), clicksToCollect (tough pixels: only the last click pays; PixelClicker.PixelHit event for the others), startingAmount, translucent / materialOverride, glow + glowIntensity (emission on a runtime material copy + point light), vacuum flag, spawnSwitch (Auto = Glass, Luminescent and the IsSpecialType types get a spawn toggle in the Toggles window, see PixelTier.CanSwitchOff; spawnDisabled saved; CanSpawn used by PickSpawnTier), flyAway/flyDirection/flySpeed (old pixel streaks away in a straight camera-relative line with a smooth tapered trail instead of falling - Meteor; flyRandomDirection + flyAngleRange 10-170 deg = a random direction on the top half of the screen), count, totalCollected, unlocked (the progress ones - count, totals, unlocked, spawnDisabled, timesCollected, Ultra / Value levels - are properties over the GameState for the clicker's live tiers: ClickerState).
- Every pixel type has a CARD in PixelBlockCatalog.cs (tags = what kind of block it is, Pixel Guide text, old-pixel text, borrowed sounds / effects): give a new pixel type its card there.
- Spawn: weighted random among unlocked tiers only (PickSpawnTier).
- Tough pixels raise PixelHit for hits 1..n-1 and PixelFinalHit (UI shows 'n/n', then the payout popup follows after toughPayoutDelay) on the breaking click. Click flow (CollectInternal(bool automatic)): AddCurrency → PixelCollected event → click effects → Vacuum() if vacuum tier → SpawnFallingCopy → roll next tier → Materialize.
- Events: CurrencyChanged, CurrencyGained, PixelCollected(tier, amount, automatic), PixelsVacuumed(vacuumTier, total, count), VacuumBreakdown(double[] perTier).
- Shop reward tiers are added to PixelClicker at runtime via EnsureTier (locked until bought). EnsureTier inserts new shop tiers before the Dragon Cubes (rare drops stay at the END of the tier list). EnsureTier and Awake must only raise Count, never wipe typed starting values.
- PixelShop.EnsureDefaultPacks() (called from Awake and editor-only OnValidate via delayCall) walks the BuiltInPacks table and inserts any missing built-in pack into the serialized list so it is editable in the Inspector. One toggle: addDefaultPacks. Context menu: "Add Missing Default Packs". Older saved data is upgraded by MigrateOldPackData(). It also removes a leftover "Pong" pack (name or minigame id pong) from the saved list and renumbers the other packs' requirements.

=== 4. BLOCK CATALOG (PixelBlockCatalog.cs: type | tags | Pixel Guide text) ===
White | None | A plain pixel: one click, one payout. Nothing special happens.
Gray | None | A plain pixel: one click, one payout. Nothing special happens.
Black | None | A plain pixel: one click, one payout. Nothing special happens.
Red | None | One of the RGB pixels: one click, one payout. In Bomb Defusal the right wire to cut is the colour of the RGB pixel you hold the most of.
Green | None | One of the RGB pixels: one click, one payout. In Bomb Defusal the right wire to cut is the colour of the RGB pixel you hold the most of.
Blue | None | One of the RGB pixels: one click, one payout. In Bomb Defusal the right wire to cut is the colour of the RGB pixel you hold the most of.
Glass | None | A see-through pixel. It is also the material for potions and the Pixel Bank's storage upgrade.
Vacuum | None | Clicking it sucks up every husk lying around: they swirl into it and their stored value is paid out at once. The old Vacuum pixel then keeps those pixels floating inside its glass body. While a Vacuum potion is running only every 10th click vacuums.
Obsidian | None | A Mining ore: black volcanic glass that takes 
Luminescent | None | A glowing pixel. Its husk stays bright as it falls and leaves a fading trail of light.
Singularity | None | Rare. Its husk becomes a gravity well: for 5 minutes it drags up to 20 nearby husks toward it, keeps them from despawning, and captures meteors that fly close. Wells packed close together starve each other, so space them out. Feeding one to a black hole counts toward the Event Horizon achievement.
Ghost | None | A gray, gelatinous blob. Clicking it is just a payout; its husk is the one that drifts away.
Meteor | None | A cratered rock that needs 3 hits; the lava cracks glow brighter with each one. Its husk shoots off across the screen, and every one that does has a chance of knocking a star (Stardust) down beside the cube.
Solar | None | A blazing sun. Every Solar click raises <b>Daylight</b> (it fades slowly): the higher it is, the faster seeds and Seed pets grow and the more hovering Solar pixels pay. Old Solar pixels hover and pay 8% of their value every 4 seconds; five touching ones never despawn and glow with a yellow aura.
Electric | None | Pays a random whole amount (
DragonCube1 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube2 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube3 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube4 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube5 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube6 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
DragonCube7 | DragonCube | A very rare drop (roughly 1 in 70,000 pixels). It pays 1 of itself and is kept in your backpack. Collect all seven stars and you can Summon the Dragon and make a wish.
Mirror | None | A chrome pixel. Clicking it pays its own value and then repeats the last payout of the previous different pixel you clicked.
Seed | Farm | A shell you dig open. Every hit throws dirt; the click that cracks it pays and plants three sprouts. Each sprout grows a random unlocked pixel over about 6 seconds (rarer types are likelier; never Vacuum or Singularity) and then pops off as a husk.
Water | Farm | A wobbly blue jelly. Its husk splashes the first time it lands, paying nothing, and instantly speeds up every growing sprout and planted Seed pet within about 5 pixel-widths (each only accepts water every 0.75 seconds).
Fire | Firemaking+Standalone | Firemaking block: a flaming pixel. Clicking it blasts a ring of flames outward from the cube.
Copper | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Tin | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Iron | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Lead | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Zinc | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Nickel | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Treasure | Standalone | A chest that needs a random 1-20 hits, decided when it appears. Opening it pays the pixel's value x the hits x (1 + 0.15 x hits) and pours out a pile of random unlocked pixels at once: 2 plus one per hit, up to 24.
Nuclear | Standalone | A radioactive pixel with a toxic glow. Its husk ages every other husk within about 4 pixel-widths 40 times faster, so they disappear without paying - clear space before it lands.
Wood | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block: a solid log. A couple of chops and it pays a fair amount.
Rock | Standalone | Rock: Mining's filler block. One click, almost no pay. At low mining level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the ores show up more.
Dirt | Farm+Standalone | Farming's filler block: it takes a few digs (shrinking each time, with digging noises) and pays almost nothing. At low farming level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the other farm blocks show up more. Old Dirt pixels lying near a growing sprout also make it grow faster.
Fertilizer | Farm+Standalone | Farming block. Clicking it gives every growing sprout and Seed pet on the field an instant jump in growth.
Pollen | Farm+Standalone+Plant | Farming block that floats. An old Pollen pixel close to a sprout when it pops doubles the pay of the pixel that sprout grows.
Wheat | Farm+Standalone+Plant | Farming block. It pays more for every sprout currently growing on the field (up to a cap).
Weevil | Farm+Standalone | Farming pest. While a Weevil sits on the cube it eats one growing sprout every few seconds until you click it away.
GoldenFruit | Farm+Standalone+Plant | Farming prize. A growing sprout has a 3% chance to produce one instead of a normal pixel; it pays a lot.
Wire | Standalone+Electrical | A copper wire, and a <b>conductor</b>: old Wire pixels carry the arcs that link devices and other conductors.
Battery | Standalone+Electrical | A conductor that takes 
Capacitor | Standalone+Electrical | Every other click you make stores one charge in it (up to 40). When you click the Capacitor it pays its value x (1 + 0.15 per stored charge), then the charge resets.
Resistor | Standalone+Electrical | A conductor that takes 
Transistor | Standalone+Electrical | Clicking it makes the NEXT pixel you collect pay double.
Fuse | Standalone+Electrical | Pays 3 times as much if you collect it within 3 seconds of it appearing, and only 0.3 times as much if you are slower.
Bulb | Standalone+Electrical | Pays 10% more for every old conductor pixel (Electric, Wire, Battery, Resistor) lying around, up to 30 of them.
Pest | Hazard+Standalone | A HAZARD. Never worth clicking: hit it 
GasPocket | Hazard+Standalone | A HAZARD. Never worth clicking: hit it 
Bark | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block: rough bark. One chop, pays a little.
Sap | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block: a blob of sticky sap. It pays well for one chop. A Sap husk doesn't bounce, and it keeps the woodcutting husks around it fresh: each one that comes inside its yellow circle lasts 5 s longer (once), and the sap gains 1 s for each of them.
Leaf | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block: a flat green leaf with a pale midrib. One chop, pays little. An old Leaf pixel drifts down slowly instead of dropping.
Heartwood | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting block, rare: the glowing red core of a tree. It takes 
Twig | Wood+Standalone | Woodcutting filler block: a tangle of thin twigs. One click, almost no pay. Pinecone and Moss are its two siblings and share its spot: at low woodcutting level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the other wood blocks show up more.
HornetNest | Hazard+Standalone | The TERMITE HIVE, a HAZARD. Never worth clicking: break it (
Berry | Forage+Standalone | Foraging block: a juicy berry. One pick, pays a little. It squelches.
Mushroom | Forage+Standalone | Foraging block: a mushroom. It takes 
Herb | Forage+Standalone | Foraging block that is light and leafy. One pick, pays little. An old Herb pixel drifts down slowly instead of dropping.

Weeds | Forage+Standalone | Foraging's filler block. One click, almost no pay. At low foraging level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the good finds show up more.
Toadstool | Hazard+Standalone | A Foraging HAZARD. One left click sets it off: its spores make you dizzy and the cube jerks away from your cursor after every click for a while (and Foraging XP drops 10% for a minute). One RIGHT click clears it safely - be quick.
Silver | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Gold | Ore+Standalone | A tough ore that chips with every hit (cracks spread, rock and metal flecks fly). There is an 8% chance a fresh ore is a <b>rich vein</b>: it glitters and pays 5 times as much. Ores only come up while Mining is the skill pack in play.
Bedrock | Ore+Standalone | The toughest rock: it takes a random 
Fish | Fish+Standalone | Fishing block, rare: a silvery fish. Clicking it starts a Weak Spots round (the camera zooms in and time slows): hit every spot to catch it and get paid, miss one and it gets away. The auto clicker leaves fish for you. Bait pellets on the floor (and fish and water in a Bait Bucket) make fish come up more often. With the Weak Spots minigame switched off in Toggles, a fish takes a random 7-25 clicks to reel in instead.
Seaweed | Fish+Standalone | Fishing block: see-through seaweed. One click, pays little. An old Seaweed pixel sinks slowly instead of dropping.
Shell | Fish+Standalone | Fishing block: a glossy shell. One click, pays a fair amount.
Pearl | Fish+Standalone | Fishing block, rare: a shining pearl. It takes 
Driftwood | Fish+Standalone | Fishing's filler block. One click, almost no pay. At low fishing level it is most of what appears; as you level up it gets rarer and the good catches show up more.
Jellyfish | Hazard+Standalone | A HAZARD, and an electric one. Never worth clicking: hit it 
Bait | Fish+Standalone) |

Whale | Fish+Standalone | Fishing SPECIAL block, very rare: a whale. Click it once and you hook it: it yanks the line and swims fast away from your mouse, dashing about the screen, while you chase it and click. It needs a random 
Mold | Hazard+Standalone | A Foraging HAZARD. One left click bursts it and the mold covers EVERY husk on the field; moldy husks die after a while unless you shake them clean (grab one and shake it). One RIGHT click clears it safely - be quick.
WoodChips | Standalone) |

GoldenSnitch | Standalone | An extremely rare drop. It never stays on the cube: it sprouts wings and darts round the screen. Keep your mouse on it to charge the bar (it does not charge while your mouse is off it), then click when the closing white ring meets the gold one. Catch it for a huge reward - 400 clicks' worth of your best pixel and maybe an Ultra pixel. Miss three times, or wait too long, and it escapes.

The trick is its HUSK: every acorn lying on the field raises the chance that a wood block turns into a Great Oak (they only have to be there - no gathering). With enough of them out the chance is far higher. Acorns despawn like other husks, so keep them coming.

GodRa | RareCollectible | One of the three Egyptian God Cubes: Ra the Winged Dragon, a blazing golden cube wrapped in a sun's corona. The rarest kind of drop in the game (roughly 1 in 7 million pixels). It pays 1 of itself and you can only ever hold one.
GodObelisk | RareCollectible | One of the three Egyptian God Cubes: Obelisk the Tormentor, a deep blue stone cube that sheds dust. The rarest kind of drop in the game (roughly 1 in 7 million pixels). It pays 1 of itself and you can only ever hold one.

=== 6. SHOP PACKS (PixelShopDefaults.cs Create...Pack: method | first displayName | hidden in shop?) ===
CreateRgbPack | RGB Pack | hidden | Red, Green and Blue pixels in the random spawn pool. Cubie hands these over on his second visit.
CreateGlassPack | Glass Pixel | shown | Adds the see-through Glass pixel to the random spawn pool (switched on by default; you can switch it off in the Toggles window) and unlocks Crafting.
CreateCharcoalPixelPack | Charcoal Pixel | shown | Adds the Charcoal pixel to the random spawn pool. Its husks are the most efficient fuel: in a Biomass Generator one burns as long as 8 wood husks. Paid with the Charcoal the Kiln makes.
CreateVacuumPack | Vacuum Pixel | shown | Adds the rare Vacuum pixel. Clicking it sucks up every husk and collects them again.
CreateLuminescentPack | Luminescent Pixel | shown | Adds the glowing Luminescent pixel, which pays more per click.
CreateGhostPack | Ghost Hunt | shown | A faint ghost cube floats across the screen now and then. Click it for a random pixel buff.
CreateGhostPixelPack | Ghost Pixel | shown | Adds the faint, see-through Ghost pixel to the spawn pool. Pays well.
CreateBombPack | Bomb Defusal | shown | A bomb with red, green and blue wires appears now and then. Cut the wire of the colour you hold the most of before the timer runs out, or it blows up your husks. Defusing earns bomb parts.
CreatePadPack | Ultra Pad | shown | Four round holes open in the floor, one near each corner of the screen. The marked hole (a spinning pixel floats in its centre) wants that pixel: drop it in for its value and a chance at an Ultra pixel. Anything else that falls into a hole is lost. The marker jumps to another hole after every correct drop.
CreateMeteorStrikePack | Meteor Strike | shown | A huge meteor drifts slowly across the screen now and then. Every click on it chips off a meteor chunk.
CreateEvilTreePack | Evil Tree | shown | Now and then an evil tree sprouts on the floor and grows into a giant. It flails about, so it is hard to hit. Chop through its health bar to fell it for Magic Wood and Woodcutting XP; if you take too long it sinks back into the ground.
CreateMeteorPixelPack | Meteor Pixel | shown | Adds the fiery Meteor pixel. When clicked it doesn't fall - it streaks away like a meteor.
CreateSortPack | Sorting Race | shown | Now and then some husks freeze in a jumbled grid. Click two pixels to swap them and group each type together before the timer runs out for a bonus payout.
CreateDashPack | Cube Dash | shown | Now and then the cube dashes around the screen, hanging in one spot before zipping to the next. The auto clicker switches off: click it as often as you can. At the end you get a bonus of 10x the pixel's worth for every click you landed.
CreateTrafficPack | Red Light Green Light | shown | A traffic light appears beside the cube. Click on green to score, never on red (it costs you), and on yellow click slowly, in time with the circle that closes around the cube. At the end you get a bonus of 10x the pixel's worth for every point.
CreateEvadePack | Cube Evasion | shown | Now and then the cube runs away from your mouse, sliding along the edges and slipping out of corners. The auto clicker switches off: corner it and click. At the end you get a bonus of 10x the pixel's worth for every click you landed.
CreateBlinkPack | Cube Blink | shown | Now and then the cube shrinks to nothing and teleports to a random spot, hides for a second or two, then pops back and stays a few seconds before teleporting again. The auto clicker switches off: click it whenever it shows. At the end you get a bonus of 10x the pixel's worth for every click you landed.
CreateBreakoutPack | Breakout | shown | Now and then (with 30+ husks on screen) the husks become a wall of bricks. Bounce the ball with your paddle and break them: each one pays 10x its worth.
CreateSnakePack | Snake | shown | Now and then every husk stops despawning and freezes. Steer a snake with the arrow keys and eat them all without biting yourself for 10x their worth.
CreateSolarPixelPack | Solar Pixel | shown | Adds the blazing Solar pixel, forged from stardust. Meteor pixels sometimes knock a star out of the sky - collect them.
CreateElectricPixelPack | Electric Pixel | shown | Adds the Electric pixel: a cube held together by crackling lightning. Pays a random amount every harvest, overcharges the auto clicker when clicked and arcs to nearby devices to keep them running.
CreateElectricianPack | Electrician's Pack | shown | Unlocks the Electrician skill and its eight pixels: Electric, Wire, Battery, Capacitor, Resistor, Transistor, Fuse and Bulb. While the skill is in play (Skills window) they replace the basic cubes. Wire, Battery and Resistor carry your device links, a Battery also sets off an Overcharge, a Capacitor stores charge from every other click, a Transistor doubles your next click, a Fuse pays big if you click it fast and a Bulb shines brighter the more wired pixels lie around.
CreateSeedPixelPack | Seed Pixel | shown | Adds the Seed pixel: a brown shell you dig at with many clicks to crack. A sprout comes out, plants itself and grows a random husk that pops off.
CreateFirePixelPack | Firemaker's Pack | hidden | TEST: Adds the Firemaking skill and its blocks: Tinder, Kindling, Firewood, Flint, Ember and Fire, with plain Ash as the filler and a Wildfire hazard. They only spawn while Firemaking is the pack in play (Skills window). Mechanics still to come.
CreateNuclearPixelPack | Nuclear Pixel | hidden | TEST: Adds the Nuclear pixel, a radioactive cube. Its husk is so radioactive that every husk lying near it despawns almost at once.
CreateTreasurePixelPack | Treasure Pixel | shown | Adds the Treasure pixel: a chest that takes a random 1 to 20 clicks to open. The more clicks it took, the bigger the reward, and when it bursts open a pile of other pixels pours out.
CreateProspectorPack | Prospector's Pack | shown | Adds the Mining skill and ten ore pixels (Copper, Tin, Iron, Lead, Zinc, Nickel, Silver, Gold, Obsidian and Bedrock): rocks that crack and shed chips as you hit them, break in a shower of nuggets and are sometimes a glittering rich vein that pays big. Obsidian and Bedrock take 15-20 clicks but pay a lot. The ores only spawn while Mining is the skill in play (Skills window), plus plain Stone as filler and a Gas Pocket to watch out for.
CreateWoodPixelPack | Lumberjack's Pack | shown | Adds the Woodcutting skill and the wood blocks: Wood, Bark, Sap, Leaf, Heartwood (rare) and the fillers Twig, Pinecone and Moss, plus a Termite Hive to watch out for and a Whetstone (hold it with Block Hold and your clicks count for more). They only spawn while Woodcutting is the pack in play (Skills window). The fillers are useless and most of what you see at first; as your woodcutting level rises it gets rarer and the other blocks show up more.
CreateForagerPack | Forager's Pack | shown | Adds the Foraging skill and five foraged blocks: Berry, Mushroom, Herb, Truffle (rare) and Weeds, in a truly random rotation, plus two hazards (Mold and Toadstool: one right click clears them, one left click sets them off). They only spawn while Foraging is the pack in play (Skills window). Weeds are a useless filler that is most of what you see at first; as your foraging level rises they get rarer and the good finds show up more.
CreateAnglerPack | Angler's Pack | shown | Adds the Fishing skill: Water (very common), Seaweed, Shell, rare Pearl and rare Fish, plus a stinging Jellyfish to watch out for, and two special finds: a message in a bottle (a treasure map!) and a mighty whale. They only spawn while Fishing is the pack in play (Skills window). Clicking a Fish starts a Weak Spots round: hit every spot to catch it, miss and it gets away. Water is a plain filler that gets rarer as your fishing level rises.
CreateFarmerPack | Farmer's Pack | shown | Adds the Farming skill and eight farm blocks: Dirt, Seed, Water, Fertilizer, Pollen, Wheat, Weevil and Golden Fruit. They only spawn while Farming is the pack in play (Skills window) and are a farm in themselves: seeds grow sprouts, water, dirt and fertilizer speed them up, pollen doubles a sprout's pixel, wheat pays more the busier your field is, and weevils eat sprouts until you click them.
CreateMirrorPixelPack | Mirror Pixel | shown | Adds the Mirror pixel: polished chrome that reflects the last other pixel you collected, so clicking it pays that pixel's amount again.
CreateBlackholePack | Black Hole | shown | A black hole sometimes opens on the floor and swallows husks, feeding the singularity.
CreateSingularityPack | Singularity Pixel | shown | Adds the tough Singularity pixel, forged from what the black hole swallowed. Pays hugely.
CreateIntervalUpgradePack | Faster Clicking | shown | Auto clicker interval: {current}s  →  {next}s
CreateBankPack | Pixel Bank | shown | Store husks in a bank. Press B for a hose: right-click sucks a pixel up, left-click spits the selected one out, scroll to choose.
CreateBlockHoldPack | Block Hold | hidden | Hold the right mouse button on the cube's block to pull it out of the rotation: the cube gives you a new one while it stays held. Let go and it goes back. Holding drains a meter; if it runs dry the block is put back and the meter takes longer to recharge.
CreateOakTreesTestPack | Oak Trees (test) | hidden | TEST: Woodcutting tree mode. While Woodcutting is in play the cube rolls filler blocks (Twig, Leaf, Wood) and every few blocks an Oak tree arrives and plays out in order: leaves, a run of wood, then its special slots. Some trees are infested and end in a Termite Hive. Timber! when the last block is chopped.
CreateRobotPack | UFO Helper | shown | A little UFO hovers about the map. Click it, choose a consumable and place its ghost: every time that device runs out it beams a new one down there, as long as you have one to spare. It can also abduct loose pixels into your Pixel Bank.
CreateBankStoragePack | Bank Storage | shown | Pixel Bank capacity: {current}  →  {next}
CreateTimeStopPack | Time Stop | shown | Press T to stop time - everything freezes, even countdowns. Press T again to resume.
CreateTimeSlowPack | Time Slow | shown | Press S to slow time down (it shares the Time Stop energy meter). Fast things, like meteor pixels, slow to a crawl - slow enough to grab with Pixel Grabbing.
CreateComboPack | Combo Meter | shown | Click fast to build a combo. Reaching each combo milestone boosts your clicks; every level unlocks a higher tier. Top multiplier: x{current}  →  x{next}
CreateClicksUpgradePack | Multi-Click | shown | Clicks per auto click: {current}  →  {next}
CreateAutoClickerPack | Auto Clicker | shown | Clicks the cube for you every {interval} seconds.
