=== 9. SCRIPT NOTES (the header comment of every script that has one) ===
--- Editor/PixelDefineSymbols.cs ---
Editor/PixelDefineSymbols.cs Editor-only ([InitializeOnLoad]): adds the scripting define PIXEL_URP to the project when the Universal Render Pipeline package is installed (and removes it when it is not). PixelFilters.cs uses #if PIXEL_URP for its post-processing volume (UNITY_PIPELINE_URP is only defined inside Unity's own packages, not in Assembly-CSharp). The first time it runs it changes Project Settings > Player > Scripting Define Symbols, which makes Unity recompile once. Menu: Pixel Clicker > Refresh Define Symbols (runs it again).
--- Editor/PixelShaderKeepAlive.cs ---
Editor/PixelShaderKeepAlive.cs Editor-only ([InitializeOnLoad], menu Pixel Clicker > Rebuild Shader Keep-Alive Materials): creates tiny materials in Assets/Resources/PixelShaderKeep with _EMISSION / transparent keywords switched on (URP Lit, Simple Lit, Standard). Needed because builds strip shader variants no saved material uses, which killed every glow / lava crack that is switched on from code with EnableKeyword (Luminescent, Solar, Meteor, bomb / pad lights). Any new runtime keyword toggle needs a matching keep-alive material. Because that alone did not fix builds, glow no longer relies on keywords only: PixelLooks.AddGlowShell draws a thin unlit coloured copy of the mesh (Sprites/Default, OverlayMaterial) over every glowing / emissive non-translucent pixel (plus a separate soft billboard halo AddGlowHalo, hidden with hideGlowHalo) (live cube UpdateGlowShell, falling copies, CreateDisplayPixel; disableGlowShell, glowShellExtra on PixelClicker - stored as "off" / "extra" so a scene serialized before the fields existed still gets the shell) and over the meteor rock (lava-crack overlay texture rockLavaOverlay). Face marks (Vacuum circles, colour-blind shapes) use PixelLooks.OverlayMaterial (render queue after the pixel).
--- Editor/PixelSystemsMenu.cs ---
Editor/PixelSystemsMenu.cs Editor-only menu for the game's system components (the list is PixelSystems.All): - Pixel Clicker > Add Missing Systems To Scene: every system that is not in the open scene yet is added to a 'Pixel Systems' object, on a child per group (UI, Core, Skills, Rares and Shows, Characters, Camera, World, Shop, Minigames). Then they exist in EDIT mode, so their Inspector values can be changed and saved with the scene (before, most were only added when Play started, and every change was lost when it stopped). Systems already in the scene, wherever they are, are left alone. - Pixel Clicker > Move Systems Off The Clicker: every system found ANYWHERE in the scene outside 'Pixel Systems' (the clicker, its children, the camera...) is moved into its group with all its Inspector values (Copy Component + Paste Component As New, then the old one is removed); if the group already had a default copy (from Add Missing Systems), that default is removed so the hand-set values win. Asks first, listing them. Both work outside Play mode only, can be undone (Ctrl+Z) and mark the scene as changed - save the scene to keep the result.
--- GameState.cs ---
GameState.cs The player's PROGRESS as plain data (no MonoBehaviour, no UnityEngine.Object references): one [Serializable] sub-state per system that has moved here (BankState, StatsState...). The one current copy is owned by PixelGameState (PixelGameState.Current): saving writes it into the save file whole (SaveData.state), loading replaces it, a new game / Restart starts a fresh 'new GameState()'. Systems read and write their sub-state THROUGH PixelGameState.Current every time (never keep a reference: a load swaps the whole object); their components keep only display, input and Inspector tuning. Husks and placed devices stay physics objects, saved the old way. ADDING A SYSTEM'S PROGRESS: docs/systems/PixelGameState.md (steps + the compile-check rule). (The old per-system save parts - IPixelSaved / PixelSaveRegistry - were retired 2026-10-24: everything is here.)
--- PixelAchievements.cs ---
PixelAchievements.cs Modular achievements: Inspector list of Achievement (id, title, description, Kind, pixelType, target). New goal types = add a Kind value + a case in GetProgress; or Register(achievement, Func<double>) from code. One "{Pixel} Pet" achievement per pixel type (RegisterPetAchievements, ids pet_<Type>, single-tier custom, earned when PixelPets.IsOwned; addPetAchievements). Three more code- registered achievements (RegisterUsageAchievements / RegisterFeedAchievements: "Event Horizon" = feed a Singularity pixel to a black hole (PixelBlackholeMinigame.PixelSwallowed event -> PixelStats.SingularitiesFed); "Potion Taster" = every buyable potion drunk once, "Gadgeteer" = every device kind placed once; progress from PixelStats PotionsUsed / FansUsed / VacuumDevicesUsed). Achievements have tiers (tiers[], earnedTiers); defaults per pixel type: tiered "Collect" (lifetime value, totalCollected: 10, 100, 1K, 10K, 100K, 1M) and tiered "Click" (PixelTier.timesCollected, saved: 10, 50, 250, 1K, 5K, 25K; Kind.ClickPixelType). The unlock POPUP sits at the MIDDLE RIGHT of the screen by default (DefaultPopupPosition, popupRightMargin), drawn at popupScale (0.68) open and collapsed, and starts expanded (pref AchievementPopupCollapsed2); hold the left mouse button on it (popupHoldSeconds 0.5, a ring fills round the cursor, PixelHold with useLeft) to pick it up, click to PIN it anywhere; the thin button on its far right collapses it to the icon box and expands it again with a shorter hold (popupCollapseHoldSeconds 0.3, saved); Settings > 'Reset achievement popup position' (ResetPopupPin) forgets the pin. The pinned position is the popup's LEFT edge; the pin is saved PER SAVE (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.AchievementPopupX/Y declared with PixelPrefs.DeclarePerSave, inside the black margins: a new game starts with it in the default place, ReloadPopupPin on a settings load), right-click to put it back. Saved as "id:tiers". Unlocks quietly/with popup, ids saved by PixelSaveGame. Auto-added by PixelLog.
--- PixelAimCursor.cs ---
PixelAimCursor.cs The AIM CURSOR for edge-click aiming (PixelBurstPop): while the pointer is on the clickable cube (PixelClicker.TryGetBlockAt, isLive) the system cursor is hidden and a code-drawn cursor follows the pointer on its own top canvas: - near the middle of the cube (where husks scatter at random) a small spinning CUBE; - towards an edge (PixelClicker.TryGetAim sharpness > 0) an ARROW pointing the way the husk will fly, turning smoothly as the pointer moves round the edge; the cube fades into the arrow as the aim sharpens (the arrow grows with it), tinted by the current block's colour; - each click gives it a little punch; it pops in when the pointer reaches the cube. Anywhere else (off the cube, over UI, menus, first person, clicks blocked, edge aiming off or the Standard node Aimed Clicks not owned - TryGetAim says no) the normal cursor is back. Only shows the cursor it hid (Cursor.visible) - first person keeps its own. disableAimCursor switches it off. A system component (PixelSystems, UI group).
--- PixelAimLaser.cs ---
PixelAimLaser.cs AIM LASERS (2026-10-20, Devices session): a see-through RED beam out of a device's coupling holes while you PLACE it (its ghost) or CARRY it by its handles, so free placement lines up exactly: the beam runs far past the whole floor (maxLengthHusks) and is CUT OFF by the first thing in its way, where a bright red DOT shows precisely what it lines up with. Husks and the device itself never stop a beam. - One manager (this component, made on demand) draws every beam asked for this frame in LateUpdate from pooled LineRenderers + dots, so nothing is built per frame and unused beams just hide. - Asking for beams (every frame you want them): Ports(root, sockets) - one beam out of each socket's coupling hole (port + half a husk up, along its facing). PixelCarryHandles calls it while a device is carried. Attach(ghost, sockets) - gives a placing ghost a PixelAimLaserPorts component that calls Ports every frame while the ghost is shown. Each device's CreateGhost does this with its own Ports(...) layout (the same sockets the real device gets). Beam(origin, dir, width, ignore) - ONE beam by hand (world space; width in world units, < 0 = the default). Openings(root, openings) - a WIDE beam out of each hole marked with PixelAimOpening (the Junction Box's top opening), as wide as the hole, along the marker's up. The placing ghost (PixelAimLaserPorts) and PixelCarryHandles call it. Colours, widths and length are Inspector settings on the manager ("Aim Lasers" object).
--- PixelAimLaserPorts.cs ---
PixelAimLaserPorts.cs Put on a placing ghost by PixelAimLaser.Attach: every frame the ghost is shown it asks for a red aim beam out of each of its coupling holes, and a wide one out of each opening marked with PixelAimOpening (found once, on its first frame).
--- PixelAimOpening.cs ---
PixelAimOpening.cs An OPENING that shines a WIDE red aim beam while its device is placed (ghost) or carried (2026-10-23): a marker on a child at the centre of a hole that husks drop into (the Junction Box's top opening first). The beam runs along the child's up (out of the hole), as wide as the hole (width, local units, scaled with the device), and is cut off by the first thing in its way - so you can line the hole up under a spout, an elevator's top or a Funnel. Drawn by PixelAimLaser.Openings (the placing ghost's PixelAimLaserPorts and PixelCarryHandles call it). A device with an opening: add the marker in its Build (ghost and real model alike): PixelAimOpening.Add(parent, localPos, width).
--- PixelArcGraphic.cs ---
PixelArcGraphic.cs A UI Graphic that draws a curved band (part of a ring) with ROUND caps at both ends and soft (feathered) edges, built as a mesh in OnPopulateMesh - no sprite, no radial fill. Angles are in degrees, 0 = east (right), counter-clockwise. radius / halfWidth are fractions of half the rect's smaller side. Set StartDegrees / EndDegrees to grow or shrink it (the Block Hold meter's track, glow, fill and ready pop).
--- PixelArcRing.cs ---
PixelArcRing.cs ONE place for making a full ring (a PixelArcGraphic from 0 to 359.5 degrees) on a canvas (2026-10-24: was a copied block in PixelGoldenSnitch (target / closing rings) and PixelPowerClick (charge / cooldown rings, shockwave)). - Create(parent, name, colour, halfWidth, anchor, out rect): the ring (radius 0.9 of its rect, 'halfWidth' thick, 1.5 px feather, no raycasts), pivot in its middle, anchored at 'anchor', switched OFF - the caller sizes, places and shows it.
--- PixelAttachment.cs ---
PixelAttachment.cs Attaching old pixels to each other (the first person BUILD controls, PixelFirstPerson): the snap maths, the glue and the prying off. * PixelAttachment.TrySnap(held, pool, ...): for the cube you hold, looks at the nearby old pixels and finds where it would sit if it were stuck to one of their faces. The held cube's orientation must be within maxAngle of an orientation ALIGNED with that pixel (one of the 24 cube rotations, NearestAligned); the spot is on one of the pixel's six faces (its centre pushed out by both half sizes) and slides along the face in half-cube steps (-0.5, 0, +0.5), so cubes can stack straight or like bricks. It must be within snapDistance of where you hold it. The result is the pose of the white ghost; clicking attaches there. * PixelAttachment.Attach(a, b): a FixedJoint from a to b (unbreakable, colliders ignore each other) and a PixelAttachment component on both. Attached pixels keep their lifetime frozen (OldPixelDespawn.Held is re-asserted every frame) so a build does not decay; they can't be picked up, only pried off: PixelAttachment.Detach(x) removes every joint to and from x (the first person player holds the RIGHT mouse button on it). Joints are not saved: loading a save leaves the cubes loose. * A whole build moves as one: Group(x) = every cube glued to x; the player carries it (PixelFirstPerson) after TakeApart removes the joints, and the links are glued back on release. * Foundations: TryGroundSnap lets a cube rest flat on the ground in any of the 24 orientations; AttachToWorld pins it with a joint that has no other body (pry it off like any cube).
--- PixelAudio.cs ---
PixelAudio.cs (UFO beam) The main-menu UFO's abduction plays ufo_beam (code-made PixelSynth.UfoBeam: a rising wobbling hum with a shimmer and a pop at the end, ~2.6 s; stopped if the abduction is aborted). Part files: PixelAudioCues.cs, PixelAudioSounds.cs (the built-in sound table + which synth clips fill it); the code-made synth PixelSynth (static partial class) is in PixelAudioSynth.cs and PixelAudioSynthShows.cs.
--- PixelAudioCues.cs ---
PixelAudioCues.cs Music CUES (part of partial class PixelAudio): the gameplay music changes at triggers and fades in / out. An Inspector list (Music Cues: name, trigger, param, priority, clip or playlist track number, volume, loop, fade in / out seconds). Triggers: Minigame (param = the minigame id: ghost, bomb, blackhole, eviltree, ...; empty = any takeover minigame; true while it has something on screen), Skill (param = the skill id in play), Night (the day / night cycle is in deep night), FirstPerson, UfoRide (riding the UFO), TitleScreen, Manual (code calls PixelAudio.SetMusicCue(name, on): the God Cube shows and the Dragon wish do). Every 0.25 s the highest-priority cue whose condition is true wins (the later one on a tie); a cue with neither a clip nor a track number is ignored. While a cue plays the normal playlist fades to silence and is PAUSED where it was (cueHoldsPlaylist) and fades back in, resuming, when no cue is wanted; a change from one cue to another fades the first out (its fade out), then the second in (its fade in). A cue that does not loop and ends by itself hands back to the playlist and is not restarted until its condition has gone false once. GameStart = the song you choose to play right after the clicker loads in (give the built-in "After load" cue a clip; Loop off = it plays once, then the playlist carries on; the param may be a number of seconds to cap it). Built-in cues (addDefaultCues: After load, Night, Bomb, Black hole, Evil tree, Ghost hunt, UFO ride, God find, Dragon wish) are added to the list by name when missing, WITHOUT clips: drop a clip (or a playlist track number) into the ones you want. disableCues turns the whole thing off. Mute / music volume apply to cues too.
--- PixelAudioSounds.cs ---
PixelAudioSounds.cs Part of PixelAudio (partial class): the built-in sound table (CreateDefaultSounds) and which code-made PixelSynth clips fill a sound that has no clips (FillSynthesizedClips / SynthFor). The system notes are in PixelAudio.cs.
--- PixelAudioSynth.cs ---
PixelAudioSynth.cs Part of PixelSynth (static partial class, the code-made sound synthesizer): the block / gathering / device sounds (Bloop ... Dig). The rest is in PixelAudioSynthShows.cs; the system notes are in PixelAudio.cs.
--- PixelAudioSynthShows.cs ---
PixelAudioSynthShows.cs Part of PixelSynth (static partial class, the code-made sound synthesizer): the hold / menu / chest / Dragon Cube / fire / ore / ghost / UFO / star sounds and the Normalize helper. The system notes are in PixelAudio.cs.
--- PixelAutoClicker.cs ---
PixelAutoClicker.cs Timer that calls clicker.AutoCollect() (paused while a takeover minigame runs or the cube is shrunk away: PixelMinigame.TakeoverActive / PixelClicker.CubeHidden); Interval / ClicksPerTick properties; Activate() from shop; startRunning for testing. PROGRESS (bought, switched off, upgraded interval / clicks) lives in PixelGameState.Current.autoClicker (GameState.cs, moved 2026-10-23): saved / loaded / reset with it. intervalSeconds / clicksPerTick here are the BASE values (used while the state says 0).
--- PixelAutosaves.cs ---
PixelAutosaves.cs Autosave history (for testing) + save screenshots. THREE parts in this file: PixelAutosaves (static): every TIMER autosave (not manual saves, quit saves) is also copied into <persistentDataPath>/Autosaves as autosave_<ticks>.json + .meta (when / total pixels / play time, so the list needs no big file read) + .jpg (the screenshot); PixelSaveGame.Save calls Add. At most PixelSaveGame.autosaveHistorySize (100) are kept: Trim deletes the OLDEST when a newer one comes in. List() gives them newest first. PixelSaveShots (static + a hidden runner): a screenshot of the game window (ScreenCapture, shrunk to 320 wide, JPG) next to EVERY save: slot saves go to <save file>.jpg, autosave history to its own .jpg. A rolling copy is taken every few seconds while the game is visible and used when a save happens while paused (the Save / Load window shows the pause menu, not the game). The rolling copy stays on the graphics card (CaptureScreenshotIntoRenderTexture, shrunk into a small RenderTexture): nothing waits for the GPU and no JPG is made until a save needs the picture (it used to read back and encode the full screen every 2.5 s - a regular hitch). The capture comes out upside down when SystemInfo.graphicsUVStartsAtTop (same rule as PixelFilters), so the shrink blit flips it there. Load(path) returns a cached Texture2D for the hover tooltips (slot rows in the Save / Load window, rows of the autosave window). PixelAutosaveWindow: the separate window opened by the small 'Autosaves' button in the Save / Load window (PixelPauseMenu.BuildSaveLoadPanel): a scrolling list (newest first) with a Load button per row and a hover tooltip with the screenshot; Load = PixelSaveGame.LoadFile (does not change the current slot) and then resumes the game.
--- PixelAxeSynth.cs ---
PixelAxeSynth.cs Code-made sounds for the first person axe and the Great Oak's bird nests (hooked into PixelAudio.SynthFor like PixelOceanSynth): axe_swing (a whoosh of filtered noise that sweeps up and fades), axe_chop (a wooden thock: a quick falling thump + a crack of noise, 3 variants), nest_fall (a soft rustle + a tiny chirp), nest_catch (two bright rising bird tweets), tree_creak (a stick-slip trunk groan: the cue that a tree is nearly down). WoodChop(seed, pitch, splinters, ring) is the shared axe-into-wood sound (blade 'tock' + damped woody body + thump + splinters) - the axe and the Wood / Bark / Heartwood block sounds use it (PixelSynth.Wood kinds 0 / 1 / 4). All ids are registered in PixelAudio.CreateDefaultSounds.
--- PixelBaitBucket.cs ---
PixelBaitBucket.cs Bait Bucket (DeviceKind.BaitBucket 14, a Supplies-category consumable; component added by PixelClicker.Awake; disableBaitBucket): right-click it in the Inventory (PixelConsumables.TryUseItem -> PixelBaitBucket.TryPlace, needs Fishing as the skill in play, one at a time) and a code-built red bucket grows in on the floor at a fixed screen spot (baitBucketScreenSpot, 0 = (0.89, 0.19), bucketScale 0 = 1.2 x the main pixel). It stays only while Fishing is the skill in play: switching skills (or Quit > Main menu) shrinks it away and everything inside pops out again as ordinary old pixels. Drag or throw loose old FISH pixels (up to fishCapacity 0 = 20) or WATER pixels (waterCapacity 0 = 40) over it (ScanPixels, like the woodchipper): they are stored with their payout instead of splashing / despawning (a Water pixel dropped in is saved from breaking). Stored fish are live bait: PixelBaitBucket.FishBonus (fishBonusEach 0 = 0.15 per fish, waterBonusEach 0 = 0.05 per water) is added to PixelBait.FishMultiplier, so Fish spawns more. Left-click the bucket (PixelPointerClaim / ClaimsPointer keep the cube and Pixel Grabbing out of it) takes the last stored pixel out into the mouse's hand. Saved (PROGRESS in PixelGameState.Current.baitBucket, GameState.cs, moved 2026-10-24: placed, spot, stored husks by type; the bucket writes its spot there each frame while it stands, and OnLoaded rebuilds it): where it stands and what it holds come back on load. Its bail is a carrying handle (shared PixelCarryHandles): drag it to move the bucket anywhere on the floor (scroll / Q / E turn it); the bucket's own click steps aside while the bail has the pointer. Which husks it may take, the fly-in and taking one out into the hand are the shared PixelHuskHolder steps.
--- PixelBalanceReport.cs ---
PixelBalanceReport.cs Dev tool (Dev Tools > Tools > 'Balance report'): writes <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots/balance_<time>.txt and copies it to the clipboard - one plain text / markdown report meant to be pasted into ANY AI. It starts with a READ ME block (what the game is, the balance goal, what every column means, what to answer), then the assumptions, a cross-skill comparison at each reference level (XP per click, minutes to reach each level at the manual and the auto clicking speed, pay per click, flagged outliers), a per-skill block table (share, clicks, pay, role) and the Inspector levers with their current values, and a CSV copy of the comparison. How the numbers are made: no formulas are copied. For every pack the report sets PixelSkills.PreviewPackId / PreviewLevel (read by PixelSkills.ActiveId and the filler / hazard share; PixelTier.CanSpawn counts the pack's own pixels as unlocked via PixelSkills.PreviewForces), so PixelClicker.SpawnShare gives the same shares the real spawn roll uses (filler fade, hazard share, Fishing's fixed table, special blocks, pets), then clears the preview again (try / finally). XP is per collected block (1, x autoXpFactor for auto clicks), as PixelSkills.OnCollected. NOT modelled: Value / pack upgrades and multipliers (base payouts only), minigame XP multiplier, bonus bank, Block Hold, skill-tree effects, the Fishing Weak Spots round, hazard defusing, guests, special blocks that are not unlocked in the save, Standard's rotation beyond what the save has unlocked. The reference speeds / levels are fields on PixelDevTools (Balance report section).
--- PixelBarEdgeFx.cs ---
PixelBarEdgeFx.cs BAR STYLES: what glows along the inner edge of the black bars (the HUD's top / bottom bars and the title screen's bar). PixelBarStyle.Current (Settings > Display 'Bar style', PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.BarStyle, the player's own like the other look settings - not per save): 0 Neon glow (the original: soft outer + inner gradient and a bright line, PixelBarGlow), 1 Pixel equalizer (columns of pixel cells rising from the edge to the music / sounds: AudioListener.GetSpectrumData, a quiet ambient wobble when nothing plays, falling peak dots), 2 Pixel wave (rolling heights, no audio), 3 Marquee (one row of cells with a light chasing along), 4 Scanner (a comet sweeping to and fro), 5 Thin line (just the line). PixelBarEdgeFx.Create(parent, edgeIsTop) builds one edge (all style parts, built lazily the first time a style needs them); the owner calls Tick(colour, strength) every frame (PixelHud for the two bars, PixelTitleScreen for the title bar's two edges). Cells are a tiled 12 x 12 sprite, columns snap to whole cells; the colour follows the glow colour (a slight hue shift along the row). To add a style: a name in PixelBarStyle.Names and a case in PixelBarEdgeFx.Tick.
--- PixelBeltJump.cs ---
PixelBeltJump.cs BELT JUMP (2026-10-19, a Dev Hub > Tester feature 'Belt Jump'): a PAIR of small ramps on a conveyor line - a LAUNCH ramp that clips onto the END of a belt and a LANDING ramp straight across a gap. SOCKETS (PixelSockets.cs; since 2026-10-20 the only way husks go in and out): two FITTED bare couplings (built in, not saved, made again on every rebuild) - 'in' (Intake) at the launch ramp's foot, where the belt ends, and 'out' (Output) at the landing ramp's end. A husk that reaches the belt's end is handed in through 'in' (AcceptHusk), sped up and thrown in a fixed ARC (a sampled parabola carried by PixelPathCarrier: the same arc every time) onto the landing ramp, slides down it and goes out through 'out' (PixelCouplings.Emit: onto the belt that STARTS there - belts snap there: SnapPoints - or into a machine's In coupling right there), or is set down on the floor when nothing is attached. A FULL belt there (OutputHasRoom false): the husk waits on the landing lip (held, never fades) until it has room. PLACING (two stages, PixelFeatureTester 'scrollStage'): 1. the ghost SNAPS to a belt end near the mouse (TrySnap, turned the belt's way) - it can only be placed snapped; click to fix it. 2. the SCROLL WHEEL widens / narrows the gap (gapStepCubes steps, minGapCubes..maxGapCubes); the landing ramp always sits DIRECTLY ACROSS, in line with the belt, so the arc always lands. Click to confirm, right click goes back. SHOP (2026-10-20): sold in Utilities (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.BeltJump, a PixelInstantItems tool: right-click starts this placing, one used up per jump). Saved with the tester save (position, turn, gap). Built with PixelFactoryKit.
--- PixelBeltSorter.cs ---
PixelBeltSorter.cs BELT SORTER (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Belt Sorter'): a low gantry that straddles a conveyor belt anywhere along it and kicks every pixel of ITS TYPE off the side. Placing: the ghost snaps to the belt under the mouse (TrySnap) and the chute is on whichever side of the belt the mouse is (the snapped yaw is turned round for the left side, the chute is always the model's +X). RIGHT CLICK (a quick tap; holding deletes it) turns the sorter round: it swaps to the other side of the belt and its chute / output swaps with it. FILTER SLOT: a tray on top of the beam holds ONE block, shown spinning above it (like a Storage Crate section): let go of a pixel you are dragging over the tray (or throw / drop one onto it: PixelGrab's 'recently held' tag) and it goes into the slot (a block already there is handed back); the slot's type is the filter. LEFT CLICK the shown block to take it out again (it jumps into your hand; PixelBeltSorter.ClaimsPointer makes PixelGrab step aside). With an empty slot the sorter lets everything pass. While a pixel of the filter type rides under the beam, PixelConveyorSystem asks PixelBeltSorter.TryDivert (called from its rider loop): the pixel leaves the belt (a paddle sweeps across), and is thrown out of the side - or put onto a belt that STARTS at the chute's mouth through its built-in bare 'chute' socket (PixelCouplings.Emit, within ~1.3 cubes), so a second belt can be fed from the side. Other types are untouched. AUTO CHUTE (2026-10-20): the chute is only BUILT while something takes husks beside the sorter - a belt starting there, a machine's In coupling, an elevator's base (Connected, checked twice a second, RefreshChute); the sorter turns round to face it. Nothing beside it = no chute, kicked husks fall to the floor. JOINT (2026-10-20): placed in the middle of a belt, it SPLITS the belt there (PixelConveyor.SplitAt) and sits at the start of the second piece; its joint cuts the line into sections for the Control Levers once a line has two or more sorters (JointsOn, PixelConveyor.CollectSection). A sorter whose belt was split (another sorter's joint) carries on on the piece under it. REMOVING a sorter (hold right, or the tester's Clear) HEALS its joint (HealJoint -> PixelConveyor.JoinAt): the two pieces become one belt again when they run straight on with the same speed / size and no other sorter stands there. SECTIONS: one sorter is enough to cut a line in two when both of the line's ends are connected (a coupling, an elevator). Its spot is measured along the belt from where the belt starts NOW (Spot, like the Combiner), so reversing the belt leaves it where it stands; saved with the tester save (spot, yaw, slot type + amount). A removed belt takes the sorter with it: the sorter goes back into the backpack (refunded) and its filter block is handed back (LoseBelt). It catches a rider anywhere on the stretch the rider crosses this physics step, so fast belts can't skip it (TryDivert). When the belt fed by its chute is full, a husk of its type rides on along the main belt instead of being thrown on the floor.
--- PixelBiomassGenerator.cs ---
PixelBiomassGenerator.cs BIOMASS GENERATORS (2026-10-19, Devices session): two placed devices built by this one component (the permanent PixelPermanentDevice stub sits next to it): - POCKET BIOMASS GENERATOR (DeviceKind.PocketBiomassGenerator 27): a small one from the shop's Consumables tab; - BIOMASS GENERATOR (DeviceKind.BiomassGenerator 28): the big one, on sale at Woodcutting level 10 (shopSkillId 'woodcutting'). They differ only in their Device entry: radius (how far the power reaches), generatorMaxCharge (seconds of power when full) and generatorChargePerHusk (seconds one wood husk adds), and the model's size (bodyDiameter). - FUEL: husks of WOODCUTTING (PixelSkills.HuskSkillId == woodcutting), plus CHARCOAL (the Charcoal pixel's husks, x charcoalFuel = 8 wood husks: the most efficient fuel) and WOOD CHIPS (the Wood Chipper's, x woodChipsFuel = 4; 2026-10-20, FuelOf) that land in the open firebox on top (dropped by hand or by physics) - or come in through its intake coupling socket - are burnt: taken off the floor (FlyIn), + generatorChargePerHusk, up to the max. Other husks are left alone. The firebox is never narrower than 1.8 husk widths (the pocket model is small) and a husk resting on its rim counts. A SMALL SUCTION (suckRadiusHusks, suckStrength) draws loose Woodcutting husks nearby up and in towards it while it has room for fuel (FixedUpdate). - POWER: every placed device within 'radius' (generators and Power Banks excepted: IsPowerSource) is powered (PixelPlacedDevice.HoldPower(fraction, colour)): its countdown stops and the generator's BLUE ENERGY BAR (PixelEnergyBar) shows over it, tracking the generator's charge. A faint blue ring on the floor shows the radius. - CHARGE: it comes FULL and drains 1 a second (drainPerSecond). Empty -> a FLASHING RED state for redSeconds (no power, the bar and the glow flash red), then it shuts down and disappears (RemoveNow). Feeding a wood husk in the red state cancels it and adds the charge. POWER BANK (2026-10-20): a PixelPowerBank in range draws its SPARE charge (GiveCharge: only above a kept fraction), with a blue arc from TopPoint. Saved: PlacedState.extra = the charge left. Device card: charge, radius, devices powered.
--- PixelBlackholeMinigame.cs ---
PixelBlackholeMinigame.cs Black hole minigame (Minigames tab, a pack with unlocksMinigame = "blackhole"): random-size swirling hole (runtime-drawn spiral textures) opens on the floor; old pixels inside are taken via PixelClicker.ReleaseOldPixel and spiral in (SpiralIn: eased start, angular speed grows as it falls, rotates to the path and stretches into a curved streak, darkens, fading trail, AbsorbFlash at the centre). While a hole is open it sets OldPixelDespawn.Frozen (old pixels stop ageing, time dilation; freezeOldPixels). A live counter (TextMeshPro, showHoleCounter) floats above each hole but is hidden until a pixel is consumed or the mouse hovers the hole (counterShowOnHover), stays counterVisibleSeconds then fades (counterFadeSeconds), and the first hole ever shows a tip box (PixelNotice, firstHoleMessage, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Hint.BlackHole). The shop row text is requirementLine ("Feed {1} pixels to black holes: {0} / {1}") and the Minigames-tab description is trackerHelp. Owns the singularity tracker (SingularityCount / SingularityThreshold, saved); extends PixelMinigame (id blackhole); the Singularity Pixel pack has a MinigameGoal requirement for blackhole. Shop shows the tracker row on the Minigames tab.
--- PixelBlinkMinigame.cs ---
PixelBlinkMinigame.cs Cube Blink minigame (id blink, pack unlocksMinigame = "blink", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack, 60K each; like Cube Dash: Takeover => true but the cube stays clickable, AutoClickerPaused meanwhile): every minInterval..maxInterval s a round of roundSeconds (30) starts: the cube SHRINKS to nothing (SetCubeShrink(0.02)), TELEPORTS while hidden (PickSpot inside the area* fractions, at least minHop away, SetCubeOffset), stays hidden hiddenMin..Max (1-2 s), grows back and stays visible visibleMin..Max (3-8 s), then vanishes and teleports again (Phase Vanish / Hidden / Appear / Visible / Return). Only manual clicks made while the cube shows count; at the end it pays rewardMultiplier (10) x the pixel's worth per click via AddCurrency (the clicks still pay normally); stats blink.rounds/clicks/best, tip minigame_blink, sounds blink_start/_vanish/_appear/_win/_lose (silent until clips are assigned), picture case in BuildMinigameModel. A success reports Clicked (bonus skill XP 15).
--- PixelBlockArt.cs ---
PixelBlockArt.cs ONE place for the code-drawn 'block art' of the characters (2026-10-24: was a copied block in each): every vendor / visitor (PixelVisitorMinigame and its subclasses), Cubie (PixelGuideVendor, PixelCubiePeek) and the Dragon Wish scene draw their figures out of plain coloured UI squares. - Block(parent, name, colour, size, pos, rotation, anchor): one square Image (no raycasts), pivot in its middle, placed at 'pos' from 'anchor' on the parent (BottomCentre (0.5, 0) for the standing characters, Middle (0.5, 0.5) for Cubie's peeking head and the wish scene). Each script keeps a one-line Block / Sq wrapper with its own anchor, so its drawing code reads the same as before. - WalkerHolder(parent, name, height, out art): the holder a menu walks about (PixelTitleWanderers): anchored at the bottom centre, 'height' tall, with a 300 x 400 'Art' child (its first child) scaled to fit; the caller draws the character into 'art'.
--- PixelBlockCatalog.cs ---
PixelBlockCatalog.cs THE BLOCK CATALOG: one entry ("card") per pixel type holding what used to be spread over several files (steps 1-2 of 'one definition per block'): - tags what KIND of block it is (PixelTag flags: Wood, Forage, Fish, Farm, Hazard, Ore, RetiredOre, Standalone, DragonCube, RareCollectible, PrimeCube, Plant, Electrical). PixelClicker.IsWoodBlock / IsHazard / IsStandalone / IsOre / IsFarm / IsForageBlock / IsFishBlock / IsRetiredOre / IsDragonCube / IsGodCube / IsInfinityStone / IsPlant / IsElectricalBlock read them (through PixelBlockTags.Has). - guide the Pixel Guide / block tooltip explanation (PixelGuideTexts.Describe; a lambda so it can read the tier's own numbers, e.g. t.clicksToCollect). - oldPixel what its old pixel does (PixelGuideTexts.OldPixel; null = the general text). - soundAs play another block's click / break sounds (PixelAudio; null = its own 'pixel_<name>' sounds). - fxAs use another block's break burst (PixelFarmFx.Break; null = its own). USER TEXTS: Assets/Resources/PixelTexts.txt (PixelTextOverrides) overrides the name / guide / husk text of any block; the texts here are the defaults. ADDING A BLOCK: give it one entry here (tags + guide at least). PixelGuideTexts keeps only the few texts that need its own helpers (the Bait numbers). The tags were generated from the old hand-written lists and the texts moved over word for word (checked to give the same answers).
--- PixelBlockHold.cs ---
PixelBlockHold.cs Block Hold (shop pack with unlocksBlockHold, Upgrades tab, cheap while testing; component added by PixelShop / PixelClicker.Awake, Activate / Deactivate like the Bank): HOLD the RIGHT mouse button on the block on the cube to pull it out of the rotation (PixelClicker.TryPullLive: the cube rolls a new block at once, so you can keep clicking while it stays held). Letting go puts it back IN LINE (PixelClicker.ReturnHeldTier: it comes AFTER the block that is on the cube now, with the hits it had - RollTier hands it out - so a hazard that came up while you held is dealt with first and holding / releasing can never reroll the cube). The hold drains a meter (holdSeconds x GripFactor: half of it at the start - startHoldShare 0.5 - plus 10% of that half at Standard level 3 and again at level 5, gripPerkBoost); letting go starts a short cooldown (releaseCooldownSeconds) before the meter recharges (rechargeSeconds for empty -> full). If the meter runs dry while holding, the block is put back anyway, the meter flashes red and the cooldown is longer (exhaustCooldownSeconds). Can't be held: hazards, Bottle / Whale / Fish / Treasure, or while a fish is being reeled in. A block with hits CAN be held and keeps them. Releasing a held block over a hazard moves the hazard to next in line (it is not removed). Filler can be held. The look (beam, aura, side slide, cursor arc meter) is described at the end of these notes. METER LOOK: the arc fill glides to the real value, goes blue -> amber -> red as it runs low (flickering faster the lower it gets), has a soft glow behind it, a bright pulsing tip riding the end of the fill, quarter-mark dots and a white "ready again" pop when a recharge finishes. All arc layers (track, glow, fill, ready pop) share ONE centre line (RingMid) and have round caps (PixelArcGraphic: a mesh band with round caps, no radial fill); the tip sits on that line and is about the bar's thickness. The meter only shows while the mouse is on the cube or a block is held (meterOnlyOnHover, 0.35 s grace while the next block materializes) and fades out over meterFadeSeconds (0.35) instead of vanishing. The hold lasts 5 seconds by default, 10 for a hold block (PixelBlockRules: Whetstone, Pesticide...). HOLD BLOCKS: what a held block gives is data (PixelBlockRules.HoldRule: rapid extra clicks / payout / XP multiplier + a label shown beside the meter); Whetstone (Woodcutting, 3 hits) = 2 extra rapid clicks (x3 at once). A partly broken block keeps its hits through the hold (TryPullLive -> ReturnHeldTier -> pendingHits), so a whetstone you only noticed after a click or two can still be grabbed. A right press within rightSpamGapSeconds (0.45) of another right press or of a hazard right click never starts a hold (spamming a hazard away must not grab the next block). STALL: PixelTier.stallsHold / stallSeconds (Great Oak): every click on such a block freezes the drain for stallSeconds and adds stallRefill (0.04) of the meter (PixelBlockHold.Stall), the meter turns icy while frozen. UNLOCKED BY THE STANDARD SKILL TREE (node 'hold', requireTreeNode on; 2026-10-16 - before that it was available from the start; requireShopPack off; the shop pack is no longer needed). Tree branches: 'lucky' (+10% stake win chance) -> 'highroller' (stakes grow 50% faster). DESIGN RULE (2026-10-16): right-hold is each block's SECOND ACTION. Only the six pure pixels get a STAKE (they are the money); skill blocks do a utility instead (PixelBlockRules hold rules: Pesticide charges, Whetstone rapid clicks...). Texts describe it that way, never 'stake on any block'. STAKE (disableStake, stake* fields): holding one of the SIX BASIC blocks (IsStakeBlock: Black, Gray, White, Red, Green, Blue - nothing else) for at least stakeMinSeconds (0.75) puts a stake on it: the value grows 1 + stakeGrowthPerSecond (0.6) x seconds held; on release the outcome is rolled (stakeWinChance 0.6, +/- stakeWinChanceChangeAtFullHold over a full meter) - pays that multiplier or stakeLossValue (0 = nothing) - and travels with the block through the queue (PixelClicker.nextInLineStake -> liveStake, payout x liveStake). It is revealed when the block comes round again: its aura glows green / red (PixelClickerAura.StakeHighlight), a top status line says what it is worth and a good / bad sound plays. While holding, the label by the meter shows the value and the chance. CLICKS: every click during a stake hold adds stakePerClick (0.1) to the stake (stakeClickBonus, in StakeValue); every energyEveryClicks (5) clicks give the meter energyPerClicks (1%), holding or not (OnClick from PixelClicker.CollectInternal; autoClicksCount off = only your own clicks). A first-time tip (stake_intro) shows after 25 manual clicks. Skill XP is not changed by the stake. Standard perk 'Crush grip' (level 8, 2026-10-18): every crushed husk refills the meter a little (Refill, from PixelHuskCrush). Not saved (the meter starts full); the pack itself is saved like any pack. No Toggles switch (right click does nothing else on normal blocks). Whether a right press is the cube's at all is decided by PixelRightClick (Owner.Cube: the live cube under the pointer, nothing ahead of it in the order).
--- PixelBlockRules.cs ---
PixelBlockRules.cs The shared foundation for SPECIAL blocks and HOLD blocks (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; Instance; the two lists are [Serializable] data, defaults are added in Awake by name if missing, so a copy on a scene object keeps its edits). Docs: docs/systems/PixelBlockRules.md. * SPECIAL BLOCK = a rare block that REPLACES a rolled block. SpecialRule: the block's tier definition (PixelTier: name, colour, clicks, pay...), the skill it belongs to (skillId; empty = any), the block types it can replace and the chance. PixelClicker.PickSpawnTier calls PixelBlockRules.TryReplace(clicker, pick) after every normal roll; the tier is created on first use (PixelClicker.EnsureSpecialTier, no spawn weight of its own). Defaults: Great Oak (Woodcutting, replaces any wood block, 1% rising to 25% with 12 Acorn old pixels lying on the field - SpecialRule.hasBoost). Fishing's Whale / Bottle / Pearl still use their own rolls (PixelFishingSpawns) - move them into this table when they are next touched (their chances feed the Pixel Guide's SpawnShare). * HOLD BLOCK = a base block of a skill that gives a bonus while it is held (Block Hold, the right button: PixelBlockHold). HoldRule: the block type, the skill, and what the bonus does: extraClicks (RAPID CLICKS like Overcharge: each manual click is followed by n extra full clicks, burstGapSeconds apart, via AutoCollect; PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> PixelBlockRules.OnManualClick), payoutMultiplier (every payout, CollectInternal) and xpMultiplier (skill XP of that skill, PixelSkills.AddXp) plus a label shown beside the hold meter. Defaults: Whetstone (Woodcutting, 2 extra rapid clicks = x3), Pesticide (Woodcutting, clearsHazard: a left click clears a Hornet Nest at once, PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> ClearHazardNow). HAZARD PENALTY (HazardHoldPenalty list): a hazard that GOES OFF (destroyed by clicks, PixelClicker.TriggerHazard) takes a fraction of the hold meter's current charge; default Hornet Nest 50%. Add a new special / hold block: give it a PixelType (append), a look and spawn data like any block (hold blocks are normal base blocks of their pack: shop pack reward tier + the skill's types list), then add ONE rule here. Behaviour beyond the numbers (the Great Oak's nests) still lives in its own script and listens to PixelClicker.PixelHit.
--- PixelBlockTags.cs ---
PixelBlockTags.cs The PixelTag flags (what KIND of block a pixel type is) and PixelBlockTags.Has / Of. The tags themselves are set on each block's card in PixelBlockCatalog.cs (this file is kept so a copy over an older Assets/Scripts folder does not leave a duplicate behind).
--- PixelBlockTextures.cs ---
Filler blocks / base block textures (PixelSkills.TryGetFiller, PixelClicker.FillerInfo, PixelBlockTextures.cs) Each skill pack can have a 'useless' FILLER block (SkillPack.fillerType = a pixel type name; Farming = Dirt, Mining = Stone, the old Rock block (enum PixelType.Rock kept for saves, now called Stone everywhere in the UI), which is now part of Mining's pixel list, unlocked with the first ore (Wood is part of Woodcutting), and comes with the Prospector's Pack instead of its own pack (packDataVersion 13 migrates saved packs: drops the single-reward Rock pack, adds Stone to Prospector's, sets it to 1 click / pay 1)). While the pack is in play the filler makes up fillerStartShare (0 = 60%) of all spawns at level 1, fading evenly to fillerEndShare (0 = 10%) at fillerFadeLevel (0 = level 20): PickSpawnTier and SpawnShare (the Pixel Guide's percentages) give it the weight others x share / (1 - share) instead of its own spawn weight. Farming / Mining skill by level, so unlocked pixels come up more as you level. Wood, Dirt and Stone have crisp 64 px pixel-art surfaces (PixelLook.blockSurface Wood / Dirt / Stone, PixelBlockTextures.Get, look colour white; looksVersion 40).
--- PixelBlockTooltip.cs ---
PixelBlockTooltip.cs Block hover tooltip (added by PixelClicker.Awake; Settings > Interface 'Block tooltips', PixelBlockTooltip.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.BlockTips, default on, per save; disableTooltip, hoverDelay 0 = 0.18, cardWidth 0 = 560): PixelClicker.TryGetBlockAt(screen, ...) finds the nearest of the clickable cube / an old pixel under the pointer; after the hover delay a card (canvas 655, glow + fancy window + accent strip in the pixel colour, real turning 3D model (PixelTierPicture), name, rarity tag from SpawnShare, worth, a bar for hits (GetLiveProgress) or old-pixel time left, PixelGuideTexts.Describe / OldPixel text, a sheen sweep) fades in beside the pointer. Hidden over UI, paused, title, first person, UFO cockpit, hose or god mode. THREE MODES (PixelBlockTooltip.Mode, Settings > Interface drop-down, PlayerPrefs BlockTipsMode, per save): 0 Full, 1 Minimal (the spinning 3D icon and the name only), 2 Off. ALT: a tap (released within altHoldSeconds 0.25) cycles Full -> Minimal -> Off -> Full and a small toast names the new mode; HOLDING Alt (longer than that) shows the other kind of the current mode while held (Full shows Minimal, Minimal shows Full, Off shows Full) and the release does not cycle. Grabbing a block keeps its tooltip open. The card keeps its spot until the pointer moves 70 units away from where it was placed, then glides there (so it no longer jitters with every mouse move). PINNED works whatever the tooltip mode: with the floating tooltip Off, the Pocket's Info card still shows what is hovered. PINNED + My Window MINIMIZED: the small card (3D block + name, like Minimal) hangs under the minimized header (dockedCompact). PINNED: My Window's Info button (PixelMyWindow.HoverDocked) moves the card into the window (UpdateDocked / Undock): it stops following the mouse, is scaled to the window's width, shows whatever is hovered (it keeps the last block while the mouse is over the window) and the window grows to fit it. WORLD OBJECTS: ShowFor(tier, note) - called every frame by a placed thing whose part the pointer is on (none right now; the Sorting Hopper's flag used it until 2026-10-18) - shows a SMALL card for that block type instead (the turning 3D block, its name and the given note; no stats / bar), whatever the tooltip mode, and never docked in My Window. HoveringBlock (static, this frame): a block / husk card is wanted - the device card (PixelDeviceCard) waits then. The pinned card is always the FULL card (or the small one under a minimized window): the tooltip mode and Alt (tap or hold) only change the floating in-game card.
--- PixelBody.cs ---
PixelBody.cs ONE place for reading / setting a Rigidbody's velocity across Unity versions (Unity 6 renamed velocity to linearVelocity). Use it instead of writing the #if UNITY_6000_0_OR_NEWER block again. - Velocity(rb): the body's velocity. - SetVelocity(rb, v, stopSpin): sets it as is (stopSpin also zeroes the spin). - Steer(rb, v, stopSpin): the same, but does nothing on a null or kinematic body (Unity warns when a kinematic body's velocity is set).
--- PixelBombMinigame.cs ---
PixelBombMinigame.cs Bomb Defusal minigame (pack unlocksMinigame = "bomb", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack): every 2-4 min a code-built rectangular bomb (casing, darker front panel, end blocks, corner screws, rails, blinking indicator light, an LCD timer (bezel + lit green screen, red when time is low) low on the front; the red/green/blue wires run straight across the top of the panel, each as two pieces hanging from pivots at its ends plus one wide invisible click box) appears beside the cube, kept fully in view (PixelUIKit.KeepOnScreen also keeps the hint / rising texts and the black hole counter on screen); cutting a wire only snaps that wire in two (CutWire: the pieces swing down); click the wire of the RGB pixel with the highest count at click time (CorrectWire) before timeLimit to defuse (+partsMin..partsMax bomb parts: bombParts = held (spent on Bank Storage via TrySpendParts), bombPartsEarned = ever earned = the tracker bar TrackerCount, like the Ultra bar; held parts are saved through PixelMinigame.ExtraValue / MinigameSave.extra); wrong wire or timeout = explosion flash + PixelClicker.ClearOldPixels(). Extends PixelMinigame (id bomb); sounds bomb_spawn/_click/_tick/_explode.
--- PixelBottleMap.cs ---
PixelBottleMap.cs The Message in a Bottle block (PixelType.Bottle 73, Fishing): PixelLook.bottleMap draws a clear, pale green GLASS CUBE (look: forceTranslucent, bevelled glass rim) with a small 3D treasure map floating inside - a parchment sheet, two rolled ends, a red ribbon and a red X (PixelBottleMap.Attach, called from PixelLooks.AddExtras so the cube, the old copies and the display models all get it). PixelBottleMapSpin turns it slowly and bobs it. looksVersion 51 replaced the saved Bottle look (the old painted-bottle texture, PixelBlockTextures.BottleGlass, is no longer used by it).
--- PixelBoundsSnapshot.cs ---
PixelBoundsSnapshot.cs Dev tool for tuning where old pixels may go (the view bounds + the floor). Dev Tools > Tools > 'Snapshot bounds (3 s)' waits boundsSnapshotDelay seconds (close the panel, set up the view you want) and then writes a text report to <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots/bounds_<time>.txt, copies it to the clipboard and saves a screenshot next to it. The report holds: screen size, bars, the camera that is on screen AND the 'home' camera that PixelViewBounds reads (pose, fov / ortho size, aspect, clip planes), the view kind / zoom / orbit state, the cube position and size, the floor (y, mesh bounds, own ground settings), PixelViewBounds' settings and its five planes, physics numbers, where the viewport edges (corners and edge middles, also inside the bars) land on the floor plane and on the cube's height, and every old pixel's position.
--- PixelBreakoutMinigame.cs ---
PixelBreakoutMinigame.cs Breakout minigame (id breakout, pack unlocksMinigame = "breakout", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack; takeover minigame): every minInterval..maxInterval s with at least minPixels (30) old pixels around, up to maxBricks of them (random) become the bricks: frozen (kinematic, colliders off, OldPixelDespawn.HoldAll), scaled to fit and glided (slideSeconds) into a wall in a court plane facing the camera (flat Sprites/Default blocks under a root, depthFraction of the way to the cube so it is in front of the old pixels; court = the whole screen between the black bars (manualCourtSize ticked: courtWidth/Height/CentreY), wallHeightFraction, brickColumns); all other old pixels shrink away and come back after the game (bystanderSeconds, same as Sorting Race) and the cube shrinks away (hideCube); the court's background is transparent by default (backOpacity 0..1, colour backColor). Mouse (projected onto the court plane) or Left/Right arrows move the paddle; the ball is a spinning CreateDisplayPixel of a random brick's type, speeds up per brick (speedUpPerBrick, maxBallSpeed); lives balls; every broken brick pays amount * brickMultiplier (10) immediately via AddCurrency and shrinks away (OldPixelDespawn.Begin); bricks left when the game ends (out of balls / timeLimit) go back to where they were and drop. Sounds breakout_start/_hit/_brick/_miss/_win/_lose, tip minigame_breakout.
--- PixelBurstPop.cs ---
PixelBurstPop.cs Where a clicked pixel's old copy lands (part of partial class PixelClicker, used by SpawnFallingCopyInner): when the ground is a real fall away (more than 2 pixel widths under the cube) the copy is not just nudged out at a random heading - TryBurstVelocity works out how long it will be in the air (gravity x gravityScale, no upward kick: burstUpSpeed 0 = -0.4..0, never above 0, and the copy appears at the cube's side popSideOffset 0 = 0.45 pixel widths out) and aims it at a random point on a ring round the cube (any direction, burstLandRadius 0 = 1.2..3.5 pixel widths), so a click throws pixels OUT in all directions and they land spread over the floor (usable as physics items) instead of dropping straight down in one stack. Short falls and disableBurstPop keep the old pop (popSpeedRange / popVerticalRange). AIMED CLICKS (2026-10-17, design 'Husk routing rework'): aiming is no longer free - it needs the Standard tree node 'Aimed Clicks' (id 'aimed', Standard 3, the alternative to the second move). TryGetAim is the ONE gate (AimingOwned): without the node it says no, so every click's husk scatters at random (the burst below) and PixelAimCursor shows the normal cursor. EDGE-CLICK AIMING (edgeAiming, 2026-10-16): a MANUAL click near the cube's edge (on the screen: aimDeadZone 0.3 .. aimFullAt 0.85 of the way from its centre to its edge) throws the husk out of that side, within aimSpread (25 deg) either way; the middle stays random (spread 180) and it blends in between. Works for long falls (burst) and short ones (pop) alike. The auto clicker and first person always scatter. AimedDirection, set up by CollectInternal (aimThisCopy). Lets the player feed a Recycler / crate while clicking.
--- PixelButtonPulse.cs ---
PixelButtonPulse.cs Button pulse: every shop button (the shop's own CreateButton adds PixelButtonPulse) sends a clean neon outline out from under it when pressed - a thin glowing ring (a code-drawn 9-sliced rounded-rectangle outline sprite, RingSprite, the same on every button) is placed directly behind the button, centred on it whatever its anchors / pivot are (PixelButtonPulseRunner: grows 12 units beyond the edge on every side and fades over 0.45 s on unscaled time). The colour is the button's colour stretched to a neon (Neon); a button that turns from dark grey to bright when clicked (a tab) uses the bright colour one frame later. PixelButtonPulse.Disabled switches it off. Other windows' buttons (PixelUIKit.CreateButton) don't have it yet.
--- PixelBystanders.cs ---
PixelBystanders.cs ONE place for the 'bystander' husks of a takeover round (Snake, Sorting Race, Breakout; 2026-10-24: was a copied block in each): the husks on the floor that are NOT part of the round shrink away while it runs and grow back afterwards, so every husk you can still see is one the round uses. A plain helper object (not a component): a minigame keeps one in a field. - Shrink(clicker, skip): freezes every husk on the floor except the ones in 'skip' (the round's own), flying ones and ones that are despawning or held: stopped, kinematic, colliders and the gravity-well / float behaviours off. - Tick(roundRunning, seconds): call every frame. Scales them towards 0 while the round runs and back to full size after it ('seconds' for the whole way); once they are full size again they get their physics back and the list empties. - Restore(): puts them back at once (OnDestroy). - Forget(body): drop one (PixelHuskPool.HuskReleasing: the husk is going away and may be reused).
--- PixelCalm.cs ---
PixelCalm.cs The shared "what is going on right now" checks, so each script stops building its own long condition (a new blocker - a new full-screen window, a new intro - is added HERE once): - InMenu: the game is not in front of the player (pause menu, title screen or its reverse swing). Autosave screenshots, saving, the View window, and every place that used to check "pause menu OR title screen" by hand (Block Hold, tooltips, keys, minigame starts, vendors, the snitch...). Code that cares about only ONE of them (the pause menu itself, the title screen itself) still checks it directly. - CameraBusy: something else owns the camera (title screen / its swing, the camera intro, a takeover minigame). Orbital / free cam / zoom. - ForTip: a calm moment for a tip box or tutorial (in the game, no other tip or tutorial dim, Cubie not visiting or about to (a visit whose trigger is met: AboutToVisit, not the whole time before his second visit), the new-game intro over, no takeover minigame, time running). Perk popups, the shop / skills / toggles tutorials. The title / intro / pause part comes from PixelGameMode (the one place for the top-level mode; 2026-10-24). Scripts with their own extra conditions keep them next to the shared check. WHO shows next when several are ready is PixelTipQueue's job (tutorials > perk popups > first-time tips > Did you know): a new tip source registers there instead of showing a box by itself.
--- PixelCameraIntro.cs ---
PixelCameraIntro.cs Camera intro (added by PixelClicker.Awake): the camera holds a close-up side view of the cube (closeDistance (1.8 cubes), closeHeightInCubes, side, optional startFieldOfView) while the title screen shows - the blurred title picture is that view - then after startDelay it swings round the cube on an arc and settles into the scene's own camera pose over introSeconds (the end pose is whatever the camera has in the scene, nothing to set up; orthographic cameras zoom instead). A mouse click skips it (skipOnClick, off by default); clicks are locked during the zoom-out of a brand-new game (ClicksLocked, lockClicksOnNewGame). PlayReverse(onDone) plays the zoom backwards (reverseSeconds) for Quit > Main menu. Player switch: Settings > Camera intro (PixelCameraIntro.Enabled, applies next start).
--- PixelCardBoard.cs ---
PixelCardBoard.cs ONE draggable / foldable CARD BOARD, used by the Dev Hub's Main tab widgets (PixelDevTools) and Pocket's Pinned tab (PixelMyWindow) - they used to have a copy each (PixelWidgetBoard / Pocket's own cards, merged 2026-10-24, to-do 62). - The OWNER builds each card (a top-left anchored RectTransform with its own background and body) and hands it to Add(id, rect, height, title): the board adds the HEADER strip you drag the card by (PixelCardGrip: the others slide out of the way, Layout), a grip icon, the title and a '-' / '+' button that FOLDS the card up to its header (every child of the card except the header parts is hidden; KeepInHeader adds an owner's own header button, e.g. Pocket's hold-to-remove x). - Look per board: Style (header height, top padding, gap between cards, title insets, fold button spot, the Dev Hub's tall side grip). - SAVING, two ways: * PrefKey set (Dev Hub): the board saves the order, the folded cards (.Min) and the favorites (.Fav) in PixelPrefs under that key itself, and Finish() puts them back (a card missing from the saved order goes right after the card built before it). * PrefKey empty (Pocket): the owner keeps order / folds in its own data: OrderChanged (after a drag) and FoldChanged(card) tell it, and it folds a card on build with SetFolded(card, true, false). Card.tag holds the owner's data for the card. - Optional extras: Favorites (hold the RIGHT button on a card: it turns gold and stays above the others), SearchTab (each card is registered with the Dev Hub's search bar; Reveal unfolds, scrolls to and flashes it), MinimizeChanged + AllMinimized / SetAllMinimized ('Collapse all'). onHeight(total) lets the owner size its scroll content (and place things under the last card).
--- PixelCarryHandles.cs ---
PixelCarryHandles.cs Shared carrying handles for placed things (first used by the Storage Crate, now also the Recycler): - BuildParts(part, halfWidth, depth, y, size, opacity): the model - on each short (+/-X) end two arms and a grip bar, all parts named "Handle", made through the caller's own 'part' callback (so each model keeps its own materials / PixelFactoryKit). 'size' = how far they stick out. - The component (AddComponent on the root, then Init): finds the child renderers named "Handle"; hovering one shows the open-hand cursor (PixelHandCursor), left-drag moves the root over the floor plane (kept on screen) with the fist cursor, scroll / Q / E turn it while dragging (PixelConsumables.TurnInput). Over(ray) / Dragging tell the owner the pointer is the handles'; static Hot (hovering or dragging any handles) is part of PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock and PixelGrab's guard. 'Blocked' (a Func the owner sets) pauses it, e.g. while the Recycler works. While carried, the device's coupling holes shine red AIM BEAMS (PixelAimLaser.Ports) so it lines up exactly. The clicker cube wins: a handle hidden behind the cube (the cube nearer along the mouse ray, CubeInFront) is not hovered or grabbed. Moves go through a kinematic Rigidbody on the root (MovePosition / MoveRotation in FixedUpdate) so loose husks are pushed, not passed through; it follows the mouse tightly (followSharpness 25 = closes the gap fast, capped at carrySpeedLimit 60 u/s; the old 7 u/s cap made it crawl and keep sliding after the drag) and husks it shoves are slowed to pushedSpeedCap (2.5 u/s) so they slide aside instead of flying. The position / yaw are the root's own, so the saved PlacedState picks up where it was carried to.
--- PixelChargeBooster.cs ---
PixelChargeBooster.cs / PixelLightningRod.cs Electric devices (DeviceKind.ChargeBooster / LightningRod, defaults auto-added like the fan, cost Electric pixels; Device.uses / chargeCapacity / superChargeSeconds / superClicksPerSecond / strikeSeconds). Both last a number of uses, not time: PixelPlacedDevice.InitUses / SpendUse / UsesLabel (uses mode: no countdown, AddSeconds ignored, Remaining = uses left so saves store it; PixelConsumables.UsesKind). Charge Booster: spends one use per auto-clicker click (PixelClicker.PixelCollected, its own super-charge clicks excluded); PixelElectricLinks.ConsumeForBooster feeds each Electric pixel that links straight to it into AddCharge and dissipates the pixel (no +1 s, no chain through it); full meter (tall meter beside the body) = click it (PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock while hovered) to start a super charge: superClicksPerSecond extra AutoCollect() calls for superChargeSeconds with crackling bolts and a light; charge is saved in PlacedState.aim. Lightning Rod: every strikeSeconds, if non-Electric old pixels lie within its radius, a bolt hits the tip and every one becomes an Electric pixel (same payout, ReleaseOldPixel + SpawnStoredPixel); one strike = one use. PixelBoltLayer (in PixelChargeBooster.cs) = shared unparented dynamic bolt mesh. Stats booster.supercharges, rod.strikes/converted.
--- PixelClickerAnimation.cs ---
PixelClickerAnimation.cs Part of PixelClicker (partial class): the cube on screen (AnimatePixel: bob, spin, pulse, squash, shrink; cube offset / shrink for minigames; needed clicks; Materialize; PixelBaseSize; click effects and sounds). The system notes are in PixelClicker.cs.
--- PixelClickerAura.cs ---
PixelClickerAura.cs A soft glow BEHIND the clicker cube to make it easier to find without touching the pixels themselves (Settings > Interface > 'Clicker glow aura', PixelClickerAura.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.ClickerAura, default ON, saved per profile). A camera-facing quad with a radial fade (Sprites/Default, alpha blended) sits just behind the cube as seen from the camera, so the cube hides the middle and a halo shows round it from any view. Its colour is the colour of the pixel being clicked (washed towards white), fades smoothly when the tier changes and breathes slowly. The cube is CUT OUT of it: PixelDepthMask.shader (Resources) draws a copy of the cube's main mesh that writes only depth, in queue 3001 between the cube and the aura (3002), so a transparent cube doesn't show the glow through itself. Hidden while the title screen shows. Added by PixelClicker.Awake. Tunables are [SerializeField] fields.

PixelClickerClick.cs Part of PixelClicker (partial class): the click (Collect, PowerClick, AutoCollect, the fishing hand-off, CollectInternal = the click flow, and its steps FishClickStops / ToughHit / ClickPayout / PlayBreakEffects). The system notes are in PixelClicker.cs.

The system notes are in PixelClicker.cs.
--- PixelClickerDatabase.cs ---
PixelClickerDatabase.cs Part of PixelClicker: its Game Database entries (IPixelDatabaseSource, see PixelDatabase.cs) - one 'Pixel' entry per tier (the Pixel Guide text + pay / clicks / weight; discovered = unlocked or collected; its 3D picture; pinnable as a currency row). Every other step links to these through PixelDatabase.Builder.Pixel(type).
--- PixelClickerHazards.cs ---
PixelClickerHazards.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: hazard blocks (Pest = Farming, Gas Pocket = Mining, Termite Hive = Woodcutting, and the other skills' hazards) - their settings (Pest bug swarm, Termite Hive), charging up and leaving by themselves (UpdateHazardCharges, UpdateHazardExpiry), defusing with right clicks (DefuseHazard, FinishDefuse, DefusePopup), clearing (ClearHazardNow), going off (TriggerHazard) and their per-skill stats (CountHazardForSkill). Which block is a hazard is a block tag (IsHazard, PixelBlockTags); penalties are in PixelSkills. Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerHusks.cs ---
PixelClickerHusks.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: everything about HUSKS (the pixels that drop off the cube; code calls them old pixels) - spawning the falling copy after a click (SpawnFallingCopy / SpawnFallingCopyInner, SpawnStoredPixel), how long they last (LifetimeFactor, AddDespawn), their amount (RollOldPixelAmount), the cap (CopyCap), the Vacuum pixel sucking them up (Vacuum, SuckRoutine), husk XP, saving / restoring them (OldPixelState), claiming / absorbing / releasing them for devices, their physics materials, trails and fly-away landing, and display copies (CreateDisplayPixel). The components ON a husk are in PixelHusks.cs. Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerLookFixups.cs ---
PixelClickerLookFixups.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: RunLookFixups, called from Awake. The scene stores the Pixel Looks list (the 'looks' array in the Inspector) and a looksVersion number; each 'if (looksVersion < N)' step below adds or replaces the looks that changed in version N, so a scene saved before a look existed still gets it. They run in order on every Play until the scene is saved with the new number (the scene's data is old, so in practice they run each time). New look changes add the next step at the END with the next number. The steps are grouped into RunLookFixupsTo<N>() methods (called in order from RunLookFixups) only to keep each method short: a new step goes at the end of the last group (start a new group, called last, when that one passes ~130 lines or ~35 branches: about 5 steps). Split out of Awake in PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerLooks.cs ---
PixelClickerLooks.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: how the block on the cube LOOKS - its mesh per Pixel Look (BaseMesh, MeshFor), colour / texture / surface (RenderColor, ApplyLookTexture, ApplyLookSurface, ApplyPixelColor, ApplyTierLook), damage cracks (SetLiveDamage), the glow shell / halo / point light for glowing tiers (UpdateGlowShell, AnimateGlowShell, UpdateGlowLight), materials (MaterialForTier, GetGlowMaterial, BuildTransparentMaterial, CreateVisualMaterial). Which look a type has lives in PixelLooks.cs; the saved list's fix-ups in PixelClickerLookFixups.cs. Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelClickerNumbers.cs ---
PixelClickerNumbers.cs Part of PixelClicker (partial class): the optional Inspector labels (NotifyChanged, ApplyUIFont, RefreshUI) and number formatting / parsing (AbbreviateNumbers, FormatNumber, FormatNumberShort, TryParseAmount). The system notes are in PixelClicker.cs.
--- PixelClickerPointer.cs ---
PixelClickerPointer.cs Part of PixelClicker (partial class): what is under the pointer (TryGetBlockAt, IsLiveCubeAt, the shared once-a-frame PointerHits ray, GetLiveProgress) and the mouse input reads (WasClickedThisFrame, PointerPosition). The system notes are in PixelClicker.cs.
--- PixelClickerSkillBlocks.cs ---
PixelClickerSkillBlocks.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: the core's side of the skill / special blocks - Solar clusters and seed boosts, plants and seed sprouts (RandomPlantPixelTier, RandomSeedPixelTier, PlantSeedSproutAt, SpawnSeedSprout), the farming blocks (Pollen, Weevil, Fertilize, Wheat / Golden Fruit settings), Water splash pieces, the Electrician's conductors and capacitor, the Treasure chest (OpenTreasure, TreasureFountain) and Fishing (its settings, Bottle / Whale / GiveFishingHaul). Most of each block's own behaviour lives in its own script (PixelFarmFx, PixelElectricFx, PixelTreasure, PixelFishingSpawns...). Split out of PixelClicker.cs (no behaviour change).

Setting up the rare tiers at start (EnsureDragonCubes, EnsureGodCubes - God and Prime Cubes and the Cubelette -, EnsureGoldenSnitch, with goldenSnitchSpawnWeight), and the "Tier helpers" section: which tier is on the cube (GetClickTierIndex), spawn picking, unlocks, counts, the Block Hold hooks. --- PixelClickerTitlePreview.cs ---
PixelClickerTitlePreview.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: a LOOK-ONLY preview of any pixel type on the cube, for the main menu (PixelTitleScreen cycles every type on the cube). ShowLookPreview(tierIndex) plays the materialize pop with that type's look WITHOUT changing the current block or raising PixelMaterialized (so the Golden Snitch, hazard hints, stakes etc. never react to it); EndLookPreview() brings the real current block back (a normal Materialize).
--- PixelClickerTypes.cs ---
PixelClickerTypes.cs Part of partial class PixelClicker: the data shapes - the PixelType enum (every pixel type; values are only ever APPENDED so Inspector data stays valid), TierUnlockMode, PixelTier (one currency / block: colour, spawn weight, payout, toughness, glow, fly-away...) and the SpawnSwitch, plus the type checks right under the enum (IsWoodBlock, IsHazard, IsDragonCube, IsRareCube... - all read the block tags, PixelBlockTags). --- PixelCollectionGallery.cs ---
PixelCollectionGallery.cs The Collection Log's GALLERY: the camera button on an entry's page (PixelCollectionLog) calls PixelCollectionGallery.Enter(entryIndex): you walk, in first person, round a PITCH BLACK room where every collectible floats and lights up its surroundings. Everything is built when you enter and DESTROYED when you leave (Esc / the Leave button): nothing waits in the background. The room is far away from the game (origin (-4000, -9000, -4000)), is seen only through its own camera drawing to a screen-sized RenderTexture that a full-screen canvas (sorting 900) shows, so no part of it can show in the normal view. * Room (settings on PixelCollectionLog, header Gallery): a roomSize (20) square floor drawn as the first person NEON WIREFRAME grid (Resources/PixelNeonGrid.shader: the lines glow only near the lit points = you and each cube) and a boundsSize (10) square you cannot leave (a faint wire wall + floor outline marks it). * Items: rows (Dragon Cubes, God Cubes, Prime Cubes, the Cubelette) of real models (PixelClicker.CreateDisplayPixel) floating, turning and bobbing, each with its own point light (only the nearest few lights are on at once). A cube you have not found is a dark '???' block with no light. The cube you look at shows its name and text at the bottom. * Darkness: the scene's directional lights are switched off while you are inside, the ambient light / reflections / fog are forced to black / off (restored on leaving), the camera clears to black. * Controls: mouse looks, W A S D / arrows walk, Shift runs, Esc leaves (PixelWindows entry); the game's own action keys are ignored meanwhile (PixelKeys.Pressed / Held check Active) and clicks on the cube are blocked.
--- PixelCollectionLog.cs ---
PixelCollectionLog.cs The Collection Log: a book of the rare collectibles (the seven Dragon Cubes, the three God Cubes, the six Prime Cubes and the Prime Cubelette). Component added by PixelClicker.Awake; opened by the small BOOK button at the top-left of the Log window (PixelLog.BuildUI -> PixelCollectionLog.Toggle()); Escape / the X close it (PixelWindows priority 70). A spread of two pages: a title page (with how many are found), then one entry per page. Click the right page to turn forward, the left page to turn back (the page squashes like a turning leaf). An entry is '???' (name, text and a black silhouette of its picture) until the cube is KNOWN = its tier's totalCollected > 0 (lifetime total, so spending it - the wish, the Gauntlet - never hides it). GALLERY: every entry page has a small CAMERA button (top right of the page) that walks you into PixelCollectionGallery (a pitch black first person room with all the cubes floating and lit, built on entering and destroyed on leaving); the settings are under the Gallery header here, and leaving brings the book back on the same page. Pictures are PixelTierPicture (a real model, turning). To add an entry: add a line to Entries (type + flavour text). The book is a standard Fixed window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, styled: false so it keeps its plain leather cover; no window title, the pages have their own): the standard grey X sits in the leather band at the top, and the pages start below the standard header height.
--- PixelCombo.cs ---
PixelCombo.cs Combo meter (shop upgrade "Combo Meter", UpgradeEffect.ComboMeter, 5 levels; level value = that tier's multiplier): quick manual clicks build a combo (breaks after 1.2 s idle). Tiered multiplier: tierCombos {10, 50, 200, 500, 1000} / tierMultipliers {1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5}; the highest tier whose combo count is reached (and that the upgrade level unlocked: UnlockedTiers = tiers whose multiplier <= maxMultiplier) sets Multiplier (x1 below tier 1; Tier); sets PixelClicker.ManualClickBonus every frame (manual payouts only); meter at the bottom-centre shows the next milestone (nextFormat): INSIDE the bottom black bar by default (inBottomBar, barPadding: PlaceMeter scales the 'Meter Root' holder - meter + every tier effect - to fit the bar's height and centres it there; off = just above the bar at meterBottomMargin; MeterTopOffset follows). Fades out after hideAfterSeconds without manual clicks. It is the last thing to arrive after entering the game (UpdateEntrance: waits for the title screen, the camera intro and the sliding margins / buttons to finish plus entranceDelaySeconds (0 = 0.6 s), then fades in over entranceFadeSeconds (0 = 1.2 s); disableEntranceFade). Tier effects (by reached tier, cumulative, runtime-drawn UI sprites): 1 mild shake, 2 yellow flame aura (UpdateFlames, pooled sprites around the edges), 3 yellow glow, 4 violent shake (violentShakeFactor) + lightning bolts (UpdateLightning), 5 explosion (flash + sparks, repeats every explosionRepeatSeconds) + super-saiyan hair spikes (UpdateHair); when the combo breaks the effects wind down over breakSeconds (shake amplitude falls, glow / flames fade, the hair drops, the meter flashes white: breakFlash*), then the meter fades out as usual; tuned in the 'Combo Effects (by tier)' Inspector group. PixelShop finds/adds it and applies it via SetUpgrade.
--- PixelConsumableGallery.cs ---
PixelConsumableGallery.cs The 3D consumables window: opened by the square menu button between the Consumables tab's drop-down and its search box (PixelUI). A large Fixed window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow) that closes every other window when it opens (PixelWindows.CloseAllExcept) and shows the consumables the player holds (PixelConsumables.ShownInInventory, the Inventory's own rule) as a grid of tiles with a REAL 3D model each (PixelModelPicture: devices from PixelConsumables.CreateDisplayModel, potions / seeds / anything without a model as the block they belong to), the name and how many. A drop-down filters: All / Devices / Potions / Seeds / Supplies (PixelConsumables.CategoryOf). * HOVER a tile: it turns, and a box beside the pointer shows its description (PixelConsumables.ItemDescription). * LEFT-CLICK: the window closes and the item is used (PixelConsumables.TryUseItem: a device goes straight into placing, a potion is drunk). * RIGHT-CLICK: a solo window ABOVE it with a large turning model and the description in a box underneath (and a Use button). X / Escape closes it. Tiles only redraw while hovered (each picture is drawn once otherwise); every studio is destroyed when the window closes (ReleaseAll). Escape closes the solo window first, then this one (PixelWindows 36 / 35).
