--- PixelContainerHose.cs ---
PixelContainerHose.cs CONTAINER HOSE (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Container Hose', placed as a ROUTE: start, any bend points, end): a see-through hose like the Funnel's that joins one device to another THROUGH COUPLINGS (PixelSockets.cs / PixelCoupling.cs, docs/systems/PixelCouplings.md). Click an OUT coupling on a device a hose can pull from (IPixelHuskSource: Storage Crate, Quantum Organizer - the crate's 'out' socket, or an organizer coupling set to Out with its teal dot), then an IN coupling on the target (Recycler back hatch, Storage Crate, Quantum Organizer, Trash Can, Kiln, Sorting Hopper...); clicks magnetize to couplings AND free sockets (Snap). An end on a FREE socket fits a coupling there the way the hose runs (AUTO-FIT, PixelCouplings.FreeSocketAt); when placed, a Both coupling with nothing else attached turns to match. If the player later flips an end's coupling (its teal dot), the hose FOLLOWS (runs the other way) when it can, else it stops and says why. Between them the player clicks any number of BEND points (scroll sets their height; PixelFeatureTester.RegisterRoute) and the hose runs through them as a smooth Catmull-Rom curve (straight with none) - no forced arc. Routed the other way round it runs the right way anyway. An idle hose says why over its middle (IdleReason). A quick RIGHT CLICK on a placed hose (PixelRightTap; a hold removes it) REVERSES its flow (Reverse): only when the far end can hand husks out (a crate / organizer) and the start can take them in; Both couplings flip to match. Every pumpSeconds (0.5 s) it pulls one stored husk out of the source (TakeHuskOut), spawns it at the hose's start (SpawnStoredPixel) and carries it along the tube (PixelPathCarrier, can't be grabbed on the way). At the far end the target coupling hands it to its device (PixelCoupling.TakeIn -> the device's one hook AcceptHusk); refused (full) = it drops out of the end. It only pumps while the target has room (IPixelHuskRoom.HuskRoom; devices without it take 6 at a time), counting what is in the tube, and only takes out husks the target TAKES (IPixelHuskFilter, e.g. the Biomass Generator: wood) when the source can pick (IPixelHuskPicker). The ends follow their couplings (carried / turned devices). A coupling that is removed leaves its end where it was: the hose idles and latches onto a coupling put back there (every half second). Removing the hose lets what is inside it fall out. Saved with the tester save (both end points and the bend points).
--- PixelControlLever.cs ---
PixelControlLever.cs CONTROL LEVER (2026-10-20, a Dev Hub > Tester feature 'Control Lever'): a floor lever you put down BESIDE a conveyor belt, on either side, anywhere along it (the ghost snaps next to the belt under the mouse, on the side the mouse is on, turned the belt's way). LEFT-CLICK it to flip it: - ON (handle forward, green lamp): nothing happens, the belts run. - OFF (handle back, red lamp): its belt's whole LINE stops - every belt joined to it end to end, either way round, corners included (PixelConveyor.CollectLine) - by calling PixelConveyor.HoldHalt a few times a second (a belt's speed is 0 while halted: riders wait in place, the chevrons stop). Click again and they all run on. Two levers on one line: either one OFF stops it. SECTIONS (2026-10-20): with TWO OR MORE Belt Sorters on the line, a lever only switches its own SECTION - the belts between the sorters' joints (PixelConveyor.CollectSection); with one sorter or none it switches the whole line - unless both ends of the line are connected (a coupling, an elevator): then one sorter already cuts it into two sections. It finds its belt again by position when needed (a belt laid again in the same spot still counts). Saved with the tester save (position, turn, on / off). Built with PixelFactoryKit; its body has colliders so it can be clicked (PixelPointerClaim blocks the cube click while the mouse is on it).
--- PixelConveyor.cs ---
PixelConveyor.cs HEIGHT / RAMPS: belt ends (a, b) are full 3D points - the y is the belt's underside. While placing, the scroll wheel raises / lowers the point being placed (PixelFeatureTester.UpdateLine, steps of half a cube, ClampEnd keeps the slope under about 37 degrees; NO OVERLAPS (2026-10-24, PlaceProblem / OverlapProblem): a new piece may not lie over or cross a belt at the same height - meeting end to end, at corners and at a Combiner inlet is fine, a bridge a cube higher is fine); posts (BuildSupports) stand on the floor (FloorY) under a raised belt. Riders measure their place along the GROUND (Length = horizontal length), PointAt gives the sloped height, RunSpeed / LiftFactor adjust speed and rest height for the slope. Snap() matches belt ends and elevator tops by SCREEN distance, so raised ends can be snapped to; FindNext only chains belts whose heights match. The Conveyor Elevator (PixelElevator.cs) lifts pixels between heights. CORNERS: where belts chain at an angle the outside of the turn is filled with a ROUNDED corner (an arc, a curved rail and a turntable plate; PixelConveyorJoint.cs). COUPLING SHROUDS (2026-10-19): a belt end on a coupling's port gets a solid box round it reaching into the machine (RefreshShrouds), tinted like the machine; the coupling's flange is hidden under it, so the belt runs seamlessly into the machine. CONTROL LEVER (2026-10-20, PixelControlLever): a lever switched OFF beside a belt halts its SECTION (CollectSection: the whole joined line - CollectLine - while it has fewer than two Belt Sorters, else only the part between the sorters' joints) by calling HoldHalt every few frames; Speed is 0 while Halted, so riders wait, the chevrons stop and nothing is carried. JOINTS: a Belt Sorter placed mid-belt splits the belt there (SplitAt). NO FADING ON A BELT (2026-10-19): a rider's lifetime is paused every physics step (OldPixelDespawn.KeepAlive), so husks on belts never despawn; it runs on once they leave. MATERIALS (2026-10-20): every belt part, highlight, shroud and the placing ghost use PixelFactoryKit's SHARED materials (rebuilt belts no longer leak). PLACING TINT (2026-10-20): the ghost's free end shows a green disc when it will join a belt start / In coupling, red when something is there but it won't. LOOK (2026-10-19): rubber belt with moving chevrons and slat seams, steel rails with bolts on a dark frame with a yellow trim line, rollers with end caps. SHOP: the Conveyor Belt Kit (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.ConveyorBelt) is sold in Utilities for everyone since 2026-10-19 (monochrome price). WIDTH: PixelConveyor.LaneCount = 1 cube wide, 2 at Electrician level 10, 3 at level 20 (every belt, PixelConveyorSystem.Update rebuilds them when it changes); the belt panel's SIZE stepper (2026-10-20) sets a belt's own lane count up to that maximum (SetLanes / WantedLanes, saved with the belt). Each rider travels in a LANE (belt-local x, PixelConveyor.LaneOffset / NearestLane): a pixel is captured into the lane it landed in, riders keep their gap per lane, and a hand-on to the next belt picks the nearest lane there. CONVEYOR BELT (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Conveyor Belt'). Placing: click a point, then click another: a belt is built between the two (the second point starts the next belt, so a series of short straight pieces makes a curve; a right click stops placing; the pieces already laid stay). Clicks near the end of an existing belt snap to it. RIGHT CLICK a placed belt (not while placing) to reverse its direction (PixelConveyorSystem). A belt is a low solid slab with side rails and moving chevrons that show the way. LOCKED RIDING (no physics): an old pixel that lands on a belt (not held, not glued into a build, not flying, and there is room: riders keep a gap of 1.1 cube widths) is CAPTURED by PixelConveyorSystem - made kinematic, centred on the line and carried along it at beltSpeedCubes (0 = 1.6 cube widths per second) by position, like the Storage Crate locks pixels in. At the end of a belt it is handed on to a belt that STARTS there (a chained piece, ends within 0.45 cube widths and not head-on); when there is no belt to hand it to, the end is detected: an In COUPLING whose port is there (PixelSockets.cs / PixelCoupling.cs, docs/systems/PixelCouplings.md) hands the pixel to its device - when that device refuses it (full, busy) the pixel WAITS at the end and the queue behind it stops (2026-10-20; it used to be thrown off); with nothing there it is EJECTED: physics back on, thrown on a little along the belt's direction (so it can fly into a Kiln mouth or onto the floor). END STATUS: the belt panel says what happens at the end (EndStatus) and a red ring floats over an end that spilled husks in the last few seconds. Riders queue when the one ahead is stopped. Flipping a belt flips its riders; grabbing a rider with Pixel Grabbing frees it. Things spat out of a machine or dropped by the player simply land on a belt and get captured. Belts are saved with the tester save (shape incl. heights, riders). Built with PixelFactoryKit. SPEED: each belt can have its own speed - in FIRST PERSON a right click on a belt opens a small window (PixelConveyorSystem speed editor: a number box in cube widths per second, Set / Reverse / Close; the mouse is freed while it is open). In the normal view a right CLICK opens the shared device panel on the belt's SECTION (2026-10-20, PixelConveyorPanel: speed stepper, Reverse, remove the piece or the section; the section glows).
--- PixelConveyorCombiner.cs ---
PixelConveyorCombiner.cs CONVEYOR COMBINER (2026-10-19, a Dev Hub > Tester feature 'Conveyor Combiner'): a box that sits ON a conveyor belt anywhere along it (snaps to the belt under the mouse, like the Belt Sorter). Husks riding the belt pass straight through it. Each SIDE has an inlet: a belt that ENDS at an inlet (belts snap there: SnapPoints, so the feeding belt comes in square to the main one) hands its husks in and they are MERGED onto the main belt at the box (PixelConveyorSystem.TryMerge) as soon as there is a gap there - the two feeds become one. While there is no gap they wait at the inlet (up to queueSize; kept alive), then the feeding belt holds its husks as at any full device. SOCKETS (2026-10-20, sockets are the only husk connection path): each inlet is a FITTED, bare Husk Intake socket ('inL' / 'inR', IPixelSocketDevice; the box's own lip is the fitting: built in, not saved, not removable, made again when the hood is rebuilt for a new belt width). A feeding belt ending there hands its husks in through PixelCouplings.HandIn -> AcceptHusk. SHOP (2026-10-20): sold in Utilities (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.ConveyorCombiner, a PixelInstantItems tool: right-click starts this placing, one used up per box). The spot is a distance along the belt (it follows the belt). When its belt is removed the box goes back into the backpack (refunded, LoseBelt). Saved with the tester save (position, turn).
--- PixelConveyorJoint.cs ---
PixelConveyorJoint.cs INSIDE TRIMS (2026-10-19): the two belts' inner rails / frames / trims are cut back to where their inner edges cross (PixelConveyor.SetTrim), the end rollers there are hidden and the chevrons stop short, so nothing runs across the other belt. GEOMETRIC (2026-10-19): a corner is where a belt's b END meets another belt's end (PixelConveyor.CornerPartner), whichever way either runs, so a single reversed piece keeps its rounded corners (pixels are still only handed on along the flow: FindNext). ROUNDED (2026-10-19): the filler is a round SECTOR about the meeting point (outer edge an arc, a curved rail of short pieces along it) with a round turntable plate over the meeting point, so chained belts turn in a smooth curve instead of a mitred corner. CORNER FILLER for chained conveyor belts: where a belt hands on to the belt that starts at its end (PixelConveyor.FindNext) and the two are not in a straight line, the square-ish gap on the OUTSIDE of the turn is filled with a slab mesh shaped by the angle (polygon: this belt's end, its outer corner, the point where the two outer edges meet, the next belt's outer corner, the next belt's start; with a sharp turn where the edges meet far away it is a simple wedge) plus matching outer rails. Built by PixelConveyorJoint.Refresh (called by each belt every 0.5 s and after it is rebuilt; it only rebuilds when the two belts' ends / heights / widths change) as a child of the belt, so it goes with the belt. Purely visual: pixels are handed from belt to belt as before. Straight chains and head-on ends get no joint.
--- PixelConveyorPanel.cs ---
PixelConveyorPanel.cs The BELT PANEL (2026-10-20, Devices session): a quick RIGHT CLICK on a conveyor belt (normal view; PixelConveyorSystem) opens the shared device panel (PixelDeviceCard.OpenSubject - the same window as every placed device) on that belt's SECTION: the belts a Control Lever would switch with it (PixelConveyor.CollectSection - the whole joined line, or the part between two Belt Sorter joints). While the panel is open the section GLOWS (PixelConveyor.SetHighlight), so you see what you are editing. The sheet: how many pieces and how long, the speed, the lane count (Electrician), husks riding, stopped by a lever or not, and a STATUS line (PixelConveyor.EndStatus: running, feeding a machine, waiting for a full / busy machine, or spilling and why - nothing attached, another height, head-on). A SPEED stepper (- / +, speedStep cube widths a second, the whole section), a SIZE stepper (lanes 1..the Electrician maximum: PixelConveyor.SetLanes), actions REVERSE (every piece of the section turns round) and REMOVE THIS PIECE (hovering it makes only that piece glow, red), and the panel's own Remove button takes the WHOLE SECTION away (asks once). A right HOLD on a belt still removes just that piece (PixelFeatureTester). Not a MonoBehaviour: one small object per opened panel (the settings below are constants for now).
--- PixelCoupling.cs ---
PixelCoupling.cs The COUPLING: the ONE snap-on piece that joins belts and hoses to a device's SOCKET (PixelSockets.cs; design docs/systems/PixelCouplings.md). - AUTO-FIT: couplings are not bought - one is fitted when a belt end / hose end attaches at a free socket (PixelCouplings.AutoFitAt / AutoFitAll). - Fit(device, socket): a child of the device's SocketRoot at the socket's port, facing out of it, so it moves / turns with the device (carry handles) and goes when the device goes. Built with PixelFactoryKit: a flange tinted a little with the device's colour (tintShare of SocketTint), a collar with a dark opening, a lip at belt height, and a flow ARROW over it that drifts the way husks go - green pointing INTO the device = In, orange pointing OUT = Out. A coupling on a Both socket (Storage Crate, Quantum Organizer) shows a small teal dot: LEFT-CLICK the DOT to switch In / Out (only the dot is hit-tested; PixelPointerClaim.ClaimButton keeps the cube and Pixel Grabbing out of that click). Its parts are named "Coupling ..." so the device's own hover / remove check (PixelPlacedDevice.CountsForHit) leaves them out. - AUTO DIRECTION (socket.autoDirection, the Junction Box): no teal dot and no click switch; the device calls SetAuto(In / Out / Idle) from what is attached; idle shows no arrow and counts both ways (IsIn and IsOut) so belts and hoses can snap to it. - TakeIn(husk) = the device's ONE hook (IPixelSocketDevice.AcceptHusk); PortWorld / FacingWorld = where belts meet it. - Fitted couplings (socket.fitted: the Elevator and Belt Sorter, which are belt fittings themselves) are built with the piece (FitBuiltIn), are not saved and cannot be removed on their own. - Keeps the same world size when its device rescales (the Recycler's Bin Size): KeepSize every frame. - PLACING by hand (dev only: Dev Hub > Tester > 'Coupling'): the ghost (CreateGhost) shows a teal ring on every FREE socket in view and magnetizes to the nearest one (TrySnapGhost: by screen distance, its arrow shows that socket's direction); a click fits it there (PlaceAt). Hold the right button on a placed coupling to remove it (the tester's delete). Saved with the device (PixelCouplings.Export / Import).
--- PixelCraftingBook.cs ---
PixelCraftingBook.cs Part of PixelCrafting (partial class): the Recipe Book side panel (known recipes, search, rows) and closing the window. The system notes are in PixelCrafting.cs.
--- PixelCraftingCube.cs ---
PixelCraftingCube.cs CRAFTING CUBE (2026-10-20, Devices session): a crafting machine. Placed from the CRAFTING WINDOW's 'Crafting Cube' button (it closes the window and starts placing through PixelFeatureTester.StartFeature("Crafting Cube"); also in Dev Hub > Tester). A carved block with glowing edges, ONE coupling socket on each side (all In) and a spell BOOK floating above it. HOVERING the cube opens the book (its cover swings open, pages fan); moving away closes it. RIGHT-CLICK (a quick tap; a right HOLD still removes it like every tester object) opens the recipe picker (PixelCraftingCubeWindow) - the crafting window's recipes (PixelCrafting.MachineRecipes). HOW IT CRAFTS: the recipe's PIXEL ingredients are paid in HUSKS of that type, by value (each husk adds its own amount, OldPixelInfo.amount) - fed in through its couplings (belts, hoses) or dropped on its top. Husks of other types are refused (a belt throws them on). It keeps up to storeMultiple x what one craft needs per type. As soon as every pixel ingredient is covered it crafts (PixelCrafting.MachineCraft: potion / device ingredients come out of the inventory, the result goes INTO the inventory, it counts for the recipe book and crafting XP) at most once per craftSeconds. Prices are the crafting window's (PricedRecipe). A ring over the book shows how close the next craft is; hovering shows what it makes and what it still needs. Saved with the tester save (position, turn, recipe, the husk value stored).
--- PixelCraftingCubeWindow.cs ---
PixelCraftingCubeWindow.cs The Crafting Cube's RECIPE PICKER (2026-10-20): opened by a right-click tap on a Crafting Cube (PixelCraftingCube). A standard Fixed window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, its own canvas above the device panel, Escape closes it: PixelWindows 60) with a scrolling list of the crafting window's recipes (PixelCrafting.MachineRecipes): each row shows the result's picture (potions: their block's PixelTierPicture), its name and the two ingredients at today's prices; the cube's current recipe is highlighted. Clicking a row sets it (PixelCraftingCube.SetRecipe) and closes the window. The cube calls Changed(this) when its recipe changes and Forget(this) when it is removed.

--- PixelCrashLog.cs ---
PixelCrashLog.cs Local crash / error reports (added by PixelClicker.Awake). One text file per session in <persistentDataPath>/CrashReports/report_<date>.txt (header with versions + hardware, then every Error / Exception / Assert as it happens via Application.logMessageReceivedThreaded: message, stack trace, a cached game-state summary and the last recentLines log lines; repeated errors are counted; size and file count capped). A running.marker file is created at start and deleted on a clean quit; if it is still there at the next start the previous session crashed / froze, the new report says so and a notice box appears. The pause menu has a Report Folder button (PixelCrashLog.OpenFolder). Testers just send the .txt files. Also writes rendering_report.txt (GPU, render pipeline, quality levels, which shaders Shader.Find can see, glow setup via PixelClicker.DebugGlowReport) at start and on F10.
--- PixelCubeIcon.cs ---
PixelCubeIcon.cs UI MaskableGraphic drawing a tiny spinning shaded 3D cube (no camera/render texture) - achievement icons. Redraws at most PixelTierPicture.MaxFps times a second (the shared redraw limit, PixelTierPicture.Due) and not at all while culled (scrolled out of a masked list); the vertex buffers are shared, so a redraw allocates nothing.
--- PixelCubeScreenSpot.cs ---
PixelCubeScreenSpot.cs Puts the clickable cube at a spot on the screen (a viewport point, 0..1) at the same distance from the camera as its home spot, so it keeps its size: PixelClicker.SetCubeOffset(world - CubeBasePosition). Used by the cube minigames that move the cube around the screen (Cube Blink, Cube Dash, Cube Evasion) and the Whale fight (PixelWhaleFight). Why: the same four lines sat in each of them (Code Health 'duplicate' round, 2026-10-24).
--- PixelCubieBill.cs ---
PixelCubieBill.cs CUBIE'S BILL - the progression spine (design: docs/GameDesign.md section 6 'Cubie's bill - the progression spine', agreed 2026-10-23). Cubie owns the only shop in existence and the player owes him for everything: ONE huge bill per PURE colour (White, Gray, Black, Red, Green, Blue; billPerColour, shown as an absurd 25,000,000,000 each - the joke). He takes money only. The player pays any amount at any time in the Bill window (PixelCubieBillWindow: one row per colour, the bar shows progress to the NEXT milestone, not to the absurd total). - MILESTONES (an Inspector list, filled with the built-in ones when empty): an amount PER COLOUR (25K / 2.5M / 250M / 25B, spaced x100 because income grows exponentially), an optional light condition (a skill level), what it opens, and Cubie's lines. Reaching milestone n = TRUST TIER n: CurrentTier is the ONE query everything asks; TierUnlocked(tier) is raised once per tier (Cubie talks: PixelGuideVendor.RequestTalk; the event log announces it). The last milestone = the bill is settled (prestige later, not built). - TRUST = MACHINES AND STOCK ONLY (decided 2026-10-24): every skill pack is on sale from Tier 0 (gathering skills in the first hour; making / operation skills open by their input skills' levels, not by trust), and trust NEVER caps a skill's level. - TRUST OPENS HIS SHOP: itemStock (device kind -> tier) and packStock (a pack by its reward pixel type or skill id -> tier) are the ONE table of what each tier puts on sale. The shop asks TierListed (hidden until the bill is revealed, so the early shop shows no mystery locks) and TierMet (on sale) through its shared requirement checks (PixelShop.IsPackRequirementMet / IsRequirementMet, PixelShopConsumables.ItemLocked); a locked row says 'Cubie's trust: Tier N'. lockShopByTier off = everything on sale. - THE TAG AND THE REVEAL: until the bill is revealed every Recycler wears Cubie's paper price tag ('Property of Cubie.', device card: 'Terms: see reverse. Amount owed: ???'; PixelRecycler builds it while TagShown). The reveal is Cubie's visit 3, the reward of a goal (PixelGoals rewardCubieVisit 3, built-in row 'billreveal'; without such a row: revealAfterMinutes of play after visit 2). He yanks the tag, it unrolls into an absurd invoice, he explains the monopoly, and the Bill window opens (Reveal). With Cubie or tips off the bill still arrives at the same point, quietly. Interest is only talk: the amounts never grow by themselves. - The Log's Goals tab pins the next milestone (PixelLog.CollectGoals -> NextGoal). The shop's header has an 'IOU' button once revealed. - PROGRESS (paid per colour by pixel type, tier, revealed) lives in PixelGameState.Current.cubieBill (GameState.cs, moved 2026-10-24): saved / loaded / reset with it; it is in place at the START of a load, so Recyclers rebuilt during the load already see TagShown right. Dev Hub widget 'Cubie's bill': Reveal / next tier / reset.
--- PixelCubieBillWindow.cs ---
PixelCubieBillWindow.cs The BILL WINDOW of Cubie's bill (PixelCubieBill, 2026-10-23): a standard Fixed window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, its own canvas above the shop, Escape closes it: PixelWindows 45). Opened at the end of Cubie's reveal visit and by the shop's 'IOU' header button. On top: the absurd total owed per colour (the joke) and the trust tier. Then one row per pure colour: name, a bar showing progress to the NEXT milestone (never to the absurd total, which would sit at 0% for hours), 'paid / needed', then the payment controls (2026-10-23): a SLIDER (0..100% of what you can pay: what you hold, capped at what is left of the bill) and an ENTRY BOX kept in step with it (type an amount like 1500, 1,500, 2.5K or 3M - PixelClicker.TryParseAmount; Enter pays), a 'Pay' button that pays the box's amount, and 'Pay max' (up to the next milestone's share, or the rest of the bill once every milestone is met). After a payment the slider and box reset. At the bottom: what the next tier opens and its condition. Refreshed on PixelCubieBill.Paid / TierUnlocked and twice a second while open (the held amounts change as you play) - no per-frame text building.
--- PixelCubiePeek.cs ---
PixelCubiePeek.cs Cubie peeks out from behind the Save / Load window (component added to the pause menu by PixelPauseMenu.BuildSaveLoadPanel, Setup(panelRect, isActive)): his head (a trimmed copy of the guide's drawing) is a sibling placed directly UNDER the window in the canvas order, so the box covers the rest of him; while the window is open and quiet (no rename / overwrite / result overlay) and there has been no mouse movement or click for idleSeconds (1, to be tuned later) plus a random extraDelay, he slides out of a random edge (left / right / top, random spot along it, showFraction of his head visible, slideSeconds), his pupils dart about (Glance) for stayRange seconds, then he ducks back; any mouse movement / click makes him duck at once; cooldownRange seconds before the next peek. Unscaled time. The slot rows in that window are now exactly as wide as the Save / Load buttons (the scroll bar sits in the margin). PixelCubiePeek.cs (more) The Cubie peek is now also on the Settings screen and the Key Bindings screen (PixelPauseMenu.AddCubiePeek(panel): one PixelCubiePeek component per panel, active while that panel is open).
--- PixelCustomKeys.cs ---
PixelCustomKeys.cs Custom keybinds: bind any item (a potion, a device, a seed, a supply...) or tool (the Pixel hose, the docked menu buttons) to a key of your own. Component added by PixelClicker.Awake; it fires the keys (Update: not while paused, on the title screen, typing or capturing a key) by doing what a My Window favourite does: item:<name> -> PixelConsumables.TryUseItem (drinks the potion, starts placing the device), tool:<id> -> PixelMyWindow.TryGetTool (the hose, or PixelMenuKeys.Click). One potion per key is fine (a black potion on one key, a white one on another). The bindings (target + KeyCode) are saved per save file as JSON in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.CustomKeys (listed in PixelSettingsSync, ReloadFromPrefs). A key used by one of the normal actions (Settings > Key Bindings) is refused; a key used by another custom binding moves to the new one. Escape cancels a capture, Backspace / Delete clears the key. The window is the KEYBINDS TAB of My Window (PixelMyWindow builds a container and calls BuildView; Refresh / SetVisible keep it current): a search bar, a drop-down of everything bindable, then one row per binding (name, key box, X removes it). PICKING an item in the drop-down (or pressing Enter in the search bar = its first match) opens a small KEY PROMPT over the tab: press a key, it is shown big (pressing another replaces it; a key of the normal actions is refused there, a key of another custom binding says it will move), Confirm adds the binding to the list (no Add button), Cancel / Escape closes it. A row's key box opens the same prompt to change that binding (Backspace clears it).
--- PixelDashMinigame.cs ---
PixelDashMinigame.cs Cube Dash minigame (id dash, pack unlocksMinigame = "dash", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack, 50K each; NOT a TakeoverActive minigame - the cube must stay clickable - but Takeover => true so the limiter keeps every other minigame from starting while it runs): every minInterval..maxInterval s the cube dashes (PixelClicker.SetCubeOffset(world offset) / CubeBasePosition, applied in AnimatePixel) between random spots inside the area* screen fractions: dashSeconds dash, dwellMin..Max s hanging at the spot drifting in a Perlin wiggle (wiggleRadius), then the next spot (at least minHop away); after roundSeconds it zips home and pays rewardMultiplier (10) x the pixel's worth per click (amountPerClick x PayoutMultiplier x ClickMultiplier / clicksToCollect) via AddCurrency on top of the clicks' normal pay. Only manual clicks count (PixelCollected / PixelHit with automatic false); the auto clicker is off meanwhile (PixelMinigame.AutoClickerPaused, read by PixelAutoClicker). The cube stays the pixel it was (SetForcedSpawnTier, skipped when a potion already forces one; a Dragon Cube / meteor falls back to White). Stats dash.rounds/clicks/best, tip minigame_dash, sounds dash_start/_win/_lose (silent until clips are assigned).
--- PixelDatabase.cs ---
PixelDatabase.cs / PixelDatabaseWindow.cs Game Database (opened by the Database tab of the pause menu's Game Guide screen, PixelPauseMenu.BuildGuideScreen -> PixelDatabaseWindow.Toggle(); the window's canvas (520) sits above the pause menu and Escape closes it first, CloseIfOpen): a search engine over the live game data. PixelDatabase.Build() (run each time the window opens) makes one Entry per pixel (name + the Pixel Guide text + pay / clicks / weight), shop pack (PixelShop.AllPacks), skill (perks + tree nodes), consumable (PixelConsumables.ItemName/ItemDescription/ItemCosts), defined recipe (PixelCrafting.DefinedRecipes), minigame / vendor (PixelMinigame.All), achievement and tip (PixelHints.AllHints + the lines of Resources/HowToPlay.txt and Controls.txt), and TWO- WAY References between them (pack rewards / costs / requirements / unlocks, skill blocks / filler / hazard, recipe ingredients, achievement pixel, and 'mentioned in tip' by whole-word name match). PixelDatabase.Search(query, max): every word must appear in the entry's name / category / description / reference text; ranked name-exact > starts-with > contains > category > text. The window (canvas 108, PixelWindows 16, PixelPop): search box + results list on the left, the selected entry's description and its references on the right (click a reference to jump to it, '< Back' returns). Not covered yet: floors / skies / pets / vendors' wares. SOURCES (2026-10-24): Build no longer holds one long hand-written list. Every system component that implements IPixelDatabaseSource (found in the scene each time, one per component type) hands in STEPS with an order (PixelDatabase.PixelsOrder ... GoalsOrder); the steps run in that order on one Builder (Add / Link + the lookups earlier steps filled: Pixels by type, Minigames by id, Packs by index, Skills by id, Items by name, VendorWares, Texts). Owners: PixelClicker (pixels, PixelClickerDatabase.cs), PixelShop (packs, pack->skill links, Operation 'Upgrade' entries, PixelShopDatabase.cs), PixelSkills (skills + tree nodes, PixelSkillsDatabase.cs), PixelMinigame.AddToDatabase (each minigame; PixelVisitorMinigame adds its wares), PixelBlockRules, PixelPets (PixelPetsDatabase.cs), PixelCrafting (recipes), PixelAchievements, PixelGoals, and the 'Special' entries: PixelWhaleFight (whale fight), PixelTreasureMap (treasure map), PixelDragonWish (the wish + every wish, from its AllWishes). Kept here as built-in steps: the minigame loop, consumables, vendors' wares links, floor / sky looks, settings, and tips + guide lines + the 'mentions' pass (TipsOrder: everything added earlier can be mentioned, except the NotMentioned categories). A NEW kind of game data: implement IPixelDatabaseSource on its system (pick an order after whatever it links to; before TipsOrder if tips should mention it) and, for a new category, add its colour in CategoryColor and a tab in PixelDatabaseWindow if it needs one. A step that throws is logged with its source and the others still run. BROWSE / PICTURES / DISCOVERY: the window has category tabs under the search box (All, Pixels, Packs, Skills, Items, Recipes, Minigames, Awards, Tips; PixelDatabase.Search(query, max, category, showAll)), a turning 3D picture of the pixel (PixelTierPicture, Entry.tierIndex) beside a pixel's description, and a 'Show ???' switch. Entry.discovered: pixel = unlocked or collected, shop pack = bought or a reward pixel unlocked, skill = PixelSkills.IsAvailable, consumable = its required pixel unlocked; recipes, minigames, achievements and tips are always shown. An undiscovered entry is listed as '???' (name, text, picture and references hidden, searchable only by its category) unless 'Show ???' is on; other entries' references to it read 'Label: ???'. MORE ENTRIES: Pet (one per pixel type that can have a pet; discovered = owned; numbers from PixelPets.SpawnWeightFactor / DespawnSlowDown / FindChancePerClick), Floor and Sky (the looks of PixelFloor / PixelSkybox, IPixelLookSource) and vendors' wares (PixelVisitorMinigame.WareNames -> 'Sells' / 'Sold by' links on minigame + consumable entries). RULES / NODES: 'Block rule' entries (PixelBlockRules special-block and hold-block rules; text = PixelBlockRules.GuideNotes, links to the pixel, the blocks it replaces, the boost block, the hazard it clears and the skill) and 'Skill node' entries (every PixelSkills.TreeNode: description, cost, 'coming soon', links to its skill, the node it needs and the block toggle it unlocks); both are discovered when their pixel / skill is. SETTINGS: 'Setting' entries = every row of the pause menu's Settings screen (PixelPauseMenu.SettingsCatalog reads the BUILT rows: section, label, tick box / slider / drop-down / look picker / button, drop-down choices, current value), so screen filters, day / night length, floor off modes and every other option can be found by searching; the Floor / Sky rows link to each look. PIN: Entry.pinType / pinKey (pixel = currency row, skill, achievement, listable consumable = favorite) drive the window's 'Pin to My Window' button (PixelMyWindow.CanPin / IsPinned / TogglePin). Known leak: a discovered entry's reference text still contains the hidden name, so searching for that exact name finds the discovered entry. CHARACTERS (2026-10-23): every visitor (PixelVisitorMinigame) is a 'Character' entry under its own name (who they are, when they visit, a greeting line, what they sell; discovered once started), and Cubie adds his own (PixelGuideVendor, ExtrasOrder: visits, his bill, links to the Recycler and the visitors). Tab 'People'. MYSTERY BAR (2026-10-18): while PixelSkills.StandardHidden (Standard below level 2) every entry whose name or text says 'Standard' is undiscovered.
--- PixelDayNight.cs ---
PixelDayNight.cs Day / night cycle (added by PixelClicker.Awake; disableDayNight): TimeOfDay 0..1 (0 midnight, 0.5 noon; a new game starts at 0.38) advances with SCALED time (stops while paused / Time Stop) over LengthMinutes (Settings > Interface 'Length of a day': 4 / 8 / 15 / 30, default 8; switch 'Day / night cycle', default on; PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.DayNight / DayNightLength, per save) and drives the scene's sun (RenderSettings.sun or the first directional light: it rises on one side of the scene, passes OVERHEAD at noon and sets on the other (an arc in the vertical plane turned by the scene sun's own yaw, sunLean 0 = 0.18 tilts it a little); the moon takes the same arc back, a bit lower; warm near the horizon, moonColor at night, intensity down to minSunIntensity 0.3), the ambient light (minAmbient 0.55) and the sky material's _Tint / _SkyTint (nightSkyColor, duskSkyColor); everything is restored when switched off. Night never goes fully dark. PixelDayNight.Level (0 night .. 1 day) is there for other systems (not used yet). The time of day is the saved stat counter daynight.time (x10000).
--- PixelDaylight.cs ---
PixelDaylight.cs Solar's daylight bar (moved out of PixelMeteorSolar.cs into its own file, because Unity can only save a component in the scene when its class lives in a file of the same name). Every Solar click raises daylight and it slowly fades; while it is up seeds and Seed pets grow faster and hovering Solar husks keep it up. A small sun + bar at the top right shows it. Not saved.
--- PixelDebug.cs ---
PixelDebug.cs PixelDebug.Info(msg, ctx): diagnostic logging that is compiled out of release builds (Editor/dev builds only). Use it instead of Debug.Log for non-error messages. Also: PixelUIKit.SetText(label, text) skips identical strings; open windows (shop, Inventory, Log, stats) refresh on a timer (rowRefreshSeconds, refreshSeconds), not every frame; each class with static state has a [RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod] ResetStatics() (Enter Play Mode without domain reload) - add one when adding static state. PixelHints warns (editor only) once per unknown Trigger id.
--- PixelDevConsole.cs ---
PixelDevConsole.cs CONSOLE (Dev Hub tab 'Console'): the game's log while it runs - also in a built game, where there is no Unity console. Reads PixelLogBuffer. * Filter buttons All / Errors / Warnings / Logs (with counts) and a search box; newest line at the bottom (the list follows new lines while you are at the bottom). Errors red, warnings yellow. Click a line to show / hide its stack trace. A repeated line shows 'x3'. * 'Copy for Claude' copies the last lines shown (with stack traces for errors / warnings) plus the game-state summary of the crash reports (PixelCrashLog.GameStateText): paste that to Claude. 'Clear' empties the list. * Error badge: when an error happens and the Console isn't showing, a small red box '2 errors' appears at the top right of the screen (its own canvas); clicking it opens the Console on Errors. Switch it off with the tick box in the tab (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.Console.Badge). One big text with <link> tags instead of a row per line, so thousands of lines cost nothing; rebuilt at most 4 times a second, only while showing.
--- PixelDevDocs.cs ---
PixelDevDocs.cs The 'Docs' tab of the Dev Hub (PixelDevHub): the DOCUMENTATION section with four permanent sub-tabs: To-Do (0), Changelog (1), Notes (2) and Ideas (3). * To-Do and Changelog: PixelDevDocs only provides their content roots (PixelDevDocs.Root(0) / Root(1)); PixelDevTools fills them (Resources/Todo.txt, Resources/Changelog.txt) and rebuilds them when PixelDevDocs.Shown(sub) fires (the Docs tab is visible and that sub-tab is showing). * Notes: your own notes, typed in-game. A list of notes on the left ('+ New note'), the selected note's title and text on the right (multi-line, scrolls), Delete (a second click confirms). Saved automatically a moment after every change, when the Docs tab is hidden and when the game closes, to <persistentDataPath>/DevNotes.json (not part of the game save). * Ideas: Resources/Ideas.txt (the project's 'maybe someday' ideas, kept by Claude) shown read-only; '#' lines are headings, '- ' lines bullets. The selected sub-tab is remembered (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.DocsSub).
--- PixelDevEvents.cs ---
PixelDevEvents.cs EVENT TRIGGERS (Dev Hub tab 'Events'): fire the things that normally happen once or at random, to test their texts and looks. * Tips: pick any first-time tip (PixelHints.AllHints) and Show it again (PixelHints.ShowNow); 'Forget all seen tips' (PixelHints.ResetSeen) makes every tip show again as it happens; 'Did you know?' shows the next general tip now (PixelDidYouKnow.DevShowNow). * Tutorials: replay one tutorial - the whole tutorial (PixelHints.ReplayTutorial), Cubie's visits (PixelGuideVendor.Replay), the Toggles / Shop / Skills tutorials (their done-stats cleared, they start at the next calm moment) or the Woodcutting guide (PixelSkills.StartWoodGuide). * Popups: a skill level-up popup (PixelSkillLevelPopup.Show, the skill's next level) and an achievement popup (PixelAchievements.DevShowPopup) - nothing is granted. Your own text: show it as a tip box (PixelNotice) or as an event-log line (PixelHints.Announce) to see how a new text reads. Minigames / visitors and rare finds already have spawners on the Main tab (Minigames, Super Rares), so they are not repeated here.
--- PixelDevHub.cs ---
PixelDevHub.cs The DEV HUB: ONE window for every dev tool (opens with PixelAction.DevTools, F9, the pause menu's Dev Tools button; PixelDevTools.OpenPanel / Close delegate here). It replaces the old Dev Tools panel, the Inspector window and the Mock up window. * The window itself is a standard Tool window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow + PixelWindowChrome): centred title, grey X, drag by the header (kept between the black bars), minimize, dock to the left / right screen edge, the corner grip scales it; the search box sits at the left of the header (no PixelPop: the hub opens with its canvas). * FILING-CABINET TABS along the top: Main (PixelDevTools' widgets), To-Do, Changelog, Inspector (PixelDevInspector), Shapes (PixelDevShapes), UI Mock (PixelDevMockup), Looks (PixelLookStudio), Setups (PixelDevSetups), Events (PixelDevEvents), Sounds (PixelDevSounds), Console (PixelDevConsole), Models (PixelModelEditor); new tabs build with PixelDevUI. Drag a tab sideways to REARRANGE the tabs; drag it down out of the strip to POP IT OUT into its own floating window (drag that window by its header, '-' minimizes it); the 'Dock' button or dropping the floating window on the hub puts it back as a tab. Order, active tab, floating tabs + their places and the hub's open state are saved (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.Hub.*). * Tools get their content root with PixelDevHub.TabRoot(id) (a top-left anchored RectTransform of the tab's design size, 700 x 780 or 500 x 780 for the mockups) and learn when they are on screen through PixelDevHub.SetTabCallback(id, visible => ...): visible = the hub is open and the tab is the active docked tab (or a floating, un-minimized window). * the Main tab's draggable WIDGETS are a PixelCardBoard (PixelCardBoard.cs, shared with Pocket): each row sits on a card with a grip on its left; drag it to move the row, the others make room; the order is saved.
--- PixelDevInspector.cs ---
PixelDevInspector.cs 'Inspector' (Dev Tools > Tools tick box, Editor / dev builds like the rest of Dev Tools): ONE draggable, minimizable window to change values while playing. * World tab: the whole simulation. Time (timeScale, fixedDeltaTime, targetFrameRate, vSync), Physics (gravity incl. a quick 'Gravity Y', solver iterations, bounce / sleep thresholds, contact offset...), live counts (FPS, old pixels, rigidbodies, awake ones) and a drop-down of every game system in the scene (PixelShop, PixelFloor, PixelPets...) with a Select button that opens that system in the Selected tab. * Selected tab: 'Pick' mode (while on, a left click on anything with a collider selects it instead of clicking the cube: PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock), Parent / Active / Destroy buttons, a filter box and a foldable list of the object's components. Game scripts show EVERY field of a supported type (numbers, bool, text, enum, Vector2/3, Color; private runtime state included), Unity components show a fixed list (Transform, Rigidbody mass / drag / velocity..., Collider bounciness / friction, Light, Camera, Renderer, AudioSource). An old pixel also shows its tier's data (PixelTier: spawn weight, payout...). * All rows are built by reflection (Member = a getter + setter), values refresh a few times a second (not while you type), enum / bool rows are buttons. 'Copy edits' puts every change you made as 'Owner.field = value' lines on the clipboard so a good value can be put into the code. Nothing here is saved in the game save (only open / tab / minimized / position in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.Insp*). Edits last until the object or the session ends.
--- PixelDevMockWindow.cs ---
PixelDevMockWindow.cs Just a doorway now. The Mock up mode WINDOW is gone: the Shape placer (PixelDevShapes) and the UI mockup builder (PixelDevMockup) are tabs of the Dev Hub (PixelDevHub: 'Shapes' and 'UI Mock'). They still ask this class for their content roots (TabRoot 0 = Shapes, 1 = UI pieces) and for the height they may use (ContentHeight); the hub tells them when their tab is on screen (PixelDevShapes.SetActive / PixelDevMockup.SetActive) and whether the hub is open (PixelDevMockup.SetWindowOpen).
--- PixelDevProbe.cs ---
PixelDevProbe.cs Dev snapshot tools that hand Claude what it cannot see (all driven by PixelDevTools: keys PixelAction.MarkMoment F10 / InspectHover F11 and Tools tab buttons; Editor / development builds only). Reports go to <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots (Save) and the clipboard. MarkMoment: screenshot + a text report of this instant (screen, view, camera, the crash log's game-state summary and last log lines). UiLayoutDump: every active overlay canvas as a tree (name, rect in the 1920 x 1080 canvas space with the origin top-left like the mockup builder, text + font size, colours, button / toggle / slider flags, raycast targets) - the UI is built in code, so this is the only way to see it as text. Inspect: what is under the mouse right now - every UI object the pointer hits (topmost first, with its rect and look) and every collider along the ray (name, scripts, old pixel tier / amount), plus where the cursor meets the floor plane. Copies to the clipboard and shows a short notice. PixelBoundsSnapshot (the floor / view-bounds numbers) writes through the same Save.
--- PixelDevSetups.cs ---
PixelDevSetups.cs TEST SETUPS (Dev Hub tab 'Setups'): save the game as it is now under a name and jump back to it with one click, to test a feature without replaying or cheating your way back to the right state. * A setup is an ordinary save file (PixelSaveGame.SaveToFile / LoadFile) in <persistentDataPath>/TestSetups/<name>.json, with its screenshot next to it. Saving or loading a setup never touches the numbered save slots or the autosave list (a manual Save after loading still writes the current slot). * BUILT-IN setups ship with the project: every TextAsset in Assets/Resources/TestSetups (a setup file renamed .json -> .json stays a TextAsset) is listed with a 'built-in' tag and can't be deleted here. Send Claude a setup file (or its name) and it can be added there so it is in every copy of the game. * Delete asks for a second click. 'Open folder' shows the files.
--- PixelDevShapes.cs ---
PixelDevShapes.cs / PixelDevMockup.cs Layout dev tools ('Mock up mode' tick box in the Dev Tools Tools tab = PixelDevMockWindow, ONE draggable window with a Shapes tab and a UI pieces tab and a minimize button; both components are created by PixelDevTools at start (Boot) so saved layouts reappear, the host window turns the tabs on with SetActive; their controls are built into PixelDevMockWindow.TabRoot; state in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.*, not in the game save). Shape placer (tab 0); click the floor to drop the brush shape (ShapeKind Cube / Sphere / Cylinder / Capsule / Ramp (runtime wedge mesh) / Wall / Disc, sizes in main-pixel widths), click a shape to select + drag it on the floor plane, Q / E (PixelAction.RotateLeft/Right) turn, the wheel turns smoothly and Ctrl + wheel resizes (the hovered / dragged shape, else the brush), a see-through GHOST of the brush follows the mouse over the floor (UpdateGhost) and the shape is dropped with the brush's size / turn, Delete removes; ShapeMode Solid (static collider) / Dynamic (rigidbody) / Ghost (no collider); colour cycle button; every shape has a billboard number label, the panel lists id / kind / mode / x z y / rotation / size and 'Copy layout' puts the text on the clipboard; 'RESET ALL SHAPES' deletes everything (one click). While open PixelClicker.DevClickBlock (part of ExternalClickBlock) keeps the cube from taking the clicks. UI mockup builder (tab 1); right-click on a piece deletes it; resize grips are a drawn corner grip (PixelUIKit.GripSprite, also on My Window); add Window / Box / Button / Text / Image pieces (own canvas 540, 1920 x 1080 units, origin top-left, no UI-scale division) - drag to move, yellow corner handle to resize, number badge, exact X / Y / W / H fields, text box, font size, opacity slider, hex colour, layer Front / Up / Down / Back, duplicate, delete; Image pieces show PNG / JPG files from <persistentDataPath>/Mockups (Folder button opens it, Refresh rescans; a checker placeholder otherwise); 'Show when closed' keeps the pieces visible after the panel is closed, 'Hide' hides them; 'Copy layout' lists every piece back to front; 'RESET ALL' deletes all.
--- PixelDevSounds.cs ---
PixelDevSounds.cs SOUND BOARD (Dev Hub tab 'Sounds'): every sound effect of the game (PixelAudio.AllSounds, the Inspector list incl. the code-made ones) in one list: a Play button, the id, how many variants it has, and a volume slider that changes the sound live (the Sound object's volume, so the game uses it at once). * Filter box (part of an id, e.g. 'wood', 'ui_', 'pixel_bounce'). 'Stop all' stops every sound; 'Undo changes' puts every volume back to how it was when the board opened this session. * 'Copy changes for Claude' copies 'id: volume old -> new' lines: paste them to Claude to make the new volumes the defaults (PixelAudio.CreateDefaultSounds). Volume edits are run-time only (leaving Play mode drops them unless Claude puts them in the code).
--- PixelDevTextEdit.cs ---
PixelDevTextEdit.cs Custom text for the Dev Hub (component added by PixelDevHub.Awake): a quick RIGHT CLICK (not a hold: holding is the widget favorite, PixelRightTap tells them apart) on any label in the hub - widget titles and control labels, tab names, tester cards, buttons - opens a small box to type your own text (Save / Reset / Cancel, Enter / Esc). A label is found by its place in the hierarchy (the names of its GameObjects up to the hub canvas), so it needs no registration; labels inside drop-downs / input fields and the search results are left alone. Edits are kept per label path in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.CustomText (JSON), which PixelSettingsSync stores in every save profile (and PixelDevTextEdit.ReloadFromPrefs re-reads). Every 2 s while the hub is open (and when it opens) the labels are scanned and the saved texts put on them; each frame the edited ones are kept at their text (code that rewrites a label's text, like 'Clear (3)', cannot undo your edit). Reset brings back the text the label had before it was edited.
--- PixelDevToolsDocs.cs ---
PixelDevToolsDocs.cs Part of PixelDevTools (partial class): the To-Do and Changelog views of the Dev Hub Docs tab. The system notes are in PixelDevTools.cs.
--- PixelDevToolsGod.cs ---
PixelDevToolsGod.cs Part of PixelDevTools (partial class): god pixel mode (scroll = pick a pixel, left click = spawn, right click = destroy) and its on-screen display. The system notes are in PixelDevTools.cs.
--- PixelDevToolsPanel.cs ---
PixelDevToolsPanel.cs Part of PixelDevTools (partial class): building the Main tab widget board (BuildPanel and its card steps, the Resource Tools card, Widget) and the world tools behind its buttons. The system notes are in PixelDevTools.cs.
--- PixelDevToolsSpawn.cs ---
PixelDevToolsSpawn.cs Part of PixelDevTools (partial class): the spawn selector and the pinned spawn / cheat widget cards (spawned pixel, minigames, middle click, focus, bill, shop unlocks, XP, husk pile, clear world, super rares, dev items) and their drop-downs. The system notes are in PixelDevTools.cs.
--- PixelDevUI.cs ---
PixelDevUI.cs Shared building blocks for the Dev Hub's tool tabs (Look Studio, Setups, Events, Sounds, Console): one set of colours, top-left placing (PlaceTL), a button with a click sound, a one-line label, a wrapping text, a drop-down and a tick box, all in the shared font. New dev tabs use these instead of their own copies (older tabs - Docs, Inspector, Tester - still have private ones: move them over when they are next touched).
--- PixelDeviceCard.cs ---
PixelDeviceCard.cs The ONE info card + panel for placed devices (design agreed in the Design Discussion chat, 2026-10-18; doc: docs/systems/PixelFactory.md 'Device card'). * HOVER a placed device for hoverDelay (0.5 s): a small card beside the pointer (PixelUIKit.PlaceTooltip, the hover-tooltip spot) with the device's essentials - its name, time / uses left as a bar, its 2-3 key stats, contents like 6 / 10. Hidden while the block / husk card is wanted (PixelBlockTooltip.HoveringBlock: a husk in the bin shows the husk's card), over a part that claimed the pointer (the Recycler's button, the hopper's flag: PixelPointerClaim.ButtonClaimed), while carrying, in a menu, in first person. * RIGHT-CLICK (a TAP: released within tapSeconds) a placed device: a small Tool window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, the theme) with the full sheet (run time, reach, Operation levels, what it did this session) and actions: Remove (Danger; asks once: 'Sure?') and Upgrades (opens the shop's Upgrades > Operation tab on that device: PixelShop.OpenOperationFor; shown only for devices with an Operation row). A right HOLD still removes the device (PixelConsumables.UpdateRemoval) - the tap just never fills that ring. Who owns the right click is PixelRightClick's Device owner. Escape (PixelWindows) / a click anywhere outside the panel / the device going away closes it. * STEPPERS: '- value +' rows (PixelDeviceInfo.AddStepper) for the Operation upgrades' level in use (PixelShop.AddOperationLevels): turn a bought upgrade back down (PlaceSteppers). * TICK BOXES (2026-10-20): 'on / off' rows (PixelDeviceInfo.AddToggle, PlaceToggles), e.g. the Sorting Hopper's outputs. * DEVICE ACTIONS: buttons a device adds itself (PixelDeviceInfo.AddAction, e.g. the Sorting Hopper's Select filter / Empty out) sit two to a row above Remove / Upgrades (PlaceActions). * NOT ONLY DEVICES (2026-10-20): OpenSubject(IPixelPanelSubject) shows the same panel for something that is not a placed device - a conveyor belt SECTION (PixelConveyorPanel: a right tap on a belt; the section glows while the panel is open). The subject fills the sheet and says what Remove does. * LEFT-CLICK is untouched (each device's own button, flag, winding...). The lines come from the device itself: PixelPlacedDevice.BuildInfo (common lines + AddInfo + IPixelDeviceInfo components; PixelDeviceInfo.cs). The panel then adds one row per husk socket (PixelCouplings.AddPortRows: what each port is joined to, from the connection resolver). Refreshed every refreshSeconds while shown (no per-frame text building).
--- PixelDeviceInfo.cs ---
PixelDeviceInfo.cs What a placed device tells the shared device card / panel (PixelDeviceCard). ONE system for every device: nothing builds a card of its own. * PixelDeviceInfo: the filled-in sheet - Title, Lines (rich text, 'label: value'), an optional Progress 0..1 with its label, and Full (false = the small hover card: only the essentials; true = the right-click panel: everything). Reused (Clear) every refresh. * IPixelDeviceInfo: a component on a placed device's root that adds lines (FillDeviceInfo). PixelPlacedDevice.BuildInfo writes the common part (name, time / uses left with the progress bar, 'Click to start', powered, attuned; Full: run time, reach, Operation levels), then its own virtual AddInfo (subclasses: Storage Crate, Quantum Organizer...), then every IPixelDeviceInfo on the same object (the behaviour components next to a thin device stub: PixelRecycler, PixelSortingHopper, PixelSweeper). * ACTIONS (2026-10-18): a device can add its own buttons to the right-click panel with info.AddAction(label, onClick, role, onHover) when info.Full (onHover, optional: told when the pointer goes on / off the button) (the Sorting Hopper's 'Select filter' / 'Empty out'); the panel shows them two to a row above Remove / Upgrades. * STEPPERS (2026-10-18): '- value +' rows in the panel (info.AddStepper): the Operation upgrades' level in use (PixelShop.AddOperationLevels), so a player with every level bought can go back to level 3. A new device: override AddInfo (a PixelPlacedDevice subclass) or implement IPixelDeviceInfo (a behaviour component); add the 2-3 key stats always and the rest only when info.Full (run time, what it did this session...).
--- PixelDialogue.cs ---
PixelDialogue.cs The user's own file for every 'Got it' box and every character's lines: Assets/Resources/Dialogue.txt (read through the shared PixelTextFile). Sections (the [bracketed] key is how the game finds the text - don't change it): [Tip: <id>] first-time tips (PixelHints): text, overlay line (the short event-log line) [Intro tips] the new-game intro boxes: log, inventory, earn, right click [Tutorial: Shop / Toggles / Skills] the guided tutorials' boxes: step 1, step 2... (only the text; the order and what each step shows stay in code) [Guide: Woodcutting] the Woodcutting guide: question, later, step 1... [Perk popup: <skill> level <n>] the popup when a skill level gives a perk [Cubie] name, next button, last button, recycler gift, visit 1 page 1..., visit 2 page 1... (pages can be added or removed: '-') [Visitor: <id>] each visitor / vendor (tinkerer, wizard, entrepreneur, farmer, prospector, lumberjack, egg, bean): name, greeting ({0} = the discount %), wares title, buttons, arrival log line, peek bubble, missed log, and their extra lines (farewell, gift farewell, dragon aside, gauntlet aside) [Minigame: Black Hole] its first-hole message ({0} = a number) 'File wins': each owner copies the file's text over its Inspector / built-in text where it sets up its defaults (Awake), so the display code is unchanged. Empty / missing = the game's own text. The file was first built from the code's built-in texts by tools/dialogue/build.py (python3 tools/dialogue/build.py - it OVERWRITES the file, so only for a fresh start); in the editor the game also adds any missing text on play (PixelTextFile.Flush) and new texts are appended later. Dev Hub > Events > 'Reload pixel texts' also reloads this file (texts set in Awake take a new play). NOT in it: Did-you-know lines (Assets/Resources/DidYouKnow.txt), Big Tim's live offer sentences (built from numbers), event-log lines.
--- PixelDidYouKnow.cs ---
PixelDidYouKnow.cs "Did you know?" tips (added by PixelClicker.Awake): every few minutes (firstDelaySeconds 180, then a random intervalSeconds 150-300) a random general hint from Assets/Resources/DidYouKnow.txt (one tip per line, '//' lines are notes, {key:Action} placeholders become the player's keys via PixelKeys.Replace, a line starting 'shop:' only shows once the Shop button is available) pops up - only when the game is NOT BUSY (Busy: PixelCalm.ForTip plus no open window, not in first person); a busy moment just waits and retries. Tips come from a shuffled bag, so none repeats before all were seen. Settings > Gameplay 'Did you know tips' (Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.DidYouKnow, per save via PixelSettingsSync). TWO PLACES (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.DidYouKnowDock / X / Y, the player's machine, not per save): - DOCKED (default: right edge, near the top) at the height where it was dropped: the box hides just past the side edge (dockY = its centre, canvas units from the middle, saved). When it is docked it slides into the side; when a tip triggers it SLIDES OUT, stays showSeconds (12 s, paused while hovered; a click sends it back at once) and slides back in. No button is left on screen. - FLOATING anywhere: the box pops in at its pinned spot and fades out the same way. MOVING it works like the achievement popup: hold the left mouse button on the box (holdSeconds 0.5, PixelHold ring) and it follows the mouse; a left click puts it down - within dockSnap of the left / right screen edge it DOCKS to that edge at that height, anywhere else it floats there; a right click puts it back to the default (docked right, top), as does Settings > Accessibility 'Reset tips location' (ResetLocation). A quick click (no hold) just dismisses the tip. Kept inside the black bars. DOCK FEEDBACK (2026-10-16): while moving, both side edges show a glowing dock zone (the one in range glows brightly and the heading reads 'Click to dock it on the right edge'); in range = the mouse OR the box's edge is within dockSnap (the box is clamped inside the screen, so the mouse test alone could miss). Right after docking the box says where it went (dockedText, dockedShowSeconds 2.5) before sliding in, and while tucked away a small gold mark sits on that edge at its height (showDockMark).
--- PixelDisplaySettings.cs ---
PixelDisplaySettings.cs Player display / accessibility settings (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.*, applied before the first scene): graphics quality preset (QualitySettings), VSync, fullscreen + resolution, UI scale (SavedUIScale takes effect on the next start; UIScale = the value in use: PixelUIKit.CreateCanvas divides the reference resolution by it; the black bars' canvas is not scaled, so PixelHud.BarHeight is in scaled-canvas units and RawBarHeight in bar units), and colour-blind marks (ColorBlind / ColorBlindChanged: PixelLook.colorBlindSides draws a triangle / square / circle on the faces of the red / green / blue pixels, PixelBombMinigame adds R/G/B letters under the wires).
--- PixelDragonFind.cs ---
PixelDragonFind.cs Dragon Cube find show (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; PixelDragonFind.Play(tierIndex, worldPos), queued): called from PixelClicker.CollectInternal (a click, manual or auto) and SeedSprout.PopOff (a grown Dragon Seed) - never from offline progress (AddCurrency alone does not play it). A canvas (690, blocks clicks) darkens the screen (darkness), the cube is drawn by a hidden studio camera into a RenderTexture on a RawImage (so nothing in the scene can dim it), zooms up from where it was found (zoomInSeconds), dances about (danceSeconds, danceRange, golden UI sparkle pool sparklesPerSecond), then hops into the backpack icon (PixelGhostMinigame.BuildBackpackSprite, same place as the Ghost's; stashSeconds) with a sparkle burst and a bump, then the screen brightens (fadeOutSeconds). Escape skips the show (a PixelWindows entry, priority 165, so it never opens the pause menu: it jumps to the backpack landing + fade and drops queued shows); heading foundFormat; sounds dragon_find / dragon_stash (code-made PixelSynth.DragonFind/DragonStash); disableShow. Under Infinite resources the Dragon Seed (always held) grows any of the seven even if you hold it (PixelClicker.PickAnyDragonCube).
--- PixelElectricFx.cs ---
PixelElectricFx.cs Looks / effects of the Electrician pixels: PixelLook.electricFx (ElectricAmbient) attaches per-type ambient particles (Wire copper sparks along the edges, Battery rising green bubbles, Capacitor crackling blue static, Resistor red heat embers, Transistor cold blips, Fuse a spark fountain on top, Bulb warm motes), Hit / Break (called from CollectInternal for IsElectricalBlock types, disableFarmFx) throw sparks plus coloured arcs (FractureFx.Play(..., core, glow); smoke for Resistor / Fuse), and each pixel has its own synthesized click sound (PixelSynth.Elec(kind, seed): twang, thunk + hum, charge whine + snap, buzz, digital blip, hiss + pop, glassy ting; used while the pixel_<type> clip list is empty); looksVersion 34. Hit / Break bursts are pooled (PixelFx.Rent), and so are the FractureFx arc flashes.
--- PixelElevator.cs ---
PixelElevator.cs CONVEYOR ELEVATOR (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Conveyor Elevator'): a square tower that lifts pixels from the END of a belt up to a higher belt. PLACING (two stages): 1. the ghost follows the mouse and MAGNETIZES to a belt end that is near it (PixelElevator.TrySnap: it turns to the belt's direction, the belt's end meets its entry face, and its base takes that belt's height); Q / E turn it when it is not latched; click to fix the base. 2. Move the mouse up or down to set the HEIGHT (the mouse ray is cut with a vertical plane through the base, quantised to heightStepCubes) and click to confirm; right click goes back to stage 1. The tower's posts run down to the floor when the base is raised. SOCKETS (PixelSockets.cs; since 2026-10-20 the ONLY way husks go in and out - no more near-the-base catching): eight FITTED bare couplings (built in, not saved, made again on every rebuild), one just off the middle of each side at the BASE ('in0'..'in3', Intake) and at the TOP ('out0'..'out3', Output). Their ports are the belt snap points (SnapPoints), so a belt end laid against a side hands its husks in (AcceptHusk -> drawn to the shaft and carried up by a PixelPathCarrier; refused while the shaft is full, so the belt waits), and so does a machine's Out coupling right beside one (Emit -> HandIn). At the top the tower uses the first 'out' socket with something attached (a belt starting at it, or an In coupling right there: RefreshLinks, every half second): PixelCouplings.Emit puts the husk out there; while OutputHasRoom says the belt start is full the husk WAITS at the top and the shaft queues up. With nothing attached at the top it is flung forward off the top. A short trough is drawn to each link. Belts can also be raised and lowered while placing (scroll wheel, PixelFeatureTester) so ramps and bridges are possible. Saved with the tester save (position, turn, height). Built with PixelFactoryKit.
--- PixelEnergyBar.cs ---
PixelEnergyBar.cs A small camera-facing horizontal BAR floating over a placed thing: a dark track and a coloured fill from the left (0..1). The Biomass Generators show their charge with it (blue; flashing red when empty), and every device a generator powers shows the SAME bar over itself, tracking that generator's charge (PixelPlacedDevice.HoldPower(fraction)). Two quads with the shared Sprites/Default material (colours by property block, no material copies); it keeps its own size on a scaled parent. PixelEnergyBar.Attach(parent, localPosition, width, colour); Set(fraction, colour) - below 0 hides it.
--- PixelEvadeMinigame.cs ---
PixelEvadeMinigame.cs Cube Evasion minigame (id evade, pack unlocksMinigame = "evade", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack, 70K each; like Cube Dash / Blink: Takeover => true, cube stays clickable, AutoClickerPaused meanwhile): every minInterval..maxInterval s a round of roundSeconds (25) starts and the cube RUNS FROM THE MOUSE (Steer, in viewport space measured in screen heights): inside fleeRadius (0.22) it accelerates (agility) directly away from the pointer at up to fleeSpeed (0.5 screen heights per second, faster the closer the mouse is), slides along the wall when it runs into the area's edge (edgeMargin, area* fractions) and, cornered on both axes, breaks back across the middle; away from the mouse it just coasts to a stop. Clicks (manual only) count; at the end it returns home and pays rewardMultiplier (10) x the pixel's worth per click via AddCurrency; stats evade.rounds/clicks/best, tip minigame_evade, sounds evade_start/_win/_lose (silent until clips are assigned), picture case in BuildMinigameModel; a success reports Clicked (bonus skill XP 15).
--- PixelEvilTreeMinigame.cs ---
PixelEvilTreeMinigame.cs Evil Tree minigame (id eviltree, pack unlocksMinigame = "eviltree", Minigames tab, needs the Lumberjack's Pack; extends PixelMinigame): now and then (firstRoundDelay 60 s, then minInterval 150 .. maxInterval 300 s, PixelMinigameLimits.AllowAnother) a sapling sprouts on the floor near the cube (PixelBait.FindGround, kept inside the view) and GROWS over growSeconds (14) into a giant tree (heightInCubes 5 cubes tall) with glowing eyes and a jagged mouth. It FLAILS so it is hard to click: the whole tree leans and jerks to new spots (the click colliders move with it), its arms whip about, and it flails harder as it grows. It has a HEALTH BAR (health 40, each manual click on it does damagePerClick 1; the auto clicker never hits it): at 0 it topples over and pays a random minWood..maxWood MAGIC WOOD (the minigame's tracker / material: PixelMinigame.MaterialName, shown in the Inventory's Materials tab and Crafting, saved like any minigame count) and Woodcutting XP only (SkillXpTarget = Woodcutting, default 30 XP, whatever skill is in training; needs the Woodcutting skill bought). If it is not felled within stayAfterGrownSeconds (30) of being fully grown it sinks back into the ground. Everything is built from primitives at run time (no assets). Magic Wood has no use yet (crafting later).
--- PixelEyedropper.cs ---
PixelEyedropper.cs Eyedropper (created on demand by PixelEyedropper.Begin(callback)): hides the pause menu (PixelPauseMenu.SetMenuVisible(false)), waits for the end of the frame, grabs a screenshot (ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotAsTexture), shows it frozen full-screen on its own canvas (950) with a small loupe (colour swatch + hex code) following the pointer; left-click hands that colour to the callback, right-click / Escape (PixelWindows 175) cancels, then the menu comes back. Used by the small eyedropper button next to the 'Neon bar glow' tick box in Settings > Display (PixelPauseMenu.AddEyedropperButton, PixelUIKit.EyedropperSprite(); the 'Bar glow colour' hue slider is gone): PixelHud.SetGlowFromColor(colour) stores an exact colour (PixelHud.GlowCustomColor, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.GlowColor, hex, per save via PixelSettingsSync), sets the hue slider to its hue and switches 'Cycle glow colour' OFF; the neon bar glow of the game's black margins (PixelHud.Update) and the main menu's bar (PixelTitleScreen) read PixelHud.GlowColourNow(...) = the picked colour while static, else the hue (drifting while cycling). Moving the hue slider clears the picked colour; switching Cycle on drifts on from the picked colour's hue.
--- PixelFactoryKit.cs ---
PixelFactoryKit.cs Small shared helper for building machines out of primitives in code (conveyor belt, kiln): materials copied from the cube's material (PixelClicker.CreateVisualMaterial, emission off unless asked), boxes / cylinders / balls, see-through ghost versions (alpha) with no colliders. DestroyMaterials() frees the materials it made (a temporary build like the studio intro). SHARED MATERIALS (2026-10-20): a kit made with shared = true takes its materials from ONE static cache (keyed by colour incl. ghost alpha, metal, smoothness, emission), so pieces that are rebuilt often (belts while placing, highlights, corner joints, elevator troughs) stop making new materials every time - they used to leak about 8 a frame while a belt was being placed. Only for builds that never change a material they got from the kit (use a MaterialPropertyBlock for per-object colour); DestroyMaterials leaves shared ones alone. PixelOwnedMesh frees a code-made Mesh with its object. Also Strut (a cylinder from a to b), Disc (a flat band round Y) and static PlainPart (a collider-less primitive in the game's material; ModelPart = placed, scaled and shadowless, the device models' one part builder - don't write a local Part() copy) - used by the device models (each device's own BuildModel, e.g. PixelFanDevice / PixelLightningRod) and the potion models.
--- PixelFanDevice.cs ---
PixelFanDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Runtime behaviour of a placed fan (device kind = Fan): pushes old pixels inside a floor wedge (cone angle/length/height) along its forward, spinning blades, timer. Placed like the vacuum, turned with scroll wheel or Q/E while placing.
--- PixelFarmFx.cs ---
PixelFarmFx.cs Farming pixel particles: PixelFarmFx.Attach (ambient loop per PixelLook.farmFx: Pollen, Dew, Crumbs, Spores, Chaff, Sparkle, Bugs; attached in PixelLooks.AddExtras), Hit (non-final hit) and Break (per-type burst, gold ring for Golden Fruit) called from CollectInternal (disableFarmFx); sounds are PixelSynth.Farm(kind, seed) (pixel_dirt/fertilizer/pollen/wheat/weevil/goldenfruit, farm_hit, farm_gleam); looksVersion 29. Seed / Water are no longer in IsSpecialType (no Toggles spawn switch). PixelSkills.Update unlocks every Farming type once the first is unlocked. Hit / Break bursts are pooled (PixelFx.Rent / Burst.Add).
--- PixelFeatureTester.cs ---
PixelFeatureTester.cs The 'Tester' tab of the Dev Hub: a place to spawn and try things that are not part of the game yet (a consumable, a device, a machine...). Each testable thing is a Feature registered in Awake (Register(name, description, ghostBuilder, placeBuilder)): the tab lists them as cards with a 'Place' button. Place = a see-through GHOST follows the mouse over the floor (PixelConsumables.TryGetFloorPoint); left click puts a real one down (and keeps placing), Q / E turn it, right click or Escape stops (PixelWindows entry); 'Clear' removes the ones placed. LINE pieces (belts): a right click while drawing CANCELS placing (the ghost goes; the pieces already placed stay - remove one with a right hold). While placing, clicks on the cube are blocked (PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock). Placed test objects ARE saved (see SAVING below). To add something to test: write a builder (see PixelWoodChipper: CreateGhost / Place) and add one Register line here. Two-point features (RegisterLine, a conveyor belt) use a marker ghost, then a preview piece between the first point and the mouse; every click builds a piece and the end starts the next. DELETE: hold the right button on any placed tester object (not while placing) to remove it (PixelHold ring; a quick right click still flips a belt / re-links a panel: PixelRightTap). BELT SELECTION card: 'Select belts' mode toggles belts by click or box-drag (teal glow, PixelConveyor.SetSelected) and sets speed / reverses them together. Placing snaps to a grid by default; SHIFT = free placement. (It used to be: SHIFT while placing snaps to a grid and angle steps (PixelSnap). SAVING: every placed object is saved with the save file (PixelFeatureTester.ExportAll / ImportAll, TesterSave = feature name + JSON from the feature's ExportState / ImportState; hooked into PixelSaveGame after the placed devices are restored). LINKING (Solar Panel): after placing, linkPanel mode - click a device inside the ring; a right click on a placed panel starts it again. The Conveyor Belt is also a real game item (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.ConveyorBelt, the Belt Kit, on sale in the shop's Utilities for everyone): its right click calls PixelFeatureTester.StartFeature("Conveyor Belt"), which places without the Dev Hub. SHOP ITEMS (2026-10-20): Wood Chipper, Conveyor Belt, Container Hose, Belt Sorter, Conveyor Elevator, Junction Box, Belt Jump, Conveyor Combiner and Solar Panel are sold in the shop, so they get no card here (SoldInShop) but stay registered: the shop item's right click places them through StartFeature. The Coupling is no longer sold (couplings fit themselves when a belt / hose attaches: PixelCouplings.AutoFitAt) - its card here fits one by hand. Features now: Vacuum Cleaner (PixelVacuumCleaner.cs: drive with W A S D / arrows, the front intake sucks husks up and the hose spits them out where the mouse aims), Container Hose (PixelContainerHose.cs: a single line from a crate / organizer to a Recycler / crate / organizer / trash can), Wood Chipper (PixelWoodChipper.cs), Conveyor Belt (PixelConveyor.cs, PixelFactoryKit.cs), Kiln (PixelKiln.cs; makes Charcoal old pixels), Solar Panel (PixelSolarPanel.cs).
--- PixelFeedback.cs ---
PixelFeedback.cs Give feedback (for testers): the pause menu's long green Give feedback button (right above Quit; no longer in Settings) or the rebindable PixelAction.Feedback key (default F8; PixelKeys enum value 14, label 'Give feedback') opens a 1400x960 window (own canvas 800, above the pause menu, PixelPop): a multi-line text box, Take screenshot (CaptureRoutine: hides this window's canvas and the pause menu's (PixelPauseMenu.SetMenuVisible(false)) for a frame, WaitForEndOfFrame, ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotAsTexture = the game without any menu; up to maxShots 4, selected with small numbered buttons in the drawing area), a pencil (8 colours, fixed width pencilWidthFraction of the picture; strokes are drawn straight into the texture's pixel array by PixelFeedbackCanvas pointer events), Undo (removes the last stroke and replays the rest over the clean screenshot, maxStrokes), Remove shot, Open feedback folder, Cancel and Save feedback: everything is appended to ONE text file <persistentDataPath>/Feedback/feedback.txt (a block per entry: date / time, game version, Unity version, platform, session minutes, the text, the screenshot file names) and each (drawn) screenshot is saved next to it as feedback_<timestamp>_<n>.png. While it is open the game is paused (restored on close; the pause menu already pauses) and PixelKeys.Typing reads true so the game's keys do nothing; Escape closes it (PixelFeedback.Open / ClosedThisFrame keep the pause menu from also stepping back). Added to the pause menu's object by PixelPauseMenu.BuildUI.
--- PixelFilters.cs ---
PixelFilters.cs Screen filters (Settings > Display > 'Screen filter' drop-down + 'Filter strength' slider; saved per profile through PixelSettingsSync: PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.Filter / FilterStrength; component added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelFilters.Instance). A filter is a Preset (Inspector list `presets`, index 0 = Off; defaults CreateDefaults: CRT TV, VHS, Wild West, Noir, Night Vision, Neon Dream) with TWO parts, both scaled by the strength slider: 1) URP post-processing (only with the Universal RP package, #if PIXEL_URP and a URP pipeline asset): a runtime global Volume with ColorAdjustments, WhiteBalance, Vignette, FilmGrain, ChromaticAberration, LensDistortion and Bloom, blended from neutral to the preset by the strength; the cameras get renderPostProcessing switched on (re-checked every cameraCheckSeconds). Without URP this part is skipped and only the overlay works. 2) A screen-space overlay canvas (sortingOrder overlaySortingOrder, above the UI so it also covers windows like a real screen): colour tint, vignette, CRT rounded corners (mask), scanlines (tiled 4 px texture, rolling), animated static noise, a VHS tracking band, film dust / scratches. All textures are generated in code once. 3) THE SCREEN WARP (presets with warp > 0, shader Assets/Resources/PixelCrtWarp.shader 'Pixel Clicker/CRT Warp'): at the end of every frame the finished picture (world AND all UI) is copied (ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotIntoRenderTexture) and drawn back through the shader: barrel warp, rounded black glass corners, scanlines, colour fringing, vignette. The game's real input is untouched (everything still reads the real pointer = a point of the unwarped picture), so the OS cursor is hidden and a software cursor is drawn by the shader at the warped position of that point (InverseWarp) - the cursor always sits on what you click. If the picture comes out upside down, tick `flipVertical`. Off = the volume is disabled, the overlay canvas hidden and the warp loop stopped, so it costs nothing.
--- PixelFind.cs ---
PixelFind.cs PixelFind.First<T>() / All<T>() / Count<T>(): the only place with the Find version checks (FindObjectOfType before 2023.1, then FindAnyObjectByType; FindObjectsByType with a sort mode until Unity 6.6, which deprecated the sort mode). Use it instead of calling Unity's Find methods directly. 'First' is any one of them: every caller looks for a single component, so the order never mattered. PixelFind.Cached<T>(): the same as First, but it remembers the answer (searches again only when the remembered object was destroyed, e.g. after Settings > Restart reloads the scene, or when none was found yet). Use it for the game's single components (PixelShop, PixelStats, PixelSkills...) anywhere that runs often; First does a full scene search on every call.
--- PixelFirstPersonGrid.cs ---
PixelFirstPersonGrid.cs The first person floor: while PixelFirstPerson is active the normal floor is hidden (PixelFloor.FloorOff includes PixelFirstPerson.Active, the ground collider stays) and this draws a bright neon grid (Resources/PixelNeonGrid.shader, world-space lines) on a quad just above the ground, only within a radius of lit points: the player, the clicker cube and old pixels resting on the ground (up to 64, nearest to the player first; collected every 0.1 s). Settings live on PixelFirstPerson (header "Neon floor"). Added/removed by PixelFirstPerson.Enter / Exit.
--- PixelFirstPersonHotbar.cs ---
