PixelFirstPersonHotbar.cs The first person HOTBAR (added by PixelFirstPerson.Enter, removed on Exit): five slots along the bottom, picked with the mouse wheel (when you are not holding a cube - then the wheel moves the cube) or the number keys 1-5. Slot 1 holds the AXE; the other slots are empty hands (pick things up, click the cube). While the axe is picked it is drawn in your hand (a viewmodel built from primitives, child of the first person camera) and StartSwing(onImpact) plays the swing: wind-up (0 - 0.3), strike (0.3 - 0.5, onImpact fires at 0.5 = the moment it connects), recover. The swing sound is played here; the hit sound and the rewards are PixelFirstPerson's (ChopOldPixel / the axe bonus on PixelCollected). Tunables are serialized on PixelFirstPerson ("Axe") and passed into Setup.
--- PixelFirstPersonWall.cs ---
PixelFirstPersonWall.cs Out-of-bounds protection for first person (added/removed by PixelFirstPerson.Enter / Exit; settings under its "Out of bounds" header). The play area is the same circle old pixels are kept in (PixelFloor.TryGetEdgeCircle: where the floor fades out). Constrain() runs after every move: the player is pushed back inside the circle (by the controller's radius), and if they ever fall far below the ground (through a gap, out of the world) they are put back at the spot they entered at. VISIBLE WALL: a neon wireframe cylinder drawn at the circle - a grid of thin quads (vertical lines + rings, SpriteDefault vertex colours) built as a small PATCH of columns round the player's angle, rebuilt every frame (about a hundred quads) and faded by distance, so it only appears when the player is within wallVisibleCubes of the edge and the lines stay put on the wall as the patch follows.
--- PixelFishingSpawns.cs ---
PixelFishingSpawns.cs Fishing's FIXED spawn table (part of partial class PixelClicker; used by PickSpawnTier and SpawnShare while Fishing is the skill in play, `disableFishingTable` = the old filler / hazard weights): Water 80.5%, Driftwood 5%, Seaweed 7.5%, Jellyfish 3%, Shell 3%, Fish 1% (`fishWaterShare` ... `fishFishShare`, 0 = these defaults; they are weights, so they are normalised if one block is missing). Everything else comes from a replacement roll: a rolled Fish becomes a WHALE `whaleChance` (0 = 5%) of the time, a rolled Seaweed or Shell becomes a message in a BOTTLE `bottleChance` (0 = 2%), a rolled Shell can instead become a PEARL `pearlChance` (0 = 6%), and collecting a Water pixel makes the NEXT pixel a Bait block with `baitChance` (0 = 10%, QueueBaitAfter). Bait pellets still raise Fish's weight (PixelBait.FishMultiplier) and active pets their type's (PixelPets.SpawnMultiplier). SpawnShare (the Pixel Guide) works out the same numbers, bait included.
--- PixelFloorDefaults.cs ---
PixelFloorDefaults.cs Part of PixelFloor (partial class): the built-in styles: EnsureDefaultStyles (the once-only upgrades of saved lists, then the missing defaults), the S / NeonVariant makers and the DefaultStyles / ThemedStyles / ExtraStyles tables. The system notes are in PixelFloor.cs.
--- PixelFloorFade.cs ---
PixelFloorFade.cs FADE INTO THE SKY (fadeIntoSky, on by default): a painted floor uses Resources/PixelFadedFloor.shader (unlit, alpha) that fades the floor itself to fully see-through past the fade circle (_Center / _FadeStart / _FadeEnd set by UpdateGroundShader, _Light follows the day / night cycle), so the solid sky-coloured ring (below) is not needed and the skybox shows all the way down; only the wireframe rim stays. PixelFloor.SelfFading = ground shader or faded picture. Floor edge fade (partial class PixelFloor, UpdateEdgeFade from LateUpdate; disableEdgeFade): the floor fades out in a circle round the player (the cube, or the first person player via PixelFirstPerson.TryGetPlayerPosition) so its edges / seams are never seen: a flat annulus mesh with vertex alpha (BuildEdgeFade: see-through inside r0, solid from r1 and far past the floor's edges, coloured like the sky's horizon (EdgeFadeColor: TryGetHorizonColor, else the scene sky tint / background / the floor's average), Sprites/Default, a hair above the floor top) plus a digital wireframe in the rim (RebuildWire: a world-aligned grid every wireSpacingCubes (0 = 1.25 pixel widths) and three rings, clipped to the rim, brightness bell-shaped across it, wireColor (alpha 0 = cyan), wireStrength 0 = 0.9; rebuilt when the centre moves). fadeStartCubes / fadeWidthCubes 0 = automatic from the floor's size (start about half the way to the nearest edge) - BUT with a base view (PixelOrbitCamera) every floor uses the STANDARD area instead (StandardRadii: fade starts at standardFadeStart 0.9 of the screen's half width on the floor at 16:9, solid at standardFadeEnd 1.3, so the floor fills the screen and only the sides / corners show the wireframe; stretched one-copy pictures are enlarged to cover it, EffectiveTileSize). The ring is solid only OVER the floor: past the floor's own edge its alpha melts away over ringSoft (UpdateRingAlpha, per-vertex, redone when the centre moves), so the sky below the horizon shows instead of a flat horizon-coloured band. Scaled by the floor's own fade-in visibility; hidden with the floor / FloorOff.
--- PixelFloorLive.cs ---
PixelFloorLive.cs The fifth batch of floors (part of partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from ShadeWorlds' fall-through -> ShadeLive; styles from LiveStyles) and the LIVE OVERLAY (PixelFloorOverlay: real 3D objects on the floor plane, updated every frame, so they can follow the mouse without redrawing a texture). GRASS (big still pictures like Lush Grass: 1024 px, stretchToFloor over 40 world units, drawn in WORLD units on worker threads, never repeated or stretched; every blade is its own tapered stroke from a 3 x 3 cell search - BladeLayer - bent by a noise wind field): Wild Meadow (hue patches, wind-bent blades, wildflower clusters, seed heads), Clover Lawn (curving mower stripes, three-leaf clover patches, daisies, worn spots, a fairy ring of mushrooms), Mossy Glade (redrawn: domed moss cushions in drifting greens lit from the top left, fallen leaves, ball-shaded stones with moss creeping up their lit side and lichen, arching fern fronds of paired leaflets, stones and ferns casting shadows down-right - GladeStone / GladeFern -, twigs, dappled sun patches), Prairie Grass (tall golden wind-swept blades, green patches, purple flowers, rocks). SEE-THROUGH LASERS (FloorStyle.hideBase: the floor picture is hidden - ApplyVisibility / the edge fade read HideBaseNow - but the collider stays): Neon Lasers (overlay Lasers: beams drifting / turning / pulsing over nothing, neon colours from the style), Laser Tracker (overlay LaserTracker: emitters round the cube shoot beams that all converge on the mouse's point on the floor, with a pulsing ring there). The beams are ONE flat mesh rebuilt every frame (Ribbon: quads lying on the floor with vertex colours, a solid core over a soft glow; it was LineRenderers with TransformZ alignment, which never showed in the game), never thinner on screen than coreMinPixels / glowMinPixels (WorldPerPixel: the floor is far below the cube, so 0.05 world units was about a pixel). The style's still picture (ShadeLaserPicture, 160 px) is never on the floor; it is the menu icon and the title screen's background picture (shown ONCE there, PixelFloor.TryGetTitlePicture: it does not tile). PICNIC BLANKET: a gingham blanket with a woven texture, fringe, folds and crumbs on meadow grass, plus overlay Ant: a small 3D ant (built from primitives, no colliders) that walks to the mouse's point on the floor with a tripod leg gait and wiggling antennae, and wanders when the mouse is off the floor. The overlay fades with the floor (Display floor / the menu fade) and follows FloorStyle.overlay. CRUMBLING FLOOR (hideBase, overlay Crumble): real stone tiles (cubes, no colliders - the floor's collider stays, so husks still rest on it) over the visible floor circle; on a clock cycle they stay whole, then crack loose one by one (rim first, shuffled: a shake with a warm crack glow, then they drop away tumbling and shrinking), the floor stays empty a moment, then rebuilds in a ripple from the cube outwards. Timings on PixelFloorOverlay.
--- PixelFloorModes.cs ---
PixelFloorModes.cs What the floor does while 'Display floor' is OFF (Settings > Display, 'When floor is off': PixelFloor.OffMode, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.FloorOffMode, global): 0 Invisible (only the sky, the default), 1 Flat plane (PixelFloor.UpdatePlane: a flat opaque lit sheet in the floor style's average colour just under the floor top, receives shadows; the edge fade melts its rim into the sky), 2 Landing pulse (PixelFloorPulse.Landed / LandedAt, called from OldPixelImpact - OnCollisionEnter plus a fall check in its FixedUpdate for pixels that stop after a fast fall without a collision event: a soft rounded-square ripple, tinted by the pixel's colour, lies on the surface the pixel landed on - turned to the contact normal and the pixel's heading, sized by the pixel and the impact - grows and fades in about 0.7 s; at most 24 at once, so it also works on slopes / shapes / any other solid surface).
--- PixelFloorRing.cs ---
PixelFloorRing.cs ONE helper for "a ring (or part of a ring) drawn with a LineRenderer": Create makes the renderer (no shadows, faces the camera, optional round caps), Draw lays out a whole or partial ring round a centre on any plane (two axes) with a radius, fill 0..1, colour and width. A whole ring (fill >= 0.999) is a closed loop of 'points' points; a partial one is an open arc from angle 0 of ceil(points x fill) + 1 points. SharedMaterial() = one Sprites/Default material (PixelShaders.SpriteDefault: unlit, vertex colours) for rings that don't bring their own. Used by: Charge Crush's charge / cooldown ring (PixelHuskCrush), the Solar Panel's link range ring (PixelSolarPanel.ShowRange), Pixel Grabbing's drop-guide ring (PixelGrab). A new floor ring uses this instead of its own LineRenderer code.
--- PixelFloorShade.cs ---
PixelFloorShade.cs Part of PixelFloor (partial class): drawing the floor textures (BuildTexture, Fill), Shade with its pattern groups (ShadeGrids / Stone / Tiles / Shiny / Arenas / Animated, then ShadeExtra in PixelFloorScenes.cs), the Pac-Man floor and the pattern helpers. The system notes are in PixelFloor.cs.
--- PixelFocus.cs ---
PixelFocus.cs FOCUS POTIONS (component added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelFocus.Instance; one potion per skill, the list is an Inspector list: displayName, skillId, durationSeconds). While one is active, the HAZARD block of its skill stops spawning (PixelSkills.TryGetHazard returns false, so SpecialInfo gives the hazard no weight and the rest of the rotation renormalises; the pre-rolled Next-up block is dropped too, PixelClicker.UpcomingValid). A hazard that is already on the cube is NOT removed. The filler is untouched (the shuffle bonus still works). Only the skill named in skillId is affected, and only while that skill is in play; the timer keeps running if the player switches away. First potion: Lumberjack's Focus (woodcutting, 60 s, holds off the Termite Hive, the Hornet Nest block's new name). Now an ITEM too (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.FocusPotion, Supplies, vendorOnly: the Lumberjack sells it now and then, focusChance; right-click drinks it through DrinkForSkill). Also consumed from Dev Tools (the 'Focus potions' widget: drop-down + Drink + Cancel) through Drink(index) / Cancel(). A shop / crafting recipe from the skill's own materials is the intended source later. Runs on game time (pauses with the game), one potion at a time (drinking another replaces it). The countdown shows as a PixelStatusLines line (key 'focus'). PROGRESS (the running potion: skill, name, seconds left) lives in PixelGameState.Current.focus (GameState.cs, moved 2026-10-23): saved / loaded / reset with it; the potion list stays on this component (Inspector). HoldsOffHazards reads the state, so it works without the component.
--- PixelFreeCam.cs ---
PixelFreeCam.cs FREE CAM (View window tick box 'Free cam', PixelViewKind.FreeCam): the scene camera is detached and flies about by hand while the game carries on and stays clickable. W / S move forward / back along the view, A / D strafe, Space up, C down, Shift = fast; look round with the middle mouse button dragged (or the arrow keys); Ctrl + mouse wheel changes the flying speed. Like the orbit camera it keeps a hidden, disabled copy of the camera at its normal pose (PixelFreeCam.Home, read through PixelOrbitCamera.HomeCamera) so the view bounds and fitted floors do not follow the wandering camera, and leaving it (another view tick, a takeover minigame, the title screen, the camera intro) snaps the camera back to that pose. It is never saved as the player's view (PixelViewWindow.SaveKind skips it): the saved view (Orbital / Top down) is what comes back. The flat (orthographic) view is turned off while it is on (PixelOrthoView), like in first person.
--- PixelFunnel.cs ---
PixelFunnel.cs The FUNNEL (consumable device, PixelConsumables DeviceKind.Funnel 16, one at a time; the placed device is the thin PixelPermanentDevice stub on the same object). A wide glass cone hangs just UNDER the clicker cube (mouth radius mouthRadius cube widths, its mouth mouthDrop below the cube's underside) and catches every loose old pixel that falls into it: the pixel is locked (kinematic, moved by position by the shared PixelPathCarrier), drawn to the throat and carried down a see-through TUBE (PixelTube, a smooth curve from the throat to the output) at tubeSpeed cube widths per second, one gap apart. The output sits on the floor where the player clicked (the object's own position; the spout points away from the cube, Scroll / Q / E turns it a bit); at the end the pixel is put back as an ordinary old pixel (physics again) with a small push - or, with an Out COUPLING on its 'output' socket (PixelSockets.cs), put on the belt that starts at the coupling (PixelCouplings.Emit; the tube waits while that belt is full: OutputHasRoom). Placing it again keeps the coupling. While placing, the output can only be put within maxReach cube widths of the cube. Placing a second funnel MOVES the first (no extra one is used up). Ghost / display (shop picture) models are built by the same code (Mode); the cone and tube are pinned to the WORLD (the object's yaw only turns the spout). Riders in the tube are plain HOLD (backpressure + physics pile): the cone has a SOLID surface (a hidden two-sided mesh collider: the sloped wall and a floor closing the throat), so caught pixels do not vanish into the tube at once: they land in the cone and PILE UP with real physics. One by one (holdFeedSeconds), while the tube has room, the lowest pixel in the cone is locked and carried down the tube. At the spout the tube waits (PixelPathCarrier canExit = ExitHasRoom) when the output coupling's belt has no room for another pixel (PixelConveyorSystem.StartHasRoom) so a flooding hose queues up the tube and then fills the cone (the pile can heap up over the rim) instead of overflowing the belt. disableHold = the old direct catch (no collider).
--- PixelFunnelDevice.cs ---
PixelFunnelDevice.cs The placed Funnel as a DEVICE (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.Funnel 16): the same permanent stub as the Wood Chipper (PixelPermanentDevice) but only the OUTPUT parts (base plate and collar) count when the game asks "is the pointer over this device" (removing it with the right button): the big cone, rim, legs and tube hang round the cube and must not catch clicks meant for it. All the behaviour is in PixelFunnel.
--- PixelGameMode.cs ---
PixelGameMode.cs ONE place that says what the game is doing at the top level, so scripts stop combining PixelTitleScreen.Showing / Reversing, PixelPauseMenu.IsPaused and PixelCameraIntro.Moving by hand (2026-10-24, to-do 'Central game mode'): Title - the title screen is up, or the camera swings back to it (Quit > Main menu: PixelTitleScreen.Reversing) Intro - the opening camera move into the game runs (PixelCameraIntro.Moving; the cube may not be clickable yet) Paused - the pause menu is open (PixelPauseMenu.IsPaused; whether it also froze time is PixelPauseMenu.GameStopped) Playing - none of these: the game is in front of the player (Title wins over the others - the camera intro's swing back to the title counts as Title - and Paused wins over Intro.) The flags stay where they are (their owners set them); their setters call Refresh(), which works the mode out again and fires Changed (old mode, new mode) when it moved - subscribe instead of checking every frame. Takeover minigames (PixelMinigame.TakeoverActive) are NOT a mode: they run inside Playing. PixelCalm.InMenu / CameraBusy are built on this. Use: PixelGameMode.Current, OnTitle, TitleOrIntro (the game hasn't started for the player yet), InMenu (Title or Paused), Playing. Code that cares about exactly one screen's own flag (the pause menu itself, the title screen itself) can still read it directly.
--- PixelGameState.cs ---
PixelGameState.cs The OWNER of the player's progress (GameState.cs; docs/systems/PixelGameState.md). - PixelGameState.Current is the one GameState. It is never null: it is made on first use, so a system that starts early never waits for a save or for this component. - SAVE: PixelSaveGame calls RaiseSaving() first (the Saving event: WORLD objects - placed devices, the running potion - copy themselves into their sub-state there, instead of every frame), then writes Current into the save file whole (SaveData.state). - LOAD: PixelSaveGame calls Replace(the file's copy) at the start of its load and RaiseLoaded() once the tiers, shop and husks are back but BEFORE the Dev Hub tester machines (2026-10-24: a Solar Panel links to a placed device and belts dock on devices' couplings, so world objects rebuilt in Loaded must exist before the machines come back). Views refresh / world objects rebuild there. - NEW GAME / RESTART: both reload the scene and call NewGame() right before it (PixelPauseMenu Restart, PixelTitleScreen New Game - the only two scene loads), so the new scene starts with a fresh 'new GameState()'. Moved systems need no reset of their own. (Not done with PixelStatics.ResetOnRestart: sceneUnloaded can come after the new scene has started, which would wipe the new game's first moments.) - INSPECTOR: this component (in PixelSystems.All, Core) shows Current under 'State' while playing, so values can be read and typed to test. It adopts an edited copy if the Inspector swaps the object.
--- PixelGelFx.cs ---
PixelGelFx.cs The Ghost pixel's look (looksVersion 39 replaced the saved look): a gray, translucent, gelatinous blob - PixelLook.blob swaps the cube for a very rounded one (PixelGelFx.BlobMesh, PixelEgg.RoundedCubeMesh(0.32) scaled 1.1x so it doesn't look smaller than the other pixels, via PixelClicker.MeshFor; hit box still a cube), PixelLook.gelFx adds a darker nucleus that drifts, turns and breathes inside (PixelGelCore, unlit vertex-coloured rounded cube) and a few tiny bubbles rising through it; stronger jelly wobble (wobbleAmount 0.2, speed 2.8); colour gray (0.62, 0.66, 0.71, alpha 0.4), glossy 0.92. Old behaviour unchanged (bounces slowly, floats off screen). Sounds (code-synthesized while their clip lists are empty): click pixel_ghost = PixelSynth.GhostClick (a wet gloopy bloop with a second bubble, a squelch and a breathy tail, 3 variants), landing pixel_bounce_ghost = GhostBounce (soft squishy splut), and ghost_float (played by OldPixelFloat.StartFloating) = GhostFloat (a hollow breathy rising 'whoo').
--- PixelGhostMinigame.cs ---
PixelGhostMinigame.cs Ghost minigame (bought in the shop's Minigames tab via a pack with unlocksMinigame = "ghost"): a faint wobbling cube floats across the screen at random intervals; the ghost carries a small glass cube with the chosen potion's pixel cube inside (PickRandomBuff at spawn, BuildPotion); clicking it drops the potion (DropPotion: rigidbody, shatters on first hit or after dropMaxSeconds, glass shards like the glass pixel, sound glass_shatter) and the free buff starts then (PixelConsumables.ApplyBuff; GrantRandomBuff is the fallback when no potion could be picked); counts toward GhostsCaught (saved); extends PixelMinigame (id ghost); the Ghost Pixel pack has a MinigameGoal requirement for ghost. PixelShop finds/adds this component. Backpack (needs Pixel Grabbing switched on in Toggles - it is unlocked from the start, no purchase, PixelGrab.CanGrab): the falling potion has air drag (grabFallDrag) and can be grabbed with the mouse (potionGrabRadius, kinematic follow, throw on release); a semi-transparent pixel-art backpack icon (bottom right above the bar, backpackSize/Margin/Alpha) shows while it falls; releasing over the icon keeps the potion (KeepPotion, PixelConsumables.AddKept, no shatter, no buff) and, if that potion is already at its limit, raises that potion's bonusCap by one (permanent, progress in the GameState: InventoryState bonus; ItemCapacity(item) = maxPotionsHeld + bonus drives ItemRoom/ClampHeld(index, n)/the shop's Full text), so e.g. 16 held can be used down to 6 and re-bought to 16.
--- PixelGoals.cs ---
PixelGoals.cs DATA-DRIVEN GOALS for the Log's Goals tab (2026-10-18, design docs/GameDesign.md section 6 'Early game feel: click, pile, crush' point 2: goals ALTERNATE clicking and husks, so crushing feels on-task). ONE table (goals, editable in the Inspector; the built-in rows are added by id when missing - DefaultGoals) instead of one hand-written goal each. A row = id, title, description, what it counts, target, when it APPEARS, and a small REWARD paid by the goal (skill XP and / or a little currency - never by a husk). - Counter: StatSinceShown (a PixelStats counter, counted from the moment the goal appeared), StatValue (a counter as it is, e.g. a best value like husk.crush.streak) or ManualClicksSinceShown. - Appears: Always, HusksOnFloor (at least appearValue husks lying out), StatAtLeast (appearKey >= appearValue, e.g. guide.stage1 = Cubie's visit 1 handed over the Recycler) or MinutesPlayed. Never during the new-game intro or on the title screen. Built-in rows: Click 50 times (start), Crush 10 husks (20 husks lying out), Recycle 20 husks (after Cubie's Recycler), Get a crush streak of 5 (minute 3). - State is saved per save in PixelStats counters: goal.<id>.shown, goal.<id>.start (the counter when it appeared), goal.<id>.done. Checked every checkSeconds; on completion the reward is paid (PixelSkills.AddRewardXp / PixelClicker.AddBonusCurrency), the event log says so with a sound, and the session recorder notes it on its timeline. A finished goal stays in the tab as complete for showDoneSeconds. - PixelLog.CollectGoals shows the active ones right after the next-pixel-unlock goal (the hand-built click / unlock goals stay as they are); the Game Database lists every row (category 'Goal', discovered once it has appeared). THE EARLY GOALS ARE THE UNLOCKS (2026-10-18, design 'Early game feel' item 2): rewards can also UNLOCK A TIER (rewardUnlocksTier + rewardTier; the tier's TierUnlockMode is ByGoal so its threshold never unlocks it while a goal does - PixelClicker.CheckUnlocks) and START A CUBIE VISIT (rewardCubieVisit 1 / 2 -> PixelGuideVendor.RequestVisit; Cubie's White / Black counts are only the fallback without such a row). afterGoal chains rows; TierTotal counts a pixel type's lifetime total. Built-in chain (goalDataVersion 1 replaced the earlier rows once): Click 50 blocks (start) -> Cubie's visit 1 (the Recycler) Crush 10 husks (20 husks on the floor) -> Gray unlocks Recycle 20 husks (after visit 1) -> Black unlocks Get a crush streak of 5 (minute 3) -> 60 Standard XP Earn 120 Black (after Recycle 20) -> Cubie's visit 2: RGB + the shop Earn 2,000 Blue (minute 40, after Earn 120 Black) -> Cubie's visit 3: his BILL (PixelCubieBill; ~1 h, the end of the early game - tune it) PACE (sized for RGB + the shop at about 6 min, ~3.5 blocks a second, about 60% of the time clicking): click 50 ~0:20 + Cubie ~0:30; Gray ~1:00; Black ~2:30-3:00 (the Recycler is placed and 20 husks land in it); 120 Black at 1/3 of blocks ~ 2.5-3 min of mixed play -> visit 2 at ~5:30, the shop at ~6:00. Re-check against the next session report (the recorder's timeline lists 'Goal done: ...'). The on-screen guide line follows the current goal (PixelUI.BuildGuideText, CurrentGuideGoal). A NEW goal = a new row (or a DefaultGoals line), no code.
--- PixelGodFind.cs ---
PixelGodFind.cs The finds of the three Egyptian God Cubes (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; PixelGodFind.Play(tierIndex, worldPos), queued; called from PixelClicker.CollectInternal when a God Cube pays out). The cubes (PixelType.GodSlifer / GodRa / GodObelisk, PixelClicker.IsGodCube, tiers made by EnsureGodCubes, spawn weight godCubeSpawnWeight = 0.000001, one of each: CanSpawn is false and AddCurrency ignores a second one) have their OWN show each, all drawn in UI space on a canvas that blocks clicks (sorting 691, unscaled time, Escape skips to the backpack like the Dragon Cube show): SLIFER - a storm: dark clouds, three lightning strikes (flash + jagged bolts), a long red lightning serpent that flies across the screen in a figure-eight with crackling arcs, then coils into the cube in a final blast. RA - a golden sphere rises and grows, bursts into a sun (rays, whiteout), then golden wings (two fans of feathers) beat around the cube to the rhythm of the sound while feathers fall, and fold in with a golden shockwave. OBELISK - the screen shakes harder and harder as rubble falls, a giant blue fist lowers into view, cocks back and SLAMS the screen: flash, crack lines radiate, shockwaves, debris; the cube rises from the crater. Then the cube hops into the backpack like the Dragon Cube's. Sounds god_slifer / god_ra / god_obelisk (code-made PixelGodSynth, timed to the shows). Settings: disableShow, showSpeed, darkness, cubeSize. The cube picture is a hidden studio camera -> RenderTexture (a trimmed copy of PixelDragonFind's studio; the two could share one helper).
--- PixelGodSynth.cs ---
PixelGodSynth.cs Code-made sounds of the three Egyptian God Cube find shows (PixelAudio.SynthFor -> PixelGodSynth.For; put your own clips in the Sounds list to replace them). Each clip is one long piece that is timed to its show in PixelGodFind (start of the show = start of the clip): god_slifer (storm rumble, three thunder cracks at 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.4 s, the dragon's roar from 1.5 s, a final blast at 4.9 s), stone_space / stone_mind / stone_reality / stone_power / stone_time / stone_soul (the Prime Cubes, 5.5 s, each one's own voice, the climax at 3.2 s) and stone_cubelette (six rising notes, the snap at 3.4 s, a chime at 5.0 s), god_ra (a rising shimmering choir, a swell at 1.2 s, wing beats from 1.6 s, a golden chime at 4.0 s) and god_obelisk (a growing rumble, the fist rising, the slam at 2.55 s, falling stone).
--- PixelGoldenSnitch.cs ---
PixelGoldenSnitch.cs The GOLDEN SNITCH (PixelType.GoldenSnitch 101): a very rare drop (a rareDrop tier like the Dragon Cubes, PixelClicker.EnsureGoldenSnitch, spawnWeight goldenSnitchSpawnWeight 0.00003) that is never clicked off the cube (CollectInternal ignores it, Block Hold can't grab it). Component added by PixelClicker.Awake. 0. ON THE CUBE: it lands (PixelClicker.PixelMaterialized) and already LOOKS like the snitch: the detailed golden cube model - engraved dark edges, a middle band, bright corner studs and face gems, gold wing knobs, a soft cube glow shell, a gold point light, sparkles - sits over the clicker cube (onCubeCover 1.03 x its size) and follows its turn / pops every frame (LateUpdate -> FollowCube), wings folded away. It waits for a MANUAL click (PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> CubeClicked; the auto clicker leaves it alone). Nobody clicks it for onCubeSeconds (120) and it slips away (escaped). 1. SPROUT: the click makes that same cube sprout two silver feathered wings right where it sits (sproutSeconds 1.1, fluttering faster and faster) while it eases off the cube towards the camera and turns to face it. The cube rolls a fresh block behind it (PixelClicker.ReplaceCurrentPixel). 2. CHASE: it darts round the screen fast (viewport steering inside the black bars: quick darts to far points, little hops, feints, a flutter), leaving a gold trail, its wings buzzing (snitch_buzz). Keep the mouse ON it (hoverScale x its size on screen) and a bar above it charges (chargeSeconds 3.2 of hovering); off it the bar does NOT charge (drainPerSecond 0 = it just waits). It flies faster and more wildly the fuller the bar. A click while it is still charging costs earlyPenalty (25%) of the bar ('Too early!'). 3. CAPTURE: with the bar full a white ring closes in on it towards a gold target ring (ringSeconds 1.1); CLICK while the mouse is on it AND the rings meet (captureTolerance 0.2) to catch it. Too early / too late = a miss: the bar drops to missKeep (35%), it bolts away; maxMisses (3) misses or its lifetime (lifetimeSeconds 30) and it escapes off the screen. CAUGHT: it freezes, folds its wings and bursts into gold sparkles: it counts as collected (the Golden Snitch tier +1) and pays rewardClicks (400) x what a click on your best unlocked pixel pays now, plus an ultraChance (50%) Ultra pixel; minigame-style skill XP (skillXp 40 to the skill in training). Status lines (key 'snitch'), event log lines, code-made sounds (PixelSnitchSynth via PixelAudio: snitch_appear / buzz / ready / catch / miss), Stats (snitch.seen / caught / escaped, Best snitch.fastest). In first person the crosshair is the pointer. Pauses with the pause menu; not saved (one in flight is simply lost on quit).
--- PixelGrab.cs ---
PixelGrab.cs Pixel Grabbing (unlocked from the start - Awake sets grabbingActive; the shop pack was removed by MigrateOldPackData; it keeps its on / off switch in Toggles > Upgrades, on by default; the switch is progress in PixelGameState.Current.grab, GameState.cs, moved 2026-10-23): hold left mouse on an old pixel (sphere-cast, grabRadius) to drag it on the camera-facing plane through it (rigidbody velocity follows the mouse, so it still collides), release to throw (throwStrength, maxThrowSpeed); a held pixel's OldPixelDespawn.Held freezes its lifetime (a held glass pixel can't shatter; releasing slower than gentleReleaseSpeed = a soft set-down: no break, speed damped, gentleLifetimeBonus seconds added via OldPixelDespawn.AddLifetime) and its ScaledGravity is paused. While time is slowed a still-flying meteor pixel (OldPixelFlight, trigger collider) can be grabbed too: PixelClicker.LandFlyingPixel turns it into an ordinary old pixel (solid, gravity, view bounds, normal lifetime); grabbing / throwing divide / multiply velocities by PixelTimeStop.SlowFactor so it still follows the mouse normally. Gentle collisions: a carried pixel is light (carriedMassFactor 0 = 0.2 of its mass, stays light lightSecondsAfterThrow 1.5 s after the throw) and every old pixel within ~1.7 widths of a light one has its speed capped to hitReactionSpeedLimit (0 = 5) for 0.7 s (LimitReactions, MakeLight), so piles react less and nothing flies far; physics still applies (disableGentleHits). Sets PixelClicker.GrabEnabled so clicks on old pixels no longer pass through to the cube. Sounds grab/drop. Steady carrying (2026-10-16, held husks used to jitter / bounce against the floor and piles): while held a husk is interpolated, uses continuous collisions, can't tumble (freezeSpinWhileHeld), is pushed out of things gently (heldDepenetrationSpeed 2) and is lifted heldAutoLift (0.35) widths off what it rested on; all of it is put back on release. PixelShop finds/adds it. TUMBLING WHEN THROWN (2026-10-17, thrown husks used to skid flat): on release StartTumble gives a husk thrown faster than minTumbleSpeed sideways a roll along the throw (axis = up x sideways velocity, speed = travel / half height x throwSpin, capped maxThrowSpin - maxAngularVelocity is raised for it, Unity's default 7 rad/s was too low - plus spinWobble on the axis) and for thrownGripSeconds swaps its collider to a grippy copy of its own material (friction >= thrownGrip, combined by Maximum; husk materials normally combine friction by Minimum so corners never caught). EndThrownGrip puts the original back (time over, or grabbed again). Tags: OldPixelInfo.heldByPlayer / lastHeldTime / RecentlyHeld(seconds) = 'recently held by the player' (held now, or let go within recentlyHeldSeconds 0 = 6, Inspector); the Storage Crate stores a tagged pixel that lands in its footprint from any height. GROUND CLAMP (2026-10-16): a held husk is never pushed into the surface under it (TryGroundUnder: highest still collider straight below, husks skipped); lowering it with the scroll wheel stops at the ground (it used to sink in, crawl after the mouse and replay the landing sound). Accessibility 'Drop guide for held pixels' (Settings, PixelDisplaySettings.DropGuide, on by default): while holding a pixel LateUpdate / UpdateDropGuide draws a faint line straight down from it to the first surface a sphere of its size would land on (ignores itself, triggers and the live cube) and a ring on that surface = where it drops if let go standing still.
--- PixelGreatOak.cs ---
PixelGreatOak.cs The GREAT OAK (PixelType.GreatOak 77, PixelBlockRules special rule): a huge Woodcutting block that takes a random 50-100 clicks. While Woodcutting is the skill in play every rolled wood block (Wood, Bark, Sap, Leaf, Heartwood, Twig) has a 5% chance of becoming one (edit it in PixelBlockRules; no spawn weight of its own). While you hit it, BIRD NESTS fall out of the tree (this component, added by PixelClicker.Awake, listens to PixelClicker.PixelHit): each hit has a nestChance (0 = 40%) to drop one, a nest (a woven ring with eggs) lands on the ground near the cube and stays for nestSeconds (0 = 9, it blinks away in the last 2 s). Click it (the pointer, or the crosshair in first person - with any item selected) before it vanishes for nestXp (0 = 30) Woodcutting XP, a chirp and a puff of feathers. At most maxNests (0 = 10) lie about at once; each nest follows a short scripted arc out of a random SIDE of the cube (no physics and no collider at all, so it cannot bounce away), lands close to the cube (up to scatterCubes, 0 = 3.2 cube widths), makes one small hop and stays.
--- PixelGuideVendor.cs ---
PixelGuideVendor.cs Cubie's intro visits only start when nothing else is open (Calm: no window in PixelWindows, no tip box, no minigame...) and only while 'Show first-time tips' (PixelHints.TipsEnabled) is on: with tips off the visits are skipped, their progress is recorded and the starter gift is handed over quietly once the shop exists (Update); Replay tutorial still plays them. Cubie, the suspicious cube guide / shopkeeper (added by PixelClicker.Awake; code-drawn teal cube with darting sideways eyes, hat, smirk; slides in like the visitors, time stops, a paged speech box, Next button or Escape = next page). Visit 1 (new game, after the intro tips, calmSeconds (1.5) of calm): a shady dealer's pitch - pixels are money 'because that's what we're all told' (a fiat-currency joke), clicking is the monotonous grind you endure until the good stuff opens up - then he leaves and you click pixels (4 pages; visit 2 has 7 and ends on the same theme: the clicking never stops, it just pays better). Visit 2 (RGB UNLOCK, 2026-10-16: when visitTwoAfterBlack (100) Black pixels have been EARNED - Visit2Due, BlackUntilVisitTwo for the guide line / Log goal): he says the basics are done, GIVES THE RGB PACK for free (PixelShop.GrantPackRewarding; the pack is hidden from the shop, so everything that needs it still works) plus a taste of Red / Green / Blue (rgbGiftMin..rgbGiftMax each, AddBonusCurrency - a gift is not 'earned') when the page containing {gift} shows (a gift box appears in his hand); the shop opens with it (PixelShop.requiredTier Red); he points at the Auto Clicker as the first buy and at a skill (Big Tim) next; with Cubie off (disableGuide) RGB unlocks quietly at the same point; says more vendors will come and can be switched off in the Toggles menu, mentions the Wizard. Progress = stat counters guide.stage1/2 (saved with the game); a save that already has the shop gets no visits and no gift (stages set silently). PixelGuideVendor.Pending/Visiting hold back the shop tutorial; Settings > Replay tutorial calls PixelGuideVendor.Replay() (clears the stage counters, both visits play again, no second gift). The vendors are free now: the Wizard / Tinkerer / Pixel Entrepreneur packs were removed from the shop (MigrateOldPackData drops them from saved lists); the guide starts them (StartVendors: Wizard right after visit 2 with a first visit in wizardFirstVisitMin..Max s, Farmer at farmerAtTiers (5), Tinkerer at tinkererAtTiers (6) unlocked pixel types, Entrepreneur at entrepreneurAtTiers (8); a vendor the player switched off in Toggles is never restarted; PixelVisitorMinigame.ScheduleFirstVisit). All texts / numbers are Inspector fields (disableGuide turns it all off). The page lists (visitOnePages / visitTwoPages) are filled with the built-in lines when empty (EnsureDefaultPages in Awake and the editor's OnValidate, like PixelHints), so Cubie's lines are edited in the Inspector. GOAL-DRIVEN (2026-10-18): visit 1 and visit 2 are REWARDS of the early goals (PixelGoals: Click 50 blocks -> visit 1, Earn 120 Black -> visit 2; RequestVisit sets guide.visit<n>.due, Visit1Ready / Visit2Ready read it). Only when no enabled goal grants a visit does the old count below apply. With Cubie off or tips off the Recycler still arrives when visit 1 would have come (the 'Recycle 20 husks' goal needs it). Fallback: visit 1 waits until visitOneAfterWhiteClicks (100) White pixels have been clicked (the White tier's timesCollected). A visit-1 page containing {recycler} hands over a Recycler (PixelConsumables.GiveDevice; text recyclerGiftText); a page list without the token still gets it when the visit ends. The speech box is the standard window as a dialog (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, no X), teal, at the right edge beside Cubie. CUBIE'S BILL (2026-10-23, PixelCubieBill): visit 1's pages hint at his monopoly and the Recycler's price tag, visit 2 adds 'Don't worry about the tag. Yet.' (cubieDataVersion 3 replaced the saved pages once). VISIT 3 (visitThreePages) = the bill reveal, due when PixelCubieBill.RevealDue (its goal, PixelGoals rewardCubieVisit 3): the {invoice} page unrolls a long paper invoice (UnrollInvoice, invoiceExtraLines), and when the visit ends PixelCubieBill.Reveal takes the tags off and opens the Bill window. RequestTalk(pages) = a short visit (kind 4) when a trust tier opens. Visit(kind, pages) plays all four kinds. With Cubie or tips off the bill arrives quietly (WillReveal false: PixelCubieBill reveals it itself).
--- PixelHazardTell.cs ---
PixelHazardTell.cs TELLS that the block on the cube is a HAZARD (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; applies to every hazard block - PixelClicker.IsHazard: Hornet Nest, Pest, Gas Pocket, Jellyfish, Mold - while it sits on the cube): a pulsing warning-orange glow shell round the cube, a bobbing red warning triangle with a '!' floating above it (a billboard), and a warning sound (hazard_warn, a two-tone warble) the moment the hazard appears, plus a dry skittering chitter (termite_chitter) for the Termite Hive. Everything pulses FASTER as the hazard's time on the cube runs out (it leaves by itself after hazardSeconds), so the pulse also tells how long is left. Switch off with disableTells; colours / sizes are serialized. Sounds (code-made): PixelHazardSynth, hooked into PixelAudio.SynthFor.
--- PixelHighlight.cs ---
PixelHighlight.cs Shared pulsing frame (the theme highlight colour, PixelUITheme.HighlightColor: gold) round ANY UI element: PixelHighlight.Set(owner, rect, colour) frames 'rect' on its own canvas (sorting order 615: above the windows, below the tip boxes at 620), Clear(owner) fades it out (only the owner that set it can clear it). One frame at a time; it follows the rect every frame (windows popping in / moving) and hides while the rect is inactive. Used by PixelShopNudge (the Shop button while the shop hold is on) and PixelSkills' perk popups (the skill's card, e.g. Standard level 2). Created on demand, no scene setup.
--- PixelHintBox.cs ---
PixelHintBox.cs A bottom-of-screen instruction line inside a window-style box (same look as PixelStatusBox, which does the same job at the top). PixelHintBox.Create(font, canvasTransform, fontSize, bottomOffset) returns the TMP label: set its .text as before (PixelUIKit.SetText also works). The box grows / shrinks with the text (wraps at MaxWidth) and is invisible while the text is empty, so callers need no show / hide code. Used by the first person hint line (PixelFirstPerson). Look values are consts at the top (no component of its own in the scene).
--- PixelHold.cs ---
PixelHold.cs LOOK (PixelHoldMeter): the meter sits to the RIGHT of the pointer (left when there is no room), bigger (ring 104, bar 300 x 30): dark plate, thick ring with a bright spark at its end and the percentage in the middle, a pulsing glow that strengthens as it fills, a pop-in; the bar version has a glossy fill, a rim and a spark. First person pry puts it beside the crosshair. PixelHold.Update(caller, target, seconds, label, colour, circle, buildSound, useLeft): hold-the-right (or, useLeft, the LEFT) mouse-button helper with a filling bar next to the pointer (PixelHoldMeter, built lazily) or, with circle = true, ONLY a ring that fills round the cursor (no text) and a rising 'buildup' sound that stops when the hold ends (PixelHold.ProgressOf(target) lets the owner react, e.g. the removed device glows red); true when the hold completes. Used by PixelConsumables.UpdateRemoval (hold on a placed device -> PixelPlacedDevice.RemoveNow(), no refund; hit-test = renderer bounds, ignoring the range disc / blow area) and PixelUI.UpdateActiveHud (hold on the active-potion line -> PixelConsumables.CancelActive). Settings (removeHoldSeconds, texts, colour) are in PixelConsumables; sound device_remove.
--- PixelHoverMarker.cs ---
PixelHoverMarker.cs A small floating DOWN ARROW over a placed device that shows only while the pointer is on the device (pops in / out, bobs, faces the camera), with an optional short text beside it (the arrow is ONE solid mesh - shaft + triangle head - since 2026-10-18; three bars read as a trident) (the Recycler's 'm:ss' left). ONE shared piece: the Recycler and the Sorting Hopper use it (Attach); a new device that wants the same 'this is where it goes' hint attaches one too. 'isOver' says whether a pointer ray is on the device (usually its PixelPlacedDevice.HitTest); the marker's own parts are named "Hover ..." so they read as part of the device.
--- PixelHud.cs ---
PixelHud.cs Screen frame: black bar along the top and bottom (barHeight) and the docked buttons. PixelHud.Ensure(gameObject) then hud.Dock(buttonRect, anchor(0|1,0|1), keepOutWhile) gives a button the standard ButtonSize and makes it slide off screen (only a sliver shows) until the mouse is near (PixelDockedButton); it stays out while its window is open. Windows that open from a docked button use hud.SideShownInset / hud.WindowOffset for position (both equal the gap the docked buttons keep from the black margin, ShownInset, so windows and buttons line up with the same gap on every side); top/bottom-anchored overlays add PixelHud.Instance.BarHeight. Used by Inventory, Shop, Log and Crafting buttons. Menu buttons setting (PixelHud.ButtonMode, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.ButtonMode, per save, Settings > Interface 'Menu buttons'): 0 peeking (sliver shows, default), 1 always showing (SlideButtons false), 2 hidden (Sliver 0 until the mouse is near). While 'always showing' (mode 1) each docked button gets its own nested canvas with overrideSorting at alwaysOutSortingOrder (0 = 90, PixelDockedButton.UpdateSorting, plus a GraphicRaycaster), below every window canvas (100+), so windows overlay the buttons; other modes use the window's own canvas order again. Bar colour: Settings > Display 'Custom bar colour' tick box + an eyedropper button beside it (PixelHud.BarColorOn / BarColorPicked / SetBarColorFromColor, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.BarColorOn + BarColor (hex); the player's own, NOT per save - see the note in PixelSettingsSync.Known_); the game's top / bottom margins (PixelHud.barImages) and the main menu's middle bar (PixelTitleScreen.bar) repaint when PixelHud.BarColorVersion changes, using PixelHud.BarTint(base) (the picked colour with the bar's own opacity; the menu bar stays 92% opaque). The docked buttons sit towards the screen edge, not centred in the bar (buttonEdgeBias, 0 = 0.55), to stay clear of the neon bar glow. The pause menu adds this component if missing.
--- PixelHuskCrush.cs ---
PixelHuskCrush.cs HUSK CRUSH (design: docs/GameDesign.md section 6, 'Standard - the ability skill'; built 2026-10-17). Free from the first minute (not a tree node): RIGHT-CLICK a husk lying on the floor and it breaks apart for a little skill XP - never currency (pixel rules: husks are never currency; only the Recycler turns them into currency, at a loss). Who gets the right click is decided by PixelRightClick (the cube, the hose, the conveyor kit, pets and device parts come first); this script only acts when PixelRightClick.Current is Husk. - XP: xpShare (0.5; 0.25 before the 2026-10-17 crush bonuses) of the XP a manual click on that block gives (C = PixelSkills.ClickXpOf: 1 for a skill block, Standard's flat XP for a pure one), to the husk's own skill via PixelSkills.AddHuskXp(crushed: true) - pure husks train Standard, wood husks Woodcutting... The crush's popup always shows, small amounts with decimals (PixelSkillsPopups). - CRUSH BONUSES (2026-10-17, design 'Crush bonuses', numbers from the 10:50 session report; CrushXp): multiplied together, then CAPPED at clickXpCap (1.0) x C - a crush never pays more than a click, so swapping clicks for crushes never pays: AIR CRUSH x airMultiplier (2): the husk has not touched anything yet (OldPixelInfo.landed, set by OldPixelImpact's first collision); LAST-GASP x lastGaspMultiplier (1.5): its last lastGaspSeconds (2) of real lifetime (OldPixelDespawn.RemainingReal); CRUSH STREAK +streakStep (10%) for each crush within streakWindow (1 s) of the one before, up to streakCap (x1.5); 1 s without one resets it - every husk a Chain / Charge Crush breaks counts as one crush; Hard Hands (below) multiplies in too, under the same cap. Every crush's '+n XP' stays where the husk broke; a bonus adds a short text BY THE MOUSE (stacked, never overlapping) (PixelSkills.AddHuskXp popupText: 'Air crush bonus!', 'Last-gasp bonus!' or 'Crush streak x1.4!' - the biggest one), at most one text every bonusPopupGap (0.35 s). Stats husk.crushed.air, husk.crushed.lastgasp; BestStreak (since ResetBestStreak) for the session recorder. - PICKING (PickAt, used by PixelRightClick): the husk exactly under the pointer, else the nearest crushable husk whose on-screen edge is within pickMarginPixels (28 at 1080p) - PixelHuskQuery.NearestOnScreen - so small, far and moving husks are easy to hit; never while the pointer is on the cube (even mid-materialize). - CanCrush: a loose husk ON the floor's husk list that a container could take (PixelHuskHolder.Takeable: not vanishing, not held / reserved by a machine - Recycler, Storage Crate, Sorting Hopper, Bait Bucket -, not riding a belt, not in the hand, not a fly-away), not a rare collectible (Dragon / God / Prime Cubes, the Golden Snitch) and not in neverCrush. - Feedback: shards of the husk itself (OldPixelShatter.SpawnShards, the glass shatter's burst), crushSoundId ('husk_crush', code-made PixelSynth.HuskCrush: crack + thump + skittering chunks, 4 variants) with a little pitch spread. - STANDARD TREE (DefaultNodes(StandardId), bottom row, beside the twists): 'chaincrush' Chain Crush (level 4): the husks of the SAME type touching the crushed one (centre within chainTouch husk widths, PixelHuskQuery.Near), and the ones touching those, crush too in quick waves (chainStepSeconds), at most chainMax extra; 'cleanbreak' Clean Break (level 6): every crush gives the husks within cleanBreakRadius widths cleanBreakSeconds of their lived time back (OldPixelDespawn.GiveBackTime); 'hardhands' Hard Hands (level 9): crush XP x hardHandsFactor (1.6 = 40% of a click). - CHARGE CRUSH ('chargecrush', level 7, needs Chain Crush; row 4 of the tree): a quick CLICK on a husk is still the crush above (Chain Crush included) - with the node owned the crush happens when the button comes UP, so a press can still turn into a hold. HOLDING right on a husk for holdDelay (0.25 s) starts a charge (mirrors Power Click): a ring on the floor round the husk grows from startReach to maxReach husk widths over chargeSeconds, with the rising 'remove_buildup' whine (stretched to the charge time, like PixelHold) and Power Click's 'snitch_ready' ding + a gold pulsing ring at full. Letting go (any time after the charge started) crushes the held husk and sends an arc (a short jagged line) to every husk of the SAME type inside the ring, touching or not, nearest first (arcStepSeconds apart, at most maxTargets); every one is a normal crush (same XP rules, Clean Break, never currency). Then a cooldown (cooldownSeconds 8): holding a husk meanwhile shows a grey ring of the time left and letting go just crushes it like a click. Whose press it is comes from PixelRightClick.PressOwner (a press that started on a husk stays the husk's while held, so Block Hold never starts from it). A charge is dropped (no cooldown) in a menu, a takeover minigame, first person or frozen time. Stat husk.chargecrush. LEFT CLICK while charging (2026-10-18) cancels it WITHOUT the cooldown: nothing is crushed, the charge is dropped and the next one can start at once. While charging the pointer is claimed as a button (PixelPointerClaim.ClaimButton), so that left click never reaches the cube, Pixel Grabbing or Power Click. - CRUSH REFUNDS (Standard level perks, 2026-10-18, CrushRefunds): from level 7 every crushed husk takes powerClickRefundSeconds (0.5) off Power Click's cooldown, from level 8 it refills the Block Hold meter by blockHoldRefill (2%), from level 9 it takes chargeCrushRefundSeconds (0.25) off Charge Crush's cooldown (not the husks a Charge Crush breaks itself). Perks 'Crush recharge' / 'Crush grip' / 'Crush rhythm' in StandardPerks. - First-time tip 'husk_crush' (PixelHints) once tipAfterClicks manual clicks were made and a crushable husk lies about (not during the intro). Stats: husk.crushed, husk.crushed.xp (the base XP handed to AddHuskXp, before XP multipliers), husk.crushed.chain (the extra ones Chain Crush broke), husk.crushed.charge (the ones a Charge Crush release broke, the held one included), husk.crushed.<skill id> / husk.crushed.xp.<skill id> (per skill); the session recorder reports them. Nothing saved.
--- PixelHuskHolder.cs ---
PixelHuskHolder.cs ONE place for the "take a husk off the floor and keep it" steps the husk containers share (pulled out of the Recycler, Storage Crate and Bait Bucket on 2026-10-17, when the Sorting Hopper needed the same steps a fourth time). A new container uses these, never its own copy: - Takeable(clicker, body): a loose husk a container may take now - not vanishing, not kept / reserved by another machine (despawn.Held, kinematic), not in the player's hand, a real pixel type, not a fly-away (Meteor) or a flying copy. - Capture(clicker, body): takes it off the floor's husk list (ReleaseOldPixel without credit) and holds its fade timer (despawn.Held): it stays a real physical husk (the Recycler keeps them like that). LetGo(clicker, body, holder): the reverse - unfrozen, its timer running again (unless it is in the player's hand), back on the floor's husk list (AdoptOldPixel). - Freeze / Unfreeze(body, holder): kinematic + parented to the holder, so it moves and scales with it (and back to a loose body). - Reserve / Unreserve(body): a husk on its way somewhere (the Sorting Hopper's slide): kinematic with its timer held but still ON the list, so Takeable says no and no other machine snatches it on the way. - FlyIn(go, target, point, growTarget): the "shrink into the container" animation for containers that keep a husk as DATA (Storage Crate, Bait Bucket): the body is switched off, slides to the target and is destroyed while the container's display model grows in its place. - SpawnIntoHand(clicker, tier, amount, at): a stored husk taken back out straight into the mouse's hand (Pixel Grabbing). ONE OWNER PER HUSK (2026-10-17 Recycler rework): every machine that reaches for a husk - the Recycler's suction / suck-in / Capture, the Magnet, the Sweeper, the Sorting Hopper - asks Takeable first, and the one that takes it marks it (Capture or Reserve: its timer Held, kinematic while carried); from then on Takeable says no to everyone else, so whoever captures a husk first owns it. No device-to-device special cases. Users: PixelRecycler, PixelStorageCrate, PixelBaitBucket, PixelSortingHopper, PixelSweeper, PixelMagnetDevice; Takeable also by every device that takes or pushes loose husks (Fan, Vacuum Device, Wood Chipper, Magnetized Sorter, Quantum Organizer, Trash Can, Kiln, Funnel, Vacuum Cleaner).
--- PixelHuskPool.cs ---
PixelHuskPool.cs HUSK POOLING: a husk whose despawn finishes (OldPixelDespawn's swell-and-shrink end) is switched off and kept, and the next husk that drops reuses it instead of a new GameObject + Rigidbody + MeshFilter + MeshRenderer. Switch: PixelClicker 'huskPooling' (+ 'huskPoolSize'), on by default. THE RULES that keep a reused husk from coming back into someone's list as a different block: - Release(go) fires HuskReleasing(body) FIRST. Every system that keeps references to loose husks across frames (links, zone effects, magnets, belts, grab, joints, caches...) listens and drops the body there and then. Pooling or not, the event always fires, so a holder cannot tell the difference. A NEW system that keeps husk references must listen too (or only keep husks it claimed). - Only husks nobody CLAIMED are pooled. ReleaseOldPixel / ClaimOldPixel / AbsorbOldPixel mark the husk (OldPixelInfo.claimed): its new owner (a device, the Bank, a minigame) destroys it the normal way. Husks taken off the list by the floor cap are not claimed. - On release the husk is switched off and every component except Transform / Rigidbody / MeshFilter / MeshRenderer and every child object is removed at once (DestroyImmediate, so GetComponent never finds the old ones and their OnDestroy cleanup - a gravity well's ring, a mold shell's material - runs now, not when it is reused); the despawn that called Release goes at the end of the frame. On reuse (Take) it is cleaned again (anything left over), the collider is made new by the spawn code (that also clears any Physics.IgnoreCollision pairs of the old collider) and the rigidbody / renderer are put back to a fresh state. Restart: the pooled objects go with the scene; the list drops them (and is cleared by PixelStatics).
--- PixelHuskQuery.cs ---
PixelHuskQuery.cs ONE place to look at the husks lying on the field (PixelClicker.OldPixels) without every system repeating the same work: - Get(body): the husk's OldPixelInfo / OldPixelDespawn, fetched ONCE per husk and cached (GetComponent every check was the old way). The cache prunes itself when it holds far more entries than husks exist (checked by Near, and by Get every 256 new husks, so it also stays small when no zone system calls Near), and is cleared on Restart. - Husk.Tier / TypeOf / Fading (vanishing or already gone) / HeldByPlayer. - Near(clicker, centre, radius, results, flat): every husk within a radius (flat = horizontal distance only), alive ones only unless asked. - CountOfType(clicker, type): how many husks of a pixel type are lying out (e.g. Acorns for the Great Oak chance). - NearestOnScreen(clicker, screen, marginPx, accept): the husk whose EDGE on the screen is nearest the pointer, within marginPx screen pixels (its own on-screen size counts, so a far, small or moving husk is as easy to hit as a near one). Husk Crush uses it when the exact ray misses. Used by the zone-style systems (Sap fields, Mold, the Great Oak boost, the Magnet, Nuclear radiation) and - since the 2026-10-17 device pass - every device / container script (Recycler, Storage Crate, Bank hose, belts, belt sorters, the Sweeper, electric links, Quantum Organizer, Funnel, hoses...). A card missing at the first look (a husk still being made) is looked for again. A NEW zone effect should use Near instead of its own loop.
--- PixelHuskSource.cs ---
PixelHuskSource.cs HUSK SOURCE (2026-10-19, a Dev Hub > Tester feature 'Husk Source', for testing machine lines): a simple box with a coupling SOCKET on each side (Out). It makes an ENDLESS supply of one chosen block type, but only while something takes them: every spawnSeconds each Out coupling with a belt starting at it (with room) or a machine's In coupling right in front of it gets a NEW husk (SpawnStoredPixel + PixelCouplings.Emit), and a Container Hose pulling from one of its couplings gets one whenever it pumps (IPixelHuskSource). Nothing connected = nothing made. LEFT-CLICK the box to choose the type in the shared block picker (PixelSortingHopperWindow, IPixelTierPickTarget: every block type, no None); the type spins over the box. Saved with the tester save (position, turn, type; its couplings go with it).
--- PixelHuskTicker.cs ---
PixelHuskTicker.cs ONE loop for the per-husk helpers in PixelHusks.cs. ScaledGravity, OldPixelImpact (physics step), OldPixelPopIn and OldPixelDespawn (frame) used to each have their own Update / FixedUpdate: with ~150 husks that was ~600 separate calls from the engine every frame. Now each one registers itself here while it is enabled (OnEnable / OnDisable, so 'enabled = false' still pauses it, e.g. a held husk's gravity) and a single hidden runner calls their Tick methods. Order between husks is the same as before (none promised). Safe to change the lists while ticking: a helper removed mid-loop is skipped, one added mid-loop starts ticking next frame. An error in one husk's Tick is logged and the loop carries on with the others (as when each had its own Update). The runner is made on first use (DontDestroyOnLoad, like the save-shot runner) - it is not a game system, so it is not in PixelSystems.
--- PixelHusks.cs ---
PixelHusks.cs Small helper components that ride on HUSKS (the pixels that drop off the cube; code calls them old pixels), split out of PixelClicker.cs: ScaledGravity (gravity scale per body; skips sleeping husks so heaps can sleep - see docs/systems/PixelRules.md 'Husks settle'), OldPixelImpact (landing sound / pulse), OldPixelPopIn (pop-in animation), OldPixelFlight (fly-away marker), OldPixelDespawn (lifetime, swell-and-shrink despawn, Frozen / HoldAll for black holes and takeovers), OldPixelInfo (what tier a husk is, its amount, held-by-player tag), OldPixelFlutter (Leaf husks fall like a leaf: pendulum swing + tilt until the first touch; PixelLook.flutter / flutterSwing / flutterPeriod / flutterTilt / flutterFallSpeed, Leaf on since looksVersion 65). PixelClicker adds them in SpawnFallingCopy. ScaledGravity, OldPixelImpact, OldPixelPopIn and OldPixelDespawn have no Update / FixedUpdate of their own: they register with PixelHuskTicker while enabled and it calls their Tick (one loop for every husk instead of one engine call per husk per helper).
--- PixelInfinity.cs ---
PixelInfinity.cs The Prime Cubes, the Gauntlet and the Prime Cubelette (a homage; names are work in progress). Component added by PixelClicker.Awake. * Six stones (PixelType.StoneSpace..StoneSoul 85-90, PixelClicker.IsInfinityStone, tiers made by EnsureGodCubes) are rare drops with exactly the God Cubes' rules (IsGodCube is true for them: one of each, same spawn weight godCubeSpawnWeight, CanSpawn false once held, backpack stash) and each has its OWN find show (PixelGodFind.DrawStone: Space tesseract, Mind thought-bolts, Reality tearing shapes, Power detonation, Time's backwards clock, Soul wisps) and code-made sound (PixelGodSynth.Stone). Looks: PixelLooks.StoneLook, looksVersion 60. * When the player holds all six (and has no Cubelette and no Gauntlet) the WIZARD offers the Prime Gauntlet (PixelWizardMinigame.ModifyWares + aside line): DeviceKind.Gauntlet 17, a golden cube consumable (vendorOnly, hold 1; price = the Device's costs, editable in the Consumables Inspector). * Right-click the Gauntlet in the inventory (PixelConsumables.TryUseItem) -> this window: a golden cube laid out flat (six faces). Pick a stone from the tray, click a face to place it (click a placed one to take it back). With all six in place the Forge button makes the Prime Cubelette: the Gauntlet and the six stones are used up, the Cubelette tier (PixelType.InfinityCubelette 91, never rolled) is added and its show plays (PixelGodFind.DrawCubelette). Closing the window just puts the stones back (nothing is spent until the forge). The Cubelette is a trophy for now (it has no use yet).
--- PixelInput.cs ---
PixelInput.cs Static input helpers for both input systems (PointerPosition, LeftPressed, RightPressed, PointerOverUI); minigames/consumables use using static PixelInput;.
--- PixelInputCaret.cs ---
PixelInputCaret.cs A blinking text cursor drawn by us for an input field (the built-in one did not show in the dev tools' fields). Add it to a TMP_InputField's object: it hides the built-in caret and draws a white bar at the caret position while the field has focus; works for single-line and multi-line fields. Used by the Inspector's fields and the Dev Hub's Notes.
--- PixelInstantItems.cs ---
PixelInstantItems.cs ONE data-driven list of the INSTANT-USE items (consumable 'devices' that are not placed: right-click in the inventory / a keybind does something at once). Each Entry: the DeviceKind, whether one is used up on success (usedUp), a stat counter, a sound, and Use(consumables, item, out message) -> true when it worked (message = the event-log line, shown on success AND failure). PixelConsumables reads this list for TryUseItem (the use rule), IsSupplyItem (the Supplies category) and the display model (BuildItemModel). A NEW instant item = its DeviceKind (appended to the enum), its CreateDefault... in PixelConsumables (name, costs, maxHeld...), one Entry here, and a model branch in PixelConsumables.BuildItemModel. Current: Combo Fuel (used by itself), Ghost Bait, Pet Treat, Bait Bucket, Prime Gauntlet (opens its window), Conveyor Belt (a tool, never used up), Lumberjack's Focus, Pesticide Spray, and the conveyor-line pieces placed through the Tester features (Coupling, Container Hose, Belt Sorter, Conveyor Elevator, Belt Jump, Conveyor Combiner, Solar Panel: PlacePieces).
--- PixelJunctionBox.cs ---
PixelJunctionBox.cs JUNCTION BOX (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.JunctionBox 26; design agreed in the Design Discussion chat, built 2026-10-18; docs/systems/PixelCouplings.md): a closed box like the Recycler's (PixelFactoryKit: plinth, walls, metal pillars and rim, a teal glowing band, a lid with a junction cross and a small top hatch; the shared carry handles on its +/-x ends) with EIGHT SOCKETS - two on each side (f0 f1 front -z, b0 b1 back +z, l0 l1 left -x, r0 r1 right +x), all flow Both, kind Husk, autoDirection. Placed and saved as a permanent device (the PixelPermanentDevice stub next to it; PlacedState.extra = filters + buffer). - DIRECTION from what is attached (UpdateDirections, every routeSeconds): a belt that ENDS at a coupling's port or a hose that PUSHES into it = In; a belt that STARTS at it or a hose that PULLS from it = Out; nothing = idle (PixelCoupling.SetAuto; its arrow shows it, idle shows none). - TOP OPENING (2026-10-18): a square hole in the lid (HoleHusks) takes husks that drop in (and any husk resting on the lid: nothing piles up on top), and a gentle suction (suckRadiusHusks / suckHeightHusks / suckStrength) draws husks just above the box towards it (FixedUpdate, Swallow). While placing / carrying, a wide red AIM BEAM shines straight up out of the opening (PixelAimOpening), so it lines up under a spout, an elevator's top or a Funnel. - IN = DELETED, OUT = NEW (2026-10-18): every husk that goes in (a coupling, the top opening, the Sweeper) is deleted - it shrinks into the box - and its type + value come out as a NEW husk spawned at an output coupling onto its belt (TakeData / SpawnOut), or wait in the buffer. - ROUTING a husk that comes in (AcceptHusk, from a belt / hose / the Sweeper / a Sorting Hopper's feed pipe / the top opening): 1) to an OUT coupling whose FILTER is the husk's type (it waits in the buffer while that output is full); 2) otherwise ROUND ROBIN across the unfiltered Out couplings (PixelCouplings.Emit onto the belt starting there, only while it has room); 3) otherwise into the BUFFER (bufferSize, 40): kept as data, retried every routeSeconds; a hose pulling from an Out coupling takes from it (IPixelHuskSource: the filter type there, or any type no filtered output wants). Buffer full = refused (a belt throws it on, as at any full device). - FILTERS (one per coupling, used while it is an output): drop a husk you were holding onto a coupling (within filterDropReach) to set it to that type; left-click / scroll a coupling to step through the unlocked types (and none). A small block of the filter type spins over the coupling; hovering the coupling shows the block card (PixelBlockTooltip.ShowFor). Clicks on a coupling are claimed (PixelPointerClaim.ClaimButton). - The SWEEPER treats it as a container with room (IPixelStrayTarget + IPixelHuskRoom = buffer room). - Device card / panel (FillDeviceInfo): buffer n / size, each coupling's way and filter; panel actions Clear filters / Empty buffer (the buffer's husks pop out on top). OVERLAP: it covers the old tester Splitter (round robin; removed 2026-10-18) and much of the tester Belt Sorter (one type to one side) - see PixelCouplings.md. Stats junction.routed, junction.buffered.
--- PixelKeys.cs ---
PixelKeys.cs Rebindable keys: PixelAction enum (TimeStop, Hose, RotateLeft, RotateRight, EventLog, TimeSlow, FirstPerson), defaults + the player's choices (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Key.*), PixelKeys.Pressed/Held(action) (both input systems; KeyCode <-> Key mapping), TryCapture (rebinding screen), Name, Replace(text) (fills {key:TimeStop} style placeholders in Controls.txt, tip boxes, device placing messages; also rewrites old literal "Q / E", "Press T to freeze time", "press B for the hose"). A key already used by another action swaps. Other scripts must call PixelKeys instead of checking fixed keys. While a text box has the keyboard (PixelKeys.Typing, once per frame) every Pressed / Held / KeyPressed / KeyHeld returns false, so typing in the recipe search or other input fields never triggers Time Stop, the hose, first person and so on. Escape (pause / close window) is not rebindable.
--- PixelKiln.cs ---
PixelKiln.cs The KILN (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Kiln'): a brick kiln with a glowing mouth on its +X side, a chimney that smokes and a chute at the back. ANYTHING that goes into the mouth from the side burns: an old pixel (not held, not glued into a build) inside the mouth zone (a box just in front of the door, mouthCenter / mouthHalf in kiln units) is taken out of the game (PixelClicker.ClaimOldPixel, no payout), drawn in while it shrinks and flares, and CHARCOAL is queued: charcoalPerPixel of it comes out charcoalDelaySeconds after the burn, one every charcoalGapSeconds, as the MATERIAL Charcoal (PixelMaterials, id 'charcoal': a "+1 Charcoal" popup over the chimney, added to the Inventory's Materials tab; 2026-10-24 - it used to spit Charcoal husks out of a back chute). Charcoal buys the Charcoal Pixel in the shop, whose husks are Biomass Generator fuel. SOCKETS (PixelSockets.cs): 'mouth' (In, +X) - a belt / connector / hose ending at an In coupling there hands its husks straight in (AcceptHusk); the old 'chute' Out socket is gone. The kiln roars while it burns (light, ember sparks, thicker smoke). Saved with the tester save.
--- PixelLogAchievements.cs ---
PixelLogAchievements.cs Part of PixelLog (partial class): the Achievements tab: its badge rows (and the badge bar / title pieces the goal rows share), the Collector / Clicker row groups, the section headers and RefreshAchievementsTab. The system notes are in PixelLog.cs.
--- PixelLogBuffer.cs ---
PixelLogBuffer.cs ONE in-memory record of the game's log (every Debug.Log / warning / error / exception, from any thread) for the dev Console tab (PixelDevConsole): the last Capacity entries with time, type, message and stack trace; the same message straight after itself is counted (x3) instead of added again. Starts listening before the first scene loads, so errors from startup are there too. Not cleared by Settings > Restart (it is a dev record, not game state). PixelCrashLog keeps its own short line list for the report files.
--- PixelLogGoals.cs ---
PixelLogGoals.cs Part of PixelLog (partial class): the Goals tab: building its list, the goal rows, collecting the goals (next unlock, PixelGoals table, shop / skill goals, minigame trackers) and RefreshGoalsTab. The system notes are in PixelLog.cs.
--- PixelLogoView.cs ---
PixelLogoView.cs The title screen's 'PIXEL CLICKER' logo as a grid of little bevelled cells (one UI image per lit pixel of the 5x7 letters, plus a shadow image and a glow image each), so every cell can move, scale, spin and change colour every frame. PixelTitleScreen.BuildLogo adds this component to the 'Logo' object and calls Init. * STYLES: a table (Styles) of 20 looks, each a small static function (Cell + time -> offset / scale / rotation / colour / glow, 'S') plus an INTRO kind that plays every time the style is shown (Drop, Fly, Zoom, Wipe, Spiral, Stack, Flip): Classic, Rainbow Wave, Neon, Shatter, Matrix, Inferno, Frost, Glitch, Jelly, Flip, Blocks, Plasma, Synthwave, Gold, Ghost, Storm, Shockwave, Disco, Ocean, Hologram. To add one: write a function, add a line to Styles. A style may also scale a whole LETTER (S.lsx / lsy, about its base / centre). * FAVORITE: a small star under each arrow (fades in with it) makes the style showing now the favourite of the save (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.LogoFavorite = index + 1, in PixelSettingsSync, so it is per save); that style is shown instead of a random one, and ReloadFavorite switches to it when a save loads. Click the star again to clear it. * ARROWS: two invisible buttons at the far left / right of the logo fade in while the mouse is near them and step to the previous / next style (wraps); the style's name shows under the logo for a moment. A random style is picked at every launch (never the same as last time; PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.LogoStyle remembers the last one only for that). * PixelTitleScreen only positions / bobs / fades (SetAlpha) the container. Runs on unscaled time (the game is frozen while the title shows). Cost: ~200 cells x 3 images, updated each frame only while the title screen is up.
--- PixelLookSource.cs ---
PixelLookSource.cs / PixelNoise.cs IPixelLookSource = anything with looks to step through (Usable, Current, StyleName, PreviewTexture/Color/Rect, Step); the pause menu's AddLookRow and the title screen's BuildLookPicker only use this (both through PixelUIKit.CreateLookDropdown). PixelNoise = shared tileable noise (Hash, TileNoise, Fbm incl. separate u/v periods, Voronoi) used by floor textures.
--- PixelLookStudio.cs ---
PixelLookStudio.cs The LOOK STUDIO (Dev Hub tab 'Looks'): change any pixel type's look while the game runs, then save it so Claude can make it the official look. * Pick a pixel type at the top. The preview (PixelTierPicture; drag it to turn it) and the cube update a moment after every change; 'Show on cube' makes only that type spawn (PixelClicker.SetDevSpawnTiers) so you can click it and watch its husks. Husks dropped after a change carry the new look (ones already on the floor keep the old one). * The settings list is built BY REFLECTION from PixelLook's public fields: bool = tick box, number = slider + exact number box (limits from its [Range] / [Min], SoftMax for open-ended ones), colour = swatch that unfolds Hue / Saturation / Brightness / Opacity sliders + hex box + eyedropper, enum = drop-down; the field's [Tooltip] is the hover tip. So a NEW PixelLook field shows up here by itself: put its name in a section of Sections (otherwise it lands in 'More'). The 'Block' section edits the tier's own colour / see-through / glow (TierFields; the tier colour is also the UI colour). * Undo (JSON snapshots per type), Reset (back to how the type looked when the studio first opened it this session), 'Copy look from...' (another type's look, or blank). * Save writes <persistentDataPath>/LookStudio/<Type>.json (Load saved brings it back; 'Use my saved looks' re-applies every saved file when the game starts) and <Type>.cs.txt = the look as a C# 'new PixelLook { ... }' line (only the fields that differ from a blank look) + the Block values, also copied to the clipboard. Paste that to Claude (or name the file) and it goes into PixelLooks.CreateDefaults + a looksVersion step = the official look for everyone. * Edits are run-time only: leaving Play mode in the Editor drops them unless saved (the PixelClicker component's list is not written back). Phase 1 = the settings looks already have. Own shapes, own textures and own particle effects are later phases (Todo.txt).
--- PixelLooks.cs ---
PixelLooks.cs PixelLook (one pixel type's extra 3D style) and PixelLooks (the helpers that build a look's runtime-drawn parts: neon edges, the dark-matter core, glow shells / halos, shatter shards), plus the small husk / look behaviours (gravity well, float, wobble, shine, spin, shatter, lightning). Part files: PixelLooksLook.cs (the PixelLook data class), PixelLooksDefaults.cs (the built-in looks table), PixelLooksTextures.cs (drawn textures), PixelLooksMeshes.cs (the code-built meshes). The shared static caches stay in this file.
