--- PixelLumberjackVendor.cs ---
PixelLumberjackVendor.cs The Lumberjack (id lumberjack, 'Big Tim the Lumberjack'; a cube like Cubie in a red plaid shirt with a big beard, a green beanie and an axe): BUY / SELL TABS (PixelVisitorMinigame.BuySellTabs). BUY tab = his wares: the WOOD CHIPPER (a placed device, PixelConsumables DeviceKind.WoodChipper, costs wood and bark), plain POTIONS of the Lumberjack pack's pixels (Wood, Bark, Sap, Leaf, Heartwood, Twig, Acorn, Whetstone, Pesticide) and NOW AND THEN (focusChance 35% a visit) the LUMBERJACK'S FOCUS item (DeviceKind.FocusPotion, 1-2 in stock), and every visit the PESTICIDE SPRAY (DeviceKind.PesticideSpray: right-click = +3 Pesticide charges; 1-3 in stock, costs 3-8 Pesticide + 50-150 of one random pure pixel). PRICES (RollVisitPrice, once per visit): a potion costs some of its own material (potionMaterialRange 10-30) + ONE random pure pixel (potionPureFraction 30-60% of its normal price); Focus costs focusHiveCost (3) Termite Hives + TWO different random pure pixels (focusPureRange 100-300 each) + some of one random woodcutting pixel you have unlocked (focusWoodRange 10-30). Each potion comes 0-2 times (potionStockLow / High) - 0 = not on sale this visit. SELL tab (2026-10-16, its own panel - PixelVisitorMinigame.CustomSellPanel): pick ANY woodcutting currency you hold (drop-down) - or (2026-10-20) CHARCOAL / WOOD CHIPS HUSKS lying loose on the floor (the Kiln's and the Wood Chipper's products; HuskWorthInWood: charcoalWorthInWood 3 / woodChipsWorthInWood 4 Wood each; on Accept he takes that many off the floor, TakeHusks) - the AMOUNT (slider + 10% / 25% / 50% / All), what to be paid in (a pure pixel you have unlocked), then 'Ask for an offer' - he names a RANDOM price; Accept or Decline. OFFER FORMULA (Offer): fair = PixelMarket.Quote (the SHARED vendor rule: the clicks the wood took x what a click on pure blocks earns x 1.2, x his market saturation for that wood, averaged over the amount - selling a lot lowers his price, it recovers over time; shown on the Sell tab) x mood (rolled once per visit, moodRange 0.85-1.15) x roll = the average of two randoms in offerRollRange 0.7-1.25 (usually near the middle, rarely an extreme) (the roll is made ONCE per wood / pay pair per visit - asking again or changing the amount never rerolls it; a new visit rolls again) x (1 + Woodcutting level x levelBonusPerLevel 1%, max 30%), rounded down, at least 1. Only visits while Woodcutting is the skill in training (CanVisit); started by PixelGuideVendor.StartVendors as soon as Woodcutting is available; counters lumberjack.bought / lumberjack.sold.
--- PixelMagnetDevice.cs ---
PixelMagnetDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Magnet (DeviceKind.Magnet 10, auto-added like the fan by EnsureDefaultPotions, Utilities card; needs Electric unlocked, 20 Electric pixels, durationSeconds 30, radius 5, pullAcceleration 24, absorbDistance 0.9 = the 'settle' distance): like the Vacuum Device it pulls loose old pixels in radius towards a point in the gap of its horseshoe (model: BuildModel in this file: red arch + legs, silver tips, base plate), but never collects them: pixels inside the settle distance just get their speed damped, so they sit in a heap (held / flying / vanishing / kinematic pixels are skipped, ageing is unchanged); placed like the vacuum (starts disarmed, click to switch on), robot-placeable (IsPlaceableKind), tip device_Magnet, stat device.placed.Magnet, not part of the Operation tab yet.
--- PixelMarket.cs ---
PixelMarket.cs ONE price rule for every vendor that BUYS goods for pure currency (the value ladder, GameDesign section 6 'Cubie's bill'): - "1x" = the pure currency one click earns on pure blocks right now (PurePerClick: the pure types' spawn shares x their pay per click x Value / Ultra / wish; the six pure colours count as equal units). - A good is worth the clicks it took: ClicksPerUnit(tier) = clicks to collect / (pay per click x its multipliers). So selling the goods of a few minutes of skill clicking pays rawGoodsRate (1.2) x what the same clicks on pure blocks would have paid. Routes (Route): RawGoods 1.2x (built), Products 2x and Orders 3x (rates ready for when those exist). - SATURATION instead of caps: each vendor + good has a 'pressure' (the clicks-worth sold to them). The price factor is 1 / (1 + pressure / saturationClicks) (selling saturationClicks of work halves it), never below minFactor; pressure halves every recoveryHalfLifeMinutes (real time, so a paused / time-stopped game still recovers). Quote averages the factor over the whole amount sold (a big dump drives its own price down as it goes). RecordSale adds the pressure when a deal is accepted. - Not saved: the market recovers anyway, a reload just starts it fresh. Used by the Lumberjack's Sell tab and the Prospector's offers; the Entrepreneur's pixel-for-pixel deals use RarityValue. Husks are never currency: a vendor buys skill CURRENCY (or a husk good he names, valued as currency); the Recycler stays the only husk-to-currency route.
--- PixelMaterials.cs ---
PixelMaterials.cs MATERIALS: things the player holds that are not pixel currency and not husks - made by machines (the Kiln's Charcoal, 2026-10-24), shown in the Inventory's Materials tab (with the minigame materials such as Bomb Parts) and spendable in shop prices. - The kinds of material are the Inspector list 'materials' (id, name, colour); the amounts held are progress: GameState.materials (MaterialsState, by id), so they save / load / reset with the game. - Add(id, n) / TrySpend(id, n) / Amount(id); Changed fires on every change. - ONE place for 'pay with something that is not a pixel': a price line (PixelShop.PackCost) whose 'minigameCurrency' names a material id or a minigame id (bomb = bomb parts) goes through CostHeld / CostSpend / CostName / CostColor here (the shop and the visitors use them).
--- PixelMenuFx.cs ---
PixelMenuFx.cs The FANCY PAUSE MENU (Settings > Interface > 'Fancy pause menu', PixelMenuFx.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.FancyMenu, default ON, saved per profile). It is a layer on top of the classic menu: nothing of the classic menu's code or layout was changed, so switching the setting off (live, no restart) goes back to exactly the old menu - that is the backup. What it adds (all components check Enabled every frame and hide / reset themselves when it is off): * PixelFancyButton on every main-menu button (PixelPauseMenu.LayoutMainButtons -> PixelMenuFx.Decorate): hover grows + brightens the button and slides its label, a coloured accent stripe on the left widens, a light shine sweeps across (on hover and every few seconds, offset per button), press squishes it, a small pixel icon (Floppy / Cog / Door) sits on the left, and every time the menu opens the buttons slide in one after the other (entranceStagger). * PixelMenuBackdrop (PixelPauseMenu.BuildUI -> BuildBackdrop): soft pixel motes drifting up behind the panel + a darkening vignette over the dimmer. * PixelMenuPanelGlow: a rounded glow behind the main panel that slowly cycles colour and breathes; PixelMenuTitleLine: a light segment sliding along a line under the title. * The SETTINGS and STATS screens get the lighter button animation (PixelMenuFx.DecorateAll: brighten, shine, press; no scaling inside scroll lists). * The docked HUD buttons also get the light animation WITH the idle shine (DecorateHud), and the SHOP gets the same treatment (its buttons, a glow behind the panel and the sliding title line; PixelShop.BuildPanel / CreateButton). * PixelQuitFx: the Quit to... box darkens the screen and pours flickering red neon light round the window (PixelPauseMenu.BuildQuitPanel). * PixelMenuCubeFall (PixelPauseMenu.BuildTitlePlate -> AddCubeFall): every so often a small cube in an unlocked pixel's colour slips out from behind a menu window's title plate and falls (gravity, a little spin) with a glowing light trail, behind the buttons, fading out before the window's bottom (pooled, clipped to the window). Everything runs on unscaled time (the game is paused while the menu shows).
--- PixelMenuKeys.cs ---
PixelMenuKeys.cs Blank-by-default keyboard shortcuts for the docked menu buttons: PixelAction.Inventory/Shop/LogWindow/Crafting/Toggles/Bank/Skills (default KeyCode.None, shown as '-', Backspace / Delete in the binding screen clears a key; PixelKeys.Pressed/Held return false for None and Set never swaps None). Each window calls PixelMenuKeys.Register(action, button) after making its dock button; the component clicks that button when its key is pressed (only if the button is active and the game isn't paused / on the title screen / typing). A new docked menu should register too.
--- PixelMeteorMinigame.cs ---
PixelMeteorMinigame.cs Meteor minigame (pack unlocksMinigame = "meteor", Minigames tab): a large glowing meteor (runtime-built: lumpy noise-displaced rock mesh lumpiness, lava-crack albedo/emission textures lavaCracks, a camera-facing glow sprite haloOpacity, a two-layer tail incl. a white-hot hotCoreTail, a particle-system ember stream embersPerSecond, flickering light) drifts slowly across the screen; each click gives chunksPerClick meteor chunks but never destroys it. Tracker "Meteor Chunks" (saved generically); the Meteor Pixel pack has a MinigameGoal requirement for meteor. Extends PixelMinigame (id meteor). It speeds up gradually across the screen (disableSpeedUp / speedUpAmount, same total time), a rumble loop (meteor_rumble) plays while it crosses, every click plays meteor_hit and throws mining effects (sparks, rock chips, dust: BuildImpactFx / EmitImpact, disableImpactFx, impactChips / impactSparks) and the glow flashes; a chunk plays meteor_click. The sounds are code-made (PixelMeteorSynth) and replaceable in the PixelAudio Sounds list.
--- PixelMeteorSynth.cs ---
PixelMeteorSynth.cs Code-made sounds of the Meteor minigame (PixelAudio.SynthFor -> PixelMeteorSynth.For; put your own clips in the Sounds list to replace them): meteor_spawn (a fiery whoosh with a deep boom as it enters), meteor_rumble (a seamless low rumble loop while it crosses, PixelAudio.StartLoop / StopLoop), meteor_hit (a dull pick-on-rock thud with gravel, every click) and meteor_click (a bright pick clink with chips, when the click gives a chunk).
--- PixelMinigame.cs ---
PixelMinigame.cs NeedsNormalCamera (default = Takeover): true while the minigame needs the camera at its normal pose (the orbit camera glides home, the zoom snaps back); Weak Spots overrides it to false. Abstract base for minigames (registry PixelMinigame.All/Find(id), Id, Running, Activate/Deactivate, optional goal tracker: HasTracker, TrackerTitle/Description/Count/Goal, RequirementFormat, GoalReached). Skill XP bonus: every Report(MinigameEvent.Clicked) (each success: ghost caught, meteor click, star collected, bomb defused, Ultra Hole drop, Snake / Sorting / Breakout win, Dash round) calls GrantSkillXp(), which gives bonus XP to the skill in training (none while Standard is trained) via PixelSkills.AddXp (so the XP popup shows too); defaults in DefaultSkillXp by id (meteor 1, black hole 1 per swallowed pixel, pad 6, stardust 8, ghost 12, dash 15, bomb 20, snake / sort / breakout 25, others 5); per minigame skillXpReward (0 = default) and disableSkillXp in the Inspector. A minigame can name its own skill with SkillXpTarget (meteor = Mining, evil tree = Woodcutting): its XP then always goes there, whatever is in training (nothing if that skill is not bought). Bought minigames get an on/off row in the Toggles window (PixelMinigame.UserDisabled, progress per minigame in the GameState - MinigamesState, with the goal counters: SavedCount / SavedExtra, 2026-10-24; the shop never auto-starts a disabled one). Shop packs refer to minigames by id (unlocksMinigame; goals via a PackRequirement of kind MinigameGoal); the shop shows tracker rows and the save system stores counts generically - a new minigame needs no changes to PixelShop or PixelSaveGame. For the dev tools' Despawn button a minigame must call Track(go) on everything it puts in the scene (roots, messages, effects) and override OnDespawned() to reset its busy flag / spawn timer (DespawnNow() stops its coroutines and destroys the tracked objects). ROUND SCHEDULE: a minigame that comes back on a timer derives from PixelScheduledMinigame (startRunning / running / firstRoundDelay / minInterval..maxInterval, Running / Activate / Deactivate, StartSchedule, ScheduleNext) instead of writing them again. SPAWN STEP: a subclass's Update counts its spawn timer with SpawnDue(ref spawnTimer, blocked) - due + PixelMinigameLimits.AllowAnother + its own 'blocked' reason, else the limits' retry time (2026-10-24: was a copied block in 13 minigames). The Visitor rolls its own way. RESULT TEXT: ResultShownLong(ref resultTimer, roundRunning, uiRoot) says when a round's result text should go (Blink, Dash, Evade, Traffic). CUBE ROUNDS (Blink, Dash, Evade, Traffic - rounds played on the clickable cube): shared steps HookCubeClicks / UnhookCubeClicks (Awake / OnDestroy), PrepareCubeRound (TryStart: camera, round pixel, forced spawn tier), ReleaseCubeRound + PayCubeRoundBonus (EndRound), DropRoundUi, RandomBetween (2026-10-24: were copied blocks). Moving the cube on screen: PixelCubeScreenSpot.Place. Takeover rounds' bystander husks (shrink away during the round): PixelBystanders. TakeoverActive, AutoClickerPaused and the floor area claims are cleared on every scene unload (Settings > Restart).
--- PixelMinigameLimits.cs ---
PixelMinigameLimits.cs Limits how many minigames have something on screen together (minAtOnce 2 / maxAtOnce 3, limitAtOnce, quietSecondsAfterPlay 30 (nothing spawns that soon after Play; PixelTitleScreen.PlayTime), retrySeconds; added by PixelClicker.Awake). Each minigame overrides PixelMinigame.Busy and asks PixelMinigameLimits.AllowAnother(this) before its timer spawns something (by default only ONE minigame / visitor may be busy at a time; the allowOverlap tick box brings back the min..max numbers, a number between them is rolled each time); SpawnNow (dev tools) ignores the limit. A new minigame must do the same. Minigames that take over the floor's old pixels (Snake, Sorting Race) override PixelMinigame.Takeover and set PixelMinigame.TakeoverActive; AllowAnother refuses every minigame (takeover or not, and the Stardust stars) while a takeover round is Busy, and Pixel Grabbing / cube clicks are off meanwhile. The Ultra Pad and Black Hole never spawn on top of each other: PixelMinigame.ClaimArea / AreaFree keep a list of the round floor areas in use, and each one's TryPickSpot rejects spots that overlap the other's. BREATHERS: when the last busy minigame / visit finishes, Update starts a breather (breatherMin 40 .. Max 90 s at random, x takeoverBreatherFactor 1.5 after a takeover round) during which AllowAnother says no, so minigames bought together no longer follow one another straight away; RetrySeconds is randomised so waiting minigames do not all poll on the same frame (disableBreathers switches it off).
--- PixelModelEditor.cs ---
PixelModelEditor.cs The MODEL EDITOR (Dev Hub tab 'Models'): load any of the game's code-built 3D models, change it part by part, and SAVE a change report that Claude turns into code (the models are built in code, so the edit is a recipe for the builder, not a new asset). * Pick a model at the top: every device (PixelConsumables.CreateDisplayModel), every pixel block (PixelClicker.CreateDisplayPixel), plus anything another system adds with PixelModelEditor.Register(name, build). It is built in a hidden studio (PixelModelPicture) and shown in the viewport: DRAG to turn it, mouse WHEEL to zoom. * The PARTS list (every piece of the model, indented by depth): click one to select it - it blinks in the viewport for a moment. SLIDERS edit it: Position (X / Y / Z, about the model's size either way from where it was when picked), Rotation (-180..180), Scale (0..3x) and Colour (hue / saturation / brightness); each shows its value beside it. A SEARCH box narrows the model drop-down. Buttons: Hide / Show, Delete (Restore brings it back), Duplicate, Add Cube / Sphere / Cylinder (a new piece inside the selected one), Reset (rebuilds the model as the code makes it). * SAVE writes the report - only what changed: CHANGED (old -> new values), HIDDEN, DELETED, ADDED (type, parent, values) - to <project>/ModelEdits/<model>.txt in the editor (<persistentDataPath>/ModelEdits in a build) and copies it to the clipboard: paste it to Claude (or name the file) and the change goes into the model's builder code. Edits are never applied to the game by themselves. Parts are named by their path from the model's root ('Body/Wall'); a name used twice under one parent gets '#2', '#3'...
--- PixelModelPicture.cs ---
PixelModelPicture.cs Shared 'real model' pictures of ANY built model (the general form of PixelTierPicture, which only does pixel types): Get(key, build, resolution) builds the model once with 'build' in a hidden studio far below the scene (its own camera + light, the camera FITTED to the model's bounds so anything from a potion to a Recycler fills the picture) and returns its RenderTexture; Render(key, yaw) turns it and redraws it (at most PixelTierPicture.MaxFps a second - call it only for pictures on screen); Release(key) / ReleaseAll(prefix) destroy studios that are no longer needed (a closed window). Fitting ignores text labels and disabled renderers (a build can switch off range markings); 'fixedHalfHeight' frames by a fixed size instead (unit cubes: glow halos must not shrink them). Users: the 3D consumables window (PixelConsumableGallery), the Model Editor, the shop's row / card pictures (PixelShopPictures, keys 'shop:...') and the shop's Operation rows (PixelShopOperation, 'shopop:...').
--- PixelMold.cs ---
PixelMold.cs Foraging's Mold block (PixelType.Mold 75, a hazard-style block that replaced the Toadstool in the Foraging pack; the Toadstool still exists but is switched off for now) and the glowing Foraging finds. Component added by PixelClicker.Awake (PixelMold.Instance, Inspector fields for every number). MOLD (Foraging hazard, 1 left click sets it off, 1 right click clears it): with wholeMap (on) setting it off molds EVERY husk on the field at once; zoneHoldSeconds 0 = 30 and killSeconds 0 = 22 (longer than before). Old notes: every left click on the Mold block on the cube calls PixelMold.Spread (PixelClicker.CollectInternal / TriggerHazard) = the mold zone around the cube grows by spreadWidths old-pixel widths (a faint green disc on the floor, up to maxWidths). Right click (DefuseHazard) / the block leaving only STOPS the spreading - the zone stays and then fades by itself after zoneHoldSeconds + zoneFadeSeconds. Every infectInterval s the zone infects the old pixels lying inside it (PixelMoldy: a green shell that darkens; the pixel dies after killSeconds - the timer waits while it is held). SHAKE a held moldy pixel (PixelMoldy.FixedUpdate adds up the change of velocity of the dragged body) to shake the mold off: shakeNeeded cleans it and gives cleanImmuneSeconds (OldPixelInfo.moldImmuneUntil) so it is not re-infected at once, and gives cleanXp (3) Foraging XP (GiveCleanXp). The shell is a pale blue-green fuzz that turns whiter (moldRipeColor) as the pixel nears death - never dark, so moldy husks don't look like Truffles. Not saved: a loaded game has no zone / mold. GLOW: PixelMold.TryAttachGlow (called from PixelClicker.SpawnFallingCopy) gives a fresh Berry / Mushroom / Herb / Truffle old pixel a glowGlowChance chance to be a glowing variant; it only starts to glow (PixelForageGlow, shell fades in and pulses) once OldPixelDespawn.AgeFraction reaches glowStartFraction (0.5 = halfway through its lifespan). The glow is a look only for now (no extra pay). Also not saved.
--- PixelMyWindow.cs ---
PixelMyWindow.cs (the window is called POCKET now; the class and the saved keys keep the old name) POCKET'S PINNED TAB IS A BOARD OF WIDGETS (the shared PixelCardBoard, like the Dev Hub's): each pinned thing is a card (BuildCard) with a header you DRAG by (the cards slide round each other, the order is saved in the entries), a '-' that folds it up to its header (Entry.min, saved), and a small x you HOLD with the left button (0.6 s, a ring round the cursor, the card turns red: UpdateRemoveHold) to remove it. Under the last card a BLANK card with a + opens a search (OpenPicker / FillPicker: every currency, skill, goal, tool, item, achievement and a note, filtered as you type); picking one pins it and a new blank card follows. With no card left only the blank one shows. There is no Edit mode any more. (Old text below that talks about Edit mode / rows describes the earlier list.) My Window (the player's own window; component added by PixelClicker.Awake): a docked 'My Window' button on the right edge under the Pixel Guide (shows once two pixel types are unlocked, showFromStart) opens ONE free window (canvas 107, not registered with PixelWindows, not closed by other windows) that is dragged by its header, resized by the corner handle (position / size / open state are global PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.MyWindow.*) and holds a vertical LIST of rows chosen in Edit mode (an add bar: type + target dropdowns + Add; each row gets ^ v x buttons, currencies a '/min' toggle). Row types (Entry.type): currency (one pixel type, optional per-minute rate sampled over 60 s), skill (a chosen skill, 'training' = follows the skill in training, 'all' = one row per available skill), goal ('next', 'all' = every row of the Log's Goals tab via PixelLog.CurrentGoals(), or mg:<id> a minigame tracker), achievement (PixelAchievements id), note (an input field) and fav (a button: item:<name> uses a consumable through PixelConsumables.TryUseItem, tool:<id> = the Pixel hose (PixelBank.ToggleHose) or opens a window via PixelMenuKeys.Click). The rows are saved per save file (JSON in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.MyWindow, listed in PixelSettingsSync.Known_, reloaded by PixelMyWindow.ReloadFromPrefs). Values refresh every refreshSeconds (0 = 0.4); rows are rebuilt when what an 'all' row expands to changes. Layout (current): opens as a tall window (defaultSize 0 = 450 x 660) at the right side just above the bottom black bar (layout version 2 ignores older saved geometry). WINDOW: a standard Tool window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, prefKey PixelClicker.MyWindow): centred title, grey X, its PixelWindowChrome drags it by the header (kept between the black bars), minimizes it ('-') and docks it to a screen edge, saving X / Y / Min / Dock / DockY; this script only saves W / H / Open / Layout. MINIMIZED: only the header remains (still draggable); the full height is remembered. EVENT LOG TAB (always there, Pinned | Event log | Keybinds; the old header LOG dock button is gone): the log lines of PixelHints.TryGetHistory, newest at the bottom, a click shows the full tip; a switch at its top turns the IN-GAME (bottom-left) event log on / off (= PixelHints.EventLogEnabled, Settings > Event log). While the tab is showing the overlay stays away (LogTabShowing); with the in-game log off, the event log key opens the tab. INFO button in the header (PixelMyWindow.HoverDocked, kept on this machine): pins the block HOVER TOOLTIP into the window (MINIMIZED window: a small card with the block and its name hangs just under the header instead - TryGetHoverSlot's 'compact'): while it is on and the window is open (Pinned tab) the tooltip card no longer follows the mouse - PixelBlockTooltip moves its card into the window's hover slot (TryGetHoverSlot / SetHoverHeight), scaled to the window's width, and it shows whatever the mouse hovers (and stays on the last one while the mouse is over the window); a hint shows until something was hovered. TABS (always shown under the header): Pinned | Event log (only while the log is docked) | Keybinds (PixelCustomKeys.BuildView: bind items / tools to your own keys). Header buttons (LayoutHeaderButtons): Info sits in the first right slot after the X and the chrome's '-' (one slot further in while the chrome's undock arrow shows), Edit (hidden, no Edit mode) after it; the title stays centred clear of them. Layout reruns when the chrome minimizes / docks (its StateChanged event). The corner grip still changes the window's WIDTH / HEIGHT (ResizeBy -> PixelWindowParts.SetHeight), not its scale. Part files (partial class): PixelMyWindowCards.cs (the Pinned board of cards + the picker), PixelMyWindowRows.cs (the row builders per type).
--- PixelMyWindowCards.cs ---
PixelMyWindowCards.cs Part of PixelMyWindow (partial class): the Pinned tab as a board of cards (RebuildRows, BuildCard, the blank + card, hold-to-remove) and the picker that searches for what to pin. The system notes are in PixelMyWindow.cs.
--- PixelMyWindowRows.cs ---
PixelMyWindowRows.cs Part of PixelMyWindow (partial class): the row builders of the pinned cards (currency with its per-minute rate, skill, goal, achievement, note, favorite) and their small UI helpers. The system notes are in PixelMyWindow.cs.
--- PixelNextUp.cs ---
PixelNextUp.cs The window is a standard Tool window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, no X): drag it by the header, minimize it to the header ('-'), dock it against the left / right screen side (slides out on hover); remembered in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.NextUp.Window.*. UNLOCK (2026-10-16): the window is the Standard skill tree node 'What's Next' (nextup, needs BOTH Block Hold and Power Click; requireTreeNode off = from the start). Its extensions 'Look Further' (nextup2) and 'Foresight' (nextup3) each add one more block AHEAD (up to three: LATER / THEN / NEXT above NOW and BEFORE); the window rebuilds taller when that count changes. The blocks ahead come from PixelClicker.PeekTierAhead (a queue of pre-rolled blocks that RollTier hands out in order). The 'Next up' window (component added by PixelClicker.Awake; shown while Settings > Interface > 'Next-up window' is on, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.NextUp, default on): a slim vertical window at the left edge with THREE spinning cubes - the block coming NEXT on top, the block on the cube NOW in the middle (bigger) and the block BEFORE it at the bottom (dim). When the block changes the cubes slide down one slot (the new next slides in from above, the old 'before' slides out below); any other change (a Block Hold swap) pops the changed cubes in and out. Data: PixelClicker.PeekTierAhead(k) (blocks put back by Block Hold that waits in line, else the pre-rolled 'upcoming' tier: PixelClicker.RollTier hands out that same tier, so the preview is true; it is re-rolled if a potion / skill / switch makes it invalid; nothing is pre-rolled while this window is hidden, so normal play rolls exactly as before), CurrentClickTierIndex and PreviousTierIndex. The cubes are the blocks' REAL models (PixelTierPicture: a hidden studio camera per block rendering into a RenderTexture, shown by a RawImage), not flat coloured cubes.
--- PixelNuclearPixel.cs ---
PixelNuclearPixel.cs Nuclear pixel (PixelType.Nuclear 33, IsStandalone (no seeds, no 3-type combo potions), spawn switch in Toggles; testing pack 'Nuclear Pixel': 10 White, no requirements, spawn weight 0.3, pays 8; looksVersion 27): glowing toxic-green look with PixelLook.radiation (PixelRadiationFx.Attach: drifting green motes). Its OLD pixel gets OldPixelRadiation (added in SpawnFallingCopy; nuclearRadiusPixels 0 = 4 main-pixel widths, nuclearAgeRate 0 = 40 age-seconds per real second, disableNuclearFx): every 0.15 s it calls OldPixelDespawn.Hasten(seconds) on every other old pixel in range (not other Nuclear pixels; held pixels, black holes, takeover minigames and the dev no-despawn switch still protect; gravity-well keep-alive does NOT), so they vanish (no payout) within a second or two; a pulsing green ring (PixelFxRing) and light show the reach.
--- PixelObsidianFx.cs ---
PixelObsidianFx.cs Obsidian flair: natural black glass (dark neutral bevel rim, no emission / violet; looksVersion 31) with PixelLook.obsidianFx = a few pale glints (Attach, from AddExtras), Hit (shards + glints, stronger with progress) and Break (glass spray + glints) called from CollectInternal (disableFarmFx), plus FractureFx (jagged white crack lines racing outward in the camera plane, flickering and fading; more / longer with progress; replaces the old rings) and shine. Obsidian's hit / break no longer use the white FractureFx lines: they throw black / grey dust that poofs sideways (PixelObsidianFx.Dust: a flat ring of swelling, fading puffs plus a few drifting up) along with the glass shards. The Obsidian sheen (PixelLook.shine) follows the jagged mesh (ShineMesh(size, jagged, seed) displaces its grid with PixelRockMesh.Displace) so it never sticks out past the chipped edges. Obsidian clicks play PixelSynth.ObsidianPing (while pixel_obsidian has no clips) with a pitch that rises per hit (PixelAudio.OnHit 0.6 -> 1.45, break 1.6). Hit / Break bursts are pooled (PixelFx.Rent); FractureFx flashes are pooled too (switched off and reused, MaxPooled 12).
--- PixelOfflineProgress.cs ---
PixelOfflineProgress.cs Offline progress: PixelSaveGame stores savedAtTicks (UTC) on every save and, on the startup load only, calls Grant(lastSaved). Rare drops (Dragon Cubes) are not averaged but rolled with a Poisson count of pixels x weight / total weight (RollPoisson). If the auto clicker is running it pays the average earnings for the (capped, scaled) time away - clicks = time x efficiency / interval x clicks-per-tick, split across unlocked tiers by spawn weight, tough pixels needing several clicks - and shows a "Welcome back" window (held back until the title screen is gone, Update + pendingGains; any later load - another slot - Dismiss()es it). Settings: max hours, efficiency, minimum away time. OFFLINE PROGRESSION XP (2026-10-16): the skill is passive - its ONLY XP is offlineXpPerClick (0.25) x the offline clicks worked out here (PixelSkills.AddOfflineXp), shown as a line in the Welcome back window; clicking while playing gives it nothing. OFFLINE TASK: the skill pack picked on the Offline card (PixelSkills.OfflineTask, per save; Standard if none / not owned) is what the character does while away - it also gets offlineTaskXpPerClick (0.1) x the offline clicks (PixelSkills.AddOfflineTaskXp), its own Welcome back line. The Welcome back window is the standard window as a dialog (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, no X; Continue closes it).
--- PixelOperation.cs ---
PixelOperation.cs / PixelShopOperation.cs Upgrades > Operation (the third sub-tab of the shop's Upgrades tab, operationSubTabText; upgradesSubTab 2): one row per consumable (Vacuum Device, Fan, Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, Combo Fuel, Ghost Bait, Pet Treat, Recycler - listed once the pixel type it needs is unlocked or you hold one, PixelConsumables.OperationListed) with a rotating 3D picture on the left (a PixelModelPicture studio per consumable, key 'shopop:' + index, redrawn only for visible rows at the shared 30/s limit, UpdateOperationPictures; the model comes from PixelConsumables.CreateDisplayModel = the real device builders with displayOnly (no timer / range) or simple item models), an orange '!' box with the consumable's description and an orange upgrade arrow that opens the shared upgrades window (OpenOperationWindow, openOperation) listing that consumable's upgrades as plain rows (name + level, cost coloured by affordability, Upgrade button) whose orange '!' tooltip shows the statistics (OperationStatText: now / next level / at max, from PixelConsumables.OperationBase). Upgrades are PixelOperationUpgrade entries (PixelOperation.cs: id, name, description, device, stat (PixelOperationStat), maxLevel, perLevel, baseCost x costGrowth^level paid in the pixel the consumable itself needs, unit) in PixelShop.operationUpgrades (the built-ins are added by id, EnsureDefaultOperationUpgrades); the level is the stat counter op.<id> (PixelStats.Best, saved generically). Effects: PixelConsumables.EffectiveDevice(d) (a clone with Duration / Radius / Pull / Blow / Uses / Charge needed / Super charge / Strike interval applied, used for placing, previewing and descriptions), Storage in ItemCapacity, Pet Treat length (PixelPets.TryUseTreat) and extra Ghost Bait ghosts (PixelGhostMinigame.UseBait).
--- PixelOrbitCamera.cs ---
PixelOrbitCamera.cs BASE VIEW: at start (Start -> ApplyBaseView, useBaseView) the scene camera is moved to baseDistance 0 = 6 units from the cube at baseTilt 0 = 89 degrees, baseTurn 180 (the game's original orientation, +Z at the top of the screen): that pose is the game's normal view and the 1.0 of the zoom slider (Enter takes it as the home pose, so the camera intro settles into it, PixelViewBounds reads it through HomeCamera and takeover minigames glide back to it). The View window keeps the camera in its start view (Orbital by default; Top down = this camera, pitch locked) whenever nothing else uses it. Orbital camera (added by PixelClicker.Awake; chosen in the View window (PixelViewWindow, key PixelAction.ViewWindow) as 'Orbital', or 'Top down' = Enter(topDown: true): the same camera with the pitch locked at 89 degrees; disableOrbit): the REAL camera swings round the cube (Enter captures the home pose; yaw / pitch / distance round CubeBasePosition, rotation = look-at-cube x aimOffset so the cube keeps its on-screen spot): middle-mouse drag (PixelInput.MiddleHeld, dragDegreesPerPixel 0 = 0.25) or arrow keys (keyDegreesPerSecond 0 = 90) turn / tilt (pitch min minPitch 0 = 6 so it never looks from under the floor), Ctrl + wheel and the View window's zoom slider (DistanceFraction, MinFraction 0.35 .. MaxFraction 2) zoom, the window's Auto orbit tick box (AutoOrbit, AutoSpeed = autoOrbitDegreesPerSecond 0 = 10) turns the camera slowly round the cube by itself, and its hold buttons (HoldTurn / HoldTilt) add to the arrow keys; dragging / arrow keys still work, followSharpness smoothing. Its controls are shown as a hover tooltip in the View window (no hint line on screen). Clicks need no change (raycasts go through TargetCamera). While PixelOrbitCamera.Active, PixelOrbitCamera.HomeCamera (a hidden disabled copy at the home pose) is what PixelViewBounds and PixelFloor.FitToView read, so old pixels stay inside the NORMAL view and fitted floors don't re-fit. Leaving glides back over returnSeconds (0 = 0.7); the title screen, the camera intro and takeover / zooming minigames (any Busy + Takeover one) end it or block it. FIELD OF VIEW (Settings > Display > 'Orbit field of view', FieldOfView, PlayerPrefs OrbitFov per save, 30-100, 0 = the normal view's own): eased in on the real camera while orbiting / top down and eased back on leaving; HomeCamera keeps the normal one, so the play area does not change with it.
--- PixelPathCarrier.cs ---
PixelPathCarrier.cs Shared helper for machines that CARRY old pixels along a path (users: the Funnel and the Conveyor Elevator; the belts have their own rider system). Add(rb) locks a loose old pixel (kinematic, no velocity); Step(...) each FixedUpdate draws new ones to the path's start (approachSeconds), then moves every pixel along the polyline at 'speed' keeping 'gap' behind the one ahead, keeps them from despawning, lets go of ones that were grabbed or destroyed, and calls onEnd(rb) when one reaches the end (optional canExit gate: while it returns false the pixel waits at the end and the ones behind queue up) (the caller then puts it back into the world with ReleaseAt). ReleaseAll() lets everything go (the machine was removed).

PixelPauseMenu.cs Pause menu (main menu = mainColumns-column button grid LayoutMainButtons, mainPanelWidth wide; fitBetweenBars keeps it and the text screens between the black bars via AvailableHeight/FitViewHeight) (Esc; the on-screen Pause button is off by default - showPauseButtonOnScreen): sets Time.timeScale = 0, Resume (alone) / Save-Load + Stats / Settings + Game Guide (Controls, How To Play, Database tabs) / Give feedback (alone) / Quit (alone; opens a 'Quit to...' box with Desktop and Main menu - Main menu saves, then PixelTitleScreen.ReturnToMenu()) (MenuEntry.fullRow) (Restart now lives on the Stats screen, Report Folder moved to the Dev Tools panel; the Stats screen shows one total "Potions used" row instead of the potions drop-down; a search box over the Stats list filters rows by name / value and shows a section when its heading or any row matches, StatRowsShown) (Settings: tick boxes for cube rotation, pulsing, "Abbreviate numbers" and "Hide purchased shop items" (static PixelShop.HidePurchased, PlayerPrefs; HiddenAsPurchased in the shop list keeps a bought pack listed while it still has upgrades to buy) -> PixelClicker.AllowRotation/AllowPulsing and static PixelClicker.AbbreviateNumbers (read by FormatNumber, PixelUI, PixelLog and the stats), stored in PlayerPrefs), PixelPauseMenu.IsPaused. --- PixelPermanentDevice.cs ---
PixelPermanentDevice.cs A thin PERMANENT PixelPlacedDevice (no countdown, needs no switching on) put on a machine that keeps its own behaviour in its own script, so the machine is bought, kept in the backpack, placed, saved / restored (position + turn), removed (hold the right button) and shown on the device card like every other device. Used by the Wood Chipper (PixelWoodChipper), the Funnel (PixelFunnelDevice), the Sweeper, the Junction Box, the Sorting Hopper, the Recycler and the Biomass Generators. (Called PixelChipperDevice until 2026-10-20: the Wood Chipper was its first user.)
--- PixelPestSwarm.cs ---
PixelPestSwarm.cs (Pest rework) The Pest block (PixelLook.blockSurface Pest, PixelBlockTextures.PestHide(level,max): black carapace plates, hazard stripes round every face, red eyes that multiply and glow with hits; jagged mesh + twitching wobble, bugs crawl over it via farmFx Bugs; looksVersion 43). Every non-final hit spills a few bugs (PixelPestSwarm.Scatter); when it GOES OFF PixelPestSwarm.Release sends a swarm of code-built bugs (body, head, red eyes, legs, feelers; max 80 at once) over the floor to every edible planted sprout (SeedSprout.GetEdible/IsEdible), they climb it and gnaw (Gnaw shakes it, more bugs = faster, pestChewSeconds 4), then SeedSprout.Devour() kills it (pixel lost) and the bugs run off; with no sprouts they just scatter. Tuning on PixelClicker: pestBugsPerSprout (0 = 6), pestBugsMin (8), pestBugSpeed (3.5), pestChewSeconds (4), disablePestSwarm (old instant KillAll). Dirt now takes 3 digs (packDataVersion 15): each hit plays seed_dig + SeedDigFx and shrinks the live cube towards old-pixel size (digShrinkTarget, reset in Materialize). Seedlings from a cracked Seed shell grow seedlingGrowSeconds (0 = 40 s; the sprout's despawn timer is extended to match). Migration 15 also adds any missing farm block (Seed, Water...) to a saved Farmer's Pack, and PixelSkills.EnsureDefaults makes sure Farming's types list has every farm block (a missing Seed was why it never spawned); migrations 13 and 14 now run in order.

PixelPetsDatabase.cs Part of PixelPets: its Game Database entries (IPixelDatabaseSource, see PixelDatabase.cs) - one 'Pet' entry per pixel type that can have a pet (find chance, spawn weight factor and husk slow-down read live; a line for the pets with a special trick); discovered = owned; linked to its pixel.
--- PixelPetsMenu.cs ---
PixelPetsMenu.cs Part of partial class PixelPets: the pet RIGHT-CLICK MENU. Right-click a pet on the field and a small menu opens at the pointer (the pet's name + 'Pet away'); 'Pet away' switches that pet off (SetOn false, the same as its switch in the Toggles window's Pets tab - turn it back on there) with a poof sound. A left / right click anywhere else, Escape, the pet vanishing or a menu opening closes it. While it is open, clicks don't reach the cube.
--- PixelPetsStudio.cs ---
PixelPetsStudio.cs Part of partial class PixelPets: the Outfit Studio (button 'Design outfits' in the Customize Pet window, own canvas customizeSortingOrder + 20, PixelWindows 170). The player builds custom outfits from basic shapes (CPiece: cube / sphere / cylinder / capsule / cone, position, size, turn, colour (16 StudioColors), opacity, optional mirror = a second copy on the other side) on a dummy: a plain grey cube or the real-look model of ANY pet they own (studioDummy; some pets look different), in a hidden studio (StudioStageHeight) shown by a RawImage (drag to turn, wheel to zoom; a yellow marker shows the selected shape's centre; CLICK a shape in the view to select it and DRAG it: the move locks to the X / Y / Z axis that points most along the drag on screen, a coloured line shows that axis, the position slider follows - StudioPointerDown / StudioPieceDrag, colliders on the studio copies only; a drag on empty space still turns the view). Outfits (COutfit: name, forType = one pet type or -1 any) are edited live (every change saved) with a piece list, nine sliders, swatches, duplicate / delete, New / Delete outfit, and 'Wear this outfit'. Outfit numbers 10000 + n are custom outfits (CustomBase; BuildOutfit handles them, so live pets, title-screen pets and previews all use them; deleting one renumbers / clears pets wearing it); the outfit arrows cycle OutfitChoices(type) = built-ins then the custom ones that fit that pet. Saved as JSON in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.PetOutfits (per save file via PixelSettingsSync, reloaded by PixelPets.ReloadOutfitsFromPrefs). The window is the standard window as a dialog (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, no X; Done closes it).
--- PixelPhysicsMaterials.cs ---
PixelPhysicsMaterials.cs ONE place that makes physics materials (bounce / friction of a collider). Unity 6 renamed the type (PhysicsMaterial / PhysicsMaterialCombine, older: PhysicMaterial / PhysicMaterialCombine); the UNITY_6000_0_OR_NEWER split lives only in this file, so callers never write it twice. - Apply(collider, name, bounce, friction, bounceCombine, frictionCombine): puts a material with exactly these values on the collider. Materials are CACHED by name + values, so every husk / pet with the same settings shares one (nothing is made per husk, nothing leaks); a changed Inspector value simply asks for (and caches) a new one. Never change a material you got from here: it is shared. - ApplyGrippy(collider, grip): Pixel Grabbing's thrown husk: a copy of the collider's own material with at least 'grip' friction, combined by Maximum (so its grip wins against slippery floors / husks). Restore(collider, original) puts the saved one back. - CopyOf(from, to): copies another collider's material (the floor reuses the scene ground's). Used by: husks (PixelClickerHusks: the shared husk material + per-look materials), Pixel Grabbing (thrown grip), pets (PixelPets), the floor (PixelFloor). The cache lives for the whole run (a few dozen tiny assets at most); materials are never destroyed (colliders on pooled husks keep using them).
--- PixelPixelGuide.cs ---
PixelPixelGuide.cs / PixelGuideTexts.cs Pixel Guide (added by PixelClicker.Awake): a docked 'Pixel Guide' button on the right edge directly under Toggles (PixelHud.Dock with a negative sideOffsetY; shows once two pixel types are unlocked, hideUntilSecondPixel) that slides out a window like Toggles (same spot, docks beside the open shop with the Currency panel's width and the shop's height via FitBesideShop; opening one closes the other: PixelToggles.CloseWindowIfOpen / PixelPixelGuide.CloseWindowIfOpen; Escape closes it, PixelWindows 15). It lists every pixel IN THE ROTATION (PixelClicker.InRotation(i) = can spawn now and not left out by the skill pack in play; found Dragon Cubes too; SpawnShare(i) = its share of spawns) as a row: a turning real-look 3D picture (the shared PixelTierPicture studio of that type, re-rendered only for rows in view), the name in its colour, a line 'Pays N - clicks - X% of pixels' and an orange '!' box whose tooltip is PixelGuideTexts.Describe(type, tier) (the exact mechanics + what its old pixel does). Special / hold blocks (PixelBlockRules) are tagged in the sub line and get their role sentences from PixelBlockRules.GuideNotes automatically. A new pixel type needs a case in PixelGuideTexts.Mechanics (and OldPixel if its old pixel behaves differently).
--- PixelPlacedDevice.cs ---
PixelPlacedDevice.cs INFO CARD / PANEL: BuildInfo(PixelDeviceInfo) fills the shared hover card / right-click panel (PixelDeviceCard): common lines here, subclasses override AddInfo, behaviour components next to a stub implement IPixelDeviceInfo (PixelDeviceInfo.cs). GENERATOR POWER (2026-10-19): HoldPower(fraction, colour) from a Biomass Generator pauses the countdown like the Electric links AND shows the generator's energy bar (PixelEnergyBar) over the device, tracking its charge (the time ring hides meanwhile). SetRemoveGlow(0..1): while the right mouse button is held on the device (PixelConsumables.UpdateRemoval + PixelHold ring) it tints red, glows and lights up red as the ring fills. Abstract base of placed devices: countdown (shown as a small RING, PixelWorldRing, once switched on - the timer text only says 'Click to start' before that and the uses-mode labels like the Charge Booster's CLICK!), shrink-away (InitCommon, OnTick, IsEnding). Armed / disarmed: every device you place starts DISARMED (Disarm() right after SpawnDevice; grey via per-renderer property blocks - ApplyTint / ClearTint, a desaturated dull version of each material's colour, emission off; the countdown does not run, OnTick is not called, the timer text shows DisarmedText 'Click to start'; subclasses must check Armed in FixedUpdate / event handlers: fan, vacuum, booster OnCollected / AddCharge, PixelElectricLinks ignores disarmed devices) until you LEFT-CLICK it once (PixelConsumables.UpdateArming: DeviceUnderPointer(true) using the virtual HitTest(ray, out distance); while the mouse is over a disarmed device PixelConsumables.HoveringDisarmed makes PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock true) -> Arm() fades the colour in over ArmFadeSeconds (armFadeSeconds 0 = 0.7) and starts the countdown (sound device_arm). Saved as PlacedState.disarmed.
--- PixelPointerClaim.cs ---
PixelPointerClaim.cs One shared "this click belongs to something in the world, not the cube" flag. A device / visitor that has the pointer over a part it reacts to calls PixelPointerClaim.Claim() every frame it has it (Quantum Organizer, Storage Crate, Recycler button, carry handles, Bait Bucket, the Egg, the Beanstalk's fruit, a Treasure Map spot); PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock reads Claimed (this frame or the last, so script order does not matter). Replaces the separate HandleHot / Hot flag each of them used to have (and the matching list in ExternalClickBlock): a new device only calls Claim(). PixelRecycler.ButtonHot and PixelCarryHandles.Hot stay as their own flags because Pixel Grabbing steps aside for exactly those. ClaimButton() = a BUTTON on a world object (the Sorting Hopper's eject button and filter flag): it claims the pointer AND makes Pixel Grabbing step aside (ButtonClaimed, read by PixelGrab's guard) - a new world button uses it instead of its own flag.
--- PixelPop.cs ---
PixelPop.cs Open / close animation for windows and tip boxes (component on the Inventory box, the Log panel, the Got-it box, the Shop panel, the Crafting window, the Toggles window and every pause-menu screen: PixelPauseMenu.SetView pops the screen in, resuming shrinks the open one): it grows from smallScale to full size on enable (growSeconds, small overshoot, unscaled time, scales around the rect's pivot) and PixelPop.Hide(go[, deactivate]) shrinks it (shrinkSeconds) before deactivating; PixelPop.Show(go) also cancels a shrink under way; PixelPop.IsOpen(go) = shown and not closing. Open / close such windows through Show / Hide / IsOpen, not SetActive / activeSelf. Fit to screen: a fixed-size window (not stretched in x / y) that is bigger than the canvas between the black bars is scaled down by PixelPop.ComputeFit (UI scale 130% makes the canvas only ~830 units tall, so the 880-tall shop would be cut off).
--- PixelPowerBank.cs ---
PixelPowerBank.cs POWER BANK (2026-10-20, Devices session; DeviceKind.PowerBank 34, a permanent placed device with the PixelPermanentDevice stub, like the generators): a big battery that STORES power and hands it out over a LARGE radius. - CHARGE IN: every Biomass Generator standing inside its radius feeds it (PixelBiomassGenerator.GiveCharge): up to chargeInPerSecond per generator, but only the generator's charge above keepInGenerator (a fraction of its max) - so a fed generator keeps running and never drains itself to death for the bank. A blue arc (a LineRenderer per feeding generator) runs from each feeding generator to the bank's top terminal while it flows. - POWER OUT: while it holds charge, every placed device within its radius (not generators, not other banks) is powered (PixelPlacedDevice.HoldPower(fraction, colour)): its countdown stops and the bank's ENERGY BAR shows over it. The charge drains drainPerDevice a second per device it powers (an idle bank keeps its charge). Empty = it just waits (it never shuts down or disappears). - Comes EMPTY. Radius / capacity come from its Device entry (radius, generatorMaxCharge). A faint ring on the floor shows the radius. Model: a plinth, three tall glass cells whose glowing cores fill with the charge, a steel top with terminals and a lit band. Built with PixelFactoryKit. Saved: PlacedState.extra = the charge. Device card: charge, devices powered, generators feeding.
--- PixelPowerClick.cs ---
PixelPowerClick.cs POWER CLICK (TEST - may be removed or parked). Unlocked by the Standard skill tree node 'power' (requireTreeNode); branches 'quick' (charge 40% faster) -> 'aftershock' (cooldown a third shorter). Hold the LEFT mouse button on the cube: the press is a normal click as always, and if you keep holding for holdDelay (0.25 s) a charge builds (chargeSeconds 1.2): a ring fills round the cube, the cube trembles harder and harder, and at full charge the ring turns gold and pulses. Let go at full charge and it fires: one click worth clicksWorth (25) clicks (PixelClicker.PowerClick - payout x25, tough blocks take 25 hits, the combo grows by 25), a white flash, a shockwave ring, a kick of the cube and a big 'POWER CLICK x25' popup with a boom. Letting go early just cancels (no cost). Then a cooldown (cooldownSeconds 45) - a thin grey ring round the cube while you hover it shows what's left. Normal fast clicking never charges (each press is shorter than holdDelay). Not on: rare drops, hazards, Fish (Weak Spots) and the Golden Snitch; never while a husk is grabbed, a block is held (right mouse), a takeover minigame runs, in first person, in a menu or while time is stopped. Switch: disablePowerClick. Stats: power.clicks. Standard perk 'Crush recharge' (level 7, 2026-10-18): every crushed husk takes a little off the running cooldown (ShortenCooldown, from PixelHuskCrush). RIGHT CLICK while charging (2026-10-18) cancels it WITHOUT the cooldown (nothing fires; a new charge can start at once). PixelRightClick gives that right press to the charge (Owner.Charge, read from PixelPowerClick.Charging), so Block Hold / hazard clearing ignore it. Component in PixelSystems (group Core); its overlay canvas sits under the windows.
--- PixelPrefs.cs ---
PixelPrefs.cs The one door to PlayerPrefs: every script calls PixelPrefs.GetInt / SetInt / GetFloat / SetFloat / GetString / SetString / GetBool / SetBool / HasKey / DeleteKey / Save instead of PlayerPrefs directly (same behaviour, nothing is saved to disk until Save, like before). Why: PlayerPrefs can't tell what TYPE a key holds, so reading a float key with GetInt quietly gives 0 - the per-save settings list once did exactly that and wiped the picked glow colour, the hues and the skill pack on every load. In the Editor / dev builds PixelPrefs remembers the type each key is written or declared with and logs a warning the first time a key is used as another type. Per-save settings: PixelSettingsSync copies the keys declared with PixelPrefs.DeclarePerSave(key, kind) into each save file (its own built-in list declares itself the same way); a new per-save setting declares its key once, from its class's ResetStatics or Awake, next to the code that reads it, with the right kind - instead of being added by hand to a list in another file.
--- PixelProspectorVendor.cs ---
PixelProspectorVendor.cs The Prospector (id prospector, 'Pip the Prospector'; a visitor drawn as a CUBE like Cubie - orange earth tones, hard hat with a lamp, pickaxe, grin - the test for turning vendors into cube characters): he BUYS mining goods for pure currency (RollTrades: up to offerCount 3 different ores you hold >= minHeld, each asks minSellFraction..maxSellFraction of your stock and pays a random unlocked pure pixel (White..Blue) at the SHARED vendor rule (PixelMarket.Quote: effort x 1.2, less as his market for that ore fills up) x luck; the base trade rows say 'Sell' via TradePayWord / TradeText). He only visits while Mining is the skill in training (VisitSkillId = MiningId, the shared vendor rule; was CanVisit = PixelSkills.TrainingId == MiningId; a failed check just waits for the next visit timer) and is started by PixelGuideVendor.StartVendors as soon as Mining is available (first visit after about otherFirstVisitSeconds x 0.5-1). Counter prospector.bought. Mining's whole 'sink' for now is selling to him; the Sell tab / skill upgrades are on the to-do.
--- PixelRecycler.cs ---
PixelRecycler.cs TIMED + UPGRADES: a placed Recycler lasts durationSeconds (300 s; PixelRecyclerDevice counts down; hovering the bin shows a small floating DOWN ARROW - the shared PixelHoverMarker, gone when the pointer leaves; the time left is on the shared device card, PixelDeviceCard), it shrinks away with whatever is inside when time is up). Upgrades > Operation (2026-10-18: one of each): SPEED (-0.08 s of RecycleDelay a level; 3x that off the button cycle's shake) and SIZE (+0.3 to the size factor a level - the whole bin, its catching area and its capacity grow; Place sizeFactor = the device's radius; a placed bin follows the level IN USE live, FollowSizeUpgrade - the device panel's - / + turns a bought level down). Size is +0.135 a level since 2026-10-18 (max 2.475, 25% smaller than with +0.3). RUN TIME (2026-10-20, back): +60 s a level, 5 levels, 300 s -> 10 minutes. (Streak Power and Suction rows were removed; their stats still work at their base values.) RECYCLER (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.Recycler 19, Utilities card - listed FIRST there - and Cubie's gift on visit 1 via the {recycler} page token): a timed open-topped bin (PixelPermanentDevice stub for removal / saving its spot; built here by Place / CreateGhost / CreateDisplay with PixelFactoryKit: plinth + feet, green walls with a dark liner, brushed-metal corner pillars and rim, a glowing band round the body and a status lamp - green idle, amber while working - recycling arrows on the front, the shared carry handles and a big red button). Drop old pixels (husks) into it - Pixel Grabbing, or anything that lands inside: a husk that comes to rest inside the bin's walls is CAPTURED (PixelClicker.ReleaseOldPixel without credit, so it leaves the floor's old-pixel list: no despawn count, no cap, no vacuum, no termites; its OldPixelDespawn is Held so it never shrinks away). The husks stay in the bin as real physical pixels until it is closed. Up to 'capacity' (10) can be inside; a small RING above the bin fills as they go in (PixelWorldRing; amber when full) and the device card says 'n / 25'. Husks inside never despawn (their OldPixelDespawn.Held is re-set every tick, so poking one with Pixel Grabbing cannot start its timer; one taken out of the bin is let go again; with the bin full, extra husks lying in it get KeepAlive). Click the red BUTTON on its front: a slatted LID extends out of the inner back edge and STRETCHES over the opening (Lid Pivot z scale 0.02 -> 1, lidSeconds), the bin SHAKES harder and harder for processSeconds (2 s, shakeStrength) while the band pulses amber, then every husk inside is recycled into payoutShare (50%) of what a click on that block pays NOW (CurrentValue: base payout x the current Value / Ultra / wish multiplier, so Value upgrades bought after the husk dropped count) as currency of its own type; the shake plays recycler_shake (PixelSynth.RecyclerShake: motor wind-up, faster rattles, clunk) (pixel rules: the Recycler is the one machine that turns husks back into money, at a loss), the husks are destroyed and the lid pulls back. Nothing is captured while it works, and the shut lid (it has colliders) keeps husks out: ones dropped on it fall in once it opens. ButtonHot (the pointer is over a button) is part of PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock and PixelGrab's guard, so pressing it neither clicks the cube nor grabs a pixel. Captured husks that lie still for 0.3 s are frozen in place (kinematic + parented to the bin: Settle / Freeze), so the pile cannot jitter or bounce and the husks scale with the bin when the floor's props fade (main menu, after a load: while the bin is scaled everything inside is frozen into it and nothing is captured or let go). Grabbing one takes it OUT (TakeOut: unfrozen, unparented, back on the old-pixel list, so it is a normal husk again and is captured anew if dropped back in). Husks above or in the opening get KeepAlive every check, and PixelGrab calls KeepIfOver on letting go, so an old husk's timer cannot run out in the moment before the bin captures it (that used to make a dropped husk vanish uncounted). Loaded husks are put straight into the bin, frozen (ImportState). SAVED: the husks inside go into PlacedState.extra ('Type,amount;', ExportState / ImportState via PixelConsumables) and are spawned back into the bin on load. HANDLES: the Storage Crate's grip handles on both short ends (shared PixelCarryHandles: BuildParts in Build, the component added by Place): drag one to carry the bin over the floor (scroll / Q / E turn it); everything inside is frozen into the bin while it is carried; not while processing. BACK HATCH: a small door in the back wall (the wall is built in four pieces round the hole, metal frame, slatted door with a handle). In first person, click it with the crosshair (within hatchReachHusks) and it slides up into the hole's top edge the same way the lid stretches (Hatch Pivot y scale 1 = shut, 0.03 = open; aim at the hole to shut it again; the door and frame glow hatchHighlightColor while the crosshair is on it), so husks can be tossed in by hand. It shuts while the bin works. MOVING: the carry handles move the bin through a kinematic Rigidbody (PixelCarryHandles), so loose husks are pushed instead of glitching through; while carried every husk inside is frozen into the bin each frame and any that still ends up outside is let go and uncounted at once (KeepContentsWhileCarried). A husk that leaves the bin is handed back to the floor's husk list (AdoptOldPixel), never left orphaned. Pressing the button freezes every counted husk into the bin first (a still-settling one could be jostled out by the shake, let go and caught again after the cycle) and POPS any UNCOUNTED husk lying in the bin (OVERFILLED past capacity) up and out over the front / back rim (EjectUncounted, overfillPopSpeed; only the front with a hopper on the back), also again before paying. AUTO RECYCLING (back on 2026-10-18 after a day of button-only): every husk that is captured is recycled on its own RecycleDelay (0.5 s; Operation: Speed -0.08 s a level) later (RecycleDue from Capture, RecycleOne), with a same-type STREAK that lasts while husks keep coming (streakTimeout 6 s). The red button is HIDDEN (showButton off; the lid / shake / pay-the-batch cycle and the overfill pop still work with autoRecycle off + showButton on). CURRENCY ONLY (2026-10-17 rework, docs/GameDesign.md section 6 'Husk routing rework'): each husk pays payoutShare (50%) of what a click on it pays now as BONUS currency (AddBonusCurrency: not towards the next tier's unlock), times the same-type STREAK within that batch (in the order the husks went in: +streakStep 0.1 per same-type husk, a different type keeps streakKeepOnSwitch of it, cap streakMaxMultiplier 1.6), never more than maxShareOfClick (95%) of a click; each '+N' popup over a recycled husk carries the streak as a small '(x1.3)' (none at x1.0); the device card shows the streak (FillDeviceInfo: inside, streak; the panel adds pay share, recycle delay, size and this session's husks / paid). NO skill XP (xpPerHusk 0). SIZE (2026-10-18): placed 1.8x as wide and deep as the first bin by default (DefaultSize 1.8 = the Device's radius - 2 for a day, then 10% smaller; Operation: Size adds +0.3 a level); it holds capacity (25) husks at that size, more by floor area when bigger (Capacity). A floating down arrow shows on hover (the time left is on the device card). SEVERAL RECYCLERS: any number can stand at once; the shop price rises with every one held or standing (Device.priceGrowthPerOwned 2, PixelConsumables Laddered). ONE OWNER PER HUSK: Capture, the suction and the suck-in only take husks PixelHuskHolder.Takeable says are free (a husk the Sweeper / hopper / another bin has reserved or captured is left alone). Stats recycler.husks / paid / xp, Best recycler.streak. The idea is that husks reach it WITHOUT the player stopping (bin placed where husks land; edge-click aiming is planned). The button / capacity / lid cycle below is the old manual mode (autoRecycle off) and still works in auto mode for anything left inside. A FULL bin makes its button flash and pulse (UpdateButtonFlash, flashColor). Removing the Recycler destroys what is inside. Not a belt acceptor yet. SORTING HOPPER: no hookup since 2026-10-20 (the hopper is standalone): a bin put under its green pipe simply catches what drops out of it. Capturing / letting go / freezing use the shared PixelHuskHolder steps. FROM ABOVE ONLY (2026-10-18, suctionFromAboveOnly): the ring and the suck-in only take husks higher than the rim (falling / flying over it, up to suctionHeightHusks); nothing lying beside the bin is drawn in. Each recycled husk shows a '+n' rising over it and adds to the currency's '+n' (PixelUI.ShowWorldGain). SUCTION RING + SUCK-IN (2026-10-17): a modest ring (suctionRadius 2 husk widths past the rim + the Suction upgrades, suctionStrength) draws LOOSE husks nearby towards the bin (a little lift by the wall); a husk over the opening or right by the rim (suckReach) is SUCKED IN like the Storage Crate - it glides into the middle of the bin (SuckOne, suckSeconds) and is caught as usual. Modest on purpose: one bin catches some of a random scatter, not all of it. FRONT SOCKETS (2026-10-18): four In coupling holes low on the front ('f0'..'f3', FrontPortX): a belt / hose there drops husks in just inside the front wall. SOCKET (PixelSockets.cs): the back hatch is an In socket ('hatch'): a coupling there takes belts / connectors / hoses: AcceptHusk counts the husk at once and lets it fall in behind the back wall (HatchInside); refused while working or full (HuskRoom).
--- PixelRecyclerDevice.cs ---
PixelRecyclerDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) The Recycler's countdown (2026-10-16: the Recycler is a TIMED consumable, 5 minutes by default, Upgrades > Operation 'Run Time'): placed by PixelConsumables.SpawnDevice with the device's (upgraded) duration, it starts at once (never disarmed), shows no timer of its own (PixelRecycler writes 'm:ss left' under its counter) and shrinks away when the time is up (PixelPlacedDevice); the husks still in the bin go with it. Saved / restored with the time left like every placed device.
--- PixelRemoveTint.cs ---
PixelRemoveTint.cs The "being cancelled" look: while the player holds the right button to remove something, it turns RED (colour towards red, a red glow in its emission, a red point light) as the hold ring fills, exactly like a placed device (PixelPlacedDevice.SetRemoveGlow). PixelRemoveTint.Set(gameObject, 0..1) does it for ANY object (added to it on demand; 0 puts everything back and removes itself): used by PixelFeatureTester.UpdateDelete (conveyor belts, kilns, elevators, solar panels, the tester's chipper). Placed devices keep their own SetRemoveGlow (it also dulls them when disarmed). The running potion line (PixelUI.UpdateActiveHud) uses Shake() / RedText() from here: the text reddens and trembles very slightly as the hold fills.
--- PixelRightClick.cs ---
PixelRightClick.cs ONE place that decides who a RIGHT click in the world belongs to (added with Husk Crush, 2026-10-17). Each user asks PixelRightClick.Current (worked out once per frame) instead of stacking its own checks. In order of priority: 1. Blocked: a menu, the pointer over UI. 1b. Charge: a Power Click is charging (PixelPowerClick.Charging) - a right press cancels it (no cooldown); nothing else gets it. 2. Tool: the Bank hose is out, a device is being placed, the Conveyor Belt Kit / a tester piece is placing - they keep the right button. 3. Pet: a pet is under the pointer (or held / its menu is open) - right-click opens its menu (PixelPetsMenu). 3b. Device: a placed device is under the pointer - unless a crushable husk is at the pointer too (PixelHuskCrush.PickAt): then Husk wins (crushing comes first, 2026-10-18; the device's removal hold only runs for a press that started as Device) (PixelConsumables.DeviceAtPointer; the cube in front wins) - a right TAP opens the shared device info panel (PixelDeviceCard), a right HOLD still removes it (PixelConsumables.UpdateRemoval). Device = that device. 4. WorldPart: anything else holding the mouse (PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock: a device part that claimed the pointer, the UFO Helper, first person, the new-game intro, dev pickers...). 5. Cube: the live cube is under the pointer, even with a husk lying in front of it - Block Hold, clearing a hazard, the shuffle tap. 6. Blocked again: a takeover minigame (its husks are its pieces), the camera intro, time frozen by something other than Time Stop. 7. Husk: a crushable husk is under the pointer, or (PixelHuskCrush.PickAt, pickMarginPixels) the nearest one on the screen is within a small margin of it - Husk Crush. Husk = that body. 8. None. THE PRESS KEEPS ITS TARGET (2026-10-17, Charge Crush): the owner worked out on the frame the right button goes down is remembered (PressOwner) while it stays held. A press that started on a husk stays the husk's: Current keeps answering Husk (Husk = that body, null once it is gone) until the button is let go, so a hold that drifts onto the cube never starts Block Hold (its deferred start re-asks Current while held) and a hold on the cube never turns into a husk charge (PixelHuskCrush only charges when PressOwner is Husk). The press is caught by whoever asks first that frame; PixelHuskCrush asks on every press frame, so it is always caught. Released = PressOwner None again. A press that cancelled a Power Click charge (Charge) is kept the same way, so Block Hold's deferred start never picks it up after the cancel. Users: PixelBlockHold.CanStart (Cube), PixelClicker's hazard / bottle right-click (Cube), PixelHuskCrush (Husk). Systems that still check the right button on their own (the belt panel tap, device / tester-object removal holds, the pet menu itself) are covered by the order above; a NEW right-click use adds its owner here instead of its own check.
--- PixelRightTap.cs ---
PixelRightTap.cs A quick right CLICK on a target, told apart from a right HOLD on it (holding is used to delete tester objects, PixelHold). One instance per user: call Update(whatIsUnderThePointer) every frame the right button could be involved (when RightPressed, RightHeld or Pending); it returns true on the frame the button is let go on the same target after at most maxSeconds. Used by the belt flip (PixelConveyorSystem) and the Solar Panel's re-link (PixelSolarPanel), which used to fire on the press.
--- PixelRingMesh.cs ---
PixelRingMesh.cs One shared mesh helper: a flat RING (annulus) in the local XY plane with some depth along Z, flat-shaded (front, back, outer and inner walls). Used by the Pixel Bank's area ring and the Fire pixel's ring. (2026-10-20: moved here from the removed ring Sorter, PixelSorterDevice.)
--- PixelRisingText.cs ---
PixelRisingText.cs ONE place for the floating 3D message of a minigame round (Breakout, Snake, Sorting Race, Stardust; 2026-10-24: was a copied block in each): a bold, black-outlined TextMeshPro in the world that rises and fades away, then destroys itself. - Create(name, message, fontSize, font, boxSize): makes the text object (centred, bold, outline 0.2, the shared UI font when given). The minigame passes the result's gameObject to its Track(...) so the dev tools' Despawn removes it. - Fade(text, seconds, place, tint): the coroutine (run it with the minigame's StartCoroutine). Every frame (unscaled time) it calls place(text, k) with k = 0..1 - the caller moves / turns the text there (each round rises its own way) - then fades it out (alpha 1 - k*k; with a tint the colour is set too). Destroys the object at the end. (Other one-off world texts - Black Hole, Meteor... - have their own looks and keep their own code.)
--- PixelRockMesh.cs ---
PixelRockMesh.cs Jagged rocky pixels: PixelLook.jagged (Obsidian, Meteor, Rock, the six ores; looksVersion 36 replaced their saved looks and removed the Obsidian bevel rim - rocky pixels have no outline) draws a chipped cube mesh (PixelRockMesh.Get(baseCube, type): 8x8 quads per face, flat shaded, only edge / corner vertices pulled inwards by a position hash so shared vertices match; faces stay flat, never bigger than the cube, same per-face UVs; edge vertices move only across the edge and the pull is capped so no triangle folds over - checked offline for 300 seeds, 2026-10-19). PixelClicker.MeshFor(look) / BaseMesh() pick the mesh: the live cube swaps its MeshFilter mesh in ApplyTierLook, old pixel copies, display pixels and their extras / glow shells use MeshFor of their own tier (never srcFilter.sharedMesh, which may be another type's jagged mesh). The hit box is still a cube.
--- PixelSapField.cs ---
PixelSapField.cs SAP FIELDS (a system component, PixelSystems World group): every Sap HUSK lying on the field has a soft transparent YELLOW CIRCLE round it (radiusHusks, 4 husk widths) and keeps the other WOODCUTTING husks inside it fresh: - a woodcutting husk (any block of the Woodcutting pack except Sap itself) that comes inside a sap circle gets +huskBonusSeconds (5 s) of lifetime, ONCE ever (a husk that leaves and comes back, or sits in two circles, still only gets it once); - the sap itself gets +sapBonusPerHusk (1 s) for every different husk that has been inside its circle (once per husk, per sap); - while a husk is inside a circle it wears a faint yellow glow shell (a slightly bigger see-through yellow cube, no collider). Checked every checkSeconds (0.2); the circles follow their sap every frame (LateUpdate), lying flat at the bottom of the sap. Not saved (the bonuses are just lifetime already added). disableSapField switches it off.
--- PixelScheduledMinigame.cs ---
PixelScheduledMinigame.cs The ROUND SCHEDULE shared by the minigames that come back on a timer (2026-10-24: was a copied block in each): Black Hole, Bomb, Ghost, Meteor, Ultra Holes (Pad), Evil Tree, Cube Blink, Cube Dash, Cube Evasion, Traffic, Snake, Sorting Race and Breakout derive from it. - The Inspector fields: startRunning (testing), running (the shop switches it on), firstRoundDelay (seconds from the start to the first round), minInterval..maxInterval (the random wait between rounds). Each minigame sets its OWN defaults in its constructor (that is what its old field initializers did); values already set in a scene carry over (FormerlySerializedAs, incl. the old first-delay names). - Running / Activate / Deactivate come from here. Activate starts the clock at firstRoundDelay; a minigame that must also stop a round when it is switched off overrides Deactivate (base.Deactivate() first). - The minigame's Awake calls StartSchedule() where it used to set 'running' and the first timer; ScheduleNext() puts the next round minInterval..maxInterval away (after a round, after the dev tools' Despawn). spawnTimer counts down in the minigame's Update with PixelMinigame.SpawnDue. Visitors (their own visit times + a per-visitor offset) and Weak Spots (runs while a skill is trained) schedule themselves differently and are not part of this.
--- PixelSessionRecorder.cs ---
PixelSessionRecorder.cs Session recorder (dev / playtest tool, never shown to players): records how a real play session goes, so balance numbers can be set from the player's actual pace instead of guesses. Component in PixelSystems (group Core). - Dev Hub widget 'Session recorder' (PixelDevTools): Start, then Stop & save -> a text report in <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots + clipboard (PixelDevProbe.Save), meant to be pasted to Claude. - Tester builds (no Dev Tools): tick recordEveryPlay on the component. Recording starts by itself when the title screen goes away and the report is written into <persistentDataPath>/<testerFolder> (default 'Feedback', the same folder as the Give feedback notes, so a tester sends one folder) every autosaveMinutes and when the game quits. What it records: only time in the game counts (not the pause menu / title screen). Clicks per second (manual and auto, per minute and the best 10 s), blocks broken, income per pixel type per minute, Standard and training-skill XP per minute, a TIMELINE of milestones found by polling once a second (pixel types unlocked, shop packs bought / levelled, skill level-ups, Cubie's visits, the shop window first opened), Block Hold and stakes (hold.count, stake.placed, stake.won / lost), husk counts (blocks broken = husks dropped; despawned unused 'old.despawned', removed by the floor cap 'old.capped', grabbed, vacuumed, recycled, shredded, banked - PixelStats counters), HUSK CRUSH (right-click a husk, PixelHuskCrush: crushes and base crush XP per minute, plain click vs Chain Crush vs Charge Crush, per skill, the share of dropped husks crushed and of Standard XP that came from crushing, XP per crush, the crush bonuses: air / last-gasp counts and the best streak; the first crush and the Chain Crush / Clean Break / Hard Hands / Charge Crush nodes go on the timeline), and the state at the start and end.
--- PixelShaders.cs ---
PixelShaders.cs PixelShaders.SpriteDefault(): the only place that looks up the Sprites/Default shader (unlit, alpha-blended, vertex colours - trails, glow shells, face marks, arrows, stars, hole trails). Shader.Find only finds shaders that are in the build, and a URP project often leaves Sprites/Default out, so it falls back to the shader of Resources/PixelShaderKeep/Sprites_Default (made by the Editor keep-alive script). Use it instead of Shader.Find("Sprites/Default").
--- PixelShopBuying.cs ---
PixelShopBuying.cs Part of PixelShop (partial class): pack state, requirements, costs, buying and the dev unlocks. The system notes are in PixelShop.cs.
--- PixelShopConsumables.cs ---
PixelShopConsumables.cs Third-and-a-half part of partial class PixelShop: the Consumables tab, a tile page (layout in PixelShopTilePage.cs). Chips: Utilities (opens first), Potions, Seeds, Supplies (ConsumableChipCategory -> PixelConsumables.CategoryOf 1 / 0 / 2 / 3; labels utilitiesCardTitle...). Tiles: price in green / red / grey when locked, owned count badge ("x3"), 'Full' when a potion is at its cap. Shift-click a tile = buy one; the buy bar has - count + and Buy (Shift-click Buy = MaxAffordable). The Recycler stands first among Utilities. Craft-only, not-sold, vendor-only items and Dragon seeds are never listed. The search box still lists matching items as rows in the main list (RefreshSearchItems in PixelShopUI.cs). Buying goes through TryBuyItems(item, count). Settings are in PixelShop.cs ("Consumables Tab").
--- PixelShopDatabase.cs ---
PixelShopDatabase.cs Part of PixelShop: its Game Database entries (IPixelDatabaseSource, see PixelDatabase.cs). - 'Shop pack' entries (PacksOrder): description + tab; discovered = bought or a reward pixel unlocked; links to reward pixels, cost pixels / minigame currencies, required packs / minigame goals and the minigame it unlocks. - Pack -> skill links (PackSkillLinksOrder, after the skills exist). - 'Upgrade' entries (ExtrasOrder, 2026-10-24): one per Operation upgrade (Upgrades > Operation; operationUpgrades): its description, what each level adds, the level bought, linked to its consumable; discovered with that consumable (or once a level is bought).
--- PixelShopMigrate.cs ---
PixelShopMigrate.cs Part of PixelShop (partial class): MigrateOldPackData (older pack fields, renames and retired packs) and MovePack. The system notes are in PixelShop.cs.
--- PixelShopNudge.cs ---
PixelShopNudge.cs (+ PixelGuideVendor.ShopHold) Shop hold after Cubie's second visit (added by PixelClicker.Awake): if the shop tutorial is still due (PixelShopTutorial.DoneKey, tips on) the visit does NOT unfreeze time when he leaves: PixelGuideVendor.ShopHold keeps Time.timeScale 0 (UpdateHold re-freezes after a pause-menu resume) until the shop window opens (PixelShop.WindowOpen), then time resumes and PixelShopTutorial.RequestStart() starts the tutorial without its start delay (leaves the hold on the title screen / tips off). While held the Shop dock button stays slid out (ShopHold in its keep-out lambda), the tier guide line (PixelUI.UpdateGuide, forced to shopGuideText) pulses gold and the Shop button (PixelShop.ShopButtonRect) gets the shared pulsing gold frame (PixelHighlight.Set, glowColor); disableNudge.

--- PixelShopPictures.cs ---
PixelShopPictures.cs Part of partial class PixelShop: rotating 3D pictures on shop rows/cards (AddRowPicture, AddPackPicture, Consumables tiles / buy bar; pixel packs cycle through their reward tiers every pictureCycleSeconds, Value rows, Consumables tiles (still, clipped to the grid) and buy bar, minigame packs use primitive models BuildMinigameModel for ghost/meteor/blackhole/bomb/pad/snake/sort/breakout - a new minigame needs a case + its id in ModelledMinigames). One shared PixelModelPicture studio per distinct picture key ('shop:' + tier:<Type> / item:<name> / game:<id>; pixel cubes framed by pictureCubeFraming, the rest fitted), drawn only while on screen (UpdatePictures); hidePictures, pictureResolution. Shop cost lines no longer print '(you have n)' (NoHave strips it from old saved format strings).
--- PixelShopPixels.cs ---
PixelShopPixels.cs Part of partial class PixelShop: the Pixels tab, a tile page (layout in PixelShopTilePage.cs) instead of the scrolling list. Chips: Toggleables (pixelsToggleablesTitle: the RGB pack first until bought, then single pixels - Glass, Luminescent...; SectionOf 0 / 1) and Skill packs (pixelsSkillPacksTitle: SectionOf 2, the Lumberjack's Pack first). A tile shows the pack's pixels one after another (PackPictureKey), its first price (green / red; grey + 'Locked' while its requirement is missing), 'Owned' once bought and a level badge on leveled packs. The buy bar shows the full description, the cost (or what it still needs) and Buy; a bought pack with upgrades turns Buy into a button that opens its upgrades window. Shift-click a tile = buy. Same listing rules as the old rows: upgrade children are left out, locked packs only with showLockedPacks, hidden when bought if 'hide purchased' is on. The search box still lists matching packs as rows in the main list.
--- PixelShopSearch.cs ---
PixelShopSearch.cs Part of partial class PixelShop: the search results, shown as a tile page (layout in PixelShopTilePage.cs, no chip row) while the search box has text (shopSearch; RefreshTilePages opens it in place of every tab).
