One page lists four kinds of results, told apart by id range: packs (id = pack index, any tab; MatchesSearch: name, description, reward pixel names) - same tiles / bar as the Pixels tab, consumables (ItemBase + item: potions, devices, seeds, supplies; name, description, category word) - as on the Consumables tab, Value upgrades (ValueBase + tier: '<pixel> Value' + 'value upgrade') - Buy = one level, Shift = as many as you can, Operation rows (OperationBase + kind index: '<device> upgrades operation') - Buy opens that device's upgrade window. Shift-click a tile = buy one (Operation: opens the window). The emptied search shows the open tab again.
--- PixelShopText.cs ---
PixelShopText.cs The shop's editable text file: Assets/Resources/ShopText.txt holds the NAME, DESCRIPTION and PRICE of every shop pack and every built-in device (consumable), so they can be edited without the Inspector. The file wins: at play, PixelShop.ApplyShopText and PixelConsumables.ApplyShopText copy what it says over the coded / Inspector values (a field left out or empty keeps them). FORMAT: '#' lines are comments. Each item is a block that starts with its key in brackets - '[Pack: <coded name>]' or '[Device: <coded name>]' (the coded default name, so renaming an item in 'Name:' keeps it matched) - followed by Name: ... Description: ... (one line; write \n for a line break) Price: 75 White, 50 Green (amount + pixel type, comma separated; K / M / B allowed: 1.5K; '@bomb' = a minigame's counter) Price L1: ... / Price L2: ... (upgrade packs: one line per level) Pixel types are written by their code name (White, HornetNest...); display names work too. Gathering skill packs (ShopPack.skillLadder) get their price from the shop's skill pack ladder, so their Price line is ignored. In the Editor the file is created on the first play if it is missing, and items it doesn't list yet are appended at the end (never overwriting what is there). Potions and seeds are priced from their pixel at run time, so they are not in the file. Renaming an item changes the name the save file stores it under (saves match packs / devices by name).
--- PixelShopTilePage.cs ---
PixelShopTilePage.cs Part of partial class PixelShop: the shared "tile page" layout used by the Pixels tab (PixelShopPixels.cs) and the Consumables tab (PixelShopConsumables.cs). Top to bottom: - category chips (the open one lit like a tab; each chip remembers its selected tile), - a scrolling grid of tiles, 160 x 176 = 5 to a row (still 3D picture - no name: the hover tooltip and the buy bar show it -, the price as colour swatches + numbers (CostStrip: SetCosts / SetCostMessage; red number = can't afford), a small corner badge, a 'Locked' mark over the picture). Click a tile = select it, Shift-click = the page's quick buy, hover = its description as the shop tooltip. - one buy bar for the selected tile: spinning picture, name, the full description, the cost (swatch + number + currency name, two columns), - count + (pages with hasCount) and the Buy button (Shift-click Buy = the page's 'as many as you can'). A page only says WHAT to show through its delegates (collect, tileName, pictureKey, description, fillTile, fillBar, purchase, quickBuy); building, layout, selection and the shared tooltip live here. Tiles are built once per id and reused. Settings: PixelShop.cs "Consumables Tab" (chipHeight, buyBarHeight, tileWidth / Height / Gap, tileFontSize, buyMaxTip). While a page is open the main scrolling list is hidden (RefreshTilePages, called from RefreshRows).
--- PixelShopTutorial.cs ---
PixelShopTutorial.cs Shop tutorial (added by PixelShop). ONLY the shop's blue '?' hold button plays it now (autoStart off: when the shop becomes available it is just marked done, DoneKey, so the tutorials waiting for it carry on; Cubie's shop hold no longer starts it). With autoStart on (old behaviour): the first time the Shop button is available (PixelShop.ShopAvailable) and the screen is calm (title screen over, PixelHints.IntroHoldsGuide false, no other tip, no takeover minigame, not paused; startDelay 2 s) a series of small "Got it" boxes (PixelNotice.Show(small: true, beside: shop.PanelRect), 440 wide, just right of the shop window) walks through the shop, each step opening its tab via PixelShop.TutorialOpen(tab, subTab): the currency list + overview, Pixels, Upgrades > Features, Upgrades > Value, Operation, Consumables, Minigames, closing hint. Before the first box the screen fades darker and the game pauses (BeginRun: a click-blocking black overlay on canvas 600, below the tip boxes, dimAmount 0 = 0.4 / dimSeconds 0 = 0.7; time is restored and the overlay fades out at the end) and PixelShopTutorial.Active makes the Shop button (and its key) ignore clicks until the tutorial is over. Steps are an Inspector list (steps, empty = the built-in ones, enableTutorial); Cubie narrates it (PixelGuideVendor.BeginNarration/EndNarration: he slides in at the bottom right at 0.85 scale, stands still and points at the shop window with a stick (pointer: arm + stick + gold tip turned towards the shop's on-screen centre every frame, ShopPointTarget; his left arm hides meanwhile) and taps it on each new step (NarratorReact), no speech box, and leaves when the tutorial ends); every box has one height (from the longest step, min boxHeight) so Got it never moves - the flow is the shared PixelTutorialRunner; the shop window has a blue '?' hold button left of its X (the pause menu's PixelHoldButton with a fill bar, helpHoldSeconds 0.7 on PixelShop) that calls Replay(); remembered as hint id shop_tutorial (PixelHints.IsTipSeen/SetTipSeen; Settings > Replay tutorial clears it, so it replays after the intro if the shop is unlocked).
--- PixelShopTypes.cs ---
PixelShopTypes.cs Part of PixelShop (partial class): the data types (pack, cost, level, requirement, tab and effect types). The system notes are in PixelShop.cs.
--- PixelShowScreen.cs ---
PixelShowScreen.cs ONE place for the screen layers the full-screen 'find' shows (Dragon Find, Egg Hatch) build on their canvas (2026-10-24: was a copied block in each). Goes with PixelShowStudio (their 3D photo studio) and PixelUISparks (their sparkles). - NewImage(name, parent, colour): a plain Image with no raycasts. - DimAndBackpack(canvas, backpackSize, out dim, out backpack): the full-screen 'Dark' layer (starts clear; it BLOCKS clicks while the show plays) and the 'Backpack' picture (starts clear, anchored bottom left, backpackSize square) the find flies into. - Titles(font, canvas, titleColour, out title, out subtitle): the big caps title (64) and the white subtitle (40) across the top, below the HUD bar.
--- PixelShowStudio.cs ---
PixelShowStudio.cs ONE place for the private photo studio of a full-screen show (Dragon Find, God Find, Egg Hatch; 2026-10-24: was a copied block in each). a root far below the scene with a transparent orthographic camera (switched off: the show calls Render() itself, once a frame) and a point light. Pictures of a pixel type for windows use PixelTierPicture / PixelModelPicture instead - a show needs its own because it moves, scales and adds parts to its model every frame. - Create(name, depth, cameraDistance, farClip, lightPos, lightRange, lightIntensity, out cam): the root (at y = depth) + camera + light. - AddPixelModel(clicker, tierIndex, parent): a 'Pivot' under parent holding the pixel type's real display model with its colliders removed (a plain cube if there is no model), for the show to turn. - Picture(name, size): a square transparent RenderTexture. Release(ref root, ref rt): destroys both. (The find shows' screen layers - dark layer, backpack, title / subtitle - are in PixelShowScreen; their sparkles in PixelUISparks.)
--- PixelShuffleBonus.cs ---
PixelShuffleBonus.cs SHUFFLE BONUS (a test; component added by PixelClicker.Awake; needs Block Hold, it builds on its quick tap): TAP the right button on the FILLER block of the skill in play (Driftwood in Fishing, Twig in Woodcutting... = PixelSkills.TryGetFiller) and Block Hold sends it behind the next block as usual (a tap = released within tapSeconds, 0.35). The first shuffle starts a combo: a 'SHUFFLE x1' box with a bar that runs down (barSeconds, 6) shows under the top bar. The combo only GROWS when the block it was shuffled behind has been broken (waiting = true until a PixelCollected arrives); when the filler comes back, shuffle it again before the bar runs out for x2, x3... (no cap yet). BREAKING the filler instead cashes the combo in: bonus XP = xpPerShuffle (3) x the combo, on top of the normal XP (PixelSkills.AddShuffleXp). If the bar runs out first the filler breaks by itself (dropped from the queue, or auto-collected if it is on the cube) and a smaller consolation bonus (expireFactor 0.35 of the cash-out) is paid. The combo block on the cube (the shuffled filler that came back from the queue, not a freshly rolled one) lights up gold: PixelClickerAura.ComboHighlight forces the cube's glow aura on in gold and makes it pulse. A skill switch (other filler) or the title screen ends the combo. UNLOCK (2026-10-16): Standard skill tree node 'Shuffle Combo' (shuffle, after High roller on the Block Hold path; requireTreeNode off = with Block Hold). Fields are Inspector values on this component (0 = the coded default); disableShuffleBonus switches the whole thing off. Not saved.
--- PixelSkillLevelPopup.cs ---
PixelSkillLevelPopup.cs A small LEVEL-UP popup for every skill (PixelSkills.AddXpFlat calls Show when a level is reached): a round badge in the skill's accent colour with the skill's icon spinning inside it (the real model of the skill's first block, PixelTierPicture; Standard / Offline use the White pixel), the text 'WOODCUTTING LEVEL 5' with '+1 skill point' on a pill beside it, and tiny FIREWORKS - three little bursts of coloured sparks around the badge that fly out, fall and fade. It pops in (overshoot), stays showSeconds (3.6) and fades; several level-ups queue one after another. It FOLLOWS THE MOUSE, sitting above the pointer over the '+n XP' popups (followMouse, mouseOffset; its canvas 960 is above theirs 106; off = a fixed spot above the bottom bar), waits while a menu is open, plays the 'achievement' sound. All on unscaled time, own canvas, no physics. A system component (PixelSystems, UI group); disablePopup switches it off.
--- PixelSkillSwitchFx.cs ---
PixelSkillSwitchFx.cs The spin-and-flash of the 'skill switch' test block (PixelClicker.ShowSkillSwitchBlock, called by PixelSkills.SetActive when another skill is chosen): added to the cube's pixel for about a second - an extra fast spin (spinDegreesPerSecond, easing out) and a white point-light flash (flashIntensity, fading) so the swap reads as an event. Unscaled time; it removes itself (and its light) when done, and adding it again restarts it. The scale pop comes from the normal materialize.
--- PixelSkillsBonus.cs ---
PixelSkillsBonus.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: the bonus XP bank. Per skill, stat counter skill.<id>.bonus (starts 0, floored at 0). PixelSkills.OldPixelConsumed(tierIndex) is called when an old pixel is CONSUMED (PixelClicker.Vacuum and AbsorbOldPixel = Vacuum pixel / Vacuum Device, the black hole swallowing, the Pet Vacuum; pixel rules: they also give husk XP, see PixelSkills.AddHuskXp) and works on the pack in play (none for Standard): a GOOD pixel (IsGood: Mining = ores, Farming = plants, Electrician = conductors; goodTypeOverrides 'packId:Type,Type') adds goodBonusXp (0 = 3) x a rarity factor (1..4, from 1/sqrt(spawn weight)); every other pixel (junk AND neutral) takes badBonusXp (0 = 1) off. In AddXp, DrawBonus DOUBLES each gain while the bank holds anything - the extra comes out of the bank (after the hazard penalty). Shown as a gold 'Bonus N' after the XP on the skill tracker; disableBonusXp. Not on the Skills window yet; other consumers (quantum organizer, bank, UFO) don't count.
--- PixelSkillsDatabase.cs ---
PixelSkillsDatabase.cs Part of PixelSkills: its Game Database entries (IPixelDatabaseSource, see PixelDatabase.cs). - 'Skill' entries (SkillsOrder): description + perks + tree node lines; discovered = IsAvailable; pinnable; linked to its blocks, filler and hazard. - 'Skill node' entries (SkillNodesOrder): one per tree node (cost, level needed, 'coming soon'), linked to its skill, the node it needs and the block toggle it unlocks; discovered with its skill.
--- PixelSkillsGuide.cs ---
PixelSkillsGuide.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: SKILL GUIDES (first one: Woodcutting). The first time the Woodcutting card shows in the Skills window (clicked, or the window opened on it) (and no other tutorial runs), a small box over the window asks 'Want a quick tutorial?' (Yes / No; stat guide.woodcutting.asked, per save). YES = the guide: small Got it boxes beside the Skills window (PixelNotice beside windowRect, back arrow / Skip), the screen dimmed and paused (PixelTutorialDim) - the basics of Woodcutting and how its blocks spawn (filler rotation, trees and their order, Timber, infested trees, the Great Oak, skill points / the Lumberjack). The texts are the Inspector list woodGuideSteps (empty = the built-ins). NO = one box pointing at the GUIDE BOOK button that sits between the skill's name and the 'Skill tree' button in the detail panel (framed with the shared gold PixelHighlight until Got it). The book (a code-drawn little book) shows for every skill that has a guide (HasGuide) and replays it any time.
--- PixelSkillsMastery.cs ---
PixelSkillsMastery.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: Mastery, one bar for the progress of ALL skills together, along the bottom of the Skills window (BuildMasteryBar, RefreshMastery, gold fill, 'Mastery 12.5% - x / y XP', tooltip). Every skill has a level cap (SkillPack.levelCap, 0 = defaultLevelCap on the component, 0 = 30; the caps only measure mastery for now - a skill keeps earning XP past its cap) worth CapXp(pack) = the XP from level 1 to the cap (baseXp x xpGrowth^n summed); MasteryProgress = sum over EVERY pack except Standard (Mining, Farming, Electrician, Offline Progression and any pack added later; skills not unlocked yet count as 0) of min(skill XP, its cap XP) / sum of all cap XP, so it only reaches 100% when every skill is at its cap. An event-log line at every 10% (counter mastery.step, saved) and 'Mastery complete!' at 100%. Not shown on the pinned tracker yet.
--- PixelSkillsMinigames.cs ---
PixelSkillsMinigames.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: the minigame XP multiplier. AddXp multiplies every gain by MinigameXpMultiplier = 1 + EnabledMinigames x xpPerMinigame (0 = 0.1, i.e. +10% each), capped by maxMinigameXpBonus (0 = no cap), disableMinigameXpBonus. EnabledMinigames counts PixelMinigames that are Running and not UserDisabled (Toggles > Minigames); visitor vendors don't count. Recomputed once a second. Shown in the Skills window header (right side, gold 'Minigames: x1.3 XP (3 on)').
--- PixelSkillsPopups.cs ---
PixelSkillsPopups.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: the '+n <Skill> XP' popups (AddXp calls QueueXpPopup). Three places, so they never pile on top of each other (2026-10-19, the user's layout): * THE XP DOCK - every skill XP popup (clicks, the auto clicker, the Recycler, rewards...): XP is gathered xpPopupGatherSeconds (0 = 0.6 s) per skill and shown as whole numbers (fractions carry over) in a small stack at the BOTTOM RIGHT of the screen (xpDockRightMargin 90 from the edge, popups lined up on their right edges - the mirror of the early skill tracker at the bottom left), just above the black bar (xpDockGap); the newest at the bottom, older ones slide up and fade. HOLD the left mouse button on one (xpDockHoldSeconds, the shared ring, PixelHold) and drag to move the dock; the spot is saved PER SAVE (PixelClicker.Setting.XpDock2X / Y, PixelPrefs.DeclarePerSave); right-click a dock popup to put the dock back in its default spot. The dock popups catch the mouse (so a click on one never reaches the cube). * HUSK CRUSH SCORES: a crush's '+0.25 Standard XP' (AddHuskXp crushed, 'at' = where the husk broke) shows at once, decimals and all, AT THAT SPOT in the world (projected every frame, so it stays put when the camera moves), rising a little as it fades. * CRUSH BONUSES ('Air crush bonus!', 'Last-gasp bonus!', 'Crush streak x1.4!': ShowMouseText) beside the mouse pointer, each in the lowest free row of a small stack, so two never overlap. LOOK: a dark rounded plate behind the text (xpPopupPlate, xpPopupPlateColor), the skill colour lightened towards white (xpPopupLighten), xpPopupSize (0 = 24), xpPopupOpacity (0.8: the game shows through a little), a quick pop as each appears, stays xpPopupSeconds (2; fades over the second half). Own canvas 106. hideXpPopups.
--- PixelSkillsSavedPacks.cs ---
PixelSkillsSavedPacks.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: the fix-ups EnsureDefaults runs on every saved skill pack - step 3 FixSavedPackContents (the blocks, fillers, hazards, perks and tree nodes added since the pack was saved) and step 4 FixSavedPackTexts (older wording). Each is cut into named steps that run in the same order as before; a new fix goes into the step for its pack (or a new step called last). Moved out of PixelSkills.cs (no behaviour change).
--- PixelSkillsStandard.cs ---
PixelSkillsStandard.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: Standard is a real skill. Every collected block gives it a FLAT standardXpPerBlock (1) XP - manual or auto, any block, whatever skill / Offline is trained, no multipliers (AddXpFlat); popups only when Standard is the one trained and the Skills tab exists; level-up log lines only once AnyTrainable). Its perks apply in EVERY rotation: Lingering pixels (StandardLifetimeBonus = (level-1) x lifetimePerLevel 5 s (husks last oldPixelLifetime 35 s by default (15 s before 2026-10-18), so Standard level 2 = 40 s, level 7 = 65 s; the field was renamed from fallingCopyLifetime so the old saved 6 s is dropped), added in PixelClicker.FallingCopyLifetime, which all old-pixel despawn timers read) and Roomy floor (StandardCapacityBonus = (level-3) x capacityPerLevel 10 from level 4 (was 5 on a 50 floor; 10 on the 120 floor = 160 at level 7), added to PixelClicker.OldPixelCap; 'Double floor' (level 10, 2026-10-18): the floor AREA twice as wide - StandardFloorScale, read by PixelFloor.AreaScale like the Dev Hub's 'Double floor area' tick box; doubleFloorLevel / doubleFloorFactor), Steady grip (level 3) and Firm grip (level 5): Block Hold's meter starts at half length and each adds 10% of that (PixelBlockHold.GripFactor reads the Standard level); 'Sharper clicks' (level 7) is still 'coming soon'. Standard shows level / XP bars in the Skills window and the tracker like any skill, has no bonus bank, filler or hazard and does not count towards Mastery. XP CURVE RETUNE (2026-10-17, from the 10:50 session report: level 4 at 8:21, plan ~20 min; Recycler streak XP 2,620 out-earned click XP 2,357): Standard's baseXp 380 -> 600 (x xpCurveScale 2 = 1,200 XP to level 2; growth stays 1.5) AND the Recycler gives a pure husk only standardXpShare (0.5) of its XP (PixelRecycler; skill husks keep it all). Modelled at the report's pace (clicks ~218 XP/min + half the Recycler's ~242 + some crushing ~ 360/min): levels 2-7 at about 3 / 8 / 16 / 27 / 44 / 69 min - Auto Clicker upgrades raise the pace later, pulling 6-7 towards the 45 / 60 min targets. Check the next report's Standard timeline and nudge baseXp / standardXpShare from there. MYSTERY BAR (2026-10-18, design 'Early game feel: click, pile, crush' point 3): until Standard level 2 its name is hidden - ShownName(pack) answers mysteryName ('???') for Standard while StandardHidden, and EVERY player-facing skill name goes through it (the early tracker, XP / crush popups '+1 ???', the XP hover box - mysteryHoverText 'Something is growing...' -, the Skills window, block tooltips, Husk Crush's guide note, the Game Database, which also hides entries that name Standard). Level 2 reveals it (RevealStandard from AddXpFlat): a fanfare (sound, gold punch on the tracker, an event log line) and then the level-2 popup, which now opens on the mystery. showStandardNameEarly = the name from the start. SKILL TREE (2026-10-16, DefaultNodes(StandardId), PixelSkills.HasStandardNode): the first point (level 2) goes on 'Block Hold' (hold; Block Hold is no longer available from the start - PixelBlockHold.requireTreeNode) or 'Power Click' (power, PixelPowerClick.requireTreeNode); branches hold -> lucky (+10% stake win) -> highroller (cost 2, stakes grow 50% faster) and power -> quick (charge 40% faster) -> aftershock (cost 2, cooldown a third shorter). 'What's Next' (nextup, row 1) needs BOTH hold and power ('requires' takes several comma-separated ids now: ParentsOf, all must be owned, the window draws a line from each) and shows the Next up window; 'Look Further' (nextup2, cost 2) and 'Foresight' (nextup3, cost 2) each show one more block ahead in it. 'Shuffle Combo' (shuffle, after highroller) unlocks PixelShuffleBonus (the filler tap combo). The bottom row (row 3) is the HUSK branch (2026-10-17, PixelHuskCrush - crushing itself is free): 'Chain Crush' (chaincrush, minLevel 4, column 1 = beside the first twists) -> 'Clean Break' (cleanbreak, minLevel 6, cost 2) -> 'Hard Hands' (hardhands, minLevel 9, cost 2); TreeNode.minLevel holds a node back. 'Aimed Clicks' (aimed, row 1 column 0, minLevel 3, 2026-10-17): edge-click aiming (PixelBurstPop.AimingOwned) - the alternative to the second move; level 3's perk popup (StandardLevel3Notice) points at that choice. Row 4: 'Charge Crush' (chargecrush, needs chaincrush, minLevel 7, cost 2): hold right on a husk to charge a ring, let go to arc-crush its type inside it. The level 2 popup now points the player to the skill point and the tree. Minigame bonus XP still skips Standard. Standard's XP popups and level-up log lines show from the very FIRST click (no longer only once a skill pack exists). Its level 2 perk carries the intro popup (StandardLevel2Notice, the perk's noticeText: 'you have been training a skill all along...'), shown once through the perk notices. The level 2 popup opens the Skills window on Standard, sits small to its right (PixelNotice beside the window, time frozen until Got it) and frames the Standard card with the shared pulsing gold frame (PixelHighlight) until Got it (Perk.noticeInWindow, any perk can use it; perkWindowVersion 1 switched it on). The Skills window has a '?' button next to the X that plays Cubie's skills intro again (StartTutorial).
--- PixelSkillsTracker.cs ---
PixelSkillsTracker.cs Part of partial class PixelSkills: the pinned skill tracker. A pin button at the top left of the Skills window (BuildPinButton, green while pinned) closes the window and shows a small draggable window (own canvas sortingOrder - 1 (below the Skills window and its tooltip), dragged by PixelPopoutDrag, which now has a locked flag) with the training skill's name, level and an XP bar (RefreshTracker: TrainingId, LevelOf). Hovering its XP bar shows the XP box (PixelSkills.XpTip: 'Skill: lv/lv', Current XP, Next level, Remainder), like the Skills window's header bar and each card's bar. Left / right arrows either side of the skill name (trackerPrev/Next, CycleTrainedSkill) switch the skill in training through Standard and every available pack. Its buttons: a padlock stick (trackerStuck: can't be dragged), downsize ('-' / '+': a one-line small version) and X (closes it; the Skills menu is NOT reopened). DOUBLE CLICKING the name (small or full; PixelDoubleClick) opens the Skills window on the skill in training with its tree beside it (never the expanded tree). Open / small / stuck / position are PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Skills.Tracker* (global, not per save); hidden on the title screen and until the Skills button exists (SkillsTabUnlocked: Standard level 2), so Standard alone can be pinned. EARLY TRACKER (trackerBeforeSkills, UpdateTrackerIntro): before that, from the first Standard XP, the full tracker shows at the bottom left (PlaceTrackerBottomLeft: above the bottom bar, right of the event log, trackerIntroMargin) with NO buttons and locked in place (name double click does nothing), so the player watches Standard level up. When Standard level 2's popup appears (SkillsTabUnlocked turns true at that moment) its buttons come back (arrows, lock, downsize, X) and it becomes the normal pinned tracker in that spot.
--- PixelSkillsTutorial.cs ---
PixelSkillsTutorial.cs / PixelTutorialDim.cs Skills tutorial (part of partial class PixelSkills, started from the window's Update by UpdateTutorialStart the first time a skill pack is available and the screen is calm: not while the shop tutorial is still due, no tip / visitor / pause / takeover; enableTutorial, tutorialStartDelay 0 = 2 s, tutorialSteps empty = the built-ins): same format as the shop tutorial - PixelTutorialDim.Begin fades the screen darker and pauses the game (a click-blocking overlay on canvas 600; PixelTutorialDim.Active is true meanwhile and the Skills tab / Shop button ignore clicks), Cubie narrates (PixelGuideVendor.BeginNarration) and his stick points at the part each step describes (PixelGuideVendor.PointOverride, TutorialFocus Window / Header / Cards / Train / Tree / Mastery / Pin), each step is a small Got it box beside the Skills window (PixelNotice beside: windowRect); the Train / Tree steps look at the first real pack with a tree and the player's own selection comes back at the end. The flow is the shared PixelTutorialRunner. Remembered per save as stat guide.skillsTutorial (TutorialDoneKey, cleared by Settings > Replay tutorial). It replaced the old one-box introText / skills_intro tip. PixelTutorialDim is shared with the shop tutorial (dimAmount / dimSeconds still live on PixelShopTutorial).
--- PixelSkillsWindow.cs ---
Skill tree window (PixelSkillsWindow.cs, PixelScrollFadeX.cs) The tree's nodes sit in a SIDEWAYS scroll view (nodeView + nodeScroll, wheel / drag; RefreshTree widens nodeArea when the tree is wider than the view) whose left / right edges fade out while there is more to see (PixelScrollFadeX: a CanvasGroup on every node and connector line, alpha by distance to the edge). A button at the tree window's TOP LEFT (treeExpandButton, arrow glyph) makes the tree cover the Skills window (treeExpanded: UpdateTreeLayout starts the tree at the window's left edge); pressing again, closing the tree or the window gives the window back. Both windows are standard Fixed windows (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow): Skills has the pin in the left header slot and ? in the right one, the tree has the expand arrow in the left slot. Both keep their own anchoring (Skills at the left edge, the tree stretched beside it).
--- PixelSnakeMinigame.cs ---
PixelSnakeMinigame.cs Snake minigame (pack unlocksMinigame = "snake", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack; every minInterval..maxInterval s when at least minPixels old pixels lie around and AllowAnother): snapshots the old pixels on the floor (not flying / vanishing / held; up to maxPixels), hides the cube (SetCubeShrink), shrinks away every old pixel that is not part of the round (they grow back afterwards) and freezes the chosen ones (isKinematic, gravity-well / float behaviours off, OldPixelDespawn.HoldAll so nothing despawns; the area is cut to the part of the floor that is in view and any pixel outside it or in the air is moved onto the floor inside it) and spawns a code-built head (green cube with eyes) that glides at speed pixel-widths/s on the floor plane (camera-relative arrow directions, no 180° turns, the area's edges just stop it); arrow keys (both input systems) or the on-screen arrow buttons steer. Touching a pixel (eatDistance) appends it to the body, laid along the head's trail (PlaceBody, segmentSpacing); biting the body past safeSegments or running out of timeLimit ends the round (pixels drop and age again); eating all pays rewardMultiplier (10) x each eaten pixel's OldPixelInfo.amount via PixelClicker.AddCurrency and shrinks them away. While PixelSnakeMinigame.Active cube clicks (ExternalClickBlock) and Pixel Grabbing are off. Sounds snake_start/_eat/_win/_lose, tip minigame_snake. Extends PixelMinigame (id snake; dev Spawn/Despawn work, Despawn ends the round cleanly).
--- PixelSnap.cs ---
PixelSnap.cs Placing SNAPS BY DEFAULT (Active = Shift NOT held); hold SHIFT for FREE placement (consumables: PixelConsumables placing; Dev Hub tester machines and belts: PixelFeatureTester; first person carried blocks). Snapping puts the spot to a GRID (gridCubes old-pixel widths, the same spacing as the first person neon floor) and the turn to ANGLE steps (angleDegrees, default 15 so 45 and 90 are hit exactly). The numbers are set on PixelConsumables (Inspector: "Shift snapping") and pushed here; until then the defaults apply. First person: PixelFirstPerson.SnapPoseToGrid snaps a carried block's resting spot (on the ground, not when it sticks to another cube) to cells of the same grid and its turn to the angle steps. Point() only snaps x / z (the height stays); Yaw() rounds a turn. Callers keep the un-snapped turn themselves so Q / E can still turn smoothly while Shift is held.
--- PixelSocketPlates.cs ---
PixelSocketPlates.cs Shows every FREE socket on a placed device as a small PORT PLATE on its opening, all the time (not only while a coupling is being placed), so the player can see where couplings go and which way husks flow there: a flat plate at the socket's port with a chevron - green pointing INTO the device = In, orange pointing OUT = Out, teal = either way (Both). A plate is a child of the device (it moves / turns with it) and goes away when a coupling is fitted on that socket (couplings fit themselves when a belt / hose attaches) and comes back when the socket is free again. Checked every refreshSeconds over PixelCouplings.Devices (PixelCouplings.FreeSockets); fitted (built-in) sockets get no plate. Plates keep their size when the device rescales (PixelCouplings.KeepSize). The same refresh runs the AUTO-FIT pass (PixelCouplings.AutoFitAll), also with plates off. Parts are named "Coupling Plate ..." so a device's own hover / remove check (PixelPlacedDevice.CountsForHit) leaves them out. disablePlates turns them off. In PixelSystems.All (World).
--- PixelSockets.cs ---
PixelSockets.cs SOCKETS: the ONE way husks get into and out of devices (design: docs/systems/PixelCouplings.md). Sockets carry HUSKS only - power is a separate mechanic and will not use these ports. A device declares its sockets as data (PixelSocket: id, port = local position where a belt end / hose end meets it, outward facing, flow Intake / Output / Both, fitted) and implements IPixelSocketDevice: its sockets, a tint for its couplings, and ONE hook for husks coming in (AcceptHusk: take it or refuse when full). Devices register in OnEnable / unregister in OnDisable (PixelCouplings.Register). A PixelCoupling (PixelCoupling.cs) sits on a socket; belts and hoses only ever talk to couplings: - AUTO-FIT: couplings are not bought. A belt END at a free socket's port gets an In coupling, a belt START there an Out one, and a hose end gets one the way the hose runs (AutoFitAt / AutoFitAll - belts placed, devices carried next to belts, every PixelSocketPlates refresh). An Intake socket only takes In, an Output socket only Out, a Both socket follows what attached. Placing snaps to free socket ports too (Ports). - a belt END / hose end at an In coupling hands its husk in: PixelCouplings.HandIn(at, husk) -> the device's AcceptHusk; - a device putting a husk out calls PixelCouplings.Emit(device, socketId, husk): an Out coupling on that socket puts it on the belt that starts at its port (PixelConveyorSystem.StartHasRoom), or hands it to an In coupling there (device to device); false = nothing attached or the belt there is full - the device does its own thing. OutputHasRoom = may the device let one out now. EmitStored = spawn a STORED husk and Emit it (crate, organizer feeding a belt). - TYPE-AWARE INTAKE: a device that takes only some husks implements IPixelHuskFilter (Takes(socket, tier)); PixelCouplings.Takes asks it (true when it doesn't). A source that can pick WHICH husk to hand out implements IPixelHuskPicker, so a hose only takes out husks its target takes. - CONNECTION RESOLVER: Attached(coupling) says what a coupling is joined to (a belt into / out of it, a hose pushing / pulling, another device's coupling, nothing) - the device card's socket rows, the Junction Box's auto direction and the hose's direction re-check all use it. - ONE reach for every link check (LinkReach, in husk widths); FreeSockets / HuskWidth are the shared helpers. Optional extras: IPixelHuskSource (a hose may pull stored husks out of an Out coupling), IPixelHuskRoom (how many more husks an In coupling's device takes now). Couplings are saved with their device: Export / Import (socket ids + direction) into PlacedState.couplings / TesterSave.couplings. An AUTO-DIRECTION socket (autoDirection, the Junction Box) has no fixed way: its device sets its coupling In / Out / idle from Attached.
--- PixelSolarPanel.cs ---
PixelSolarPanel.cs The SOLAR PANEL (a Dev Hub > Tester feature, PixelFeatureTester 'Solar Panel'): a flat panel of blue cells on the ground with an indicator post. It is POWERED while Solar old pixels (PixelType.Solar) lie on it (its pad, a little bigger than the panel). It links to ONE placed device (PixelPlacedDevice: vacuum device, fan, magnet...): right after placing, the tester shows a ring of linkRadiusCubes (0 = 8) cube widths round the panel and you click a device inside it to connect; a right click on a placed panel (not while placing) starts the linking again. A straight BLUE LINE (with a bead running along it while powered) joins the panel to the device; while the panel is powered it calls the device's HoldPower() every frame, so the device's countdown stops. SOLAR USE: a Solar pixel on the pad that is NOT part of a complete group (5+ touching, the clicker's UpdateSolarClusters keeps those alive: OldPixelDespawn.IsKept) is slowly USED UP - one every consumeSeconds (0 = 8) - while a group of 5 on the pad is never used and powers the panel for good. The indicator is blue (powered), blue-white (powered by a complete group, lasts) or dim red (no solar). Saved with the Tester objects (PixelFeatureTester Export / Import: position, turn, link position).
--- PixelSortMinigame.cs ---
PixelSortMinigame.cs Sorting Race minigame (id sort, pack unlocksMinigame = "sort", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack; takeover minigame like Snake): every minInterval..maxInterval s, with at least minPixels old pixels of 2+ types lying around, up to maxPixels of them (types taken in turn so groups are similar in size) are frozen (kinematic, OldPixelDespawn.HoldAll), shuffled and slide into a grid centred on the screen's floor (shrunk to fit the view); the old pixels that are not in the race shrink away and come back afterwards (shrinkOthers, bystanderSeconds); the cube shrinks out of the way during the race (cubeShrinkScale, PixelClicker.SetCubeShrink) and the grid never takes more than maxScreenFraction of the screen; a timer (baseSeconds + secondsPerPixel * n) starts once they are in place; click one pixel then another (screen-distance pick, clickRadiusPixels) to swap them (swapSeconds); sorted = every type forms exactly ONE unbroken run in reading order (Runs() == distinct types) -> each pixel pays amount * (rewardMultiplier + speedBonusMultiplier * timeLeftFraction) via PixelClicker.AddCurrency and shrinks away; timeout = they drop and age again. Sounds sort_start/_ready/_pick/_swap/_win/_lose, tip minigame_sort.
--- PixelSortingHopper.cs ---
PixelSortingHopper.cs SORTING HOPPER (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.SortingHopper 22, Utilities; design: docs/GameDesign.md section 6, 'The early husk setup'): a STANDALONE sorting funnel (2026-10-20: it no longer latches onto a Recycler / Junction Box, and nothing snaps to it). Built like a MINECRAFT HOPPER: a big square funnel bowl on four legs (bowl rim at BowlTopHusks), narrowing underneath (two tapered blocks and a little spout). Built here by Place / CreateGhost / CreateDisplay with PixelFactoryKit; the placed device is the permanent PixelPermanentDevice stub on the same object - bought, kept, placed, saved, removed like every device. It stands where it is put (turn it while placing); SIZE = a default Recycler's (PixelRecycler.DefaultSize) x its own Operation 'Size' (SizeMul, UnlatchedSize). - IT HOLDS HUSKS: a husk that drops into the bowl is taken in as DATA (type + amount, PixelHuskHolder FlyIn into the bowl's throat; no fade timer) - up to holdRoom (20). Full = husks just lie in the bowl and fade. Nothing feeds it through sockets (not a Sweeper target either): husks only drop in. - SORTING: every FeedInterval (0.35 s; Operation 'Speed' shortens it) the oldest held husk that can go somewhere goes: the GREEN output's filter type -> drops out of the mouth of the FEED PIPE (front, -z; SendOut) - put a Recycler under it, or start a belt at its socket; a SIDE output's filter type -> drops out of that side pipe's mouth (SendOut); every OTHER type -> straight down out of the BOTTOM SPOUT (2.2 husks off the floor, between the legs). No filter chosen on any output = it just holds everything. The pipes keep their own straight shape. - OUTPUT SOCKETS (2026-10-20, sockets are the only husk connection path): every SHOWN output pipe (unlocked and switched on) has a Husk OUTPUT socket ('green', 'orange', 'blue', 'pink'; IPixelSocketDevice, NOT fitted) a husk below its mouth, facing out along the pipe. A belt STARTING there gets an Out coupling (auto-fit) and that output's husks go onto it (PixelCouplings.EmitStored) - and while that belt's start is full they WAIT in the bowl (the next held husk that can go somewhere goes instead). An Out coupling with another device's In coupling right in front hands them over (Emit). Nothing attached = exactly as before: they drop out of the pipe's mouth. The bottom spout has no socket (rejects just drop). The socket list is rebuilt when the shown pipes change (couplings taken off, auto-fit puts them back where a belt still starts). AcceptHusk always refuses (no inputs). - OUTPUTS (Operation 'Outputs' +1 a level, 3 levels): side pipes on the LEFT, RIGHT and then BACK, each with its own filter (a block of that type spins over its mouth). Named by their mouth ring's colour (Green = the feed pipe, Orange / Blue / Pink = the side pipes; OutputName / OutputLabel): a wide glowing ring round each mouth and a band round each pipe. Each has a tick box in the device panel (PixelDeviceInfo.AddToggle): OFF hides its pipe and nothing goes out of it (its filter type goes on to the other outputs or the bottom spout); saved as ';O<n>,off'. - THE DEVICE CARD / PANEL (PixelDeviceCard, FillDeviceInfo): hover = the outputs' filters, holding n / room; right-click tap = the panel with what it holds per type, the feed gap, this session's totals, a tick box per output and its buttons: a filter button per output (opens PixelSortingHopperWindow, a grid of every unlocked husk type + None) and EMPTY OUT (everything out of the bottom spout). - The green output's filter also shows on a little flag on the bowl's back-left corner (tinted its colour, the block spinning on top). A floating down arrow (PixelHoverMarker) shows on hover. - MOVE HANDLES: carrying handles on the bowl's sides (shared PixelCarryHandles) - drag to move it, scroll / Q / E to turn it. Removing it lets everything it holds out of the bottom spout first. Unlock: vendorOnly + shopSkillId 'standard' / level 3 = it appears in the shop at Standard level 3 (no vendor sells it); bought for White. Saved in PlacedState.extra: 'F,Type' (or 'F,-'), ';S<k>,Type' side filters, ';O<n>,off' outputs off, then ';Type,amount' per held husk. Stats hopper.husks (out of the green pipe), hopper.sided, hopper.rejected (out of the bottom spout), hopper.ejected (Empty out). Tip device_SortingHopper.
--- PixelSortingHopperWindow.cs ---
PixelSortingHopperWindow.cs The Sorting Hopper's FILTER PICKER (2026-10-18): opened by the 'Select filter' button the hopper adds to the shared device panel (PixelDeviceCard: right-click tap a hopper; PixelDeviceInfo.AddAction). A standard Fixed window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, its own canvas, Escape closes it: PixelWindows 60) with a scrolling grid of every UNLOCKED husk type (not fly-aways, each with its PixelTierPicture and name) plus 'None' (= the hopper just holds); the current filter is highlighted. Clicking one sets it (PixelSortingHopper.SetFilter) and closes the window. SHARED (2026-10-19): it picks for any IPixelTierPickTarget (the hopper's outputs; the Husk Source's block, all types, no None). The hopper calls Changed(this) when its filter changes (the highlight redraws) and Forget(this) when it is destroyed (the window closes).
--- PixelSprinklerDevice.cs ---
PixelSprinklerDevice.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Sprinkler (DeviceKind.Sprinkler 9, auto-added like the fan by EnsureDefaultPotions, Utilities card; needs Water unlocked, 40 Water pixels, durationSeconds 45, radius 4.5, Device.sprayInterval 1): BuildModel in this file (base, post, spinning 'Spinner' head with 'Nozzle 0/1'), droplet jets (PixelFx.Make per nozzle) and every sprayInterval s PixelClicker.WaterSplashAt(point, reachOverride) - the same instant growth jump a splashing Water pixel gives every sprout / planted Seed pet in reach (each target still accepts water only every seedWaterCooldown); placed like the vacuum (starts disarmed, click to switch on), robot-placeable (IsPlaceableKind), tip device_Sprinkler, not part of the Operation tab yet.
--- PixelStardustMinigame.cs ---
PixelStardustMinigame.cs Stardust minigame (id stardust, passive: Running = the Meteor pixel is unlocked, no pack of its own; added by PixelShop like the others): every meteor pixel that shoots off (PixelClicker.FlyAwayLaunched static event) has starChance of knocking a star down; after fallDelaySeconds a runtime-built five-pointed star (vertex-coloured mesh, Sprites/Default, trail, point light) falls beside the cube onto the old-pixel floor (rigidbody, gravityScale), waits starLifetime s (blinks then shrinks away) and is collected by clicking within clickRadiusPixels of it (+stardustPerStar stardust, rising text). Tracker "Stardust" (saved generically, goal stardustGoal); the Solar Pixel pack (PixelType.Solar, Pixels tab, needs the Meteor Pixel pack + the stardust MinigameGoal) is gated by it. Tip minigame_stardust, sounds stardust_fall/_collect (code-synthesized while their clip lists are empty: PixelSynth.StarFall a falling glassy whistle with tinkling sparkles, StarChime a rising three-note chime; + the automatic stardust_spawn/_click). Magic dust (a world-space PixelFx particle system, gold / lavender sparkles, ~60 per second while the star falls or moves, none once it lies still) trails the star, and a white twinkle (a four-point sprite that pulses every ~2.4 s) shows while it is still (Star.dust / Star.twinkle). Has a Toggles-window switch and dev Spawn/Despawn like every minigame.
--- PixelStatics.cs ---
PixelStatics.cs One registry for "clear this static game state when the scene is reloaded". Statics outlive Settings > Restart (it reloads the scene), so game state kept in a static (a status line, a held penalty, a takeover flag...) carried into the new game - the old 'Oak 3 / 9' tree text came back after a Restart that way. A class with static GAME state registers its reset once, from its [RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod] ResetStatics: PixelStatics.ResetOnRestart(OnRestart); and puts the clearing in that method. Registering the same method twice is ignored. Keep DEV switches (Dev Tools tick boxes) out of the restart reset; they are meant to survive it. References to the new scene's objects are re-found by the new scene's Awake, so clear only state, not something the new scene may already have set.
--- PixelStats.cs ---
PixelStats.cs Play stats. Typed fields (manual/auto clicks, play time, pixels spent, ghosts/meteors clicked, meteors/black holes spawned, fans/vacuum devices placed, singularities fed, potions used per name, highest combo) plus generic counters: any script calls static PixelStats.Count("key", n) / Best("key", v) (keeps the max) / Fastest("key", v) (keeps the min); they are saved in the GameState (StatsState.counters) and need no other wiring. Counters in use: shop.purchases/items, snake/sort/breakout.win/loss, snake.length, sort.fastest, bomb.defused/exploded, ultra.<minigame id> (PixelMinigame.UltraRoll + the Pad), ghost.kept, combo.tier, old.vacuumed/banked/grabbed/shattered, crafts, holes.fed, sessions, session.longest, rate.best (best pixels per minute, sampled each second), timestop/timeslow.seconds/uses. GetLines(number) builds the Stats screen's rows (headings General / Pixels / Clicks per pixel / Husks (+ Recycler cycles and payout) / Block Hold (holds, stakes won / lost - counted when shown - best stake) / Skills (level + hazards set off / cleared per skill: hazards.triggered.<id>, hazards.defused.<id>) / Combo and time / Crafting and items / Devices placed (every DeviceKind) / Rare finds (Dragon x/7, God x/3, Prime x/6) / Pets / Minigames (Ultra sources from PixelMinigame.All); per-pixel-type earned rows; a minigame's rows only once it runs or has data; Ultra earned, stardust, bomb parts and achievements are read from their owners); the pause menu's Stats screen just renders that list (RebuildStatRows, rebuilt only when the row list changes). To add a stat: record it with PixelStats.Count/Best/Fastest and add a row in GetLines. PROGRESS lives in PixelGameState.Current.stats (GameState.cs, moved 2026-10-23): saved / loaded / reset with it; this component keeps only listeners, the per-session numbers (rate samples, session timer) and the Stats screen.
--- PixelStatusBox.cs ---
PixelStatusBox.cs The shared top-of-screen status box: potion timer, device placing line and the takeover minigames' status lines (Blink, Breakout, Dash, Evade, Snake, Sorting, Traffic, Weak Spots, Ultra Holes). PixelStatusBox.Create(font, canvasTransform, fontSize) returns the TMP label (use it as before: PixelUIKit.SetText, .color); the label sits in a rounded window-style box that SLIDES OUT FROM UNDER THE BLACK BAR (a RectMask2D strip starts at the bar's inner edge, so the box is clipped until it has slid below it and is drawn OVER the bar's edge effect). It slides back under the bar when the text is empty or Show(label, false). Create(..., stackBelow: true) hangs the box under the other boxes (PixelStatusLines uses it). Show(label, bool) / IsShown(label). Box size follows the text (boxPadding, maxWidth). A thin accent line along the bottom takes the label's colour. Fixed look values live in the consts at the top (not Inspector fields: the box has no component of its own in the scene). Cheap when idle: the text is measured only when it (or the font size) changes, and the layout is only written when something moved (slide, bar height, the box above, text, colour), so a settled box - hidden or shown - costs a few comparisons a frame.
--- PixelStatusLines.cs ---
PixelStatusLines.cs Keyed text lines for the top status box (PixelStatusBox, hanging under the potion / minigame status boxes): PixelStatusLines.Set(key, text, colour, seconds) shows or replaces the line for that key (seconds > 0 = it goes away by itself, 0 = stays until Clear(key)); Clear(key) removes it. Several lines stack in one box (first line's colour = the accent). The box text is only rebuilt when a line was set, cleared or ran out (version). The lines are cleared when a scene unloads (Restart reloads the scene; old lines must not come back). Used for the Black Hole counter, the Bomb timer / result, the Ghost / Meteor / Evil Tree messages that used to float in the world.
--- PixelStorageCrate.cs ---
PixelStorageCrate.cs (extends PixelPlacedDevice) Storage Crate (DeviceKind.StorageCrate 13, auto-added like the fan, Utilities card; 300 Black + 200 Blue, hold 3, bodyDiameter 2.5 = width, bodyHeight 0.3; model BuildModel (this file): a wooden tray with two rows of five compartments): permanent and never disarmed (no countdown: duration 1e9, timer text hidden; PlaceDevice / RestorePlacedDevice skip Disarm). Drag old pixels over it with Pixel Grabbing and left-click the crate: loose old pixels lying within its footprint (FindLoose, nearest first) are stored in the next free section (row 1 left to right, then row 2; ReleaseOldPixel + FlyIn, their payout is kept, a spinning CreateDisplayPixel model sits in the compartment). Left-click a stored pixel (PickSection, screen distance) to take it out: SpawnStoredPixel at the compartment and PixelGrab.BeginHold puts it in the mouse's hand. A pixel you hold and let go of above the crate is stored at once (any pixel tagged 'recently held by the player' - OldPixelInfo.RecentlyHeld, PixelGrab.recentlyHeldSeconds - that ends up in the footprint is stored too, from ANY height: CaptureRecentlyHeld) (TryStoreReleased: PixelGrab.HeldBody was a pixel last frame and is not now, and it is inside the footprint and below ~3 units above it). Two grip handles on the short (+/-X) ends (the shared PixelCarryHandles: parts by PixelCarryHandles.BuildParts from BuildModel, behaviour by the component): hovering one shows the open-hand cursor (PixelHandCursor: code-drawn open hand / fist textures set with Cursor.SetCursor, requested per frame, normal cursor returns when nobody asks), left-drag moves the crate over the floor plane (DragTo, kept on screen) with the fist cursor, and while dragging scroll / Q / E turn it (PixelConsumables.TurnInput; also when placing it; the yaw is saved in PlacedState). PixelStorageCrate.ClaimsPointer() makes PixelGrab step aside when the click belongs to the crate; PixelPointerClaim blocks cube clicks. Removing it (Ended) lets everything out. Saved in PlacedState.extra ('S,slot,tier,amount;'). Tip device_StorageCrate. DEVICE CARD: AddInfo (stored n / 10; the panel lists each section). SOCKETS (PixelSockets.cs): two on the front - 'in' (left, Intake): a coupling there takes belts / hoses in (AcceptHusk); 'out' (right, Output): a coupling there lets a Container Hose pull stored husks (TakeHuskOut / HuskRoom), and a BELT starting at it is fed one stored husk at a time while it has room (FeedBelt, every beltOutSeconds) - so a belt snapped to the out coupling really is fed. Which husks it may take, the fly-in and taking one out into the hand are the shared PixelHuskHolder steps.
--- PixelStudioIntro.cs ---
PixelStudioIntro.cs The GALACTIC ARCADE studio intro (design agreed in the Design Discussion chat). Plays ONCE per launch, before the title screen's launch intro (PixelTitleIntro.IntroRoutine starts with `yield return PixelStudioIntro.PlayAtLaunch()`), ~11 s; any key / click / Esc skips to the fade. Settings > Display > 'Studio intro' turns it off (PixelPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.StudioIntro, on this machine). Dev Hub > Events > 'Studio intro' replays it (PixelStudioIntro.Replay) over whatever is on screen. Everything is built in code when it starts and DESTROYED when it ends (objects, meshes, textures, materials, render textures: the 'owned' list): * Its own little world far below the game (worldOffset), filmed by its own two cameras into RenderTextures, shown on its own top canvas, so it never touches the game scene. The SPACE camera films Earth + the galaxy into a 16:9 texture that is the ARCADE CABINET's screen; the CABINET camera starts framed exactly on that screen (so it looks like plain space) and pulls back for the reveal. The canvas shows the cabinet camera's picture. STORYBOARD (all lengths Inspector values): 1 Earth close-up: a turning sphere with a code-drawn texture (oceans, continents, ice caps), a cloud shell, a night side (a soft shadow disc in front), a blue atmosphere glow, a cratered Moon passing in front, a sun glare and a shooting star; a starfield and coloured nebulae behind. 2 a fast exponential pull-back (studio_whoosh): Earth shrinks to a dot and a spiral galaxy fills the view (galaxyStars points in one mesh, pink star-forming clumps on the arms, a glowing core), slowly turning, over the nebulae and the far starfield. 3 'GAME OVER' blinks over it with an RGB split, a score line, a blinking 'INSERT COIN', scanlines, a CRT vignette and glass glare; the screen's light flickers with it (studio_gameover jingle). 4 the cabinet camera pulls back: the galaxy was on the screen of an arcade cabinet floating in space (its own starfield, nebulae and tumbling pixel cubes; studio_hum loop): side art, neon bezel frame, speaker grilles, a marquee reading studioName ringed by chasing bulbs, a two-player control panel, a coin door with two lit slots, a kick plate. A little green cube ALIEN stands at it, slumped (head down, antennae drooping). 5 he perks up, turns round to the camera, winks and smiles, and turns back (alienSeconds); 6 he drops a spinning coin in the slot (studio_coin, the slot flashes, 'CREDIT 01' + 'PRESS START'), presses 1P START (studio_start, the screen flashes white, 'GET READY') and hops for joy (coinSeconds); 7 the camera rises to the marquee while the bulbs chase fast and 'presents' fades in (presentsSeconds); then fade to black and the title intro. All music waits while it plays (PixelAudio checks HoldsMusic: playing, or still due this launch with a 12 s time-out; the title screen calls SkipThisLaunch when its launch intro won't run). At launch it first waits on black until frames come smoothly (maxSettleTime), and the timeline moves at most maxFrameStep per frame, so loading hitches slow it down instead of skipping scenes. The space set is built small and scaled up (SpaceScale) to keep float precision far from the origin. No per-frame allocations (everything is cached when it is built; texts only change at fixed moments; the timeline runs in one coroutine on unscaled time).
--- PixelStudioSynth.cs ---
PixelStudioSynth.cs Code-made sounds of the Galactic Arcade studio intro (PixelAudio.SynthFor -> PixelStudioSynth.For; put your own clips in the Sounds list to replace them): studio_whoosh (the fast pull-back from Earth: rising filtered noise and a falling tone), studio_gameover (an 8-bit square-wave 'game over' jingle: four falling notes and a low buzz), studio_hum (a soft seamless arcade-cabinet hum loop, PixelAudio.StartLoop / StopLoop), studio_coin (the classic two-note coin-in ding) and studio_start (a quick rising 8-bit arpeggio when START is pressed).
--- PixelSwarmKit.cs ---
PixelSwarmKit.cs ONE place for the building bits of the code-built critter swarms (the Pest's bugs PixelPestSwarm, the Termite Hive's termites PixelTermiteSwarm; 2026-10-24: was a copied block in each): - GroundY(clicker, at): the floor under a point (the lowest surface without a rigid body - the cube and husks are higher). - Material(clicker, colour): a plain lit material (the clicker's visual material, else a sprite / Standard one). - Part(parent, name, localPos, localScale, unit, mat, euler): one colliderless, shadowless cube part, sized in 'unit's.
--- PixelSweeper.cs ---
PixelSweeper.cs THE SWEEPER (PixelConsumables DeviceKind.Sweeper 25, Utilities; design: docs/GameDesign.md section 6 'The Sweeper'): an early stray-husk helper, NOT tied to Electrician. Built from the old Tiny Robot (the tester's belt-feeding hover bot, same fetch loop) into a little wind-up CLOCKWORK CUBE with a broom: it rolls out from its home pad, fetches husks that are CLOSE TO FADING (it sets off once RemainingReal <= fadingSeconds + headStartSeconds + the trip there; the most urgent one within rangeCubes of home first), carries them on its head (kept alive on the way) and drops them into the NEAREST container with room that is an IPixelStrayTarget (Storage Crate, Recycler - nearest to where the husk was picked up), through the container's one husk hook (IPixelSocketDevice.AcceptHusk; room = IPixelHuskRoom). So the player's placement decides where strays go: a crate nearest = stored safe, a Recycler nearest = straight to recycling. It MOVES husks, never processes them. After a drop it pauses pauseAfterDrop (0.35 s), then goes straight to the next stray; with none it rolls back to its pad (it looks every searchSeconds). Refused (full) or no container: it puts the husk down where it stands. Husks held by the player, kinematic (riding / kept by a machine), flying, attached or high above the floor are left alone; one the player grabs from it is let go. WIND-UP: it runs runSeconds (60) after a wind, slowing to a stop over the last slowDownSeconds (the key on its back turns slower too); stopped, it shows 'click to wind' and a LEFT CLICK on it winds it again (PixelPointerClaim.ClaimButton: the cube and Pixel Grabbing leave that click alone). motorFitted is the hook for the planned Electrician MOTOR (fitted through a future Power socket): with it on, it never runs down. The placed device is the permanent PixelPermanentDevice stub (bought, kept, placed, saved, removed - hold the right button on it - like every device). Unlock: vendorOnly + shopSkillId 'standard' / level 5 (on sale in the shop from Standard 5, pure currency). Saved: the wind left (PlacedState.extra 'W,seconds'). No carry handles: it roams away from its home pad, so it is moved by picking it up (removing) and placing it again. Stats sweeper.rescued. DEVICE CARD (PixelDeviceCard, FillDeviceInfo): wind left as the bar, what it is doing; the panel adds range and this session's rescues.
--- PixelSystems.cs ---
PixelSystems.cs ONE list of the game's system components (Awake used to add them one line at a time). Each entry = a component type + the group it belongs to (UI, Core, Skills, Rares and Shows, Characters, Camera, World, Shop, Minigames), in the order they must start. - The Shop and Minigames groups (the shop's features and every built-in minigame / visitor) are added by PixelShop.Awake itself (AddMissing with ShopGroups: on the shop's object, before it sets up its packs) and in edit mode by its OnValidate; PixelClicker.Awake's AddMissing covers them too if the shop has not woken yet. - At run time PixelClicker.Awake calls AddMissing: a system that is NOT in the scene yet is added to the clicker object (as before), and in the Editor a warning names them, so they can be put in the scene for editing. - In the Editor, the menu 'Pixel Clicker > Add Missing Systems To Scene' (Editor/PixelSystemsMenu.cs) puts every missing system on a tidy 'Pixel Systems' object with one child per group, so their Inspector values can be edited and SAVED in edit mode (a component only added in Play mode loses every change when Play stops). 'Pixel Clicker > Move Systems Off The Clicker' moves the ones sitting on the clicker object into those groups, values included. A new system goes in this list (with its group); every system script must sit in a file of the same name and find PixelClicker with PixelFind (not only GetComponent), so it works on any object.
--- PixelTalk.cs ---
PixelTalk.cs Talking noises for dialogue boxes: while a box types its text out (PixelNotice tip / tutorial boxes, Cubie's speech panel) PixelTalk.Typed plays a short code-made blip (sound id talk_blip, six voiced squeaks from PixelSynth.TalkBlip) for every second letter, its pitch following the letter (vowels a little higher) so it mumbles like speech without being words. Both boxes have a small speaker button next to their back button (PixelTalk.AddMuteButton) that mutes / unmutes the blips (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TalkMuted, kept per save profile by PixelSettingsSync). The speaker icon is a pixel-art sprite drawn from text rows (SpeakerSprite).
--- PixelTermiteSwarm.cs ---
PixelTermiteSwarm.cs The Termite Hive's termites (Woodcutting's hazard, PixelType.HornetNest keeps its old enum name so saves stay valid). Every non-final hit on the hive spills a few termites that run off (Scatter); when it GOES OFF (PixelClicker.TriggerHazard) Release sends one termite for EVERY old pixel lying about (up to PixelClicker.TermiteMax, 0 = 60): they spill out of the cube, scuttle over the floor to their pixel and start eating it - a small bar over the pixel counts down (PixelClicker.TermiteEatSeconds, 0 = 5). CLICK the old pixel (or grab it) before the bar runs out and the termite gives up and runs off; if the bar empties the pixel is eaten: removed with no payout. A pixel that vanishes some other way (despawn, vacuum, collected) just frees its termite. Cosmetic code-built termites (body, head, legs, feelers; at most 80 at once), nothing is saved. Tuning on PixelClicker: termiteSpeed (0 = 3.8 cube widths / s), termiteEatSeconds, termiteMax, disableTermites (the hive then just costs the usual XP penalty).
--- PixelTextFile.cs ---
PixelTextFile.cs ONE reader / writer for the hand-editable text files in Assets/Resources (sections of 'field: value' lines). Used by PixelDialogue (Dialogue.txt) and PixelTextOverrides (PixelTexts.txt); PixelShopText (ShopText.txt) still has its own copy of the parser - move it here when it is next touched. FORMAT: '#' lines are notes. '[Section]' starts a block; then 'field: value' lines (one line each; '\n' = a new line in the text). A field that is missing or left EMPTY keeps the game's own text. A section may appear twice (the fields are merged). USE: Text(section, field, current) returns the file's text or 'current'; Apply(section, field, ref value) / ApplyList(...) copy the file's text over a field (the 'file wins' model: call it once where the defaults are set, the display code stays as it is). LISTS (tutorial steps, Cubie's pages): fields '<prefix> 1', '<prefix> 2'...; a higher number adds an entry, '-' removes one. IN THE EDITOR every Text / Apply that finds its field missing queues it with the game's current text, and Flush() appends the queued fields to the end of the file (creating it with its header if needed): the file fills itself in on the first play and keeps up with new texts; nothing already written there is changed. Reload() reads the file again (edits saved while playing).
--- PixelTextOverrides.cs ---
PixelTextOverrides.cs The player-facing texts of every pixel type in ONE text file the user edits himself: Assets/Resources/PixelTexts.txt (built 2026-10-18 from the code's names, PixelBlockCatalog's guide texts and husk texts). One section per pixel type, keyed by its PixelType name: [Sap] name: Sap Pixels guide: Woodcutting block: a blob of sticky sap... (the hover card / Pixel Guide explanation) husk: ... (what its husk does; the 'Husk:' line) A key that is missing or left empty keeps the game's own text. '\n' in a text = a new line; rich text (<b>, <color=...>) works. Placeholders filled with the tier's live numbers: {clicks} {clicksMin} {clicksMax} {payMin} {payMax}; Bait: {pellets} {percent} {seconds}. Hooks: PixelGuideTexts.Mechanics (guide), PixelGuideTexts.OldPixelOwn (husk), PixelClicker.Awake + EnsureTier (name, ApplyName). Read through the shared PixelTextFile (Resources 'PixelTexts'); Reload() reads it again (Dev Hub > Events > 'Reload pixel texts', which also renames the live tiers). IN THE EDITOR a pixel type missing from the file is appended to it at start (AppendMissing) with the game's current texts, so the file always lists every type (new ones included); nothing already in the file is touched.
--- PixelTierPicture.cs ---
PixelTierPicture.cs Shared 'real model' pictures of pixel types: PixelTierPicture.Get(clicker, tier) returns a RenderTexture of the block drawn with its REAL look (PixelClicker.CreateDisplayPixel in a hidden studio far below the scene with its own camera and light), PixelTierPicture.Render(tier, yaw) turns it and re-renders it (call it only for pictures that are on screen). Rebuild(clicker, tier) builds the model again after its look changed (Look Studio); asking for a bigger resolution resizes the same texture in place. Users: Next up, the block tooltip, Collection Log, Database, skill level popups, Look Studio, the Log's achievement / goal pictures and the Pixel Guide. One studio per pixel type is SHARED by all of them (two windows showing the same type at once show the same turn). Other models (potions, devices, minigame shapes, the shop's pictures) use PixelModelPicture; full-screen shows that move their model every frame (Dragon / God Find, Egg Hatch) use PixelShowStudio. Don't add another private studio. Redraw limit: Render redraws a picture at most MaxFps times a second (and once per frame when two pictures show the same block). Each Render is a full camera pass, so redrawing every frame cost one extra pass per picture on screen. PixelModelPicture uses the same limit through Due(ref lastDrawn). Rebuild makes the next Render draw at once.
--- PixelTimeControl.cs ---
PixelTimeControl.cs Dev tool behind the Dev Hub's 'Time control' widget (PixelDevTools): Pause, Play (normal 1x), Slow motion, Fast forward and Rewind. - Slow / Fast set Time.timeScale to Speed (Time.fixedDeltaTime follows below 1x so slow physics stays smooth, like Time Slow does). Pressing the same button again steps through its presets (slowPresets / fastPresets / rewindPresets). - While Slow, Fast or Rewind is on, the plain mouse wheel (not over UI, not with Ctrl, not in God mode) raises / lowers Speed by speedStep (0.1x; fastWheelStep 0.5x from 2x up), between minSpeed and maxSpeed (0.1x - 10x). The mode follows the speed: Slow below 1x, Fast above it. - REWIND: the husks' positions are recorded every recordInterval seconds of game time (up to recordSeconds, a ring of snapshots). Rewind freezes the game (timeScale 0) and walks the husks back through the snapshots at Speed x real time; Pause / Play / Slow / Fast stop it there, drop the history after that point and give the husks their recorded velocity back. ONLY husks rewind: currency, timers, minigames and devices stay as they are. A husk reused by the pool (PixelHuskPool.HuskReleasing) is skipped for the snapshots taken before it was reused. - Other systems still set timeScale (menus, Time Stop, notices): Slow / Fast are put back whenever time runs at another speed outside a menu, Pause holds 0 outside menus. The title screen turns the tool off. A small label at the top of the screen shows the mode while it is on. Only works in the editor / development builds (allowInFullBuild), like the other Dev Hub tools.
--- PixelTimeStop.cs ---
PixelTimeStop.cs Time Stop (shop pack with unlocksTimeStop, Upgrades tab): press T to set Time.timeScale = 0 (everything incl. countdowns) and again to resume. Has an energy meter (maxStopSeconds, refillSeconds, minStartFraction, hideDelaySeconds): drains (unscaled time) only while stopped, auto-resumes at 0, refills immediately after resuming, shown at the top only while stopped / refilling / briefly after full. Static PixelTimeStop.IsStopped: PixelClicker still takes cube clicks while it is true, animates the cube with AnimDelta (unscaled), and uses timeStopStockpileMax instead of maxFallingCopies, so spawned old pixels hang at the cube (no physics step) and burst out on resume (payouts are immediate). Switch in the Toggles window (UserDisabled: progress in PixelGameState.Current.timeStop, GameState.cs, moved 2026-10-23). A screen haze (radial tint + vignette, showHaze, hazeColor/hazeEdgeColor, fades in/out, canvas below other UI) shows while stopped. Sounds time_stop/time_resume/time_denied plus the looping hum time_stop_loop (loopSoundId; started/stopped via PixelAudio.StartLoop/StopLoop). PixelShop finds/adds it. Time Slow (shop pack unlocksTimeSlow, Upgrades tab, needs Time Stop; ActivateSlow/DeactivateSlow): press S (PixelAction.TimeSlow) to set Time.timeScale = slowTimeScale (0.15; Time.fixedDeltaTime scaled too) using the same energy meter at slowDrainFactor of the stop drain, lighter haze (slowHazeStrength), sounds time_slow/_end/_loop, tips timeslow / timestop_slow. Static PixelTimeStop.IsSlowed / SlowFactor / EndAll() (called before returning to the main menu); PixelClicker.AnimDelta is unscaled while slowed so the cube still feels normal.
--- PixelTipQueue.cs ---
PixelTipQueue.cs ONE queue for everything that pops up to teach or tell the player (UI unification step 6): the guided tutorials (shop, skills, Toggles), the perk popups (PixelSkills), the first-time tips (PixelHints) and the "Did you know?" box (PixelDidYouKnow). Each source registers once (Register(owner, priority, name, ready, show)) and keeps its OWN queue and conditions ('ready': e.g. the first-time minigame tips may show during a minigame, the tutorials want PixelCalm.ForTip). Every frame the queue picks the highest priority source that is ready - only while nothing else is up (Busy: a PixelNotice box or a tutorial dim) and after a short gap (gapSeconds) - and calls its 'show'. So two sources ready in the same frame take turns instead of one box replacing the other. Adding a new kind of tip = one Register call. Priority: Tutorial > PerkPopup > FirstTimeTip > DidYouKnow.
--- PixelTitleCubeShow.cs ---
PixelTitleCubeShow.cs Part of partial class PixelTitleScreen: while the main menu shows, the cube behind it changes to a random pixel type ON THE MUSIC'S BEAT (cubeShowOnMusicBeat: PixelAudio.TryGetMusicBeat, the song's tempo from PixelAudio's Music Tempos list, 90 bpm when not listed; every cubeShowBeatsPerChange beats); with no music playing it keeps its own cubeShowBpm clock (90 bpm) - every type in the game (locked ones too; retired ores left out) - through PixelClicker.ShowLookPreview (look only: the game does not change). When Play is pressed (after a Continue has loaded the save) the menu cube POPS OFF like a husk - a snapshot of its mesh / materials thrown out sideways and up that spins and shrinks away within huskPopSeconds (0.6 s), on unscaled time, no physics, never a real husk - and the player's own block materializes in its place (EndLookPreview); then the usual camera intro plays. cubeShow off = the menu shows the real block as before.
--- PixelTitleIntro.cs ---
PixelTitleIntro.cs Part of partial class PixelTitleScreen: the main menu's INTRO when the game launches (Open(false), not when coming back from the game, not when a New game auto-starts). The screen starts fully black; once the blurred picture behind is ready (and introHoldSeconds more) the black FOLDS DOWN from the top and bottom into the middle bar (introCollapseSeconds, easing in and out, its colour turning into the bar's), the bar gives a small thump, then everything else comes in: the bar's glow fades up, the logo drops in from above, the Play / Load / Settings / Quit buttons and the other widgets pop in one after another with a little overshoot (introStagger apart), the rest of the foreground fades in. Buttons work once it is done. Any key or click skips straight to the end. playIntro off = the old instant screen.
--- PixelTitleMusic.cs ---
PixelTitleMusic.cs (The list always shows what is audible now: a cue's clip that is not one of the tracks is named in the caption: PixelAudio.PlayingClip.) The main menu's background music selector (created by PixelTitleScreen.Build under its foreground, showMusicPicker): a small bottom-centre box with a MUSIC caption, previous / next arrows and a drop-down listing every track (PixelAudio.MusicTrackCount / MusicTrackName / CurrentMusicTrack / SelectMusicTrack; the track names are the clips' names). It is SHOWN when the title screen opens and fades away after hideSeconds (musicPickerHideSeconds, 0 = 5) without activity (the mouse over it, a click, the list open); it fades back in while the mouse is over the spot. SHARED by the pause menu (PixelPauseMenu.BuildUI: same bottom-centre spot, no caption, hidden after pauseMusicHideSeconds 0 = 1.5 s and fading out in 0.15 s instead of 0.8 s; open = paused and not on the title screen): Create(parent, font, hideSeconds, showCaption, fadeOutSeconds, isOpen) - it shows when its screen opens (isOpen turns true) and while hovered. A SHUFFLE button right of the speaker toggles PixelAudio.MusicShuffle (per save file; lit = on, dim = in order). A speaker button at its right end mutes / unmutes the music (right click opens Settings > Audio directly: PixelPauseMenu.OpenAudioSettingsFromTitle) (PixelAudio.MusicMuted, per save file, also a Settings > Audio tick box); the playlist is per save too. Starts the chosen track at once (not remembered: every launch starts on a random song); the usual shuffle / order carries on after it. Hidden entirely when there are no tracks.
--- PixelToadstoolDaze.cs ---
PixelToadstoolDaze.cs The Toadstool's spores (Foraging hazard, set off by one left click): a temporary status effect. For 'seconds' (12) every one of the player's own clicks on the cube makes the cube JERK AWAY from the mouse cursor (a jump of jumpSize cube widths straight away from the pointer on the screen, kept within maxDistance of home), then it drifts slowly back towards its home spot. Uses PixelClicker.SetCubeOffset (like Cube Dash) and gives way to a takeover minigame. A top status line counts the time down. Component created on demand (PixelToadstoolDaze.Begin from TriggerHazard).
--- PixelTogglesTutorial.cs ---
PixelTogglesTutorial.cs Toggles tutorial (added by PixelToggles.Start; part of the "Show first-time tips" intro together with the skills tutorial): when the Auto Clicker is bought with tips on, PixelShop.ApplyPurchase calls PixelTogglesTutorial.Request() (stat guide.togglesDue, saved) INSTEAD OF the plain hint box beside the Toggles tab (with tips off the old hint box still shows). At the next calm moment (title screen over, no Cubie visit, no other tip / window / tutorial, shop closed, not paused, startDelay 2 s) the screen dims and pauses (PixelTutorialDim) and Cubie narrates like the shop tutorial (BeginNarration, stick at the part each step describes via PixelGuideVendor.PointOverride, small Got it boxes that follow the Toggles window - PixelNotice nearScreen): the Toggles tab, the Upgrades group (where the Auto Clicker is ticked on), the other groups. (The old last step, a hint at skills and the Lumberjack's Pack, was removed: the Skills tab now appears with Standard's level-2 popup.) Steps are an Inspector list (empty = the built-ins), run by the shared PixelTutorialRunner. Done = stat guide.togglesTutorial (Settings > Replay tutorial clears it and, when the Toggles tab is there, asks for it again). Skipped entirely when TipsEnabled is off.
--- PixelTrafficMinigame.cs ---
PixelTrafficMinigame.cs / PixelTrafficSynth.cs Red Light Green Light minigame (id traffic, pack unlocksMinigame = "traffic", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack, 80K each; like Cube Dash: Takeover => true, cube stays clickable, AutoClickerPaused meanwhile): every minInterval..maxInterval s a round of roundSeconds (30) starts. A code-built traffic light (dark housing, red / yellow / green lamps with halos, unlit Sprites/Default materials, billboarded to the camera each frame, lightOffsetCubes 1.9 cube sizes to the right of the cube) cycles Green (greenMin..Max 2-5 s) -> Yellow (3-5 s) -> Red (1.5-3 s) -> Green...; Judge() scores every manual click (PixelCollected / PixelHit with automatic false): green +1, red -1; yellow: a click within hitWindow (0.22 s) of the moment the closing ring meets the cube is +1 (once per beat, lastGoodBeat), any other click -1. During yellow a LineRenderer circle closes round the cube once every beatSeconds (0.9, starts ringStartScale 3.2 cube sizes out, brightens / turns white as it nears; a tick sounds on the beat). The status line is coloured like the light and flashes white on a mistake. At the end it pays rewardMultiplier (10) x the pixel's worth x max(0, points) via AddCurrency (the clicks still pay normally); stats traffic.rounds / traffic.best, tip minigame_traffic; a round with points > 0 reports Clicked (bonus skill XP 20). Sounds are code-made (PixelTrafficSynth, filled in by PixelAudio.SynthFor while the clip lists are empty): traffic_start/_green/_yellow/_red/_tick/_good/_bad/_win/_lose.
--- PixelTrashCan.cs ---
PixelTrashCan.cs TRASH CAN - A DEV TOOL ONLY (2026-10-20 decision: never sold; it clears test setups): a Dev Hub > Tester feature (PixelFeatureTester 'Trash Can'): a plain cylinder you put on the floor. Within suctionRadiusCubes (default 2.5 cube widths) of it every loose old pixel - things thrown out of a Funnel hose, a machine or the end of a conveyor belt, or simply lying there - is pulled towards its mouth and, once it gets there, shrinks away and is DELETED (PixelClicker.ClaimOldPixel, no credit). SOCKETS (PixelSockets.cs): 'front' and 'back' (In): a belt, connector or hose ending at an In coupling there hands its husks straight in (AcceptHusk). Pixels the player holds, ones riding a belt / carried by a machine (kinematic), flying pixels and attached ones are left alone. Saved with the tester save (position).
--- PixelTreasure.cs ---
PixelTreasure.cs Treasure pixel (PixelType.Treasure 32, IsStandalone, spawn switch in Toggles; pack 'Treasure Pixel': 50K White + 25K Black, spawn weight 0.1, clicksToCollect 10 = the average for estimates): a tough pixel with a RANDOM click count - PixelTier.randomClicks / randomClicksMin..Max (1-20), rolled per fresh pixel in Materialize (liveNeed, NeededClicks(tier) replaces tier.clicksToCollect in CollectInternal, SetLiveDamage and the live texture). The hit popup shows only the hit number (PixelHit gets needed = -1; the final hit shows n/n). Payout = amountPerClick x need x (1 + need x treasureRewardGrowth (0 = 0.15)) (TreasureMultiplier). Opening (PixelClicker.OpenTreasure): TreasureFx.Open (coins, glitter, splinters, gold ring + flash, sound chest_open) and a pile of treasureBasePixels (2) + need (max treasureMaxPixels 24) pixels of random unlocked types (not rare drops / fly-aways / Singularity / Treasure) is PAID at once (RollOldPixelAmount x (1 + need/10), one AddCurrency per type) while the same number of old pixels fountain out for show with amount 0 (TreasureFountain); stats treasure.opened, treasure.most_clicks; disableTreasureFx. Each non- final hit: TreasureFx.Hit (splinters + sparks, chest_hit). Look: PixelLook.chestTexture + damageCracks (PixelLooks.ChestTexture(step 0-8): 64 px planks, gold straps / frame / lock plate / rivets, a lid seam that leaks more light per step; looksVersion 26).
--- PixelTreeMode.cs ---
PixelTreeMode.cs TREE MODE (step 1 of docs/systems/WoodcuttingProgression.md; tested in Unity 2026-10-16 - it plays well, timings / numbers still being tuned; component added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelTreeMode.Instance). While the skill named in skillId (woodcutting) is in play (since 2026-10-16 always - it is how Woodcutting plays; requireTestPack on = only while the hidden 'Oak Trees (test)' pack with unlocksTreeMode is owned, the old test switch), the cube stops rolling the pack's blocks by weight and alternates between: - the FILLER rotation: random picks from 'fillers' (Twig, Leaf, Wood, Bark; Moss and Sap from level 2; Bark added 2026-10-24 - it had no source). At least a minimum of them, then a growing chance per block until a tree is certain at a maximum. The run SHRINKS with the level: 12-30 at level 1 (minFillerAtLevel1 / maxFillerAtLevel1) down to 3-10 at fillerLevelSpan (20) and above (minFillerAtTop / maxFillerAtTop), so trees are a treat early on; - a TREE: a species picked by weight among the unlocked ones (Oak only for now) plays out its slot list in order (Fixed / Run / Wildcard / Optional / Hazard). An INFESTED tree fills its Hazard slot (the Termite Hive, always last): chance infestedChance at infestFromLevel rising to infestedChanceAt20 at level 20, 0 while a Focus potion holds hazards off; it is announced on the tree bar from its first block. PixelClicker.PickSpawnTier asks TryPick first (tree picks skip the filler family swap and the special-block replacement).
