7. Collectibles and meta systems

| System | Connects to | State |
|---|---|---|
| Dragon Cubes (wish), God Cubes, Prime Cubes + Gauntlet | Collection Log, gallery, Wizard | Built |
| Pets | pixel types (a pet per type), Pet Treats | Built |
| Crafting | potions, attuned devices | Built |
| Pixel Bank | husk storage for later use (e.g. Electric husks before Electrician), one shared capacity; stored husks carry no payout (Designed) | Built; storage-only rework Designed |
| Achievements, Collection Log | everything (goals) | Built |
| Combo meter | clicking | Built |
| Time Stop | clicking, devices | Built |

8. Open gaps to decide (from the tables above)

1. Foraging: what does it give other skills, and what does it need? (e.g. Herbs -> potions, Mushrooms -> Farming fertilizer)
2. Fishing: an outgoing link (e.g. Water -> Farming, Pearls -> crafting).
3. Mining: an incoming link (e.g. Electrician powers a drill; Charcoal fuels a smelter). Its metal husks as machine repair material (Designed) give it an outgoing link.
4. Electrician as the factory skill: confirm it is the home of belts, splitters, elevators, hoses, couplings, sorters and the Sweeper's motor.
5. Dead-end outputs: Magic Wood needs a use (Charcoal and Wood Chips: Biomass fuel + the Lumberjack, 2026-10-20).
6. Minigames: give each one a skill it feeds.
7. Pixel rules rework: step 1 built (husks stop paying; Vacuum gives husk XP, Solar keeps daylight up, Bank is storage). Open: should the husk minigames (Snake, Sorting Race, Breakout) keep paying currency per husk?
8. Shop relics: most shop pixels and devices are left over from early development. Decide which consumables stay shop-only from the start and which move to unlocks later (and from which skill).
9. Breakdowns: which machines can break, which inputs break each one, whether wear exists, and which husks repair which machines.

The pruning test

A skill, block, machine or feature earns its place by passing at least two of these:

1. Unique action: it asks the player to do or decide something new.
2. Connected: it gives to, or needs from, at least two other things on this map.
3. Pressure: it creates or answers one of the pressures in section 4.

Anything that fails two of the three: merge it into something else, or cut it.
--- docs/systems/ConveyorAudit.md ---
Conveyor belt audit (2026-10-20)

Two read-only passes: one on the code (belts, riders, couplings, the tester factory and every piece that feeds or takes from a belt), one on the player and designer side (placing, feedback, purpose, feel). The two strongest bug claims (B1, B3) were checked again by hand. Builds on DeviceConsumableAudit.md and doesn't repeat it. Nothing changed yet. Effort: S < 1 h, M = a few hours, L = a day.

Also done 2026-10-20 (belt core): B3, B4, B9, B10, the Elevator's room check, the belt panel Status line + spilling-end marker, the green / red end disc while placing, and undo while placing (right click takes the last piece of the chain back).

Done 2026-10-20 (pieces + joins): B1 (sorter spot from the belt's current start), B2 (sorter / combiner refunded when their belt goes, the sorter's filter block handed back, tester list tidied), B5 (sorter catches the whole stretch crossed per step), B6 (one LinkReach 1.3 widths, nearest belt wins), B7 (Emit never fills a full belt; Kiln / Wood Chipper hold their output, Belt Jump queues on its landing lip, the Belt Sorter lets the husk ride on when its chute belt is full), B8 (Belt Jump locks the husk before HandIn), B11 (the Storage Crate feeds a belt on its out coupling), polish 1 (placing hints shown in the HUD status box when placing from the shop), polish 5 (stale docs). B3, B4, B9, B10 and polish 2-4 are the belt-core half.

Already good:
- Riders never fade. Husks on a belt never despawn.
- Corners and joins: corners and shrouds close the gaps, and snapping is generous.
- Belt panel: it shows the section, speed and size, Reverse and Remove.
- Most outputs wait when the belt is full: the Funnel, Junction Box and Combiner hold back.
- One way to connect: couplings are a clean idea.

1. Bugs (confirmed by reading the code)

| # | Where | Bug | Fix | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | PixelBeltSorter.cs:218 | The sorter is placed by along, measured from the belt's start when it was placed. Reversing the belt teleports it to the mirror spot, and sorting / joints then use the wrong spot. (The Combiner already does it right: belt.Along(transform.position).) | Same as the Combiner. | S |
| B2 | PixelBeltSorter.cs:214, PixelConveyorCombiner.cs:158 | Removing their belt silently destroys them: the sorter's filter block is lost, nothing is refunded, and dead entries stay in the tester's list. | Hand back / refund; tidy the list. | S |
| B3 | PixelFactoryKit (materials cached per kit) + belt Rebuild (PixelConveyor.cs:402) | A new kit is made on every rebuild and DestroyMaterials is never called. While placing, the preview rebuilds on every mouse move, about 8 new materials a frame, never freed. Same in highlights, shrouds, elevator troughs, corner joints (their Mesh is never destroyed either) and each lever's lamp. | One static material cache keyed by look; destroy the joint meshes. | S |
| B4 | PixelConveyor.ImportState (:773) | A reloaded reversed belt shows the wrong arrow colour (no PaintArrows). | Call it. | S |
| B5 | PixelBeltSorter.TryDivert (:357) | Diverts only within 0.2 cube of the sorter, but belts run up to 30 cubes/s (0.6 cube per physics step), so fast belts skip it. | Test the stretch crossed this step. | S |
| B6 | PixelSockets.cs:212-232 BeltEndingAt / BeltStartingAt | Reach 1.3 cubes and the FIRST match, while IntakeAt / HandIn use 1.0 and FindNext 0.45. A grid-snapped end 1.0-1.3 cubes off a Junction Box shows IN, but the belt throws the husk off. | Nearest match, one shared reach. | S-M |
| B7 | Emit / PutOnBelt | Don't check room themselves. The Elevator's PutOut, Belt Jump Land, Sorter Eject, Kiln (:307) and Wood Chipper (:346) put husks onto full belts, where they pile up or fall off. | Check room inside Emit (one place). | S |
| B8 | PixelBeltJump.Land -> HandIn | Hands in a husk whose physics is already on, while HandIn expects a locked (kinematic) one; the Elevator and Hose lock it first. | Lock it first. | S |
| B9 | Belt ends at a machine (PixelConveyorSystem.FixedUpdate) | When the machine refuses (Recycler busy / full / gone, Crate full), the belt throws the husk on the floor instead of waiting. This is the biggest "why are my husks on the floor" moment. | At an In coupling, hold the front rider (as the Funnel's OutputHasRoom does); only a bare end spills. | S-M |
| B10 | PixelPathCarrier.carriedAll | Cleared only on a domain reload, never on Restart. | PixelStatics.ResetOnRestart. | S |
| B11 | Storage Crate out coupling | Likely: a belt can snap to it, but the crate never Emits (hose only), so that belt stays empty. | Don't offer that port to belts, or make the crate emit. | S |

2. Player-facing polish

1. Placing hints are invisible from the shop. The live line ("Scroll: raise / lower... Height 2.0 cubes (snapped to an end)") goes to the Dev Hub Tester tab's label, which is closed when placing a Belt Kit / Elevator / Jump / Combiner / Sorter from the shop. Players never learn that scroll raises a belt, or whether an end snapped. Show it on a HUD hint line while placing from the shop. S
2. No reason shown when a line stops or spills. The hose has an idle reason; belts have none. Add one state per section ("end: spilling, no coupling", "Recycler full", "stopped by lever", "next belt at another height") to the belt panel, plus a small marker at a spilling end. S-M
3. Ends that look joined but aren't:
   - FindNext needs ends within 0.45 cube at the same height (±0.45), so head-on pieces or a small height step become spill points;
   - the 120° turn limit only says "pick a gentler angle" on click.

   Fix: tint the ghost's end green / red when it will or won't chain. S
4. Undo and moving:
   - no undo while placing (the hose has right-click to take back the last piece);
   - belts can't be moved, only deleted piece by piece (0.8 s hold each) and laid again.

   Right-click while placing removes the last piece (S). Moving comes with the shared placed-object base (section 4). M
5. Stale docs:
   - PixelFactory.md says "right click reverses";
   - PixelCouplings.md lists the Hopper's removed back socket;
   - the tester header and ConnectionMap.md say belts are "Electrician 5", but they're sold to everyone;
   - the Elevator header says it puts husks out via its top coupling, but it uses its own links (that socket is unused). S

3. Speed

- Capture loop (PixelConveyor.cs:1018-1036): every loose husk x every belt per physics step, about 5 checks + 2 lookups + InverseTransformPoint each. Skip sleeping bodies and keep a world bounds box per belt for a cheap first reject. Eligible is also a private copy of PixelHuskHolder.Takeable. S-M
- Rider gaps are O(n²): RoomAt, RidersOn, StartHasRoom (which also scans every husk on the floor) are linear; the same in PixelPathCarrier.Step. Fix: per-belt / per-lane rider lists sorted by position. Fine at tens of husks, slow at hundreds. M
- Chevrons: about 20 per belt are moved one by one every frame. Moving their parent by Repeat(offset, spacing) would mean only the end ones change. S
- Polling with string building:
  - every 0.5 s each belt rebuilds its joints and shrouds with string keys;
  - the Elevator's RefreshLinks is O(belts²);
  - a lever that's OFF runs CollectSection every 0.15 s (O(L²·B), a new array per belt pair).

  Fix: a world "topology version" number, so this reruns only when something changed. M
- Holding right-click scans everything: the tester's ObjectUnderPointer runs GetComponentsInChildren on every placed piece (~100 renderers per belt) every frame while right is held; BeltUnder does RaycastAll + a sort with a new lambda. M

4. Shared systems worth building

1. A belt link helper (proposed as PixelBeltLink, never built under that name: the one reach and the room check landed in PixelSockets / PixelCouplings instead - LinkReach, Emit):
   - one reach / tolerance for every end check;
   - a cached belt topology (next, line, section, corner partner) rebuilt on a version bump;
   - nearest-match lookups;
   - one Output(device, socket, husk) that always checks room and holds or spills by one rule (fixes B6, B7, B9 and the polling).
2. One carrier core:
   - lane queues sorted by position, despawn keep-alive, let go when grabbed;
   - pool release and save / restore of carried husks;
   - used by belt riders, PixelPathCarrier and the Combiner's queue (today three copies).
3. Static material / mesh cache in PixelFactoryKit, keyed by colour, metal, smoothness, emission and ghost (fixes B3 for every factory piece).
4. One declaration per tester piece + one placed-object base:
   - one entry per piece gives its ghost / place / snap / save / stage (today about 15 hand-written blocks of about 6 delegates in PixelFeatureTester);
   - a shared "mounted on a belt" component for the Sorter / Combiner / Lever (spot from the belt, re-find after a split, refund when lost);
   - every piece goes through PixelRightClick, the device card and the normal removal hold.

   This is the same job as DeviceConsumableAudit.md section 3 item 4, so do them together. It's also what Build mode (to-do 117) needs.

Also hand-wired:
- (Done 2026-10-20: sockets are the only way in; the Elevator, Belt Jump and Combiner have built-in sockets.) Was: Three ways into a machine: coupling AcceptHusk, IPixelAutoIntake.CatchNear (since removed; the Elevator catches any belt end within 3.35 cubes), and the belt-end throw. Nothing in the world shows which one is active.
- Spawn-and-grab: 7 places "spawn a husk then take the last one in the list" because SpawnStoredPixel returns a bool instead of the body.
- Missing from the system list: PixelConveyorSystem isn't in PixelSystems.All (made from 4 places).

5. Design (for Design Discussion / Devices)

- No reason to build a line yet.
  - The kit (250 Gray + 150 Black, about 6 min in, never used up, free length) is sold beside the Coupling, Hose, Junction Box, Sorter, Elevator, Jump and Combiner.
  - What a normal player can feed a belt with: chance drops, the Wood Chipper, or sorter outputs that need a feed themselves. The Funnel, the natural feeder, is dev-only.
  - The only real end is the timed Recycler.

  The planned fixes, in order of impact:
  1. Build mode (to-do 117), with the factory parts gated together behind the first transport;
  2. vendor orders + delivery pad (118), the first reason to route particular husks;
  3. products (119-120), so machine outputs have a use.

  The Workbench (109) is a strong fourth.
- Dead ends:
  - (2026-10-20: Charcoal is now Biomass fuel and the Lumberjack buys it; the Trash Can stays a dev tool.) The Kiln burns any husk (no filter) into Charcoal as a pixel type (against the "machines make products" rule); keep it out of the shop until Charcoal has a use (Biomass fuel fits now);
  - cut the Trash Can;
  - the Control Lever has no job until Signal sockets exist.
- Throughput is free (guess). About 1.45 husks/s per lane at the default speed. The panel allows up to 8 cubes/s at no cost, which removes the one thing to tune. Belt speed could be the Electrician lever, or later run faster while powered.
- Feel:
  - riding is smooth and predictable;
  - the Elevator and Jump are forgiving;
  - a U-turn takes two pieces;
  - (guess) a husk landing on a full spot lies on the belt as a loose body and looks like a jam.
--- docs/systems/CouplerAudit.md ---
Coupler audit (sockets + couplings, 2026-10-20)

A read-only pass on PixelSockets / PixelCoupling / PixelSocketPlates, every device with sockets, the hose and how couplings are bought, placed, saved and shown. It skips what ConveyorAudit.md and DeviceConsumableAudit.md already cover:
- one join reach and the nearest match;
- Emit waiting for room;
- the Storage Crate's out port (fixed by making the crate feed a belt);
- belts spilling at a busy device;
- the material leaks;
- the shared placed-object base.

Effort: S < 1 h, M = a few hours, L = a day.

Done 2026-10-20 (sockets as the only husk path, Devices work): C1, C2, C3, C4, C5; polish 1 (auto-fit + snap to free ports), 2 (moot: the Coupling item is retired, couplings fit themselves), 3 (device panel port rows; no red end marker yet), 4 (tester text); design "separate purchase" (dropped); code structure 1 (moot: sockets are husk-only, power will not use ports), 2 (FreeSockets / HuskWidth), 3 (Occupy / Vacate / Occupied gone; the Elevator / Hopper items are the parallel fork's); shared systems 2 (Attached resolver) and 3 (IPixelHuskFilter / Takes, used by the hose). Still open: speed item 4 (On dictionary, cached attached belt).

Bugs (confirmed by reading the code)

| # | Where | Bug | Fix | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | PixelQuantumDevice.cs:104 | Its side socket is Both; set to Out a belt can start there, but the organizer never Emits, so the belt stays empty (the same bug the crate had). | Make it emit like the crate now does, or don't offer Out on devices that never emit. | S |
| C2 | PixelContainerHose.cs:298, :311 | The hose asks only HOW MANY a target takes, not WHICH type, and takes the husk out of its source before the target agrees. A mixed crate hosed into a Biomass Generator (wood only), the Crafting Cube or the Kiln (no room check: 6 at a time) spills the refused husks out of the hose end. | Ask the target first: Takes(socket, tier) on the room interface, or a "look before taking" call on the source. | S-M |
| C3 | PixelBiomassGenerator.cs:202 / :219 | AcceptHusk says yes even when Burn bails out (ReleaseOldPixel failed), so the husk stays locked at the belt / hose end. | Burn returns a bool. | S |
| C4 | PixelCoupling.cs:111, PixelSocketPlates.cs:76, PixelRecycler.cs:676 | Couplings and port plates set their scale to 1 / device scale once. The Recycler later rescales itself (Bin Size upgrade), so couplings fitted before (or restored on load) grow or shrink with the bin. | Rescale when the device's scale changes, or hang sockets on an unscaled root. | S |
| C5 | PixelCoupling.cs:207-216 | A click anywhere on a Both coupling flips it (the click test uses the whole coupling's bounds, not the teal dot), and a hose already attached keeps pumping the old way (it never re-checks IsOut / IsIn). | Hit-test the dot only; the hose re-checks direction. | S |

Player polish

1. Free socket plates look like working ports, but belts and hoses don't connect to them. The link queries only look at couplings, so a belt ended on a bare plate doesn't snap and spills with no message. Snap to free plates too, and either fit a coupling automatically or say "needs a Coupling". This is the biggest mistake a player can make here. S-M
2. Bought couplings are lost without a refund (40 Gray + 40 Black each) when:
   - one is removed with the right hold;
   - its device is removed (couplings are its children);
   - a timed device ends (the 5-minute Recycler).

   Give them back. S
3. Nothing shows what's connected. The device card has no coupling rows and a coupling has no hover card. Add "In: belt from X / Out: idle (no belt)" per socket, plus a red end marker when carrying a device away breaks its links. S-M
4. No coupling tip or tutorial line (the Junction Box has one). The tester card text (PixelFeatureTester.cs:145) still mentions the Hopper's removed back socket and "click an organizer dot". S

Design

- Should the coupling be a separate purchase? Probably not.
  - Every socket already shows its plate and direction, and only the Quantum Organizer's Both socket is a choice (the Junction Box's are automatic).
  - Buying couplings adds cost and a step, but no decision.

  Proposal: fit the coupling automatically when a belt or hose snaps to a free socket. Drop the item, or turn it into the planned Coupling-settings upgrade (to-do 115). M
- The Sorting Hopper has no sockets. It has 1-4 sorted outputs (pipes, spout) but is outside the socket system: a 4th hand-made output path. Out sockets on its pipes would make it the early belt feeder the game lacks (see ConveyorAudit section 5). M
- Planned work that matters most here:
  - Build mode (117) is the natural home for the socket / connection view and the auto-fit.
  - Power sockets: power is already wired by hand in 4 places (Solar Panel click-link, Biomass radius, Power Bank, Electric links). The Solar Panel is sold now, so every new power source adds to the later move-over.
  - Coupling settings (115) overlaps the Junction Box filters; do it after Build mode.

Code structure

1. Not ready for Power / Signal yet.
   - IPixelSocketDevice forces AcceptHusk on every device (:86).
   - IntakeAt, NearestCoupling (:284), Ports, Emit and the hose's Snap (:127) don't filter by kind, so a Power coupling would be offered to belts and handed husks.

   Add a kind filter on the shared queries and one hook interface per kind (IPixelHuskIntake, later IPixelPowerIntake - proposed names, never built: sockets stayed husk-only, see Done above). Do it before Power. S-M
2. The same "free Husk socket" loop is copied 4 times (PixelCoupling.cs:248, :290, :330, PixelSocketPlates.cs:45), and the husk-width getter 4 times. Use one PixelCouplings.FreeSockets(kind). S
3. Dead code:
   - Occupy / Vacate / Occupied (PixelSockets.cs:152-160) have no callers (since removed);
   - PixelElevator still implements IPixelSocketDevice with zero sockets (:17, :137, :195);
   - the header still mentions the Hopper and connectors. S
4. Speed (fine at today's scale):
   - PixelCoupling.On is a linear scan;
   - every Emit / OutputHasRoom / OutputConnected also scans for the belt starting there, and the crate, Husk Source and Junction Box ask every tick;
   - the plates rebuild string keys every 0.3 s.

   Use a dictionary keyed by (device, socket id) and cache the attached belt per coupling on a topology version (the same idea as ConveyorAudit's PixelBeltLink proposal). S-M

Shared systems worth building

1. Per-kind hook interfaces + kind-filtered queries in PixelCouplings, so Power and Signal plug in without touching the belt code.
2. One "connection" pass:
   - auto-fit the coupling on snap;
   - one place that resolves what each coupling is attached to (belt, hose, device);
   - used by the card rows, Build mode's view, the Junction Box's auto direction and the hose's direction re-check.
3. A type-aware intake question (Takes(socket, tier)), used by the hose, Sweeper, belts and Junction Box before a husk is moved.

Guesses:
- How often C2 happens depends on players hosing a mixed crate into a Biomass Generator or the Crafting Cube.
- C4 may also hit devices that grow in when placed; it's only confirmed for the Recycler.
--- docs/systems/DeviceConsumableAudit.md ---
Devices + consumables audit (2026-10-20)

Three read-only passes: device code, consumable code, and game design. The design pass used the design docs, the to-do / ideas lists and the real numbers in code. Key claims were checked by hand. Nothing has been changed yet. Effort: S < 1 h, M = a few hours, L = a day.

Done 2026-10-20: all of section 1 (B1-B9) and section 2 (quick wins), plus: PixelClicker.DisplayNameOf (player name of a block type, used by potions and crafting), PixelBody (one velocity helper), PixelPlacedDevice.IsMarking + marking-name constants, IsEnding (IsDying / IsRemoving merged), PixelChipperDevice renamed PixelPermanentDevice. Not done from section 2: 'for a minute' in the Pet Treat / Focus texts (their real durations live outside the item). Sections 3 and 4 are open.

1. Bugs (confirmed by reading the code)

| # | Where | Bug | Fix | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | PixelShop.TryBuyItems / PixelShopConsumables.MaxAffordable | Buying several at once multiplies the CURRENT price by the count, ignoring the rising price. 3 Recyclers cost 225 White instead of 75+150+300 = 525. | Sum the price steps, or limit laddered items to one per purchase. | S |
| B2 | Inventory save part (order 210) vs Operation levels (in PixelStats, order 320) | Held counts are clamped to their caps BEFORE the Storage upgrade levels load. 5 Combo Fuel with +2 Storage loads as 3. | Don't clamp on Import, or clamp again after the stats load. | S |
| B3 | Potion / combo / attuned names, Describe | Enum names show to players ("HornetNest Potion" for the Termite Hive, other CamelCase names). | Use the tier's displayName everywhere. | S |
| B4 | PixelVacuumDevice (:57), PixelWoodChipper (:224), PixelFanDevice (:62) + hand-copied subsets in MagnetSorter, Quantum, TrashCan, Kiln, Funnel, VacuumCleaner | They break the "one owner per husk" rule. The Vacuum absorbs husks in the player's hand or reserved by the Hopper / Sweeper; the Chipper claims reserved ones; the Fan pushes held ones. | Use PixelHuskHolder.Takeable everywhere (with an allow-held option where needed). | S |
| B5 | PixelMagnetSorterDevice (since removed with the Magnetized Sorter) | Its stored stock is lost when it ends (no Ended handler); Crate / Quantum / Hopper release theirs. | Release the stock on end. | S |
| B6 | PixelBiomassGenerator.PowerAround | Also "powers" permanent and disarmed devices (energy bar shows, counted, nothing to pause). | Skip Permanent / !Armed. | S |
| B7 | PixelJunctionBox.dropsSeen, PixelConveyorCombiner.waiting, PixelRobotWorker.bankTarget | They keep husk references with no HuskReleasing listener (pooled husks can come back). | Listen to HuskReleasing. | S |
| B8 | RobotPlace / PlaceDeviceNow | Never disarm and skip onDevicePlaced. RobotPlace uses up a Funnel even when it only moves it. | Route through the normal place path. | S |
| B9 | Bait Bucket, running Focus potion | Not saved: the Bucket (and its contents) is lost on stopping Fishing or loading; the running Focus ends on load. | Save parts (IPixelSaved). | S-M |

2. Quick wins (S, low risk)

- Device card info: the card shows only name + time for 10 of ~20 devices (Fan, Vacuum, Magnet, Sprinkler, Sorter, Magnetized Sorter, Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, Wood Chipper, Funnel). Add their radius / pull / force / stock / charge / health via AddInfo.
- Inventory tooltips: they show a generic "Right-click to drink" hint instead of the item's description (the shop and the 3D gallery do show it).
- One name for the category: "Devices" (Inventory, gallery) vs "Utilities" (shop).
- Swap warning: drinking a potion while one runs replaces it silently; warn or say so.
- Description placeholders: descriptions hard-code numbers ("3 Pesticide charges", "Hold up to 5/10", "For a minute"). Add {held} / {charges} / {uses} placeholders.
- Stale text: "a conveyor belt opening latches on" (Wood Chipper, Funnel, enum comments) dates from before couplings, and 6 device texts still said "old pixels" (done 2026-10-20: every player text says husks).
- Database: PixelDatabase adds an entry for every combo and attuned item (thousands); filter with ItemListable and drop its try/catch.
- Pointer check: cache DeviceUnderPointer once per frame (it runs 2-3 times per frame).
- Naming:
  - rename PixelChipperDevice (the generic permanent stub) to PixelPermanentDevice;
  - merge IsDying / IsEnding / IsRemoving (done: one IsEnding);
  - one shared velocity get/set helper (13 private copies).
- Range markers: replace the "Range" / "Blow Area" magic strings (matched in 3 files) with a marker component.

3. Shared systems worth building (the "3rd hand-wired copy" rule)

1. One item definition per consumable / device kind. Today one item is spread over about 8 places: CreateDefault*, ShopText overrides, the PixelInstantItems use rule, the BuildItemModel model, SpawnDevice, EffectiveDevice, the Operation rows and the shop's UtilitiesFirst order. There are about 124 DeviceKind branches in PixelConsumables and DeviceKind is used in 37 files. One table entry per kind would hold name, description template, prices, cap, category enum, use rule (placed / instant / auto / planted), feedback (sound, stat, message), model / spawn builder, needs-arming, permanent, capabilities (attune, carry, upgrade, power) and Operation stats. The device class supplies its own builders, as the newer devices already do. L
2. A stable item id. Items are matched by display name (saves, keybinds, favourites, pictures) or by an index that shifts when pixel types are added. A ShopText rename silently drops owned counts and keybinds. One ItemId (category, kind / type, second, third, attune) with dictionary lookup, plus combo / attuned entries made on demand instead of 3,500+ up front. M
3. (Started 2026-10-24: the 8 device models - Fan, Vacuum, Magnet, Sprinkler, Lightning Rod, Charge Booster, Quantum Organizer, Storage Crate - moved into their own scripts as BuildModel; shared Disc / Strut / PlainPart on PixelFactoryKit; the file is ~3,660 lines.) Split PixelConsumables.cs (4,062 lines) into item data, inventory (owned / caps / use / save), the device placer (place / remove / arm input) and per-device body builders moved into each Pixel*Device.cs (~600 lines of Build*Body). Do it together with 1. M-L
4. One placed-object base for consumable devices and Tester factory pieces. Today they have two placing / removal / tint / save systems:
   - Tester removal starts on the first right press, with a fixed 0.8 s and no right-click owner check.
   - The two red tints are near-identical.
   - Saves differ: PlacedState vs TesterSave.
   - Factory pieces get no device card.

   Add an IPixelDeviceState (Export / Import) that both save paths loop over, which also removes the "charge rides in the aim field" hacks. L
5. A husk intake helper in PixelHuskQuery: Takeable with options, a per-husk cooldown set that cleans itself on HuskReleasing (copied 5 times today), one FlyIn absorb animation (copied 3 times), and optionally a once-per-step spatial bucket so ~13 devices stop scanning every husk every physics step. M
6. One timed-effect runner for the plain potion, the Focus potion and the Pet Treat: timer, status line, saving, "replaces the running X" notice. S-M

4. Design findings (decisions for you / the Design Discussion session)

Biggest friction
- Recycler lifetime. (Done 2026-10-20: Run Time is back, +1 minute a level up to 10 minutes.) Every device chain ends in the Recycler (Sweeper, Hopper, hose, crate), but it lasts 5 minutes and vanishes with its contents. The Run Time Operation row that came with the 5-minute change (2026-10-16) has since been removed, so only a Woodcutting Biomass Generator can keep it running. Options: make it permanent with its price ladder, bring Run Time back, or keep it timed on purpose. (Its script header says "permanent" and its doubling factor is 1.8, but the data says 300 s and 2.)
- Too much at once. (2026-10-20: the user will tier the unlocks themselves later.) When the shop opens (~6 min) Utilities already lists Recycler, Coupling, Hose, Belt Kit and Junction Box (all gated by White), plus the Crate (Gray): four factory parts with nothing worth connecting. Gate the factory parts behind the Funnel at about Standard 6-7, using the planned shared "needs skill X level N" table (to-do line 21). Three different gating mechanisms exist today: requiredType, shopSkillId, devOnly.
- Dead zone after the Sweeper (Standard 5, ~30 min). The Magnet needs the Electrician pack, the Fan needs Glass, and the Funnel, Organizer and Sorter are dev-only. That leaves no new general husk tools for an estimated 1-3 hours.

Items that need a clearer job
- Magnet: its heap keeps ageing, so it does NOT answer despawn (despite the Connection Map), and the Recycler's suction only takes husks from above the rim. Make its settle zone pause ageing. Also, its price is about 100x below the Fan's.
- Fan: about 2 minutes of income for 30 s of pushing. Reprice it to around the Magnet's tier; the attuned Fan (one type only) is the interesting version.
- Vacuum Device (dev-only): really an automatic Husk Crush. Rework it into a permanent "Crusher" that auto-crushes for about 0.35 of a click's XP, so the Recycler automates money and the Crusher automates XP.
- Storage Crate: stored husks have no use until the Workbench exists (to-do lines 109-111); it costs more than the Hopper and does less. Prioritise the Workbench, or cheapen the Crate meanwhile.
- Quantum Organizer (dev-only): a storage device on a timer that empties it. Make it permanent at Standard 12+ (as planned).
- Dead outputs: (Done 2026-10-20: Biomass Generators burn Charcoal x3 and Wood Chips x4, and the Lumberjack buys both husks off the floor.) Charcoal (Kiln) and Wood Chips have no use. Make them Biomass fuel (planned, to-do line 120, but it fits the existing generator now).
- Unreachable recipes: the Magnetized Sorter and the attuned Vacuum need the dev-only Sorter / Vacuum. Re-recipe them (for example Magnet + Junction Box).
- Bait Bucket: Fishing is on sale but its only tool is dev-only.

Cut or merge candidates
- Trash Can (tester): (Decided 2026-10-20: it stays a dev tool, never sold.) Does what the despawn timer already does.
- Sorter ring (dev-only): (Done 2026-10-20: removed.) Dates from before husks stopped paying; Aimed Clicks and the Funnel cover it.
- Belt Sorter (tester): a third filter device; merge into the Junction Box filters (which also overlap the Hopper's Outputs upgrade).

Bigger direction (already in Ideas: "Power... plan this one next"; 2026-10-20: power comes later as its own mechanic, not through the husk sockets)
- Power as the one upkeep rule: replace the per-device timers (Fan 30 s, Magnet 30 s, Sprinkler 45 s, Organizer 600 s, Magnetized Sorter 180 s) with "runs while powered". Power would come from Biomass (wood), Solar, Electric husks / Charge Booster and Charcoal. That gives Electrician, Woodcutting and Solar outgoing links (Connection Map gaps) and replaces the pile of timers with one rule.

Stale docs: ConnectionMap.md still lists the belt as "Electrician level 5", the Vacuum Device as plain "Built" and the Recycler as "Designed" under despawn.
--- docs/systems/GearDesign.md ---
Gear, first person unlock and the alien vendor (design, not built yet)

Status: ideas agreed in chat (2026-10-15/16), nothing built. Parked for AFTER 1.0, maybe (2026-10-16) - see docs/GameDesign.md section 9b. The to-do list has one line per step (Assets/Resources/Todo.txt, "Gear system" onwards).
This doc collects what was agreed and, more importantly, the blank spots: the decisions nobody has made yet. Each blank spot has a
suggested default (marked Suggestion) so building can start without waiting, but the user decides.

The four pieces and their order

1. Gear system: one gear slot (you're a cube), free swapping, later more faces.
2. First person as a mid-game unlock: the Cube Visor, the first gear piece.
3. The alien vendor: only reachable in first person; sells relics and face sockets.
4. Legendary gear: the Prime Gauntlet's powers, God Cube relics, the Dragon Radar, the Seeker's Charm.

Each step needs the one before it. Building out of order means hand-wiring things that should come from the shared gear system.

Goals

- Gear changes HOW you play, not just your numbers. Each piece is a new action or a new rule, not "+10% pay". The game already has
  many % layers (potions, Focus, pets, perks, combo, stakes, upgrades, Operation).
- Choices matter. One slot means wearing one thing means not wearing another.
- The super rares finally pay off. Today the God Cubes and Prime Cubes mostly sit in the Collection Log, and the Cubelette is a trophy.
- First person becomes a place with things to do, and reaching it is a "wow" moment instead of a key you press on day one.

---

1. Gear system

Agreed
- One slot, on the top face, worn like a hat. Cubie's line: *"Where would you even put a second one? You don't have arms."*
- Free, instant swapping, with a quick-swap key, so gear is picked for the moment.
- Later, more faces: "face sockets" unlock the other faces one at a time, up to six; the bottom face comes last ("you're sitting on it").
  The alien sells sockets as its priciest item.
- Data-driven: one entry per gear item (name, slot, where it comes from, its effect); effects plug in through shared hooks the way
  PixelBlockRules works, never checks scattered through PixelClicker.
- Lives in Pocket (PixelMyWindow) as a new Gear tab. Saved per save file; a Database category; Stats rows.

Blank spots
- Is gear a new kind of item, or a consumables Device? The Prime Gauntlet is already a Device (DeviceKind.Gauntlet), and devices have
  owned counts, the inventory, prices and the shop.
  Suggestion: gear items are Devices with a new "equippable" flag, so the shop, inventory, crafting and saving all work already;
  the equipped item is one saved field.
- Can you own duplicates? Suggestion: no, gear is one of each (like the God Cubes).
- What does a second face DO when gear is worn there - the same as the top face? Or does each face have a theme (top = active power,
  sides = passive, bottom = ???)? Suggestion: all faces equal at first; themes only if it feels flat.
- How many face sockets, at what price, and where else do they come from (only the alien, or also skill levels / Mastery)?
- Swap cooldown? Free swapping could let a player cycle gear every second to stack effects.
  Suggestion: no cooldown, but an item's power cooldown keeps running while it is off, so cycling gains nothing.
- Does gear show on the cube? A tiny hat / glove / visor on the top face would be charming, but every item then needs a model.
  Suggestion: yes, small and simple, built from primitives like the vendors are.
- Does the auto clicker trigger gear powers, or only your own clicks? Suggestion: only your own clicks.

---

2. First person as a mid-game unlock

Agreed
- Today first person is available from the start (its key only checks for menus / takeovers: PixelFirstPerson.CanEnter).
- It becomes a gift: the Cube Visor, the first gear piece, given at a mid-game milestone.
- First use plays a camera dive down into the floor.
- Hidden until then: the View window's First person tick box and the first person key.
- Dev Hub: the Unlock widget can give it for testing.

Blank spots
- Which milestone? Candidates: buying a second skill pack, Standard level 5, a Mastery %, Cubie's third visit, owning N devices.
  Suggestion: a Cubie visit triggered by Standard level 5, so it fits his existing visit system and the Standard skill.
- Who gives it? Cubie (the guide) or the alien (but the alien needs first person to be reached at all).
  Suggestion: Cubie gives the Visor; the alien's ship is what you discover with it.
- Does the Visor have to be WORN to use first person? If yes, first person costs your one gear slot, which clashes with the alien's
  relics (also first-person gear). Suggestion: no, once received, first person is unlocked for good and the Visor is a free bonus item
  (or it doesn't take the slot). This needs a decision before building.
- The Collection Log gallery is walked in first person. Should it stay usable before the unlock? Suggestion: yes, the gallery is its
  own room; it doesn't spoil the main floor reveal.
- What does the dive look like, and is there a line from Cubie after it?

---

3. The alien vendor

Agreed
- A 3D alien whose UFO lands at the edge of the floor, a spot never seen from the normal top view. You walk over in first person.
- Ties the game's UFO (the UFO Helper, the main menu UFO) into the world.
- Built on the shared vendor base PixelVisitorMinigame (wares, prices, dialogue), adding only the 3D body and the walk-up trigger -
  not a hand-wired fourth vendor.
- Sells relics (first person gear) and face sockets.

Blank spots
- Is the ship always there once unlocked, or does it visit on a timer like Cubie and the other vendors?
  Suggestion: it visits (lands, stays a few minutes, leaves), so walking out is a small event, and the first landing is scripted to
  happen right after the Visor.
- What does it charge? Ordinary pixels, a new currency, husks, or something only first person gives (phase pixels - see relics)?
  Suggestion: phase pixels, so first person feeds its own shop.
- Name and personality. Every vendor has one (Cubie, Pip the Prospector, Big Tim the Lumberjack). Also: does it talk in text, or in
  bleeps with subtitles?
- What if the player never goes into first person? Does anything nudge them (a Cubie line, a beam of light visible from the top view)?
  Suggestion: a faint light beam at the floor edge that you can just see from the normal view.
- How does "walk up and talk" work? Look at it and press a key, click it, or just walk close? Suggestion: walk close, a prompt
  appears in the bottom hint box (PixelHintBox), press the interact key.
- Does it ever appear on the main menu with the other wandering vendors?

---

4. Alien relics (first person gear)

Agreed (the ideas)
| Relic | What it does |
|---|---|
| Grav Boots | Low gravity and high jumps; reach husks stacked up high |
| Tractor Glove | Pull husks to you from across the floor, then throw them (e.g. into the Recycler) |
| Xeno Lens | Shows hidden phase pixels that only exist in first person and pay well |
| Blink Core | A short teleport dash |
| Probe Drone | A little bot that follows you and clicks the cube while you are away from it |

- Relics are first-person-only on purpose, so first person needs enough to do there.

Blank spots
- Phase pixels need their own design: what they look like, where they appear, how often, what they pay, whether they are a currency.
  They are the backbone of first person's reason to exist, so they deserve their own short doc before the relics are built.
- Does first person have enough to do with these five? Today you can walk, click the cube and grab husks. The relics add movement and
  husk handling; is that enough to make it a real way to play, or does it need a goal (e.g. the machine repair idea already on the to-do)?
- Is the Probe Drone too strong? It is basically an auto clicker that works while you're away. Suggestion: it clicks slower than the
  Auto Clicker and only while you're in first person.
- Tractor Glove vs Pixel Grabbing: grabbing already exists. What makes the glove different enough? (Range? Several at once? Throwing?)
- Do relics do anything at all outside first person? Agreed: no, on purpose. Confirm, since it means top-view players never use them.

---

5. Legendary gear (the super rares)

Agreed (the ideas)
- The Prime Gauntlet becomes gear instead of being used up by forging. Each Prime Cube set in it unlocks a power with a cooldown:

| Stone | Power |
|---|---|
| Space | Pull every floor husk into the Recycler or crate at once |
| Time | Rewind the last 10 s of clicks, or a free long Time Stop |
| Power | One punch breaks any tough block at 50x pay |
| Reality | Turn the current block into any block you pick |
| Mind | See the next 5 blocks and choose which one comes up |
| Soul | Pets much stronger for a minute |

  All six = the Snap: half the husks on screen vanish and pay out; long cooldown.
- The Cubelette becomes what the Gauntlet turns into after so many Snaps.
- Dragon Radar charm: a hint when a rare is about to roll. The wish stays as it is.
- God Cube relics: Slifer = every 20th click chains lightning to nearby husks; Ra = a sun phase, a golden hour for a minute;
  Obelisk = tough blocks take double hits.
- Seeker's Charm from the Golden Snitch: later snitches are easier to catch and pay more.

Blank spots
- Super rares are 1 in 70,000 to 1 in 7 million. Most players will never own legendary gear. Is that fine (bragging rights), or should
  there be a slow guaranteed path too (e.g. the Wizard sells a lesser copy)?
- How do you GET a God Cube relic? Is the God Cube itself the relic (wear the cube), or does finding it unlock a crafted relic?
  Suggestion: the find unlocks a relic you can equip; the cube stays in the Collection Log.
- "Rewind the last 10 s" needs the game to record recent clicks and undo them (currency, husks, the cube's block). That is a big piece
  of work on its own. Suggestion: keep only the "free long Time Stop" half for the first version.
- Mind and Reality could break the skills' designed rhythms (Woodcutting's tree shapes, Fishing's spawn table). Do they work during
  trees / special blocks, or only in the normal rotation?
- How many Snaps make the Cubelette, and what does the Cubelette do once it exists (still a trophy, or the strongest gear piece)?
- Gauntlet with only some stones: usable from 1 stone, or only once all six are set? Suggestion: each stone's power works as soon as
  it is set; the Snap needs all six.
- The Dragon Radar's hint: a sound, a glow on the cube, a line in the status box? And how early before the rare roll?

---

Fits the game / could be better

- Reuses: the Devices list (owning, prices, saving), Pocket for the tab, the vendor base for the alien, PixelBlockRules-style hooks
  for effects, the Gauntlet window for setting stones, the first person system and its neon floor, PixelHintBox for prompts.
- Needs first: the rare set table cleanup already on the to-do (one entry per super-rare family), so legendary gear reads its data
  from one place instead of enum ranges.
- New pieces: the gear entry list + effect hooks, the equipped-item save field, phase pixels, the alien's 3D body and landing.
- Watch for: each gear effect becoming another scattered check. Effects should ask the gear system ("is the Tractor Glove worn?")
  through one call, the way PixelCalm answers "is the game in a menu?".
--- docs/systems/MainMenuFlow.md ---
MainMenuFlow

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

Main menu camera / floor / welcome-back flow

The camera swing is smoother (PixelCameraIntro.Apply turns against an 'up' that blends from world up to the scene camera's own up (upRef), so a TOP-DOWN scene camera no longer makes the picture flip 180 degrees at the very end of the swing: yaw / pitch / distance interpolation instead of a slerp between the two offsets, one continuous look-at blend, quintic Smoother ease, frame time clamped to 0.05 s). The floor-fade switches are keepFloorOnTitle (false = fading on) and floorFadeSeconds (0 = 1): a serialized bool that defaults to true / a float that defaults to 1 would read false / 0 on a scene saved before the field existed - new serialized defaults must be 'off' / 0 = default. Margins slide: PixelHud.Update slides the top / bottom black bars (and PixelDockedButton.Apply pushes every docked button outward by the same amount, PixelHud.RevealEased, revealSeconds 0 = 0.9) away while PixelTitleScreen.Showing or PixelTitleScreen.Reversing (the camera swinging back) and in as the camera moves on Play. Props fade: husks and placed devices shrink away / back with PixelFloor.propsK (ApplyProps, PropsFadeIn() called after a save load, PixelClicker.CollectOldPixelTransforms): on Quit > Main menu they go first, then the floor, then the camera; on arrival the floor comes first, then they grow back. Blur background OFF draws the live scene unblurred at full size (PixelTitleScreen.UpdateSharp, a full-resolution camera render in strongImage). While the textured floor is hidden / fading a flat floor-coloured copy of the floor mesh sits just under it (PixelFloor.UpdateFlatBase, AverageColor, queue 1990), so the menu background is the floor's colour and the textured floor fades in from it. Live background ON (PixelTitleWanderers.LiveBackground): the main menu and the camera intro show only the sky - no floor: PixelFloor.LateUpdate fades the floor with FloorVisibility (0..1 over floorFadeSeconds, 0 = 1 s; off when keepFloorOnTitle is ticked): hidden while the title screen shows or the camera is moving (PixelCameraIntro.Moving), so after Play the camera swings as normal and the floor fades in once it has settled; the fade draws see-through copies of the floor's materials (PixelClicker.BuildTransparentMaterial, EnsureProxies / DropProxies, tiling copied by ApplyTiling), at 0 Renderer.forceRenderingOff hides it (the renderer stays enabled), and the husks and placed devices shrink away first and come back last (propsK: they fade out, THEN the floor; the floor fades in, THEN they do; pets are not). Quit > Main menu: PixelTitleScreen.BeginReturn first calls PixelFloor.FadeOutThen(ReverseCamera) (the floor fades out), THEN PixelCameraIntro.PlayReverse zooms the camera back. Live background OFF: no camera intro at all (PixelCameraIntro.LateUpdate keeps the camera at its normal pose, PlayReverse returns false) - the title shows the blurred floor picture and Play just fades it into the default view; the floor is never hidden. The 'Welcome back' offline-progress window now waits for the title screen AND the camera intro (PixelOfflineProgress.Update checks PixelCameraIntro.Moving).
--- docs/systems/OlderNotes.md ---
Older notes (formerly docs/roadmap.md)

History and system notes moved from CLAUDE.md long ago. The open IDEAS from here now live in Assets/Resources/Ideas.txt (Dev Hub > Docs > Ideas); this file keeps the background (balance decisions, reworks, profiling).

- Handoff + first optimization review (2026-10-07). The old HANDOFF.md (removed 2026-10-20: its rules recap and branch / attribution lines were outdated, CLAUDE.md is the rule book) held a 29-item optimization list. Its items were done or carried into OptimizationReview2.md (old "HANDOFF #N" references there point to this list); still open from it: one time-scale owner (#26: pause, Time Stop and the title screen each save / restore Time.timeScale), a pack-effect list instead of one bool per effect (#25), the title-screen blur's Camera.main.Render() on URP overlay cameras (#28), and further big-file splits (#29).
- Lessons kept from that handoff:
  - Patching by "first text match" put code in the wrong function twice: when editing with scripts, assert the match is unique or anchor on the function name, then grep -n the result.
  - Time.timeScale = 0 is used by the pause menu, Time Stop and the title screen: anything that must run while frozen uses Time.unscaledDeltaTime.
  - A fresh UI element's rect is not valid until the canvas updates: compute sizes from Screen / the canvas scale instead.
- Optimization review 2 (2026-10-17, whole codebase): docs/systems/OptimizationReview2.md - ranked list, none of it done yet.
- The UI helper (PixelUIKit) and minigame base (PixelMinigame) refactors are verified in Unity by the user.
- Cleanup still worth doing: (PixelShop is now split into three partial-class files; a deeper split into separate logic and UI classes is still possible); (the shop's "add missing defaults" code is now one table-driven loop; potions/devices/achievements keep their own small versions); optionally replace scene searches with one shared game object that owns the references (the version-check duplication is already gone via PixelFind).
- Balance direction (agreed 2026-10-09): idle game played over days, with many more pixels and systems planned (numbers going up). Phase 1 done (long number suffixes up to ~1e306 in FormatNumber; meteor chunkClickCooldown). Phase 2 started: Value upgrades + potion/device prices scale with Value. Built-in pack prices re-laddered for the Value economy (RGB 100 each, Auto Clicker 1K each, then roughly x2-10 per step up to 5M; upgrade levels x5-x10 per level); saved scene prices don't follow code defaults, so PixelShop has an Inspector tick box resetBuiltInPricesNow / context menu "Reset Built-in Pack Prices" (ResetBuiltInPackPrices in PixelShopDefaults.cs: copies costs + level lists from the Create…Pack defaults). Meteor goal field renamed chunkThreshold -> chunkGoal (default 1000) on purpose so old serialized 100s are dropped (the same rename trick works for any other serialized default that must change). Value cost scales by amountPerClick (payout per collect) but deliberately NOT by spawn weight: user wants rarer pixels to be slower to upgrade. Electric is the exception (an early, cheap boost). Rare-currency pack prices are set from earn rate = spawnWeight x amountPerClick relative to White (Glass 0.5, Vacuum 0.2, Obsidian 2, Luminescent 1.5, Ghost 2.4, Meteor 3.75, Singularity 5). (2026-10-09 later: every pixel / minigame pack from Vacuum onward was cut about 30% - Vacuum 17.5K Glass + 35K RGB, Ghost Hunt 35K Black + 17.5K Glass, Obsidian 100K Black + 50K Glass, Luminescent 175K Glass + 700K Obsidian, Ghost Pixel 1.4M Glass + 4M Luminescent, Ultra Pad / Breakout 175K, Sorting Race 70K, Snake / Bomb 35K, Meteor Strike 1.75M Black + 3.5M Obsidian, Meteor Pixel 4M Luminescent + 550K Vacuum, Solar 700K Meteor + 7M Luminescent, Black Hole 350K Black + 70K Vacuum, Singularity 2.8M Obsidian + 280K Vacuum; upgrades / features were left alone.) Earlier ladder: Snake 50K, Sorting Race 100K, Breakout 250K, Time Slow 500K each, Husk Capacity 2.5K-5M each, Solar Pixel 1M Meteor + 10M Luminescent. Potions (agreed 2026-10-09): stay forced (only that pixel spawns) and chainable - the user likes the all-one-type look and chaining. Balanced by price instead: PixelConsumables.potionPriceFromOutput -> price = expected output / potionTargetReturn (3), paid in the potion's own pixel; expected output = duration x (autoClicker rate once bought, even if toggled off, + assumedManualClicksPerSecond 4) x amountPerClick x PayoutMultiplier x ClickMultiplier / clicksToCollect, plus for Vacuum the husks re-collected (ExpectedPotionOutput, PotionPriceFromOutput, PotionShopPrice); typed potion costs are ignored while it is on. PixelClicker.potionVacuumEvery (10): while a potion forces the Vacuum tier only every 10th Vacuum click vacuums (VacuumThisClick). Crafting: recipesFollowShopPrices -> ScaleRecipe copies a recipe with the potion's own pixel at craftPotionPriceFraction (0.75) of its shop price (combo potions: the second pixel), other pixel ingredients x Value (Ingredient.amountOverride / Amount, runtime only). Stockpiling is limited by PixelConsumables.maxPotionsHeld (10 per potion type, combo potions too, devices unlimited; ItemRoom, ClampHeld - shop purchase card shows "Full", crafting refuses, loading a save clamps). Still to do: formula-based auto clicker levels, minigame goals measured in hours, and later prestige (wanted) and passive production. Intro tiers (Gray/Black) unlock from lifetime totals at 100 each; shop purchases always spend held currency.
- Possible next steps (not requested yet – confirm before building): more shop packs/tiers, particle polish, filling in sound clips, balancing pass on spawn weights and costs, prestige, prefabs for the runtime-built UI.
- Known gaps: offline progress only covers the auto clicker (no minigames/devices offline); PixelLog keeps a duplicate number formatter (FormatAmount) separate from PixelClicker.FormatNumber – could be unified.
- Fishing rework: Water (the Farming pixel) is Fishing's filler (very common: fillerStartShare 0.75 -> 0.5) and replaces Driftwood (retired, LeftOutOfSpawns); the pack's types are Fish, Seaweed, Shell, Pearl, Water, Jellyfish (packDataVersion 21). A block can belong to several packs (PackForTier / LeftOutOfSpawns prefer the pack in play). Fish is rare (weight 0.15, 1 click, pays 12): its click (manual only, the auto clicker waits) calls PixelWeakspotMinigame.TryStartFishing(done) (a Weak Spots round with the fish still on the cube); PixelClicker.FishingDone(caught) then pays it through a normal click (fishingPass) or says 'The fish got away!' and rolls the next pixel. If the round can't start (minigame switched off, another minigame busy...) the click just catches the fish.
- Fishing, part 2: the Jellyfish is electric now (9 clicks; PixelLook.lightning look, PixelElectricFx Hit / Break cases, sounds pixel_jellyfish / jelly_sting = PixelSynth.Zap / Charge; when it goes off in TriggerHazard it stings jellyfishBlastFactor (1.5) x wider, fires a big FractureFx and fries every bait pellet). Bait (PixelType.Bait 72, in the Angler's Pack, packDataVersion 22, looksVersion 47): collecting one calls PixelBait.Drop (PixelBait.cs: baitPellets 6 small spheres hop out round the cube onto the floor, on screen, max baitMaxPellets 24, rot after baitLifetime 60 s); PixelBait.FishMultiplier (1 + pellets x baitPerPellet 0.35) multiplies a Fish block's spawn weight in SpawnWeightOf; PixelBait.DestroyAll(zap) removes them. Fish without the minigame: when PixelWeakspotMinigame.FishingAvailable is false (switched off in Toggles) a fish takes a random 7-25 clicks (fishNeed / fishReel, manual or auto), the last one pays; with it on, only a manual click starts the round.
- Simple save flow (main menu): nothing loads by itself while the title screen shows (PixelSaveGame.Start skips the startup load when PixelTitleScreen.Showing). The big main button reads Continue when there are saves and none has been loaded / written this run (PixelSaveGame.HasLoadedSave): pressing it loads the most recent file (TryGetMostRecentSlot by write time, ContinueMostRecent, pays the time away) and plays; New Game when there are no saves at all; Play once a save was loaded / written (e.g. after Quit > Main menu: just resumes). The floppy icon opens the slot window: a filled slot loads, and from the title an EMPTY slot turns the Load button into New Game (PixelTitleScreen.StartNewGame(slot): sets the slot (UseSlotForNewGame) and plays; if the game was already played this run (PlayedOnce) it sets PixelSaveGame.AutoStartNewGame, resets tips / achievements and reloads the scene, and the title then presses Play by itself). Autosave / quit-save always write into CurrentSlot (the slot loaded or chosen for the new game).
- Docked button layout: TOP: Inventory (left corner), Shop (right corner); BOTTOM: Log (left corner), Dev Tools (centre, dev builds), Crafting (right corner); Bank + Hose on the middle of the LEFT edge with Skills directly under them (group), Toggles + Pixel Guide (directly under it) on the middle of the RIGHT edge (they slide sideways). PixelHud.Dock(button, anchor, ...) accepts a FRACTION in anchor.x for top / bottom buttons (0 = left corner .. 1 = right corner; pivot = the fraction); a top / bottom group pivots on its left edge. My Window is a square window-icon button on the Toggles tab's inner side (a DockInGroup pair like Bank + Hose: the Toggles tab is ButtonSize.x - ButtonSize.y wide, the square next to it; PixelToggles.BuildMyWindowIcon, shows once PixelMyWindow.Available, hover name box, calls PixelMyWindow.ToggleOpen(), tucks away with the Toggles tab while its window is open). Every docked button has the same colour (PixelHud.SharedButtonColor, applied each frame by PixelDockedButton.ApplyColor; useOwnColor exempts the hose (green while on) and Dev Tools buttons).
- Minigame tip boxes (minigame_<id> hints, PixelHints.Update) now stop time and darken the screen until 'Got it': PixelTutorialDim.Begin(null) (fade + pause + click block) then a small PixelNotice with freezeTime and onClosed: PixelTutorialDim.End; the first ghost tip still sits beside the ghost. PixelWeakspotMinigame.Update waits while PixelTutorialDim.Active so its slow-motion scale isn't re-applied over the freeze.

Profiling (added with optimization step 2)
Profiler markers (Window > Analysis > Profiler, CPU > Timeline / Hierarchy, search "Pixel."): Pixel.FloorRedraw (animated floor texture), Pixel.ElectricLinks.Scan, Pixel.SolarClusters, Pixel.QuantumScan. Reading the code, the husk scans are timer-limited (0.1-0.15 s) and O(n) with early exits, and GetComponent<OldPixelInfo> only appears in timer-driven scans (PixelViewBounds already caches it), so a shared pixel registry / pooling is NOT worth doing until a profile with ~150 husks shows these markers (or the per-object Update / FixedUpdate of OldPixelDespawn / ScaledGravity) taking real time.

- Click lag (Editor): per-click click-effect bursts are pooled (PixelBasicFx Burst pool + PixelFx.Configure), the spawn path reuses one MaterialPropertyBlock and caches the live cube's colliders, and Profiler markers Pixel.SpawnFallingCopy, Pixel.Materialize, Pixel.ClickFx show what is left. Still unpooled: the husk GameObjects themselves (about a dozen components each), PixelFarmFx / PixelElectricFx bursts, +N popups. Profile before pooling more.
- Pixel rules (value / body / use): a click gives a block's currency and drops its husk (material, never currency); holding a utility block gives charges. Design in docs/systems/PixelRules.md; Vacuum, Pixel Bank and the Solar trickle need a new job under it.
--- docs/systems/OptimizationReview2.md ---
Optimization review 2 (2026-10-17)

The first review (the old HANDOFF.md section 4, 2026-10-08; summarised in OlderNotes.md) covered ~22k lines. This one covers all ~133k lines (293 scripts). Six read-only passes each took about 22k lines and followed the callers to confirm how often the code runs. The top items were then checked again by hand. There is still no profiler data, so "cost" below is read from the code. Profile before doing the L items.

Effort: S < 1 h, M = a few hours, L = a day. Risk = chance of breaking behaviour.

Done: A1 (rolling save picture stays on the GPU; read back + JPG only when a save needs it), A2 + A3 (shared redraw limit PixelTierPicture.Due, 30/s, used by Render, shop pictures, shop Operation, Log icons; shop picture keys cached), A4 partly (the Log icons use the limit; its studio copy is not moved onto PixelTierPicture yet - its camera size differs). A5, A6, B3, B5 (per-click work: RefreshUI only formats tiers that have a label, the Skills window refresh is capped to 10/s on XP, the Log skips its '+N' while closed; spawn roll asks LeftOutOfSpawns once per tier per roll (FillSpawnableNow), PixelSkills.Find is a plain loop, pack type settings parsed once (PixelSkills.ParseTypes), PixelClicker.TypeName caches enum names). A7 (+N popup pool in PixelUI, maxPopups). A8 (PixelClicker.PointerHits: one NonAlloc pointer ray per frame for TryGetBlockAt / IsLiveCubeAt; device and pet hover checks test themselves first and cast at the cube only on a hit; devices cache their hit parts (CountsForHit); pets cache their collider and share one physics material; belt sorter NonAlloc). Section C leaks (ring mesh + colour block, Mold shells / zone disc, ShineMesh cache, Weak Spots / Meteor / Breakout / Evil Tree materials, Ghost backpack sprite, PixelHuskQuery prunes from Get every 256 new husks). B1 manager loop (PixelHuskTicker: ScaledGravity / OldPixelImpact / OldPixelPopIn / OldPixelDespawn tick from one loop). B1 pooling done (2026-10-18): PixelHuskPool - HuskReleasing signal that the ~25 holders of loose husks listen to, claimed husks never pooled, stripped on release, cleaned on reuse; switch PixelClicker.huskPooling. Save registry (old HANDOFF #23) done: PixelSaveRegistry. Cleanup batch (2026-10-18): A9 (cube icons + Ultra swatch on the 30/s limit, off-screen skipped), D1/D2/D4 (flying set, view-bounds look flag cached, gravity well, stream NonAlloc, Pad / PathCarrier / Nuclear / splash lookups, Solar scan only when unlocked), B4 (skill XP / node keys cached, LevelOf cached), section E (scene searches -> Cached, floor renderers / props, rain, day-night retry, UFO property block, autosave choice + no pretty JSON, settled HUD buttons / combo) and the per-frame strings (PixelKeys.Replace cache, status boxes / lines, guide / potion lines, device timers, tooltips). B2 done (2026-10-19): one PixelFx burst pool (PixelFx.Rent) for every per-hit effect and the click pop. F4 done: Log / Pixel Guide / shop pictures / Operation use PixelTierPicture / PixelModelPicture. Still open: D3 (machine scans), PixelHints rows, the other shared helpers in F. Untested in Unity.

A. Biggest wins (normal play, always on)

| # | Where | Problem | Fix | Effort/Risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | PixelAutosaves.cs:209 ShotRunner.Update (CacheSeconds = 2.5) | Every 2.5 s during play it does a full-screen ScreenCapture.CaptureScreenshotAsTexture (GPU stall + ~8 MB), then a Blit, a ReadPixels (a second stall) and a JPG encode on the main thread, just to keep a thumbnail ready for saves made while paused. This is a regular hitch. | Capture only when a save happens and when the pause menu is about to open. Alternatively, use a small RT with AsyncGPUReadback. | S-M / low |
| A2 | PixelNextUp.cs:226 LateUpdate -> PixelTierPicture.Render | Once "What's Next" is owned, each chip does a full Camera.Render every frame, including duplicate tiers and fading chips. That is 3-5 extra render passes per frame. | Inside PixelTierPicture.Render: skip a tier that was already rendered this frame and cap renders to ~25 fps. This also fixes the BlockTooltip, CollectionLog, Database, SkillLevelPopup and LookStudio callers. | S / low |
| A3 | PixelShopPictures.cs:161, PixelShopOperation.cs:310 | Every visible shop picture runs cam.Render() every frame while the shop is open (~10 passes). It also builds a "tier:"+type key string per slot per frame. | Same fps cap / round-robin, plus cached key strings. | S / low |
| A4 | PixelLog.cs:423-494 SkinIcon / RenderSkin | Achievements/Goals tab: one Camera.Render per tier per frame. It is also a private copy of PixelTierPicture. | Switch to PixelTierPicture (it then gets the A2 cap). | S-M / low |
| A5 | PixelClicker.cs:1975 RefreshUI (from every AddCurrency) | Runs FormatNumber for all ~145 tiers on every click, including every vacuumed husk. The labels it fills (countLabel, summaryLabel, nextUnlockLabel) are never assigned in code. | Return early when no label is set; format only the tiers that have a label. | S / none |
| A6 | PixelSkillsWindow.cs:288 Changed += RefreshWindow | AddXpFlat raises Changed twice per block, so with the Skills window open every click (auto clicks too) fully re-lays out cards, perks and the tree. This bypasses the 0.4 s timer. | Have Changed only mark the window dirty (refreshTimer = 0). | S / none |
| A7 | PixelUI.cs:970 PopupRoutine | Every "+N" popup is a new GameObject + TextMeshProUGUI + coroutine + Destroy, with no cap. Setting the outline per popup probably creates a font material instance every time (possible leak, one draw call each). | Pool of ~16 labels, oldest reused first, sharing one outline material. | M / low |
| A8 | PixelClicker.TryGetBlockAt (:1036) / IsLiveCubeAt (:1067); PixelConsumables.UpdateArming (:1446); PixelPets.PetUnderPointer (:727) | Several Physics.RaycastAll + sorts every frame from AimCursor, BlockTooltip, BlockHold, PowerClick, device arming and pets (pets run even with zero pets). Husk checks use oldPixels.Contains (O(n)). Device hit-tests call GetComponentsInChildren<Renderer> + .name per device per frame. | One cached pointer query per frame in PixelClicker (RaycastNonAlloc, static buffer, PixelHuskQuery.Get instead of Contains). Return early when there are no devices/pets. Cache device renderers when the device is built. | S-M / low |
| A9 | PixelCubeIcon.cs:38-68 (HANDOFF #7, still open) | Every icon calls SetVerticesDirty every frame and allocates UIVertex[4]/Vector3[4]. With the Log open that is dozens of mesh rebuilds per frame. PixelUltraSwatch likewise forces the pinned Inventory canvas to rebuild every frame. | Static buffers, ~25 fps, skip culled icons; give animated bits their own nested Canvas. | S / low |

B. Per-click churn (grows with auto-click speed)

| # | Where | Problem | Fix | Effort/Risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | PixelClickerHusks.cs:578-810 (HANDOFF #12/#13) | Each husk is a new GameObject + 6-12 AddComponents, and each husk runs its own Update/FixedUpdate (Despawn, PopIn, ScaledGravity, Impact; plus Wobble/Spin/Shine/... looks). OldPixelImpact.OnCollisionEnter calls GetComponent on every contact. | Short term: collision.rigidbody + PixelHuskQuery; cache the per-type bounce sound id, trail curves and gradients. Long term: a husk pool + one manager loop. | S now, L pooling / medium |
| B2 | PixelFarmFx.cs:101/116, PixelFirePixel.cs:166, PixelElectricFx.cs:72-146 | Every hit on gathering / fire / electric blocks builds new GameObjects + ParticleSystems (and a FractureFx Mesh), destroyed seconds later. Not covered by the earlier click-fx pool. | A small pool in PixelFx, re-armed with PixelFx.Configure. | M / low |
| B3 | PixelSkills.LeftOutOfSpawns (:881), Find (:639), GuestOn (:977); TryGetFiller/Hazard | Picking the next block calls these for every unlocked tier, three times, with a closure Find(p => ...), type.ToString(), Enum.TryParse and Split on every roll. | Plain for-loops, a HashSet<PixelType> for guests, parse filler/hazard once per pack. | S-M / low |
| B4 | PixelSkills.AddXpFlat / LevelOf, HasStandardNode (:1109) | Builds "skill."+id+".xp" 3x and loops up to 99 levels twice per click. HasStandardNode builds a string on each call, every frame, from PowerClick, BlockHold, NextUp and Shuffle. | Store keys on SkillPack/TreeNode; a node-unlocked bool cache reset on unlock/load. | S / none |
| B5 | PixelLog.OnPixelCollected (:647) | string.Format + TMP text on every click, even with the Log closed. | Return early when the panel is closed. | S / none |

C. Leaks (objects created and never destroyed)

All S / low-none: give each a shared material + MaterialPropertyBlock, or destroy it with its owner.
- PixelFirePixel.cs:119 PixelFxRing: a new ring Mesh + material instance per spawn (~20 call sites: Fire, farm, treasure...). Cache one ring mesh.
- PixelMold.cs:197-247: a new Material per moldy husk / forage glow.
- PixelLooks.cs:1162 ShineMesh: not cached, and PixelLookShine.Awake orphans the original after Instantiate.
- PixelWeakspotMinigame.cs:497 (also used by Fishing: 2 per spot), PixelMeteorMinigame.cs:373/638/683, PixelBreakoutMinigame.cs:434, PixelEvilTreeMinigame.cs:221, PixelGhostMinigame.BuildBackpackSprite, PixelPets.cs:613 PhysicsMaterial.
- PixelHuskQuery.cs:39-55: the cache only prunes from Near(), so dead husk entries pile up all session. Remove the entry in OldPixelInfo.OnDestroy, or prune every N inserts.

D. Per-physics-step husk scans

| # | Where | Fix | Effort/Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | PixelClickerHusks.IsFlyingPixel (:896): uncached GetComponent<OldPixelFlight> per husk per step (PixelViewBounds:139 always on, 24 caller files); IgnoresViewBounds -> PixelLooks.Find per husk per step | A flight flag on OldPixelInfo / the PixelHuskQuery entry; cache the looks result in ViewBounds' infoCache | S / low |

E. Per-frame strings / layout / scene searches (small each, many of them)

- PixelFind.First in per-frame code -> Cached: PixelFloorModes.cs:111 (every painted floor, always), :177, PixelFloorLive.cs:896, PixelFloor.cs:889, PixelSkyboxRain.cs:34, PixelAchievements.cs:881, PixelSkillsTutorial.cs:80, PixelCrashLog.cs:359 (4 searches every 2 s), PixelBlockTooltip.cs:270, PixelShop.cs:2267.
- PixelFloor.ApplyVisibility (:939) runs GetComponentsInChildren<Renderer> every frame; ApplyProps (:885) runs at full cost every frame on the title screen.
- PixelKeys.Replace builds ~30 strings per call and runs every frame from the Time Stop meter (PixelTimeStop.cs:438) and the placing message (PixelConsumables.cs:2229). Cache inside Replace, cleared on PixelKeys.Changed.
- PixelStatusBox.LateUpdate calls GetPreferredValues every frame for every box (hidden ones too), and PixelStatusLines.Host.Update rebuilds rich text every frame.
- Text formatted every frame while it does not change: PixelUI.UpdateGuide (:2330), UpdateActiveHud (:2398), PixelPlacedDevice.cs:293 timers, PixelCollectionGallery.cs:432, PixelLog.UpdateAchievementTip (:607), tooltips re-measuring (PixelSkillsWindow.UpdateTip, PixelShopCurrency, PixelCrafting.UpdateTip).
- PixelRobotWorker.cs:984/441: new MaterialPropertyBlock per light per frame.
- PixelSaveGame.Update (:530) reads PlayerPrefs.GetInt every frame; the autosave writes pretty-printed JSON (:735). Turn pretty-printing off.
- Settled animations still writing every frame: PixelFancyButton (PixelMenuFx.cs:231), PixelDockedButton (PixelHud.cs:541), PixelCombo effects at alpha 0.

F. Shared helpers that would remove whole classes of the above

1. Pointer / floor raycast helper (HANDOFF #19): one per-frame cached RaycastNonAlloc query + sorted hits. Replaces about 10 copies.
2. PixelHuskQuery as the only husk lookup: add flight / despawn fields, prune on destroy, and a "loose husks this step" list for the machines.
3. Shared tinted materials + one static MaterialPropertyBlock (SetColor(renderer, color)), a cached outlined TMP font material, and a cached ring mesh: these fix most of section C.
4. Throttle inside PixelTierPicture.Render: fixes A2-A4 in one place.
5. Per-enum string table (bounce sound ids, stat keys "skill."+id+".xp", "charges.used."+type).
6. A "format only when the input changed" UI helper next to PixelUIKit.SetText (TMP already skips identical strings, so the waste is in building them).

Checked and fine

Floor animated redraw (throttled + buffer reused), PixelAudio voice pool, PixelBasicFx pool, block/look texture caches, Shop/Inventory/Log/Bank/Toggles/Stats refresh timers, PixelStats, PixelSystems (one-time searches), Recycler capture (10 Hz + PixelHuskQuery), dev tools (all return early when closed), minigame idle Updates, PixelPrefs.Save (only on settings changes).
--- docs/systems/PixelAudio.md ---
PixelAudio

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelAudio.cs

Sound system. Alt-tab keeps the music track: OnApplicationFocus pauses the music source on focus loss and resumes it (same position) on return (musicHeldByFocus); UpdateMusic also remembers the playing clip / time every frame (musicLastClip / musicLastTime) and, when the source has stopped mid-track while the app is focused, resumes that clip at that time instead of picking a new random track (and never picks a track while !Application.isFocused). Named Sound list (id, clips[] picked at random, volume, pitch range, cooldown) filled in the Inspector - an empty/unknown sound is silent; music tracks (shuffle/loop); Master/Effects/Music volume + mute (saved in PlayerPrefs, sliders in the pause menu's Settings via PixelUIKit.CreateSlider); a voice pool; looping sounds via static PixelAudio.StartLoop(id)/StopLoop(id) (first clip of that sound, fades by loopFadeSeconds). Plays by itself from existing events (clicks click/auto_click/pixel_<type>, hit, vacuum, pixel_land (from OldPixelImpact -> static PixelClicker.OldPixelLanded(intensity); volume scales with impact speed), combo (pitch rises), purchase, craft, drink, device_place, achievement, <minigame id>_spawn/_click, ui_click from every PixelUIKit.CreateButton (the shop adds its own), ui_scroll from PixelScrollSound, a pixel type's own bounce pixel_bounce_<type> (e.g. pixel_bounce_electric: OldPixelImpact plays it, volume by impact speed, instead of pixel_land when that sound has clips; Electric's ships as a code-synthesized crackling zap, PixelSynth.Zap, used only while the sound's clip list is empty - assign your own clips to replace it) which is added to every ScrollRect the game builds); other code calls PixelAudio.Play("id"). PixelAudio.Stop("id") cuts off every voice still playing that sound (used by Time Stop / Time Slow when time resumes). The older per-script sound slots (purchaseSound, drinkSound, craftSound, caughtSound, hitSound) still exist. The pause menu adds this component if missing.

Track choice: MusicTrackCount / MusicTrackName(i) / CurrentMusicTrack / SelectMusicTrack(i) (starts the track now, shuffle / order carries on after it); used by the main menu music selector (PixelTitleMusic.cs).

Shuffle / playlist: the FIRST song after the game loads is always random (then shuffleMusic decides: random or in order). Settings > Audio > "Custom playlist" (shown with 2+ tracks) opens a checklist screen (PixelPauseMenu.BuildPlaylistPanel): only ticked songs play (IsTrackEnabled / SetTrackEnabled / EnableAllTracks, PlayerPrefs PixelAudio.PlaylistOff = unticked clip names, so new songs start ticked); the last ticked song cannot be unticked. The main menu selector can still start any song.

Per save (PixelSettingsSync): MusicMuted (the speaker button next to the main menu music selector + Settings > Audio "Mute music"), the custom playlist (PixelClicker.Setting.PlaylistOff). A song picked in the main menu selector (SelectMusicTrack) plays at once but is NOT remembered (2026-10-20: it used to be saved per save file and restarted on every launch / save load, so one song played all the time); every launch starts on a random song and loading a save never changes the song. Named PixelClicker.Setting.* so a new game resets them. Husk vanish: OldPixelDespawn plays pixel_vanish (code-made PixelSynth.Bloop: a small high upward chirp, quiet, 0.04 s cooldown, pitch 0.88-1.3) when the shrink ends.

Playlist preview / speaker right click: each song row of the custom playlist has a small play button left of its tick box (PreviewTrack(i) plays ~8 s starting 30% into the song on its own AudioSource, the music goes quiet meanwhile, again = stop, closing the checklist stops it). Right clicking the main menu speaker opens Settings > Audio directly (PixelPauseMenu.OpenAudioSettingsFromTitle: Audio section unfolded and scrolled into view).

Music cues (PixelAudioCues.cs, partial class PixelAudio): the gameplay music changes at triggers and fades in / out - an Inspector list Music Cues (name, trigger Minigame / Skill / Night / FirstPerson / UfoRide / TitleScreen / Manual, param, priority, clip or playlist track number, volume, loop, fade in / out). The highest-priority true cue plays on its own AudioSource while the playlist fades down and is PAUSED where it was, then resumes when no cue is wanted. Built-in cues (Night, Ghost hunt, Bomb, Black hole, Evil tree, UFO ride, God find, Dragon wish) are added by name WITHOUT music - assign clips to the ones you want. PixelAudio.SetMusicCue(name, on) switches a Manual cue (the God Cube shows and the Dragon wish do). disableCues, addDefaultCues.

Music tempo / beat (TryGetMusicBeat): musicTempos (Inspector list: clip, bpm, firstBeatSeconds) gives each song its tempo; songs not listed use defaultMusicBpm (90) with the first beat at 0 s. PixelAudio.Instance.TryGetMusicBeat(out double beat) returns where the audible music (the cue's source while a cue plays, else the playlist's) is in beats, from the source's sample position; false while no music plays. Used by the main menu cube show (PixelTitleCubeShow.cs), which changes block on each beat.
--- docs/systems/PixelBank.md ---
PixelBank

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

Intended job (design, not built yet): the Bank collects husks for their EFFECT, never to cash in later. It is where you keep husks you cannot use yet (e.g. Electric husks before Electrician is unlocked) and spit them out when a machine or use wants them. Under the pixel rules (PixelRules.md) a stored husk carries no payout (today BankState.Entry.value keeps it and a spat-out pixel pays it again; that goes, with a save migration). Capacity stays one shared total across all types (IsFull = total >= capacity, already the case); the window keeps listing counts per type.

PixelBank.cs

Pixel Bank (shop pack with unlocksBank, Upgrades tab): a Bank tab docked to the middle of the left edge (opposite Toggles) opens a window listing stored pixels per type (click a row to select it); the Bank tab is half width (bankTabWidthFraction 0.5) and a square hose button with the drawn hose icon (DrawHoseIcon, same picture as the window's hose button) sits on its right, so it is the part that slides out first (PixelHud.DockInGroup(..., leftOffset, groupWidth): both buttons slide as one group; PixelDockedButton.PointerNear/SlideAmount); hovering either hose button shows a tip (hoseTipText, "click here or press {0}") and, like the B key, the button equips the hose. While the Bank window is open both buttons stay where they are, so they can be clicked again to close it (FadeTab keeps them visible and clickable; they used to tuck away and fade back in); a hand-drawn hose icon button at the top left of the window (BuildWindowHoseButton) switches the hose on / off (green while on). Hose = cubic-Bezier tube (PixelTube.Build) hanging from the ceiling to a nozzle above the mouse spot. While equipped (PixelBank.HoseOn, sets PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock so the cube ignores clicks; Pixel Grabbing and device removal step aside): mouse wheel cycles stocked pixel types, left-click spits one (a spat-out vacuum-tier pixel runs PixelClicker.Vacuum(tier, target, exclude) like clicking one, but the husks swirl into the SPAT pixel (it keeps flying, they chase it) instead of the cube; PixelClicker.SpawnStoredPixel: a real husk with its own tier look and its stored payout), hold right-click (areaHoldSeconds) = area suction: a ring (areaRadius) around the nozzle pulls in husks of the selected type at areaRate per second (nothing selected -> the type of the pixel nearest the nozzle; stops when the bank is full); a plain right-click sucks up the husk nearest the mouse (within suckRadius, any type except fly-away) into the bank (capacity; ReleaseOldPixel + a fly-in coroutine).
