The hose hangs from the CEILING and the nozzle always points straight down (rewritten: the flick / screen-edge entry point and the X shot arc were dropped as 'wonky'): the mouse ray's first hit (UpdateHose, ignoring the main cube) gives a spot, the nozzle hangs nozzleHeightCubes (0 = 2.5) main-pixel widths above it (lowered so it stays below the top of the screen), the hose is drawn UNLIT with vertex colours (HoseVertexColor: hoseColor x a hand-made light from RenderSettings.sun, alpha fading to 0 from hoseFadeStart (0 = 3) over hoseFadeLength (0 = 4) world units above the nozzle - PixelTube.Build's optional colorOf), so its top fades out instead of being clipped by the camera; the cubic hose runs from hoseOffscreenExtra (0 = 14) units above the nozzle straight down into the swivel ball at the nozzle's back (its top swayTop and middle control trail behind, followSharpness, so it swings). A thin red laser beam (FindBeam: a Raycast straight down from the mouth + a forgiving SphereCast of radius suckRadius / 2 that snaps the dot onto a pixel it brushes; UpdateAimLine, hideAimLine, aimLineAlpha 0 = 0.55, aimLineWidth 0 = 0.025) ends in a pulsing red dot (beamPoint); beamTarget = the husk it is on. Left-click spits the selected pixel straight down the beam (spitSpeed) onto the dot, right-click sucks the pixel the beam is on, hold right-click = area suction in a flat ring round the dot (areaRadius; no selection -> the type under the dot). The nozzle is a modelled assembly, scaled as a whole by nozzleScale (0 = 1.6; the hose end and the hang height use the scaled length) (BuildNozzle, PlaceNozzle: hose collar + orange ring, metal body, grip ring, narrowing front with a flared lip and dark opening, built along -Y from the mouth; +X = screen-up side): a glass cube (displaySize) on a mount on its upper side holds a spinning CreateDisplayPixel model of the selected type (real look), and a dark counter plate on the camera-facing side of the body carries the stored count as 3D text (counterText; a floating text only shows 'Bank full' / 'Bank empty'). The Bank Storage shop upgrade (leveled, UpgradeEffect.BankCapacity, requires the Pixel Bank pack; 5 levels -> capacity 100/160/250/400/600, paid in glass + vacuum pixels + bomb parts) calls PixelBank.SetCapacityOverride. A PackCost can now spend a minigame counter via minigameCurrency (a minigame id; PixelMinigame.TrySpendTracker; shop helpers CanAffordCost / SpendCost / CostName). Stored counts and total value per pixel type are progress in the GameState (PixelGameState.Current.bank, BankState, moved 2026-10-23: saved, loaded and reset with it). Sounds hose_toggle, bank_suck/_spit/_select/_full/_empty. PixelShop finds/adds it.
--- docs/systems/PixelBlockRules.md ---
Special blocks and hold blocks (PixelBlockRules.cs)

One data-driven place for two kinds of skill block. A component (added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelBlockRules.Instance) holds two [Serializable] lists; built-in rules are added by name at start if missing (context menu "Add Missing Default Rules").

Special blocks - rare replacements
SpecialRule: tier (the block's PixelTier: name, colour, clicks, pay), skillId (empty = any skill in play), replaces (block types it can take the place of), chance.
PixelClicker.PickSpawnTier ends with PixelBlockRules.TryReplace(clicker, pick); the tier is created on first use by PixelClicker.EnsureSpecialTier (no spawn weight, unlocked so the cube shows it).
Default: Great Oak (Woodcutting, replaces Wood / Bark / Sap / Leaf / Heartwood / Twig, 1% rising to 25% as Acorn husks lie on the field (hasBoost, boostBlock, boostFullCount 12, maxChance), 50-100 clicks, pays 150; nests: PixelGreatOak.cs).
Not migrated yet: Fishing's Whale / Bottle / Pearl (PixelFishingSpawns, their chances feed the Pixel Guide's SpawnShare) - move them here when next touched.

Hold blocks - bonus while held
HoldRule: type, skillId, extraClicks, burstGapSeconds, payoutMultiplier, xpMultiplier, clearsHazard + hazardType, label. Held = Block Hold (right button on the cube, PixelBlockHold). While held:
- extraClicks: RAPID CLICKS like Overcharge - every manual click is followed by n extra full clicks, burstGapSeconds apart (PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> PixelBlockRules.OnManualClick -> AutoCollect; the burst never starts another burst).
- payoutMultiplier: every payout (CollectInternal).
- xpMultiplier: that skill's XP (PixelSkills.AddXp).
- label: shown beside the hold meter (e.g. "Clicks x3").
- clearsHazard / hazardType: a manual left click on that hazard block clears it at once, safely (PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> ClearHazardNow; no explosion, no XP penalty, no payout).
Defaults: Pesticide (Woodcutting base block, 3 hits, clears Termite Hives (enum HornetNest)) and Whetstone (Woodcutting base block, 3 hits, 2 extra rapid clicks = 3 at once).

Adding one
1. Append a PixelType, give it a look (PixelLooks default + a looksVersion migration) and spawn data.
2. Hold block: make it a normal base block of its skill pack (shop pack reward tier + a packDataVersion migration for saved packs + the skill's types list). Special block: nothing else (the tier is made by the rule).
3. Add ONE rule to the list. Behaviour beyond numbers stays in its own script (listen to PixelClicker.PixelHit / PixelCollected).

On the Pixel Guide
Special and hold blocks appear on the guide by themselves: PixelBlockRules.EnsureActiveSpecialTiers makes the tiers of the skill in play when the guide refreshes (so the Great Oak is listed from the start of Woodcutting), the row's sub line is tagged "SPECIAL, 5% replaces a block" / "HOLD block", and PixelBlockRules.GuideNotes adds the role sentences to the '!' tooltip (which blocks it replaces and the chance; what holding it does). Spawn shares include the replacements (PixelClicker.SpawnShare = AdjustShare(SpawnShareBase)): a special block shows chance x the share of what it replaces, and those blocks show a little less. Each new block still needs its own flavour case in PixelGuideTexts.Mechanics.

Stall (a trait on any block)
PixelTier.stallsHold (+ stallSeconds, 0 = 1.5): every click on such a block freezes the Block Hold meter's drain for that long and adds stallRefill (0 = 4%) of the meter (each click restarts it; PixelClicker.CollectInternal -> PixelBlockHold.Stall). The meter turns icy white-blue while frozen. Independent of hit count and of the special / hold roles. Tagged "STALL" on the Pixel Guide with its own sentence. Default: the Great Oak (so a held Whetstone / Pesticide keeps its bonus while you work through an oak).

Hazards that drain the hold meter
HazardHoldPenalty list: when a hazard GOES OFF (destroyed by clicking it, PixelClicker.TriggerHazard -> PixelBlockRules.OnHazardWentOff) the Block Hold meter loses loseFraction of its CURRENT charge (PixelBlockHold.LoseFraction; the meter flashes red briefly). Clearing the hazard safely (right clicks, a held Pesticide) does not count. Default: Hornet Nest, 50%.

Charge blocks (HoldRule.chargesWhenHeld)

A hold rule with chargesWhenHeld > 0 turns its block into a CHARGE block: while it is held (Block Hold) it shrinks to nothing over chargeSeconds and hands out its charges one at a time, then it shrinks to husk size and pops off as a husk worth nothing (PixelBlockHold.UpdateCharging / UseUp, PixelClicker.SpawnStoredPixel with amount 0; it does not go back in line). Letting go after the first charge also uses it up, so tapping cannot farm charges. Charges are stat counters charges.<Hazard> (saved with the stats): PixelBlockRules.Charges / AddCharges / UseCharge. When that hazard is on the cube for PixelClicker.chargeClearDelay (0.35 s), one charge clears it automatically and safely (PixelClicker.UpdateHazardCharges -> ClearHazardNow, stat charges.used.<Hazard>).
Default: Pesticide = 3 charges over 1 second against the Termite Hive (HornetNest); it no longer clears a hive with a click while held (its old clearsHazard rule is switched off at start). The tree bar shows the charge count on an infested tree.
--- docs/systems/PixelConsumables.md ---
PixelConsumables

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

Inventory progress (2026-10-24): what you hold (Potion.owned / bonusCap, Device.owned) lives in the GameState (InventoryState, by key; see docs/systems/PixelGameState.md); placed devices and the running potion copy themselves in on PixelGameState.Saving and are rebuilt on Loaded. Overrides older save wording below.

Device models (2026-10-24): each device builds its own look in its own script: PixelFanDevice.BuildModel (+ WedgeMesh), PixelVacuumDevice, PixelMagnetDevice, PixelSprinklerDevice, PixelLightningRod, PixelChargeBooster, PixelQuantumDevice, PixelStorageCrate (all BuildModel(clicker, root, device, isPreview, previewOpacity)). BuildDeviceObject here only picks one and adds the shared range disc, suction point and timer. A NEW device's model goes in its own script the same way (the newer machines already use CreateGhost / CreateDisplay / Place).

Attuned devices (PixelConsumables.AppendAttunedDevices, PixelCrafting.FindAttuneRecipe)

Crafting a Vacuum Device, Fan or Magnet with a plain (single-type) potion (ResultKind.Attuned, any box order, uses up one of each) makes an attuned copy that only affects husks of the potion's pixel type. For every potion type a craft-only Device clone (attuned, attuneType, name 'Attuned <device> (<Type>)', colour tinted by the pixel) is appended at runtime AFTER all normal devices (so indexes never shift; FindDeviceOfKind skips them; ItemAttuned, FindAttunedDevice); the saves keep only the ones you hold. PixelPlacedDevice.AttuneTier / Accepts(body) (set in SpawnDevice) is checked by the Vacuum / Fan / Magnet FixedUpdate. The crafting materials list always shows those three device kinds (when held).

PixelConsumables.cs

Potions (one per pixel type, editable list: name, type, costs, duration, owned) and placeable devices (Vacuum Device: right-click in inventory → cylinder follows mouse → left-click floor to place; right-click cancels). Unified Item* accessors (potions first, then devices) used by shop/inventory/save. Drinking one calls PixelClicker.SetForcedSpawnTier for its duration. Instant-use items (the Supplies category: IsSupplyItem, CategoryOf 0 potion / 1 device / 2 seed / 3 supply; a Supplies card in the shop's Consumables tab (suppliesCardTitle) and a 4th Inventory category) are devices of kind ComboFuel / GhostBait / PetTreat (not placed; Device.maxHeld = hold limit, 0 = unlimited, drives ItemCapacity / ItemRoom / ClampDeviceHeld and the shop's Full text; defaults auto-added to saved scenes like the fan): Combo Fuel (hold 3) is used automatically by PixelCombo when the timer bar would run out (TryUseAutoItem): the combo is kept, the bar rebuilds over fuelRefillSeconds in fuelBarColor, a click ends the refill, otherwise it then runs down normally. Ghost Bait (hold 1; right-click in the inventory = TryUseItem -> PixelGhostMinigame.UseBait, needs the minigame running): the next ghost comes within baitFirstDelay and baitGhosts (3) ghosts follow baitGap apart, each crossing baitSlowFactor times slower. Pet Treat (hold 5; right-click -> PixelPets.TryUseTreat, needs a pet out): for treatSeconds (60) pets are hyper (hop treatHyperFactor x as often, a bit harder; the Ghost pet drifts faster), glow (a point light + halo child "Treat Glow") and the extra spawn chance they give is multiplied by treatExtraFactor (2). Seeds are devices of kind Seed (Device.seedType, one per growable pixel type, auto-added after the other devices by EnsureDefaultPotions; not Meteor / Seed / Dragon Cubes; IsSeedItem / SeedTypeOf): price worked out in ItemCosts from the pixel's payout (seedPriceFactor 3 x amountPerClick x Value multiplier x (1 / spawn weight)^seedRarityPricePower 0.5, min seedMinPrice 3, paid in that same pixel, so low tiers are cheap and rare ones dear), hold limit seedMaxHeld 200; right-click in the Inventory (Consumables > Seeds category) = placement mode like devices (a sprout preview follows the mouse; each left click on the floor plants one via PixelClicker.PlantSeedSproutAt(point, tier) -> a SeedSprout that grows exactly that pixel type (SeedSprout.ForceTier), keeps planting while seeds remain, right-click / Escape stops; stat seeds.planted); excluded from the Tinkerer and the Gadgeteer achievement. PixelShop sells them (Consumables tab); PixelShop auto-adds this component if missing.

Prices (repriced 2026-10-17, design in docs/GameDesign.md section 8)
- Every pure-currency device costs 2-3 of the six pure colours: monochrome (White / Gray / Black) for machines, RGB for boosts and timed items, one RGB accent allowed on a machine. Priced in minutes of income (the 10:50 session report: White 113, Gray 85, Black 80, Red 135, Green 128, Blue 160 per minute): a cheap tool ~1 minute of each colour it costs, a milestone tool ~3-5.
- Skill items (Wood Chipper, Bait Bucket, Conveyor Belt Kit, Lumberjack's Focus, Pesticide Spray) stay in their skill's blocks; costs in a pack's own material (Glass, Electric, Water, Vacuum, Obsidian) are kept. Potions and seeds are priced at run time from their pixel's output (potionPriceFromOutput, seedPriceFactor), in that pixel.
- The Recycler's price doubles for each one held or standing (priceGrowthPerOwned 2).
- The coded defaults reach a saved scene through consumablePriceVersion (1 = this repricing) -> ResetDefaultDevicePrices (also the context menu 'Reset Built-in Device Prices'); DefaultDeviceMakers is the one table of built-in device kinds. Bump the version after the next repricing.

Dev Tools-only devices (2026-10-18)
- Device.devOnly: never sold by the shop or a visitor (ItemNotSold covers it, so the shop tabs, search, TryBuyItems and visitors' wares all skip it); Dev Hub > Main > Dev items widget gives one (PixelConsumables.ItemDevOnly). Saved scenes took the flag through consumablePriceVersion 2.
- Dev-only now: Quantum Organizer, Funnel, Sprinkler, Vacuum Device, Bait Bucket, Conveyor Belt Kit (the kit is also placed from Dev Hub > Tester > Conveyor Belt). Attuned Vacuum Devices still exist in Crafting but need a Vacuum Device from the Dev Tools.

Fixes 2026-10-20 (audit). Buying several of an item at once adds up its rising price step by step (ItemCostsTotal, used by the buy bar, Max and TryBuyItems). Held counts are cut to their caps only after the Operation Storage levels have loaded (the inventory part keeps the saved counts; the world part's Import clamps, ClampAllHeld). Potion, combo and attuned names and {pixel} use the tier's display name (TierName). Device texts can use {held} (the cap incl. Storage), {charges} (Pesticide Spray) and {uses} besides {radius} / {duration}; scene-stored texts are switched to these placeholders and to 'husk' wording on start (UseDescriptionPlaceholders). Drinking a potion while one runs says so in the event log. The Inventory item tooltip shows the item's name, description and how to use it. The category is called Utilities everywhere. Devices the UFO places stay switched on (its job); dev-placed ones start disarmed like the player's (StartsDisarmed). The Bait Bucket (baitBucket) and the running Focus potion (focus) are saved.
--- docs/systems/PixelCouplings.md ---
Sockets and couplings - one way to connect belts and hoses to devices

Agreed in the Design Discussion chat (2026-10-17), BUILT in the Devices session the same day (not yet tested in Unity). Code: PixelSockets.cs (sockets, kinds, the device
hooks, the registry, auto-fit, the type filter, the connection resolver) and PixelCoupling.cs (the snap-on coupling piece). Since 2026-10-20
couplings FIT THEMSELVES when a belt or hose attaches (below); Dev Hub > Tester > Coupling still fits one by hand. Sockets carry HUSKS only:
power will be a separate mechanic and will not use sockets.

The problem it replaced
- Six machines each had their own belt intake (IPixelBeltAcceptor in PixelConveyor.cs: Kiln, Storage Crate, Trash Can, Splitter, Elevator,
  Conveyor Connector), each with its own "is this point at my opening" zone; the Container Hose fed them through the same interface.
- The Kiln and Crate also had a separate opening marker for the Conveyor Connector (IPixelPort).
- Outputs were hand-wired: the Funnel's floor output, the Wood Chipper's chute (plus its placing latch onto a belt), the Elevator top, the
  Splitter sides and the Connector's far end each had their own copy of "find a belt that starts here and drop the husk on it".
- The Recycler and the Sorting Hopper could not take a belt at all.

The design
- Sockets (data). Every device declares its SOCKETS: a few lines each - id, local position (the PORT, where a belt end / hose end meets
  it, at belt-underside height), outward facing, flow = Intake / Output / Both (and kind, always Husk). Free sockets always show a small PORT PLATE (PixelSocketPlates: a chevron, green In / orange Out / teal Both). Sockets sit on openings the device already has:
  Kiln mouth, Storage Crate front, Trash Can side, Recycler back hatch, Quantum Organizer side, Wood Chipper chute, Funnel output, Elevator entry / top, Junction Box (eight, auto direction), Belt Sorter chute.
- Couplings (one shared piece). ONE model (PixelFactoryKit) and ONE component. It is fitted automatically (auto-fit, below); it is parented to
  the device so it moves with it (carry handles, turning); removing the device removes its couplings. It is tinted a little with the device's
  colour (SocketTint) so it reads as a flange, not a bolt-on.
- Direction is shown on the coupling: a green arrow pointing INTO the device = Intake, an orange arrow pointing OUT = Output, teal arrows
  both ways = Both.
- Fitted couplings. The factory pieces that ARE belt fittings (Elevator, Belt Sorter; the Splitter until it was removed) come with couplings fitted on their sockets
  (PixelSocket.fitted): built with the piece, not saved, not removable on their own. Everything else gets one by auto-fit.
- Belts and hoses connect to a COUPLING, never to the device directly:
  - a belt END at an Intake coupling's port hands the husk in; a belt START at an Output coupling's port is fed by it;
  - a Container Hose starts on an Output (or Both) coupling, runs through any bend points the player clicks, and ends on an Intake one; a quick right click on it reverses its flow;
  - placing snaps to coupling ports AND free socket ports (PixelCouplings.Ports, PixelContainerHose.Snap).
- ONE device hook for husks in: IPixelSocketDevice.AcceptHusk(socket, husk) - the husk is kinematic and still on the husk list; the device
  takes it (true) or refuses (false, e.g. full). A refused husk is ejected by whatever brought it, like before (a belt throws it on, a hose drops it).
- Out: a device that emits a husk calls PixelCouplings.Emit(device, socketId, husk): with an Output coupling on that socket and a belt
  starting at its port (with room), the husk goes onto the belt; otherwise it returns false and the device does its own thing (fling / drop).
  PixelCouplings.OutputHasRoom lets a device wait (the Funnel's backpressure).
- Optional extras (not every device): IPixelHuskSource (a hose may pull stored husks out: Storage Crate, Quantum Organizer, Junction Box, Husk Source),
  IPixelHuskPicker (the same, but only a husk a 'want' test accepts), IPixelHuskRoom (how many more husks fit: a hose only pumps what fits) and
  IPixelHuskFilter (which types it takes: Biomass Generator = wood, Crafting Cube = what its recipe still needs). No default interface methods (Unity).
  PixelCouplings.EmitStored = a storing device lets one stored husk out onto what is attached (Storage Crate out, Quantum Organizer side set to Out).

Auto-fit (2026-10-20)
- A belt END at a free socket's port fits an In coupling there, a belt START an Out one (PixelCouplings.AutoFitAll: run when a belt is placed and on every
  PixelSocketPlates refresh, 0.3 s, so a device carried next to a belt or loaded later joins too). A hose end fits one the way the hose runs (AutoFitAt from the hose's latch).
- An Intake socket only takes In, an Output socket only Out; a Both socket follows what attached (SetDirection); an auto socket (Junction Box) works it out itself.
- A just-laid hose may also turn an existing Both coupling to match, but only while nothing else is attached to it.
- Auto-fitted couplings stay (they are not taken off when the belt goes) and save with the device like before (Export / Import).
- The Coupling shop item is retired (dev-only, consumablePriceVersion 7); the Dev Hub's Dev items / Tester card still fit one by hand.

Type-aware intake (2026-10-20)
- PixelCouplings.Takes(device, socket, tier) asks IPixelHuskFilter (true when the device doesn't filter).
- The Container Hose pumps only while the target has room AND, when its source is an IPixelHuskPicker (crate, organizer, Junction Box), takes out only a husk
  the target takes - a mixed crate hosed into a Biomass Generator sends just the wood. Its idle line says "Nothing the target takes in the source".
- The Kiln takes any amount (each husk burns on its own); the Crafting Cube's room is per type (its filter covers it).

Connection resolver (2026-10-20)
PixelCouplings.Attached(coupling) = what it is joined to: BeltIn / BeltOut (a belt ending / starting at it), HoseIn / HoseOut (a hose pushing / pulling),
DeviceOut (another device's In coupling right in front), Nothing. Worked out on demand, never per frame for everything. Used by:
- the device panel's port rows (AddPortRows -> Describe: "Port side: Out: feeds a belt", "Port in: free - a belt or hose attaches here");
- the Junction Box's auto direction;
- the hose: when the player flips an end's coupling (its teal dot - only the dot is clickable now) the hose FOLLOWS (reverses) when the other end can work
  that way, else lets go of that end and idles until it is switched back.
- Saving: couplings are saved WITH THEIR DEVICE - PlacedState.couplings for shop devices, TesterSave.couplings for tester machines
  (a list of socket ids); belts re-attach after a load by position as before.
- Unchanged on purpose: a device's own natural intake (husks falling into the Kiln mouth, the Recycler's open top, the Trash Can's suction)
  works with or without couplings - couplings are only for belts and hoses.

Husks only (2026-10-20)
Sockets carry husks only: PixelSocketKind keeps just Husk = 0 (the field stays so saved data reads). The Power / Signal / Water / Heat / Link kinds
planned earlier were dropped - power will be its own mechanic, not ports. Signal / Water / Heat ideas stay in Assets/Resources/Ideas.txt.

Later (noted, not built)
- BOUGHT (2026-10-17; the Coupling RETIRED 2026-10-20, couplings fit themselves): the Coupling (40 Gray + 40 Black, hold 10) and Container Hose (80 White + 80 Black, hold 3) are shop items in Utilities, right after the Recycler and
  Sorting Hopper; right-click one to start placing (PixelInstantItems -> PixelFeatureTester.StartFeature with a 'spend one' callback: one is used up per
  coupling fitted / hose laid, placing stops when none are left). The Dev Hub Tester cards still place them free. Unlocking them by progression is still open.
- A device can have more sockets than you fill (the Junction Box has eight).

Build order (how it was moved over)
1. Sockets + coupling + hook next to the old interfaces. 2. Devices moved one at a time: Storage Crate, Kiln, Trash Can, Splitter, Elevator,
Container Hose, Connector, then the Funnel and Chipper outputs, then the Recycler + Sorting Hopper (+ Quantum Organizer for hoses) as new
intakes. 3. IPixelBeltAcceptor, IPixelPort and the hand-made output latches deleted, so there is one way to connect. 4. The Conveyor Connector
and Hose Adapter were then deleted as no longer needed, and the Container Hose became freely routed (bend points) with a right click to reverse it. The Belt Sorter's
chute (the same "find a belt that starts here" copy) moved onto a built-in bare socket too. Kiln got a second socket, its charcoal chute (Out).

Where each device's sockets are
| Device | Sockets |
|---|---|
| Storage Crate | in (In, front left: belts / hoses in) and out (Out, front right: a hose pulls stored husks; a belt starting there is fed one stored husk at a time while it has room) |
| Quantum Organizer | side (Both, like the crate; In drops husks onto the pad; Out: a hose pulls stored husks, a belt starting there is fed one every 0.6 s) |
| Biomass Generator | intake (In, wood husks only: IPixelHuskFilter) |
| Crafting Cube | front, back, left, right (In, the types its recipe still needs: IPixelHuskFilter) |
| Kiln | mouth (In, burns it), chute (Out, charcoal) |
| Trash Can | front, back (In) |
| Recycler | hatch (In, the back hatch), f0..f3 (In, four holes low on the front) |
| Funnel | output (Out, the spout end; the tube waits while its belt is full) |
| Wood Chipper | chute (Out, on the floor in front of the outlet) |
| Conveyor Elevator | 8 built-in bare sockets (2026-10-20): in0-in3 (Intake) at the base on each side and out0-out3 (Output) at the top on each side, at the belt snap points; puts husks out with Emit, waits while the belt is full, flings when nothing is attached. |
| Belt Jump | Built-in bare sockets: in (Intake, ramp start) and out (Output, landing end); the landing waits while the belt there is full. |
| Conveyor Combiner | Built-in bare sockets: inL / inR (Intake), one per side inlet; husks merge onto the main belt or wait (up to queueSize, then it refuses and the feeding belt holds). |
| Sorting Hopper | green / orange / blue / pink (Output, not built-in: a coupling fits itself when a belt starts there), one per shown output pipe, one husk below its mouth; EmitStored, waits while full; with nothing attached husks drop from the mouth. No inputs. |
| Husk Source (tester) | front, back, left, right (Out: endless husks of the picked type while something takes them) |
| Junction Box | f0 f1 (front), b0 b1 (back), l0 l1, r0 r1 - all Both, auto direction |
| Belt Sorter | chute (Out, fitted, bare) |

The Junction Box (built 2026-10-18, Devices session; design from the Design Discussion chat)
PixelJunctionBox.cs, DeviceKind.JunctionBox 26: a closed box like the Recycler's (carry handles) with eight sockets, two per side, all Both + autoDirection.
- Auto direction (PixelSocket.autoDirection, PixelCoupling.SetAuto): a coupling's way comes from what is attached - a belt ENDING at its port
  (PixelCouplings.BeltEndingAt) or a hose PUSHING into it (PixelContainerHose.Pushes) = In; a belt STARTING there (BeltStartingAt) or a hose PULLING
  from it (PixelContainerHose.Pulls) = Out; nothing = idle (no arrow; counts both ways, so belts and hoses can snap to it). Rechecked every routeSeconds.
- Routing (AcceptHusk): 1) the Out coupling whose FILTER is the husk's type (waits in the buffer while that one is full); 2) else round robin over the
  unfiltered Out couplings with a belt that has room (Emit); 3) else the BUFFER (bufferSize 8, data); full = refused. Hoses pulling from an Out coupling
  take from the buffer (IPixelHuskSource: the filter type there, or any type no filtered output wants).
- In = deleted, out = new (2026-10-18): every husk that goes in is deleted (it shrinks into the box) and its type + value come out as a NEW husk
  spawned at an output onto its belt (or wait in the buffer). Top opening: a square hole in the lid takes husks that drop in, with a gentle suction
  for husks just above the box (Inspector: suckRadiusHusks, suckHeightHusks, suckStrength). A Sorting Hopper's feed pipe reaches over the opening.
- Filters: one per coupling - drop a husk you were holding on it, or click / scroll it (unlocked types + none); a small block spins over it; hover shows the card.
  The Sweeper sees it as a container with room (buffer room).
- Saved as a placed device (PlacedState.extra: filters + buffer). Price / unlock: the Shops session (monochrome, as machines are; a placeholder Gray + Black cost is in the entry, vendorOnly so it is not sold yet).

Overlap with the old tester pieces (reported 2026-10-18)
- Splitter (tester): round robin out of three sides - fully covered by an unfiltered Junction Box. Removed the same day at the user's request.
- Belt Sorter (tester): sends one type off to one side of a belt - covered by a Junction Box with one filtered Out coupling and one unfiltered one.
  It still differs: it sits IN a belt line (no couplings needed) and is cheaper in space. Recommendation: retire it once the Junction Box is on sale
  and players have it; until then keep it as the in-line option.

Adding a device
Implement IPixelSocketDevice (SocketRoot, Sockets, SocketTint, AcceptHusk), build the socket list once the device knows its size, call
PixelCouplings.Register / Unregister in OnEnable / OnDisable, and put husks out with PixelCouplings.Emit(this, id, husk) (false = do your own thing).
A piece that is a belt fitting itself marks its sockets fitted (and bare if its own shape shows the way) and calls PixelCoupling.FitBuiltIn(this)
after building (and PixelCoupling.RemoveAll(this) before a rebuild). If it takes only some types, implement IPixelHuskFilter; if it stores husks a hose can
pull, IPixelHuskSource (+ IPixelHuskPicker). Nothing else: auto-fit, the port plates, the device panel rows, saving, snapping and the belt system pick it up.

Link rules (2026-10-20, conveyor audit). One reach for every link check: PixelCouplings.LinkReach (1.3 husk widths, flat): a belt end hands in to a coupling (HandIn) and a coupling finds the belt ending / starting at it (BeltEndingAt / BeltStartingAt, nearest wins) at the same distance, so a belt that shows as joined really is. Emit never puts a husk on a FULL belt (it returns false); devices that must not drop it ask OutputHasRoom first and hold it (Kiln, Wood Chipper, Belt Jump landing queue, Funnel, Junction Box, Husk Source, Storage Crate). The Belt Sorter lets a husk ride on when the belt off its chute is full.
--- docs/systems/PixelCrafting.md ---
PixelCrafting

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelCrafting.cs

Crafting (shop pack with unlocksCrafting, Upgrades tab): bottom-right "Crafting" button opens a window laid out like the SHOP: the window takes the shop window's size (PixelShop.PanelSize, capped to the space between the black bars) and a Materials panel hangs off its left exactly where the shop's Currency panel is (BuildMaterialsPanel, width / gap = PixelShop.CurrencyPanelWidth/Gap): a scrolling list of one row per owned item formatted like the Currency rows (spinning cube - or a glass cube with a smaller pixel cube inside for potions - on the left, small name on top, bold amount underneath; drag a row into a box or click it). The window itself holds the two drop boxes, a recipe/cost readout and a Craft button in a Body that FitWindow centres a little in the free height. Drag (or click) grid items into the boxes; Recipe list in the Inspector (two ingredients, pixel currency or potions, any order -> a potion or device); defaults = each pixel + Glass -> that pixel's potion; plus automatic combo recipes: a potion + ANY pixel -> a 2-type combo potion, and a 2-type combo potion + one more pixel -> a 3-type combo potion (Vacuum and Dragon Cubes can't be part of a combo) (FindComboRecipe, ResultKind.Combo / Triple; PixelConsumables craft-only potions that spawn their 2-3 types evenly via PixelClicker.SetForcedSpawnTiers(a, b, c?); ALL pair and triple potions (~1100) are generated once at start by AppendComboPotions (flagged craftOnly, hasThird / thirdType, looked up by a bit mask comboLookup, FindComboPotion(a, b[, c])) so item indexes never shift; hidden from the shop; the save stores only the ones you hold (InventoryState.held, key "p:type:second:third"; the running potion's activeSecond / activeThird); the Inventory makes a potion's row only when first held, CreatePotionRow). Combo ingredients are Ingredient.combo/second/hasThird/third (runtime) and Item.hasThird/third. Easier crafting: clicking a row in the Materials list puts that item in the first free box (QuickPlace); clicking a KNOWN recipe in the Recipe Book fills both boxes with its ingredients (FillBoxes / Decompositions; auto-filled boxes are pinned so they stay even if you lack the item); with one box filled, every Materials row that makes nothing with it is greyed out and can't be clicked / dragged (Cell.disabled); Craft x1 / x5 / Max buttons above Craft (craftAmount, MaxCraftable, the Craft button reads 'Craft x N'; recipe stats are counted per canonical key recipe.c.<sorted types>); the Recipe Book lists every Recipes entry + every 2-type combo (??? until crafted) + only the 3-type combos you made, has a search box (known recipes only, bookSearch) and its known rows are clickable (fill the boxes). Right-click a box to empty it. The boxes are sunken frames with a glowing rim and a breathing '+' while empty (AnimateSlots), and the how-to text is gone: hovering a box shows slotTipText and hovering a material materialTipText in a small tooltip that follows the mouse (craftTip, UpdateTip; hidden while dragging). A hand-drawn book button (top-left of the window) toggles the Recipe Book (BuildBook): a panel to the RIGHT of the window, as tall as it and as wide as the Materials panel (like the shop tutorial's 'Got it' boxes), recipes as uniform two-line rows (BookLine: the result in bold, what it is made of dimmer underneath); Escape closes it first: AllRecipes() = the Recipes list + every automatic combo recipe; a recipe is shown once it was crafted (PixelStats counter RecipeKey(r) = recipe.<kind>.<potion>.<second>.<device>, set in Craft; owning the result also counts) and as ??? otherwise. Crafting level (placeholder, nothing uses it yet): every craft gives XP (XpPerCraft: potion xpPotion 5, 2-type combo xpCombo 12, 3-type xpTriple 25, device xpDevice 30, per craft made, plus xpDiscovery 20 the first time a recipe is crafted; 0 = default) into the stat counter crafting.xp (saved generically); CraftingLevel / LevelOf (level 2 needs xpFirstLevel 40, each next xpGrowth 1.35 x more); a level bar with 'Crafting level N - x / y XP' runs along the bottom of the window (BuildLevelBar, RefreshLevelBar), a level-up posts an event-log line, and every craft shows its own cyan '+n XP' that rises over the bar (ShowXpPopup, separate from the cursor / cube +n popups). Spends via PixelClicker.TrySpend / PixelConsumables.TryRemoveItem, adds via AddItem. PixelShop finds/adds it.

Crafting update (current). Every pixel except the Dragon Cubes has a single potion now (pixel + Glass; IsStandalone types too: ores, skill blocks, Fire...). Combo potions (2 / 3 types) still use only the non-standalone pixels (AppendComboPotions, FindComboRecipe: standalone pixels would make tens of thousands of combos and their enum values are past the 64-bit Bit mask). The Recipe Book is EMPTY at the start: RecipeKnown is only true once the recipe was crafted (stat RecipeKey), no more ??? rows and owning the result no longer counts. The Materials panel has a search box (materialSearch, filters by name, ignores folded categories while searching) and foldable category headers Pixels / Potions / Devices (materialsCollapsed, MaterialHeader pool, RefreshList lays headers and the pooled cells out by y).
--- docs/systems/PixelDevTools.md ---
PixelDevTools

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelDevTools Changelog tab

The Changelog moved from the pause menu to the Dev Tools panel as a third tab (BuildChangelogView / RebuildChangelog, changelogResource 'Changelog', newest first); the right half of the tab row is shared by the To-Do and Changelog buttons. The pause menu's Changelog button / screen are no longer built (the old fields stay unused).

PixelDevTools.cs

Dev tools (Editor/dev builds only unless allowInFullBuild is ticked on the component; removes itself otherwise): the Dev Tools window (it was first opened by a glowing dock button at the bottom-centre of the screen, since removed: see 'Keys and the dock button' below; PixelHud.Dock supports anchor x = 0.5 = centred; no longer in the pause menu) is a panel (registered in PixelWindows, Escape closes it): pixel dropdown + amount field + Add, add-to-all (both also give the same number of Ultra pixels via GiveAmount, alsoAddUltra), minigame dropdown + Spawn (PixelMinigame.SpawnNow/DisplayName), Unlock all shop items (PixelShop.DevUnlockAll), tick box for Q = PixelClicker.ClearOldPixels, Close is an X button at the top right of the panel (no bottom Close button). Layout (current): pixel drop-down | amount field | green plus button (Add) | yellow paw button (Give pet: the selected pixel's pet, or every pet quietly for All pixels; there is no separate give-all button) - both drawn in code by DevIcons (PlusSprite/PawSprite/FileSprite); 'Give next Dragon Cube', 'Unlock all shop items', 'Reset all counts to 0'; a file-icon button at the TOP LEFT of the panel opens the crash report folder; the god mode row reads 'God Pixel Mode' with its controls as a hover tooltip (AddTip / PixelDevTip, PixelHoverTip). The panel uses PixelUIKit.StyleWindow like every window. Input row "Clicks per click" (clickCount, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.ClickCount, synced per save): sets static PixelClicker.DevClickCount N - one click counts as N clicks everywhere: payout x N (on top of any multiplier), tough pixels take N hits per click (extra hits carry over), timesCollected += breaks, and the combo grows by N (PixelCombo.AddClick). tick box "Infinite resources" (PixelClicker.InfiniteResources: TrySpend/CanAfford/Ultra boosts cost nothing, and consumables are unlimited too: PixelConsumables.ItemOwned reports 99999 of every item, using / placing / planting / crafting never decrements and the shop never says Full; the Pixel Bank too: it never fills up (TakeIntoBank skips the capacity check, window shows 'Stored: N / Infinite'), spitting never lowers the stored counts and area suction runs 10x faster; the hose can spit every unlocked ordinary pixel type even if none is stored - PixelBank.Has - paying RollOldPixelAmount, and the nozzle plate shows an infinity sign). Dev tool settings (tick boxes, amount, drop-down choices) are remembered in PlayerPrefs (PixelClicker.Dev.*, rememberSettings). The pixel drop-down's first entry is "All pixels" (replaces the old add-to-all button). "Unlock all" leaves everything with a Toggles switch (auto clicker, grabbing, time stop, combo, minigames, spawn switches) unlocked but OFF. Tick box "Disable husk despawns" (OldPixelDespawn.DevNoDespawn). Tick box "Disable husk limit" (disableOldPixelLimit, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.NoLimit, PixelClicker.DevNoOldPixelLimit: CopyCap returns 0 = no cap on how many husks lie around, the floor cap is ignored). Tick box "God pixel mode" (PixelClicker.GodMode blocks cube clicks / grabbing / device removal): small display next to the cursor, scroll = pick a pixel, left = SpawnStoredPixel at the cursor, right = destroy husks near it. Tick box "On-screen spawn selector" (spawnSelectorEnabled; also holds the minigame drop-down + Spawn button): a drop-down row at the top of the screen (with the minigame drop-down + Spawn + Despawn buttons; Despawn = PixelMinigame.DespawnNow) (normal / Monochrome pack = White+Gray+Black / RGB pack = Red+Green+Blue / every pixel type incl. locked ones) that calls PixelClicker.SetDevSpawnTiers so only those types spawn. Middle-click swap: a third drop-down at the end of the on-screen selector row ('Middle click: <pixel>', every type incl. locked, swapDropdown, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.SelSwap); with the selector on, a middle click (PixelInput.MiddlePressed, not over UI) calls PixelClicker.DevSwapCurrentPixel(type) which replaces the pixel on the cube at once (devSwapped keeps even a locked type until it is clicked; the next pixel rolls normally). Uses PixelUIKit.CreateDropdown/CreateInputField. Escape closes the panel first. To-Do tab (Tools | To-Do buttons at the top of the panel; BuildTodoView, RebuildTodo, todoResource = Assets/Resources/Todo.txt): a scrolling list of that file's lines ([ ] open, [x] done, # Heading, // hidden note). Button "Reset all counts to 0" (second click confirms; PixelClicker.ResetAllCounts + PixelBank.SetState(null)): zeroes every pixel count, Log lifetime total and click count and the Bank, keeps unlocks, Ultra pixels, shop packs and achievements.

Keys and the dock button (current). The Dev Tools window opens / closes with PixelAction.DevTools (default F9, rebindable in Settings > Key bindings; the three dev keys are hidden outside the Editor / development builds). The glowing dock button and its tick box were REMOVED (the Dev Hub window made them obsolete; so were the button's halo effect, the fade-when-idle of the dock button and the old on-screen spawn selector, and the "Skip intro" button). The old "Unlock All" button is gone: the Unlock widget's 'All shop items' does PixelShop.DevUnlockAll (every shop item and ordinary pixel type, switchable things left off) and the Max XP widget fills skills (see "Unlock and Max XP widgets"). PixelAction.MarkMoment (F10) and PixelAction.InspectHover (F11) call PixelDevProbe.MarkMoment() / Inspect() at once (screenshot + state report; what is under the mouse). Tools buttons 'Snapshot bounds', 'Mark this moment' and 'UI layout dump' wait boundsSnapshotDelay seconds first (so the panel can be closed). Reports land in <persistentDataPath>/Snapshots and on the clipboard (PixelDevProbe.Save). Mock up mode (one tick box) = PixelDevMockWindow: one draggable window with a Shapes tab (PixelDevShapes) and a UI pieces tab (PixelDevMockup) and a minimize button that leaves a thin header; replaces the two separate 'Shape placer' / 'UI mockup builder' tick boxes (both are tabs of the Dev Hub now, see below).

PixelDevInspector.cs

Tools tab tick box 'Inspector' opens a draggable, minimizable window (header notes in PixelDevInspector.cs): World tab (time scale, fixed step, gravity / Gravity Y, solver iterations, thresholds, live counts, a drop-down of every game system) and Selected tab (Pick mode = click any collider, Parent / Active / Destroy, filter, foldable components; game scripts list every supported field by reflection, Unity components a fixed list; husks also show their PixelTier). 'Copy edits' copies every change as Owner.field = value lines.

Dev Tools window (PixelWindowChrome)

The Dev Tools panel is no longer a modal dialog: no dimmer, so the game stays clickable. PixelWindowChrome (header notes in the script) gives it drag by the title (kept between the black bars), Minimize ('-'), and docking to the left / right edge (drag against a side; slides out on hover or while typing; undock arrow). Prefs PixelClicker.Dev.Window.*. My Window, the Mock up window and the Inspector still have their own copies of this logic.

Dev Hub (PixelDevHub.cs) - replaces the Dev Tools panel, the Inspector window and the Mock up window

One window for every dev tool: drag, minimize, dock to a screen side (PixelWindowChrome), filing-cabinet tabs (Main, To-Do, Changelog, Inspector, Shapes, UI Mock) that can be reordered by dragging and popped out into floating windows (drag a tab down; 'Dock' button or dropping the window on the hub puts it back). The Main tab is PixelDevTools' rows as WIDGETS: every row is a card with a grip (PixelCardBoard, order saved as PixelClicker.Dev.WidgetOrder2). The 'Inspector' and 'Mock up mode' tick boxes are gone (they are tabs now). Tools get their content with PixelDevHub.TabRoot(id) and PixelDevHub.SetTabCallback(id, visible => ...); PixelDevMockWindow is only a doorway to the hub's tab roots. The Inspector applies an edited value while you type and again on Enter, reads it back and reports the result in its status line (so a value the game overwrites is visible).

Hub tabs: icons, Docs, Notes

Hub tabs are small: each shows only a pixel-art icon, the active tab also shows its name (PixelDevHub.LayoutTabs, icons drawn from text in TabIcon). Tabs now: Main, Docs, Tester, Inspector, Shapes, UI Mock, Looks (PixelLookStudio.cs: the in-game pixel look editor), Setups (PixelDevSetups.cs: named test setups), Events (PixelDevEvents.cs: replay tips / tutorials / popups), Sounds (PixelDevSounds.cs: sound board), Console (PixelDevConsole.cs + PixelLogBuffer.cs: live log + error badge); see each script's header. New tabs build their UI with PixelDevUI and get a public PixelDevHub.OpenTab(id). Docs (PixelDevDocs.cs) has three permanent sub-tabs: To-Do, Changelog and Notes (your own notes typed in-game: list on the left, title + multi-line text on the right, auto-saved to <persistentDataPath>/DevNotes.json, Delete confirms with a second click). PixelInputCaret draws the text cursor in the Inspector / Notes fields.

Spawn widgets (replaced the on-screen spawn selector)

The 'On-screen spawn selector' tick box and the row at the top of the screen are gone. The Main tab now has a PINNED widget board above the scrolling one (pinned because a drop-down's list would be clipped inside a scroll view; since merged into the one scrolling board, see 'One widget board, favorites'): Add pixels (the husk drop-down | amount | Add | Give pet row), Spawned pixel (drop-down: normal / Monochrome / RGB / every pixel; Spawn = only that pixel spawns (SetDevSpawnTiers) and a single exact pixel replaces the one on the cube now, the button is green while active; Despawn = back to normal), Minigames (drop-down, Spawn = SpawnNow, Despawn = DespawnNow of the chosen one or every busy one) and Middle click (drop-down, first entry 'off': a middle click swaps the pixel on the cube, DevSwapCurrentPixel). Built by PixelDevTools.BuildSpawnWidgets; saved: Selector (override active), SelPixel, SelMinigame, SelSwap.

Feature Tester tab + Wood Chipper

The hub's Tester tab (PixelFeatureTester.cs) lists things that are not in the game yet as cards with Place / Clear. Place = a see-through ghost follows the mouse over the floor, left click puts a real one down (keeps placing), Q / E turn it, right click / Esc stops; placed test objects are not saved. Add a feature = one Register(...) line in PixelFeatureTester.Awake. First feature: Wood Chipper (PixelWoodChipper.cs): a detailed machine built from primitives (trailer, tyres, hopper, rollers, chute, engine, controls, beacon...) that pulls husks in and shreds them (PixelClicker.ClaimOldPixel, no payout) with chips, shaking, blinking beacon and exhaust smoke.

Wood Chipper rules: a wood pixel (PixelClicker.IsWoodBlock) gives Woodcutting XP (woodXp, 3, via PixelSkills.AddXp with the usual popup); any other pixel does damagePerOtherPixel (14) of maxHealth (100): the paint darkens, sparks fly, the exhaust gets thicker and blacker, a health bar floats over it; at 0 it is broken for good (stops, no beacon, 'BROKEN' label) and has to be cleared and replaced.

Hub resize, widget headers
- The Dev Hub window has a grip in its bottom-right corner (PixelWindowChrome.EnableResize): dragging it SCALES the whole hub (0.55x - 1.6x, remembered as PixelClicker.Dev.Hub.Window.Scale). It is a scale, not a re-layout: the tabs keep the layout they were built with. A real re-layout would need every tab to build against a variable size.
- Every widget card (PixelCardBoard) has a header strip with its title and a '-' button: it folds the card up to just the header (hidden controls are restored on '+'); remembered per board (<board prefKey>.Min). The pinned cards at the top push the scrolling list down / up as they fold.
- Button labels inside widgets shrink to fit (PixelUIKit.FitOneLine), so long labels like DESPAWN no longer spill out of their button.

One widget board, favorites
- The Main tab is ONE scrolling PixelCardBoard (saved order key Dev.WidgetOrder2): the old fixed "pinned" top half is gone. Drag a card by its whole header strip (title bar, easy to hit); the small grip icon is part of it.
- HOLD the right button on a card (0.7 s, a ring fills round the cursor, PixelCardBoardDriver -> PixelHold) to make it a FAVORITE: it snaps to the top, is tinted gold, and stays above the non-favorites (Partition); hold again to let it go back to the top of the others. Saved as <board key>.Fav.
- Drop-downs inside the scrolling board: PixelUIKit.LetListEscapeMasks moves an opened list to the root canvas so the scroll view's mask cannot clip it (untested in Unity: if a list still looks cut off, this is the place).

Search bar, custom text, docked drag
- The Dev Hub header has a SEARCH field (PixelDevHub.BuildSearch / RegisterSearch(label, tabId, reveal, keywords)): it finds tabs, Main-tab widgets (PixelCardBoard.SearchTab registers every card) and tester features; picking a result (or Enter for the first) opens its tab, unfolds / scrolls to the card (PixelUIKit.ScrollTo) and flashes it. Other tabs (Docs, Inspector...) are not searched inside.
- RIGHT CLICK any label in the hub (a quick click; a hold is the widget favorite) to type your own text (PixelDevTextEdit: Save / Reset / Cancel). Labels are found by hierarchy path; edits live in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Dev.CustomText and are stored per save profile by PixelSettingsSync. Drop-down captions, typed text and search results are left alone.
- Docked windows (PixelWindowChrome, My Window) can be dragged off the edge by their header: the drag undocks them (PixelPopoutDrag.onBegin).
- A 'Collapse all' button above the Main tab's widgets folds every card up to its header (it reads 'Expand all' while everything is folded; PixelCardBoard.SetAllMinimized).

Unlock and Max XP widgets

Main tab widget Unlock (shopunlock): a drop-down with 'All shop items' and then every shop pack (marked [not in shop] for hideInShop packs and (unlocked) / (level n) for owned ones), an Unlock button (free, as if bought; upgrades go to their top level) and a Relock button (takes the pack back, locks its reward pixels, upgrades to level 0). 'All shop items' + Unlock = PixelShop.DevUnlockAll (every pack AND every ordinary pixel type, switchable things left off; it no longer touches skills); + Relock = every pack relocked. The old separate Unlock everything button is gone. Main tab widget Max XP (maxxp): a drop-down with 'All skills' and then every skill with its level, a Max XP button (that skill / every skill, Standard included, to its level cap: PixelSkills.DevSetSkillXp(pack, true)) and Reset XP (back to level 1). Texts shopUnlockText, shopRelockText, shopAllText, maxXpText, resetXpText, allSkillsText; the shop pick is remembered (SelShopPack).

Super Rares widget

Main tab widget Super Rares (superrares) replaced the three separate 'Give next Dragon Cube', 'Give next God Cube' and 'Play next God show' widgets: a drop-down of every rare collectible tier in the game's order (the seven Dragon Cubes, the three God Cubes, the six Prime Cubes, the Prime Cubelette, the Golden Snitch; (held) marks ones you have), a Spawn button that puts the picked one on the clicker cube in place of the current block (PixelClicker.DevSwapCurrentPixel; click it to collect it like a real find, the God / Prime shows play then; the Golden Snitch waits for your click and flies) and a Gauntlet button that adds one Prime Gauntlet (DeviceKind.Gauntlet) to the inventory. A second row holds the "Farmer always has dragon seeds" tick box (moved here from its own widget; PixelFarmerMinigame.DevAlwaysDragonSeeds). Widget cards can now have several rows (Widget(..., rows), WidgetRowGap). Texts spawnRareText, giveGauntletText; the pick is remembered (SelRare). Every remembered Dev Tools choice is kept PER SAVE: the keys are listed once in PixelDevTools.IntPrefs / StringPrefs and declared with PixelPrefs.DeclarePerSave; a new remembered choice goes in that list.

Dev reports widget

Main tab widget Dev reports (devreports, 3 rows of two buttons) replaced the six separate report widgets: Open crash report folder (greyed out when crash reports are off) | Snapshot bounds; Mark this moment | UI layout dump; Balance report | Session recorder (Start, then Stop & save with the time recorded on the button; PixelSessionRecorder). Old widget ids report, bounds, moment, uidump, balance, session are gone.

Resource Tools widget (2026-10-20)
One Main-tab card 'Resource Tools' (BuildResourceToolsCard) holds what used to be 11 separate widgets: Infinite resources, God mode, Husk despawn, Husk pile, Clear key, Husk limit, Spawned pixel, Middle click, Add pixels, Focus potions, Click count (ResourceToolIds). Widget() keeps their controls aside instead of making a card; the combined card stacks them in that order, with a small sub-title above the tools that have no label of their own. The Tester tab no longer shows the 10 conveyor / device features that are now shop items (PixelFeatureTester.SoldInShop; still registered for StartFeature).

Time control widget (2026-10-20)
Main-tab card 'Time control' (id timecontrol): buttons Rewind / Pause / Play / Slow / Fast plus a status line (mode, speed, seconds of history). The work is done by PixelTimeControl (PixelSystems, Core): see its header comment. Pressing Slow / Fast / Rewind again steps through its presets; while one is on, the plain mouse wheel changes the speed by 0.1x (0.1x - 5x). Rewind only moves the husks back through recorded snapshots (30 s by default).
--- docs/systems/PixelDeviceCard.md ---
Device card and panel (PixelDeviceCard.cs, PixelDeviceInfo.cs, PixelWorldRing.cs)

One shared info card and panel for every PLACED device (design agreed in the Design Discussion chat, built 2026-10-18).

How the player uses it
- Hover a placed device for hoverDelay (0.5 s): a small card beside the pointer (the hover-tooltip spot, PixelUIKit.PlaceTooltip) with the essentials: name, time / uses / wind left as a bar, the 2-3 key stats, contents (6 / 10). The Sorting Hopper's flag card was the model.
- Right-click (tap) a placed device: a small Tool window (PixelUIKit.CreateWindow, theme colours) with the full sheet (run time, reach, Operation upgrade levels, what it did this session) and two buttons: Remove (asks once: "Sure?") and Upgrades (opens the shop on Upgrades > Operation with that device's upgrade window, PixelShop.OpenOperationFor; only for devices with an Operation row). Escape (PixelWindows, priority 45), a click outside it, or the device going away closes it.
- Right-hold still removes a device straight away (PixelConsumables.UpdateRemoval, unchanged); a tap is a press released within tapSeconds (0.3 s).
- Left-click is unchanged (Recycler button, Crate storing, hopper flag, Sweeper winding, Organizer release, switching a device on...).

How it fits together
- Who owns a right click: PixelRightClick has a Device owner (after Tool / Pet, before WorldPart / Cube / Husk); PixelRightClick.Device is the device. The card asks the same owner for its hover, so it never fights the pet menu, tools, the cube or husk crush.
- The card waits while the block / husk card is wanted (PixelBlockTooltip.HoveringBlock: pointing at a husk in a bin shows the husk), over a part that claimed the pointer (PixelPointerClaim.ButtonClaimed: the Recycler button, the hopper flag), while carrying (PixelCarryHandles.Hot) and while the right button is held.
- Lines come from the device: PixelPlacedDevice.BuildInfo(PixelDeviceInfo) writes the common part (name, time / uses left bar, 'Click to start', powered, attuned; Full: run time, reach, Operation levels via PixelShop.AddOperationLevels), then the subclass's AddInfo override, then every IPixelDeviceInfo component on the same object (behaviour next to a thin device stub). info.Full = panel.
  - Providers so far: Recycler (inside n / cap, last streak; panel: pay share, shake time, size, session husks / batches / paid), Sorting Hopper (filter, feeds a Recycler or not, holding n / room; panel: each type held, feed gap, session into the bin / out of the bottom spout, and its own buttons Select filter + Empty out), Sweeper (wind bar, what it is doing; panel: range, session rescues), Storage Crate (stored n / 10; panel: each section), Quantum Organizer (stored n / cap, each bound type x count; panel: bound types, session taken in / let out). Every other device shows the common lines.
  - A new device: override AddInfo or implement IPixelDeviceInfo; nothing else to wire.
- Upgrade steppers (2026-10-18, Devices session): in the panel each Operation upgrade with levels bought is a '- Lv n / max +' row (info.AddStepper, PlaceSteppers): the player can use FEWER of the bought levels (PixelShop.AppliedLevel / SetAppliedLevel, saved with the stats as opuse.<id>; buying a level clears it). OperationBonus reads the level in use, so everything that asks for a bonus follows it; the Recycler and the Sorting Hopper resize live, other devices pick it up when next placed.
- Device actions (2026-10-18, Devices session): in the panel (info.Full) a device can add buttons of its own with info.AddAction(label, onClick, role); PlaceActions shows them two to a row above Remove / Upgrades (a last odd one full width) and refreshes the panel after a click. First user: the Sorting Hopper (Select filter opens PixelSortingHopperWindow, Empty out).
- Not only devices (2026-10-20): PixelDeviceCard.OpenSubject(IPixelPanelSubject) shows the same panel for something that is not a placed device: it fills the sheet itself, says what Remove does and gets Opened / Closed (to highlight what is edited). First user: conveyor belt SECTIONS (PixelConveyorPanel, a right click on a belt; the section glows; speed stepper, Reverse, Remove this piece, Remove = the whole section).
- Refreshed every refreshSeconds (0.25) while shown, not every frame.

Floating world text trimmed (2026-10-18)
The card now carries the numbers, so the floating text over devices became a small camera-facing ring (PixelWorldRing, two LineRenderers, redrawn only when the fill changes):
- Recycler: the 'n / 25' counter, its 'full - press the button' line and the after-payout 'Type streak x1.3 pay' text -> a ring that fills with the contents (amber when full). The hover arrow's 'm:ss left' -> arrow only.
- Quantum Organizer: the list of bound types with counts and 'n / 60' -> a ring for how full it is (the 'Drop a pixel on the pad' hint stays until a type is bound).
- Every timed device (Vacuum, Fan, Magnet, Sprinkler, Quantum Organizer...): the '30s' countdown (and its POWERED line) -> a ring that empties with the time (amber under 20%, blue while powered).
- Kept as text on purpose: 'Click to start' on a device not yet switched on, the Sweeper's 'click to wind', the uses-mode labels (Charge Booster CLICK! / SUPER!, Lightning Rod 'n x'): they tell the player what to do.
--- docs/systems/PixelDragonWish.md ---
PixelDragonWish

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelDragonWish.cs

Dragon Cubes + the wish. Seven rare-drop tiers (PixelType.DragonCube1..7, PixelTier.rareDrop, helpers PixelClicker.IsDragonCube/DragonCubeStars; created by PixelClicker.EnsureDragonCubes, switch disableDragonCubes, weight dragonCubeSpawnWeight (0 = default 0.0001 each, ~1 in 70,000 blocks each)) spawn with no unlock - since 2026-10-16 every rare drop rolls ON ITS OWN before the normal pick at a FIXED chance per block = weight / rareReferenceWeight (7), so the odds no longer depend on how many pixel types are unlocked (PixelClicker.TryPickRare; before, with only White unlocked, a Dragon Cube was ~1 in 1,400) (CanSpawn / GetClickTierIndex honour rareDrop), pay 1 of their own currency, show in the Inventory / currency panel only once held, and are kept out of the unlock chain, Value upgrades, potions, crafting (recipes, crafting grid, combos), pets and achievements (IsDragonCube / rareDrop filters). Look (PixelLook.starCubes 1-7 + starColor, PixelLooks.StarCubesMesh): glassy orange translucent cube with a thin outline and 1-7 dark orange small cubes inside laid out like dragon-ball stars (unlit vertex-coloured mesh via OverlayMaterial, so they show through the body; looks added by looksVersion 10). First find of each: event-log line + tip dragon_first; holding all seven (PixelClicker.HasAllDragonCubes) shows a pulsing "Summon the Dragon" button under the top bar. Summon (SummonRoutine, all unscaled time): uses up the seven cubes, Time.timeScale 0, sky dims, seven block-drawn cubes rise into a ring, orbit faster with lightning flickers, spiral together in a white flash, then a follow-the-leader cube dragon (BuildDragon / AnimateDragon: body segments sit where the head was lag ago; head with mane, horns, eye, whiskers) bursts out; it offers three random wishes (PickWishes): Fortune (fortuneSeconds of current income at once), Golden Hour (PixelClicker.WishMultiplier x3 payouts for 10 min), Ultra Blessing (5 Ultra on random unlocked types), Potion Stock (3 random potions), A New Friend (an unowned pet via PixelPets.Award(type, false)); the first of the three wishes is always 'Dragon Friend' (GiveDragonPet: a Dragon Cube pet - DragonCube1..7 are valid pet types - you don't own yet, offered until all seven are owned) and the other two are random. Escape does nothing meanwhile (registered window). Sound dragon_summon (code-synthesized PixelSynth.Summon while its clip list is empty); stats dragon.summons/wishes. Added by PixelClicker.Awake.
--- docs/systems/PixelEggMinigame.md ---
PixelEggMinigame

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelEggMinigame.cs / PixelBeanMinigame.cs

Two very rare cat visitors (ids egg, bean; the FIRST visit ever (FirstVisitNow, counter <id>.met, TracksMeeting) gives the special gift below, later visits bring a random present of pixels (GivePixelGift: giftTypes 3 random unlocked types, each worth giftClicksMin..Max (30-120) normal clicks of that pixel); started by PixelGuideVendor.StartVendors at 4 / 6 unlocked pixel types, first slot 10-25 min later; PixelVisitorMinigame got the hooks IsGift (accepting runs OnGift() after the optional second speech box instead of opening a wares window), TracksMeeting (the first real visit sets <id>.met; dev Spawn doesn't count), VisitChance (rolled once each time the visit timer runs out; a failed roll waits for the next timer) and CanVisit, plus AcceptLabel / RejectLabel). Egg = an orange cat; gives an egg (PixelEgg: a white cube with rounded corners built by PixelEgg.RoundedCubeMesh, stands in for the cube (it takes the cube's place and size, the cube shrinks away via SetCubeShrink so the auto clicker pauses, and returns when the egg hatches), clicked via a screen-distance test, PixelPointerClaim blocks cube clicks; 1-50 clicks rolled when given (clicksMin/Max), thin dark crack slivers appear as it takes hits; the last click starts the hatching show (PixelEggHatch.cs, component added by PixelClicker.Awake, disableShow; the egg hides and stays until it ends): a dark canvas (691) over the screen, the egg and pet drawn by a hidden studio camera into a full-canvas RenderTexture (1 studio unit = 100 canvas units; egg = a shell + a detachable lid of rounded cubes with glowing crack slivers): zoom to the middle, shake harder (shakeSeconds) with chips and cracks lighting up, a flash as the lid flies off, the pet peeks out of the shell, hops out and jumps about with sparkles (jumpSeconds), then hops into the backpack icon like a Dragon Cube; Escape skips (PixelWindows 165). The pet is PixelPets.Awarded when it ends (FinishHatch, quietly - the show replaces the found popup; if the show can't play the old shell burst + popup are used) (PetCandidates: not rare drops / standalone); state in counters egg.pending/need/clicks, restored after a load). Bean = a grey tabby cat; gives Magic Beans (a seed-kind device with Device.beans, craft-only, hold 1, PixelConsumables.BeansItem(); planted like seeds from the Inventory's Seeds category: PlantSeed -> PixelBeanstalk.Plant, needs a Dragon Cube you don't hold, PickMissingDragonCube): PixelBeanstalk grows a twisting stalk over 7 s with leaves and 5-7 random pixel cubes hanging on stems, one of them the Dragon Cube; click a cube to pick it (ordinary = a paid husk at 3x, Dragon Cube = AddCurrency + PixelDragonFind.Play, PixelPointerClaim blocks cube clicks); left alone it withers after 15 min and still hands over the Dragon Cube; the pending cube is kept in bean.pending (tier + 1) and the stalk regrows after a load.
--- docs/systems/PixelFactory.md ---
Machines in the Tester: conveyor belt and kiln

Both are Dev Hub > Tester features (PixelFeatureTester), built from primitives in code with PixelFactoryKit (shared materials / boxes / cylinders / ghost versions). Not saved.

Conveyor belt (PixelConveyor.cs)
- Place: click a point, click another: a belt between them; the second point starts the next piece (short pieces = a curve); right click (or Esc) stops placing; the pieces already laid stay. Clicks near a belt end snap to it (PixelConveyor.Snap). Two-point features are RegisterLine in the tester (marker ghost, then a preview piece).
- Quick right click a placed belt (not while placing): opens the BELT PANEL on its section (PixelConveyorPanel: speed, lanes, status, Reverse, Remove); a right HOLD removes that piece (PixelFeatureTester).
- PixelConveyorSystem.FixedUpdate LOCKS every husk that lands on a belt (not held, not kinematic, not glued into a build, not flying, room for it) onto the belt and carries it along by position at beltSpeedCubes (0 = 1.6 cubes/s), no physics (like the Storage Crate). At the end it is handed to a belt that starts there, otherwise (an end is detected) it is ejected with physics back on, flying on in the belt direction. Riders keep a gap (queue when blocked). Flipping a belt flips its riders; grabbing one frees it.

Kiln (PixelKiln.cs)
- Anything inside the mouth zone (in front of the +X door) burns: ClaimOldPixel, drawn in while it shrinks, then after charcoalDelaySeconds it gives charcoalPerPixel CHARCOAL, one per charcoalGapSeconds, as a MATERIAL (2026-10-24): PixelMaterials.Add("charcoal"), a "+1 Charcoal" popup over the kiln (PixelUI.ShowWorldText), shown in the Inventory's Materials tab. Fire light, embers, smoke (thicker while it burns).
- Charcoal buys the Charcoal Pixel in the shop (Pixels tab: 50 Charcoal + 1,500 Gray + 1,500 Black, needs the RGB pack); that pixel spawns on the cube and its husks are the most efficient Biomass Generator fuel (charcoalFuel 8 wood husks; Wood Chips 4). Before 2026-10-24 the kiln spat Charcoal husks out of a back chute and Charcoal was a Firemaking block.
- A belt can lead into the mouth through a coupling on its 'mouth' (In) socket (the old 'chute' Out socket is gone).

Wood Chipper discharge arrow and chute socket
While placing, the ghost chipper shows a flat arrow from its chute outwards (PixelWoodChipper.BuildArrow; the chips shoot out along it). The chute is an Out SOCKET on the floor in front of the outlet: with a coupling there, its Wood Chips blocks go straight onto the belt that starts at it (see docs/systems/PixelCouplings.md). (The old placing latch onto a belt opening is gone.)

Belt speed (first person)
Right click a belt in first person: a small window (PixelConveyorSystem speed editor) with a number box (cube widths per second, 0 stops it), Set, Reverse and Close; Esc closes it; the mouse is freed while it is open (PixelFirstPerson playable). In the normal view a right click still reverses the belt.

Belt ends feed machines (couplings)
At a belt end with no belt after it, PixelConveyorSystem hands the pixel to an In COUPLING whose port is there (PixelCouplings.HandIn -> the device's one hook AcceptHusk); none there, or the device is full, and it is ejected. Machines declare SOCKETS and couplings are fitted to them: see docs/systems/PixelCouplings.md (this replaced the old IPixelBeltAcceptor / IPixelPort interfaces on 2026-10-17).

Lumberjack: Buy / Sell tabs, Wood Chipper device, potions
PixelVisitorMinigame got BuySellTabs (a vendor that sells wares AND buys pixels: Buy tab = wares, Sell tab = trades). The Lumberjack sells the Wood Chipper (DeviceKind.WoodChipper 15, Device.vendorOnly = not listed in the shop or sold by the Tinkerer; costs wood + bark; placed like the Fan with the turn input, its chute is an Out socket for a coupling; thin PixelPermanentDevice : PixelPlacedDevice on the machine PixelWoodChipper.Place builds, so it is saved / restored / removed like any device) and plain potions of the Lumberjack pack's pixels; and buys those pixels (Wood, Bark, Sap, Leaf, Heartwood, Twig, Acorn, Whetstone, Pesticide - not the Great Oak) for pure currency.

Solar Panel + saving the tester machines
- PixelSolarPanel.cs (Dev Hub tester feature): a flat panel powered while Solar pixels lie on it; it calls HoldPower() on its ONE linked device (blue line). Solar pixels on it are slowly used up unless they form a complete group of 5 (the existing Solar cluster keep-alive). After placing, click a device inside the blue ring to link; right click a placed panel to re-link (PixelFeatureTester.BeginLink).
- All tester machines save with the save file: SaveData.testerObjects = PixelFeatureTester.TesterSave[] (feature name + JSON from each feature's ExportState/ImportState). Imported after the placed devices (panels re-link to the nearest restored device by position). Belt riders are saved with their belt (PixelConveyorSystem.ExportRiders/ImportRider) and skipped by the normal loose husk save.

Heights, ramps and the Conveyor Elevator
- Belt ends are 3D points (y = underside). Scroll while placing a belt raises / lowers the point (posts appear), so belts can slope; Snap finds belt ends / elevator tops by screen distance. Riders move by ground distance (Length), with PointAt / RunSpeed / LiftFactor handling the slope.
- PixelElevator (tester feature): magnetizes to a belt end, click for the base, mouse height, click to confirm. Entry on its -Z face and exit on the +Z top face are sockets with couplings built in (a belt ends at the entry coupling; a new belt starts at the top one). Uses PixelPathCarrier.

Conveyor Connector and Hose Adapter (removed 2026-10-17)
Both were deleted once couplings existed: a belt ends at a coupling or starts at one, and the Container Hose is routed freely between couplings, so neither the boxed channel nor the funnel-spout trough is needed.

Corner joints
PixelConveyorJoint.cs: belts chained at an angle get a corner filler (slab + outer rails) on the outside of the turn, built automatically by each belt every 0.5 s (see the file header).
--- docs/systems/PixelFirePixel.md ---
PixelFirePixel

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelFirePixel.cs / PixelProspector.cs

Fire pixel + Prospector's Pack (PixelType.Fire 25, ores Copper..Nickel 26-31; PixelClicker.IsOre / IsStandalone = no integration: these types get no potions, seeds, crafting recipes / combos, pets, achievements, Value upgrades, Entrepreneur trades or guide gifts - the filters are at each of those sites; they still spawn, pay, show in the Inventory / Log / currency panel). Fire (testing pixel, pack 'Fire Pixel': 5 White, no requirements; look = glowing orange + PixelLook.flames (PixelFlames.Attach: a stream of flame squares rising off the cube) + burn; tier glow): its click calls FireBurstFx.Play (a horizontal ring of flame squares flying outwards from the cube, sparks, an expanding PixelFxRing shock ring and a light flash, sound fire_burst); its husk gets OldPixelBurn (falls normally while it slowly shrinks away over fireBurnSeconds (0 = 6) shedding embers and crackling (fire_crackle), doesn't age, then is released with no payout); disableFireFx. Prospector's Pack (10 White, no requirements): six ore tiers, tough (clicksToCollect 2-5) with PixelLook.oreTexture (PixelLooks.OreTexture: 64 px pixel-art grey rock with veins / flecks of oreColor metal, dark cracks spreading per hit via damageCracks); every non-final hit shows OreFx.Hit (rock chips + metal flecks, sound ore_chip), the break OreFx.Break (rocks, nuggets, dust; the ore's own pixel_<ore> click sound is a synthesized break); rich vein: a fresh ore is a rich vein with richVeinChance (0 = 8%) - it glitters (OreFx.Glitter) and pays richVeinMultiplier (0 = 5) x with a gold burst, shock ring and ore_gleam (first one per session is logged); ore husks clink when they land (pixel_bounce_<ore>); disableProspectorFx. Prospector mode (chosen as the Mining skill in the Skills window, no longer a Toggles row: PixelSkills.PackSetting == Mining): the ores are NOT in the random spawns unless the mode is on (PixelSkills.LeftOutOfSpawns(type) in PickSpawnTier: Mining not in play = ores never spawn; with a pack other than Standard in play White, Gray, Black, Red, Green, Blue, Glass and Luminescent (PixelProspector.IsBase) are left out and the pack's blocks spawn instead); a switch takes effect from the next spawn (PixelSkills.SetActive; a Glass 'skill switch' block replaces the cube's pixel at once, ShowSkillSwitchBlock). All sounds are code-synthesized while their clip list is empty (PixelAudio.SynthFor: PixelSynth.FireBurst/FireCrackle/OreChip/OreBreak/OreGleam/OreClink). Shared particle helper PixelFx.Make(parent, name, Spec) (little squares, Sprites/Default).

PixelFx burst pool (2026-10-19). One-shot hit / break effects (Fire burst, ore chips / break, farm / wood / fish / forage, electric, Obsidian, treasure chest, meteor hits) use PixelFx.Rent(centre, seconds) + burst.Add(name, spec) instead of a new GameObject + ParticleSystems per hit; a runner (PixelFxPoolRunner) returns them after 'seconds' (at most 32 spare kept). Configure fully resets a reused system (incl. shape scale, one burst without allocating); FadeGradient results and size curves are cached (assign them, never change their keys). The click pop in PixelBasicFx uses the same pool (its own cap: 6 pops at once).
--- docs/systems/PixelFirstPerson.md ---
PixelFirstPerson

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelFirstPerson.cs

First person mode (added by PixelClicker.Awake; rebindable key PixelAction.FirstPerson, default F; disableFirstPerson): shrinks the player to heightFraction (0 = 1.5, three times the old 0.5; walking and jumping scale with it via SizeScale) of a husk's edge and drops a CharacterController player on the floor in front of the cube (WASD / arrows, Shift run runFactor, Space jump jumpHeight, mouse look lookSensitivity, walkSpeed in husk widths per second; unscaled time so it works during Time Stop; PixelFirstPersonPush nudges husks you run into: each contact only brings the pixel up towards pushSpeed (0 = 3 husk widths per second, a fraction of the gap per contact), along your direction, no lift, spin damped, never a shove while you stand on it - nothing flies). Carrying: a cube under the crosshair (FindPickup: nearest husk within carryReach (0 = 3.5 player heights) and a few degrees of the view) is picked up (PickUp: kinematic, colliders off, OldPixelDespawn.Held, gravity script off) and held in front of you (PlaceCarried, follows the view); the controls are in 'Build controls' below (the first version's right-click stack of up to three cubes is gone); leaving first person drops what you hold (DropAll). PixelFirstPerson.TryGetPlayerPosition gives the player's spot (the floor edge fade centres on it). The normal camera stays where it is but its culling mask is set to 0 (so PixelViewBounds, fog, floor fitting and Camera.main users are unaffected) and a second camera (CopyFrom of it, near clip 6% of the player's height) at the eyes draws instead. Left-click while the crosshair is on the cube, at any distance (AimingAtCube: angle to the cube vs its apparent radius + aimAssistDegrees 3, occlusion ignored), calls PixelClicker.ManualClickFromAfar() (same rules as a mouse click: paused / intro lock / minClickInterval). PixelFirstPerson.Active makes PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock read true (cube clicks, grabbing, device removal step aside), suppresses Time Slow's S key and the hose key, and a registered window (priority 120) makes Escape leave it; it can't start (or ends) during the title screen, pause, popups, visits, takeover minigames or while the hose is on. A crosshair (green when aiming at the cube) + a hint line are the HUD; cursor locked while playing and freed while paused. Windows are closed on entering (the cursor is locked, so UI can't be used inside).

Update: the F key is retired. First person is now the 'First person' tick box of the View window (PixelViewWindow, key V): PixelFirstPerson.TryEnter() / Leave(), Escape still leaves, and while the View window is open the mouse is free (the player doesn't look / move). First person is always the 3D view: PixelOrthoView forces perspective while it is active (ForcePerspectiveNow on entering).

Build controls (attaching cubes) - replaces the old right-click carrying

LEFT click now grabs: aiming at a free husk (FindPickup(false), within reach) picks it up, otherwise a left click aimed at the clicker still clicks it. You hold ONE cube straight ahead along the view (PlaceCarried). Mouse wheel moves it closer / farther (holdDistance, wheelStep), holding the right button and moving the mouse turns the cube instead of the view (Look -> heldRotation, turnSensitivity). When one of its faces is near another cube's face (PixelAttachment.TrySnap: within attachAngle of a grid-aligned orientation and attachDistance of a spot on the face, sliding along it in half-cube steps) a white pulsing ghost cube shows where it would stick. Left click then attaches it there (AttachCarried: a FixedJoint to the other cube, PixelAttachment.Attach, lifetime frozen so builds don't decay); with no ghost, left click sets the cube down. Attached cubes can't be picked up: hold the right button on one (pryHoldSeconds, a ring fills round the crosshair, PixelHold) to pry it loose (PixelAttachment.Detach: all its joints and the ones holding on to it). The old stack of three is gone (throwing came back: see 'Throwing' below). Joints are not saved. Details: header of PixelAttachment.cs.

Builds, foundations and prying (follow-up)
- Left click on a cube that is part of a build picks up the WHOLE build (PixelAttachment.Group; joints removed with TakeApart while carried, glued back on release). The wheel / right-hold turn work on the whole build; every carried cube has its own white ghost.
- A held cube or build can also snap flat onto the ground (PixelAttachment.TryGroundSnap, any of the 24 grid orientations relative to the world; the carried cube that must lift the most makes the contact). Clicking pins it with a world joint (AttachToWorld) so it stays as a foundation to build on.
- Prying (hold right on a cube of a build, ring on the crosshair via PixelHold's at argument) works again: FindPickup used to skip cubes whose lifetime is frozen, which every attached cube is.

Neon floor
In first person the normal floor (and its wireframe circle) is hidden (PixelFloor.FloorOff includes PixelFirstPerson.Active; the ground collider stays) and PixelFirstPersonGrid draws a neon grid (Resources/PixelNeonGrid.shader) that only shows within a radius of the player, the clicker cube and husks lying on the ground. Colour, spacing, radii and brightness are on PixelFirstPerson ("Neon floor").

Throwing
While holding a cube or build: a quick left tap (under 0.25 s) sticks it / sets it down as before; HOLD left to charge a throw - a ghost line (LineRenderer, Sprites/Default) draws the arc to where it lands, longer and orange as power builds (throwSpeed full power, throwChargeSeconds to charge) - and let go to throw along the view.

Hotbar, axe and the Great Oak
- PixelFirstPersonHotbar (added by Enter): 5 slots, wheel (when not carrying) or keys 1-5.
