Slot 1 = the AXE, drawn in hand (primitives on the camera) with a wind-up / strike / recover swing; the other slots are empty hands. With the axe, left click on a wood husk chops it (XP + 150% pay) and on the cube swings and clicks it (bonus XP, +50% pay when a wood block breaks); non-wood things are still picked up. Settings under "Axe" on PixelFirstPerson. Sounds: PixelAxeSynth (axe_swing, axe_chop, nest_fall, nest_catch).
- GREAT OAK: PixelType.GreatOak 77 (see PixelGreatOak.cs header): 5% replacement of a rolled wood block while Woodcutting is in play, 50-100 clicks, shakes bird nests loose that you click for XP before they vanish.

Whetstone
Lumberjack's Pack base block (PixelType.Whetstone 78, 3 clicks, weight 0.35, pack migration packDataVersion 27). Hold it with Block Hold (right button on the cube): while it is held every manual click is followed by 2 extra rapid clicks, like an Overcharge burst (a HoldRule in PixelBlockRules, see docs/systems/PixelBlockRules.md). A partly broken block keeps its hits through the hold, so one you only noticed after a click can still be grabbed.
--- docs/systems/PixelFloor.md ---
PixelFloor

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

> LATER CHANGES: Spiral Galaxy and Black Hole were REMOVED (enum values Galaxy / BlackHole stay for saves; defaultsVersion 10). Radar has no circles any more (no range rings, no round dimming, no ping rings; the blips are spread over the whole screen). Liquid Flow (cells 12, detail 3), Circuit Flow (cells 12, thicker traces), Cyber Hex (cells 13) and Watching Eyes (cells 11) are zoomed in (bigger features).

> UPDATE (big floors, 2026-10): the 8-bit floors (Platformer, Invaders, Brick Breaker, Pong, Snake, Block Stack, Dungeon, Crossing, Road Racer, Fighter) were REMOVED (their enum values stay so saves keep working; defaultsVersion 9 drops them from saved lists). Big non-repeating floors (PixelFloorLayers.cs): Marble, Lava, Deep Space, Ice, Matrix Rain, Radar, Battle Crater, the four Auras, Death Energy, Liquid Flow, Spiral Galaxy, Black Hole, Moon Surface, Red Planet, Circuit Flow, Cyber Hex, Haunted Tiles, Graveyard and Watching Eyes are now ONE huge picture (about 36-40 world units, 512-1024 px) instead of a small repeating tile: FloorStyle.detail multiplies the noise lattices so features keep their size (shadeDetail), static ones use stretchToFloor (one copy, no repeat), scrolling ones use a huge tile (Lava, Space, Matrix, auras, wisps, Liquid). Big textures are drawn a few ms per frame (FillSliced, UsesSlicedFill; a soft blurred version of the picture (FillPreview, sampled on a 96 x 96 grid) shows until the sharp one is done, PixelFloor.Drawing). Menu icons are built small (PreviewTexture clones the style at <= 160 px). Overlay styles (IsOverlayPattern: Radar, Circuit Flow, Watching Eyes): the still picture is drawn once (shadeBase), then OverlayFrame repaints only what moves (radar blips + pings, circuit sparks, blinking eyes) into a cached Color32 copy - far cheaper than redrawing the whole texture. Craters (ShadeCraterField, PrepareCraters): one big crater where the camera looks (SetCraterFocus), several medium ones (cells) and many pits, lit from the upper left; variant 0 battle (scorch + glowing cracks), 1 moon (rays), 2 red planet. Haunted Tiles flickers by dimming the material (UpdateHauntedFlicker). Spiral Galaxy / Black Hole were drawn once at 1024 px and only pulsed (both floors were removed later, defaultsVersion 10; their enum values stay). Laser Corridor is scaled up (tile 18). Rain (PixelSkyboxRain.cs) is now one big sheet in front of the camera so it covers the whole view. Animation cost: animated floors are never redrawn faster than animFpsCap (0 = 10 per second) and not at all while the floor is invisible (faded out on the menu / 'Display floor' off; unseen in Update). Anything below that still mentions 8-bit floors or the old small tiles is out of date.

PixelFloor.cs

Floor styles (added by PixelClicker.Awake): restyles the scene floor (floorRenderer, else the collider straight below the cube, else an object named Floor/Ground/Plane) with textures drawn in code at runtime - Inspector list of FloorStyle (name, FloorPattern, three colours, resolution, pixelated, tile size, smoothness, glow + pulse, scroll, cells, seed; defaults added by name via EnsureDefaultStyles): Classic (the floor's own material), Neon Grid, Retro Checker, Pixel Grass, Wood Planks, Cobblestone, Marble, Lava, Deep Space, Ice, Disco (tiles change colour every beatSeconds), Honeycomb, Bricks, Circuit Board, Sand Dunes, Ocean (scrolling caustics), Snow, Tartan, Tread Plate (uses metallic), Hologram, Crystal, Matrix Rain (scrolling code), Hazard Stripes, plus Dragon Ball themed floors (fan-inspired, all drawn in code): Tournament Stage, Radar (was Dragon Radar); Planet Namek, Battle Crater, Gravity Room, Time Chamber; Tournament of Power; the auras Super Aura / Divine Aura / Blue Aura / God Aura (renamed from the Super Saiyan / Ultra Instinct ones, grouped together) and Death Energy (was Destruction Energy); the Dragon Balls and Yu-Gi-Oh Card Back floors were removed (defaultsVersion 4 renames / removes / regroups saved lists and the saved pref name); Laser Corridor (LaserHall, animated: teal metal panels with lit seams and wall-light dashes, red security beams that sway and blink in both directions, white-hot where they cross); animated Neon variants right after Neon Grid: Neon Drift (slides + hue cycle), Neon Merge (every 2nd / 4th line fades so the squares grow bigger and back), Neon Wave (rippling lines), Neon Prism (a rainbow sweeping across) = NeonAnimated, 128 px, animateFps 20, cells divisible by 4 - new patterns TournamentTiles, DragonRadar, DragonBalls, KiAura (one pattern, colours per style), Craters, GravityRoom, PowerArena, EnergyWisps; Namek / Time Chamber reuse PixelGrass / Checker. Second batch (PixelFloorScenes.cs, partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from Shade via ShadeExtra; helpers Sstep = shader-style smoothstep (Mathf.SmoothStep is NOT that - it lerps), ScEll/ScBox/ScSeg/ScMix): BattleCrater (reworked Battle Crater), space Galaxy / BlackHole / Hyperspace (+ Moon Surface / Red Planet on Craters), tech DataStream / CircuitFlow / CyberHex / Glitch, trippy Psychedelic / HypnoSpiral / Plasma / OpArt, horror HauntedTiles / Graveyard / WatchingEyes (Bloody Boards and Noir Smoke were removed, defaultsVersion 6), scenes Skyline / GroundSky, mining MineTrack / OreVeins / Strata, farming CropRows / WheatField / Patchwork; Circuit Flow has two bright sparks that random-walk along the traces (PrepareSparks, worked out once per frame like the Fighter) (cells 1-4 picks meadow / desert / arctic / alien twin moons); the scene ones are fitToView and use shadeAspect; styles come from ExtraStyles(); defaultsVersion 5 renames Planet Namek / Time Chamber / Tournament of Power, removes Gravity Room (GravityRoom pattern code stays) and replaces the saved Battle Crater. Third batch (PixelFloorRetro.cs, partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from ShadeExtra's fall-through to ShadeRetro; styles from RetroStyles(); all animated, fit to view, square virtual pixels; the 8-bit ones used a virtual picture P rows tall, string-bitmap sprites and triangle waves for deterministic motion): retrowave / cyberpunk = SynthSun ('Retro Sunset Grid'), NeonHighway, GridTunnel, RainAlley (tiles), VaporChecker, CityMap (tiles), LightCycles (tiles), ArcadeCarpet (tiles); 8-bit games = Platformer, Invaders, BrickBreaker, PongCourt, SnakeGrid (green LCD), BlockStack, Dungeon, Crossing (names '8-Bit ...'; all the 8-bit floors were removed later, defaultsVersion 9, their enum values stay). Also Yu-Gi-Oh Card Back (CardBack, one big card over the whole floor: brown frame + gold line, spiral vortex, dark oval centre; no logos/text), Liquid Flow (animated warped waves), Moving Clouds (CloudSea, scrolls), Kaleidoscope (animated, one big mirrored pattern) and Pac-Man (animated: PacMaze 19x21 filling its tile (FloorStyle.tileAspect = 19/21 so cells are square), tunnels on all four sides and wrapped wall lookups so neighbouring copies join up seamlessly, Pac-Man runs a verified loop PacWaypoints, pellets vanish as he passes and return each lap, four ghosts follow). 8-Bit Road Racer (RoadRacer: a car weaving through traffic on a scrolling road) and 8-Bit Fighter (Fighter: two fighters trading blows with health bars and a K.O.) went with the other 8-bit floors; the square-pixel trick they brought stays: static shadeAspect = the fitted view's width/height (viewAspect, menu previews use 1). FloorStyle.stretchToFloor = show ONE copy, Tile World Size across, centred on the floor (clamp wrap; used by the card back); every style's tiling is now centred on the floor's middle (baseOffset); saved style lists get one-off size fixes through defaultsVersion; FloorStyle.fitToView (Card Back, Moving Clouds, Kaleidoscope, Pac-Man) = one copy fitted to the camera's view of the floor between the black bars (FitToView: rays through the view's bottom-left / bottom-right / top-left onto the floor plane, WorldToFloorUv via an affine map from one mesh triangle (BuildUvMap; bounds fallback for unreadable meshes) -> tiling/offset; re-fitted whenever the camera moves, keeps the last fit while the view misses the floor; fitted pictures are clamped (one copy, never repeated over the floor model) and while the camera is away from its normal view the tiling is BLENDED from 'the picture spread once over the whole floor' (title close-up, PixelCameraIntro.Progress 0) to the fitted view (1) (FitBlend, ApplyTiling, TryWholeFloorTiling); animated floors redraw at most 8x a second while the camera swings; while the camera intro moves the camera the fit uses the pose it settles into (PixelCameraIntro.TryGetFinalPose + a hidden fitCam), so the floor doesn't swim / stretch during the zoom at start and when returning to the main menu); animateFps / animSpeed = redraw the texture N times a second (Fill(tex, style, time, reuse), static shadeTime; keep animated textures small, 128-256). New patterns are appended to the end of FloorPattern so saved enum values stay valid. Picked in the pause menu's Settings (Display section, "Floor style" row) or the small picker right of the title screen's Play button (PixelFloor.Instance.Step/SetStyle, PreviewTexture; implements IPixelLookSource). Animations use unscaled time (they move on the title screen) and freeze during Time Stop, saved in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.FloorStyle.

Game-made floor (added later): PixelFloor.InitFloor (Start, then once a second until it works) builds Pixel Ground with BuildOwnFloor: a flat code-made quad (ownFloorSize 0 = 80 world units, UVs 0..1 like a Unity plane) with a thin BoxCollider whose top is groundFallDistance (0 = 10 world units) under the cube's resting spot - the husks' falling distance. The scene's floor object is found (FindFloor, which ignores dev shapes) only to be hidden, its collider switched off and its collider material copied (else friction 0.6); tick useSceneFloor to keep using it (and the game still builds its own when the scene has none). Every floor style works on the game-made floor exactly as on a scene floor. 'Display floor' defaults to OFF (only the sky shows, the collider still catches pixels) and PixelFloorModes.cs adds 'When floor is off' (Settings > Display, PixelFloor.OffMode): Invisible / Flat plane (an opaque lit sheet in the style's average colour, rimmed by the edge fade) / Landing pulse (PixelFloorPulse.Landed from OldPixelImpact: a rounded-square ripple on the surface the pixel hit, turned to the contact normal).

World-space ground shader (first slice): Assets/Resources/PixelWorldGround.shader (unlit CG, alpha, works in the built-in pipeline and URP; loaded with Resources.Load so builds include it) draws Lava / Marble / Ice / Deep Space from the WORLD position per pixel (no tiling, no stretching, sharp at any zoom / angle, animated by the shader's _Time, faded round _Center between _FadeStart and _FadeEnd). A style opts in with FloorStyle.shaderGround (1 lava, 2 marble, 3 ice, 4 space, 5 data streams, 6 aura flames (Super / Divine / Blue / God Aura), 7 wisps (Death Energy), 8 Matrix Rain, 9 Liquid Flow, 10 Laser Corridor; 0 = painted texture) and shaderScale (world units per pattern cell, 0 = 6); PixelFloor.ApplyShaderGround (PixelFloorModes.cs) builds the material (colours A/B/C, glow, seed) and falls back to the painted texture if the shader is missing; the menus' preview / title pictures stay CPU-painted, the edge-fade ring is off for shader grounds (the shader fades itself, UpdateGroundShader), ApplyVisibility just sets _Fade. Patterns 6-10 are ports of the painted floors (same maths, q = world / shaderScale, where shaderScale = the painted Tile World Size, so one q unit = one painted picture; random values come from world cells, so nothing repeats; defaultsVersion 15 moves saved lists over). The shader's clock is _Clock, driven by PixelFloor.TickGroundShader (unscaled, freezes in Time Stop, like painted animations; it also feeds _Pulse from pulseSpeed / pulseAmount); _Scroll = scrollSpeed x Tile World Size (world units per second); the noise lattice wraps every 4096 cells so a long scroll keeps its randomness; Matrix Rain fades its glyph dots to their average when they get smaller than a screen pixel. While a ground shader is on, Update skips tiling, redraws and the emission pulse. The game-made ground is lifted until it is on screen (UpdateGroundHeight, every 0.5 s, normal view via PixelOrbitCamera.HomeCamera; allowGroundOffScreen turns it off): below the screen's bottom edge PixelViewBounds' bottom plane acts as a false floor and pixels never touch the real ground (no landing pulse, odd falling in the flat view). OldPixelImpact also fires the landing pulse for soft landings (>= 0.6 speed, own 0.08 s gap).

Edge fade update: FadeRadii (PixelFloorFade.cs) gives the fade's start / end radii to the ring, the wireframe AND the ground shader (one rim, smoother-step alpha). A painted one-copy picture (stretchToFloor) is faded out before its own edge (tileWorldSize * 0.5 * 0.94), so its clamped edge pixels never show smeared outwards. Shader grounds keep the digital wireframe rim (their own alpha does the fade, the opaque ring is hidden).

Fourth batch - ten big world floors (PixelFloorWorlds.cs, partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from ShadeRetro's fall-through to ShadeWorlds; styles from WorldStyles()): Lush Grass, Nebula Field, Still Pond, Graffiti Wall, Leaf Litter, Zen Garden, Coral Reef, Casino Felt, Stained Glass (Persian Rug was removed, defaultsVersion 16). Coral Reef (redrawn): reef rock patches with clustered irregular domed colonies (ReefCoral: brain / branching (ReefTips) / table / soft coral / lace sea fans, shadows down-right), seagrass meadows (BladeLayer), starfish, urchins, shells; its moving caustic light is the Caustics overlay (FloorOverlay.Caustics: one flat quad over the floor with Resources/PixelCaustics.shader, additive, world-space moving cell edges, fades with the floor rim via PixelFloor.TryGetFadeRadii; size / speed / strength / colour on PixelFloorOverlay). Sstep (PixelFloorScenes.cs) now accepts falling edges like the shader's smoothstep (it used to return a hard step for them). Template = the big still floors: 1024 px, stretchToFloor, tileWorldSize 40, drawn once (time-sliced fill), no animation. Unlike older big floors they draw in WORLD units (Wu(s) = tileWorldSize, Per(s, feature) = noise lattice period for a feature of that many world units, hash grids in world units), so features keep their size at any resolution and in the small menu previews. New enum values LushGrass ... StainedGlass are appended after Crossing; saved lists pick the new styles up by name (EnsureDefaultStyles). The Graffiti Wall's bubble-letter tag (PIXEL) is drawn from stroke segments (TagLetters, TagDistance): outline, fill, highlight, drop shadow, overspray, drips.

Moon Surface / Red Planet (PixelFloorPlanets.cs, patterns MoonSurface / MarsSurface, defaultsVersion 13 switches saved styles): their own pictures (Moon: highlands + dark basalt seas, terraced big crater with a central peak, rays, secondary crater chains, a rille, boulders with shadows, rover tracks; Mars: dunes with lee slopes, basalt streaks, dust-filled craters, cracked lakebed, dust devil trails). Both read the shared crater list. FloorStyle.tiltCorrect (Stained Glass, Casino Felt, Nebula Field) narrows a stretched picture by sin(base view pitch) (StyleAspect, PixelOrbitCamera.TryGetBaseTilt) so round things do not look squashed. Threaded drawing: ThreadSafePattern lists the patterns drawn on worker threads (see PixelFloorLayers.cs); a new pattern may join only if its Shade code reads state and never writes it.

Fifth batch - varied grass, see-through lasers, picnic (PixelFloorLive.cs, partial class PixelFloor, dispatched from ShadeWorlds' fall-through to ShadeLive; styles from LiveStyles()): Wild Meadow, Clover Lawn, Mossy Glade, Prairie Grass and Picnic Blanket are big still world-unit pictures (1024 px, stretchToFloor 40 units, thread-safe, cached) where every grass blade is its own tapered stroke (BladeLayer: a grid turned to the wind with cells lenMax long x laneW wide, a few blades per cell, random lean + gust wave, random overlap order), so nothing repeats or stretches. New FloorStyle fields: hideBase (the floor picture is hidden via HideBaseNow in ApplyVisibility and the edge fade; the collider stays) and overlay (FloorOverlay: None / Lasers / LaserTracker / Ant), handled by PixelFloorOverlay (added by ApplyLive, called from Apply): real 3D objects on the floor plane updated every frame - Neon Lasers (flat LineRenderer beams, core + glow, drifting / turning / pulsing in the style's three colours), Laser Tracker (emitters circling the cube, every beam ends at the mouse's point on the floor, smoothed, with a pulsing ring), and the Picnic Blanket's ant (primitives, no colliders, walks to the mouse point with a tripod gait and wiggling antennae, wanders when the mouse is off the floor). The overlay reads PixelFloor.TryGetLiveFrame (floor height, cube centre, fade radius, visibility) and fades with the floor; the mouse point uses the first person view ray when in first person.
--- docs/systems/PixelGameState.md ---
PixelGameState / GameState

Started 2026-10-23 (Phase 1: the owner, plus the Bank and the Stats as the pilot; then the Auto Clicker, the Focus potion, Time Stop and Pixel Grabbing, the Bait Bucket and Cubie's bill, the Combo switch, the event log, the UFO Helper, the pets, the achievements, the potion / device inventory, the skill choices, the minigame switches / counters, the shop packs and the currencies - PixelClicker, the last one). The player's PROGRESS moved out of the MonoBehaviours into one plain object, one system at a time; finished 2026-10-24, when the old IPixelSaved / PixelSaveRegistry per-system save parts were retired.

Why
- Saving: each system saved its own part (IPixelSaved) in load-order bands.
- Restart: it needed hand-registered resets (PixelStatics.ResetOnRestart).
- Startup order: "is the save / the system there yet" checks were everywhere.
- No simulation: the rules couldn't run without the scene.

With one state object, saving writes it, loading replaces it and a new game makes a fresh one.

The pieces
- GameState.cs:
  - GameState is plain [Serializable] data with no UnityEngine.Object references. It holds one sub-state per system (BankState, StatsState, ... ClickerState).
  - GameState.MovedSystems lists the systems' class names (the compile-check rule reads it).
  - Repair() fills in missing parts after a load.
- PixelGameState.cs (the owner, in PixelSystems.All, Core):
  - PixelGameState.Current: never null. It is made on first use, so early callers never wait.
  - Replace(state): PixelSaveGame calls it FIRST in a load.
  - RaiseLoaded() / the Loaded event: called once tiers, shop and husks are back, but BEFORE the Dev Hub tester machines (a Solar Panel links to a placed device, belts dock on devices' couplings). Views refresh and world objects rebuild here.
  - RaiseSaving() / the Saving event: called right before a save is written. World objects that change all the time (placed devices, the running potion) copy themselves into their sub-state here instead of every frame.
  - NewGame(): called right before the scene reloads for Restart (PixelPauseMenu.Restart) and New Game (PixelTitleScreen.StartNewGame). These are the only two scene loads.
  - It does NOT use PixelStatics.ResetOnRestart, because sceneUnloaded can arrive after the new scene has started.
  - The component shows Current in the Inspector while playing ("State"), so you can read values and type them in to test.
- Saving: SaveData.state holds Current whole, through JsonUtility. PixelSaveGame itself still writes the husks on the floor, the per-save settings, the Dev Hub tester machines and the slot summary.

Rules for a system with progress
- Read the state through PixelGameState.Current.<part> every time. Never keep a reference to it: a load swaps the whole object. The usual shape is private static BankState B => PixelGameState.Current.bank;.
- The component keeps only display, input, per-session numbers and Inspector tuning ([SerializeField] + [Tooltip] as always). Anything that must survive save / load / restart goes in the sub-state.
- No PixelStatics.ResetOnRestart for progress in its script. tools/compile-check/rules.sh checks this for every name in GameState.MovedSystems. A restart reset that clears something that is not progress (the old scene's event listeners) is marked // restart-reset-not-progress: <what> on its line.
- Key data by stable ids (PixelType as int, string keys), not by tier index or list position. Tier indices shift when pixel types are added. Turn them into tier indices in the component (see PixelBank.CountAt).
- Physics objects stay outside: husks, placed devices and tester machines are copied out when saving and rebuilt when loading, as before.

Adding a NEW system's progress: the steps
(Every system was moved by 2026-10-24 and the old per-system save parts - IPixelSaved / PixelSaveRegistry - are gone. These are the steps for a new feature that has progress to keep.)
1. Split its fields into progress (must survive save / load / Restart) and the rest (UI, input, timers of the current session, Inspector tuning, caches).
2. Add a [Serializable] public class <Name>State to GameState.cs with the progress fields, keyed by stable ids.
   - Add public <Name>State <name> = new <Name>State(); to GameState, and a line for it in GameState.Repair() (plus a Repair() on the sub-state if it has lists).
   - Unity-serializable types only: no Dictionary. Use a List of small entries plus a [NonSerialized] lookup, as StatsState does.
3. In the component: private static <Name>State X => PixelGameState.Current.<name>; and read / write only through it (or properties over it, as PixelTier / ShopPack / the minigames do).
4. World objects (bodies in the scene): copy what is needed to rebuild them into the state on PixelGameState.Saving (if they change all the time) or the moment they change (if they rarely do), and rebuild them on PixelGameState.Loaded.
5. Anything else to redo after a load (a selection, a view, a "don't announce again" flag): subscribe to PixelGameState.Loaded in Awake (unsubscribe in OnDestroy). If it needs ANOTHER system's state in a fixed order during the load, stop and ask (a core step like the shop's ApplyLoadedPackStates / the clicker's ApplyLoadedTiers is called from PixelSaveGame at its step).
6. No PixelStatics.ResetOnRestart for progress (a new game's fresh state resets it). Add its class name to GameState.MovedSystems so the compile check enforces that.
7. Docs: its script header / docs/systems file, a row in the table below. Then tools/compile-check/check.sh, a changelog line, and what to test: save -> quit -> load, Restart, New game.

What lives where
| System | Sub-state | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| PixelBank | BankState (stored husks by pixel type: count + total value) | Loaded picks a stocked selection. Dev Tools' reset calls ClearStored. Capacity and 'bought' come from the shop (not progress). |
| PixelStats | StatsState (typed numbers, potions drunk, generic counters) | PixelStats.Count / Best / Fastest / Total now work without the component. Loaded counts the session if the 3-second count hasn't run. |
| PixelAutoClicker | AutoClickerState (bought, switched off, upgraded interval / clicks per tick) | The Inspector intervalSeconds / clicksPerTick are BASE values, used while the state holds 0 (the pattern for "an upgrade's value vs the tuning default"). The shop's load step (SetPackState) doesn't touch it, so there are no order issues. |
| PixelFocus | FocusState (running potion: skill id, name, seconds left) | The potion list stays on the component. HoldsOffHazards reads the state, so it works without the component. Loaded redraws the status line. |
| PixelTimeStop | SwitchState (timeStop: switched off in Toggles) | The old Import's side effect (a switched-off Time Stop ends any stop / slow) runs in Loaded. 'Bought' (timeStopActive, timeSlowOwned) still comes from the shop packs; it moves with the shop. |
| PixelGrab | SwitchState (grab: switched off in Toggles) | Loaded lets go of a held husk if it is off and re-applies clicker.GrabEnabled. Grabbing itself is always unlocked. |
| PixelBaitBucket | BaitBucketState (placed, spot, stored Fish / Water husks by type with their payout) | The first WORLD object moved: the bucket writes its spot into the state each frame while it stands (it can be carried) and Loaded rebuilds it from the state. The pattern for a placed thing: the state holds what's needed to rebuild it, the object is only the view. |
| PixelCubieBill | CubieBillState (paid per colour by type, trust tier, revealed) | The old part loaded early on purpose (Recyclers rebuilt during the load ask TagShown); with Replace at the START of the load this now holds by itself. Its ResetOnRestart clears event listener lists (not progress), marked // restart-reset-not-progress: so the compile-check rule allows it. |
| PixelCombo | SwitchState (combo: switched off in Toggles) | Loaded drops the running combo if it is off and redraws the meter. |
| PixelHints (event log) | EventLogState (the log's lines: short text + the tip behind it) | Only the event log's lines moved: the 'seen' flags of first-time tips stay global PlayerPrefs (they are per machine, not per save). Loaded redraws the log from the top with no box. |
| PixelRobotWorker (UFO Helper) | UfoState (switched off; the job: device by display name + spot, or banking one pixel type) | SetJob / SetBankJob write the state, so the runtime job and the state never drift; Loaded rebuilds the job's ghost and re-applies the switch. |
| PixelPets | PetsState (owned pets in the order found: type, off, outfit, name) | Each live Pet keeps a reference to its state entry (Pet.saved; type / off / outfit / customName are properties on it), so every change is the state's at once - the pattern for a list of world objects. Loaded rebuilds the bodies. |
| PixelAchievements | AchievementsState (earned tiers + when, by achievement id) | The achievement list stays Inspector config; earnedTiers / EarnedAtOf / StampTier read and write the state by id (no stored reference). The old 'ResetOnNextStart' flag that Restart / New Game set is gone: a new game's fresh state has nothing earned. Loaded re-grants anything already reached quietly. |
| PixelConsumables (inventory) | InventoryState (held potions / devices by key, the ghost backpack bonus; the WORLD part: placed devices + the running potion) | Potion.owned / bonusCap and Device.owned are properties reading the state by key (potion "p:type:second:third", device "d:<display name>", the key cached on the item). Placed devices and the running potion change every frame, so they copy themselves in on PixelGameState.Saving and are rebuilt in Loaded - the pattern for world objects that change all the time. The last IPixelSaved part. |
| PixelSkills | SkillsState (the pack in play, the guest blocks switched on, the offline task) | These three were per-save SETTINGS (PlayerPrefs copied into each save by PixelSettingsSync); now plain progress, and taken off PixelSettingsSync's list. Skill XP, bought nodes, the bonus XP bank and Mastery were already StatsState counters, so they moved with the Stats. The hazard-penalty restart reset is marked not-progress. Loaded raises Changed. Side effect: the title screen no longer takes the newest save's skill pack with its other settings (it shows the plain rotation until Continue). |
| PixelMinigame (+ every subclass) | MinigamesState (per minigame id: switched off, goal counter, second number) | The base class has UserDisabled, SavedCount and SavedExtra over the state; a subclass keeps its counter as a PROPERTY over them (private double chunks { get => SavedCount; set => SavedCount = value; }), so its code is unchanged. OnProgressLoaded (virtual) fixes up after a load (goal already announced, bomb parts earned >= held); a switched-off one stops on Loaded. The Ultra Holes' counter is PixelClicker's Ultra-ever-earned: until PixelClicker moves, the Pad copies it in on Saving and back on load. PixelSaveGame no longer writes SaveData.minigames. |
| PixelShop | ShopState (per pack display name: bought, level) | ShopPack.purchased / level are properties over the state. Applying a pack's effects on load must stay at its FIXED point (right after the tiers, before the rest of the load: not-bought packs switch their features off, some upgrades re-apply), so PixelSaveGame still calls it there - shop.ApplyLoadedPackStates() - and it reads the state instead of the file. The rule for a core step whose ORDER matters: PixelSaveGame keeps calling it at its step; only where the data comes from changes. PixelSaveGame no longer writes SaveData.packs. |
| PixelMaterials | MaterialsState (amount held per material id, e.g. charcoal) | New 2026-10-24 (built straight on the state). The kinds of material are Inspector config on the component; nothing to redo on Loaded (the Inventory reads the amounts each refresh). |
| PixelClicker (currencies) | ClickerState (per pixel type: count, total, unlocked, spawn off, times collected, Ultra / Value levels; Ultra held + ever earned) | The last one. PixelTier's progress fields are PROPERTIES: a LIVE tier (one in the clicker's list: MakeLive, called in Awake, by EnsureTier and as a cheap check each Update) reads its state entry by type and re-binds by itself when a load swaps the state; a tier that is only a DEFINITION (a shop pack's reward tier, a Clone) keeps its own values until the clicker takes it in (a type the state has no entry for starts from them: starting amounts, new shop tiers locked). ApplyLoadedTiers() is the fixed FIRST core step of a load (before the shop). The Ultra Holes' stopgap is gone. The save keeps a small totalPixels summary for the slot list. |

The remaining systems, simplest first, are on the to-do list.
--- docs/systems/PixelGodCubes.md ---
PixelGodCubes

The three Egyptian God Cubes (PixelGodFind.cs, PixelGodSynth.cs, parts of PixelClicker.cs)

Slifer the Sky Dragon, Ra the Winged Dragon, Obelisk the Tormentor (PixelType.GodSlifer 82 / GodRa 83 / GodObelisk 84, PixelClicker.IsGodCube, tiers made by EnsureGodCubes, switch disableGodCubes, weight godCubeSpawnWeight 0 = 0.000001 each, about 1 in 7 million pixels, 100x rarer than a Dragon Cube = the rarest thing in the game). Rare-drop tiers like the Dragon Cubes (rareDrop: spawn without unlocking, show in the inventory only once held, pay 1 of themselves) and kept out of potions, crafting, pets, achievements and the Value upgrades through PixelClicker.IsRareCube (Dragon or God). Only one of each can be held: PixelTier.CanSpawn is false while count >= 1 and AddCurrency ignores a second one (and caps a payout at 1).

Looks (PixelLooks, looksVersion 59): Slifer = a red glass cube held together by storm lightning (the existing lightning look, red / yellow bolts, gold outline); Ra = polished gold with the sun's corona (sunFx, no hover / trickle for it) and a white-gold outline; Obelisk = deep blue stone with a cyan outline that sheds dust (FarmAmbient.Crumbs).

Shows (PixelGodFind.Play(tierIndex, worldPos) from CollectInternal, queued, canvas 691 blocks clicks, unscaled time, Escape skips to the backpack, disableShow, showSpeed, darkness, cubeSize; sounds god_slifer / god_ra / god_obelisk from PixelGodSynth, timed to the shows): Slifer = storm clouds + three lightning strikes + a lightning serpent that weaves round the screen and coils into the cube in a blast; Ra = a rising golden sphere bursts into a sun, golden wings (feather fans) beat in time with the sound and fold in with a shockwave; Obelisk = rising rumble and falling rubble, a giant blue fist lowers, cocks back and slams the screen (flash, crack lines, shockwaves, debris) and the cube rises from the crater. Then the cube hops into the backpack.

Tips: god_first on the first find of any. Dev Hub: 'Give next God Cube' (plays the show) and 'Play next God show (preview)'.

Prime Cubes, Gauntlet, Cubelette (PixelInfinity.cs)
- Six stones (PixelType.StoneSpace..StoneSoul 85-90) reuse the God Cube machinery completely: IsGodCube is true for them (and for InfinityCubelette 91), so the one-of-each clamp, CanSpawn, the backpack stash and EnsureGodCubes apply. IsInfinityStone picks them out. Looks: PixelLooks.StoneLook (looksVersion 60).
- Shows: PixelGodFind.DrawStone(stone, ...) (shared timeline, climax at 3.2 s, a different signature effect per stone) and DrawCubelette (six colour streams, hexagon, snap at 3.4 s). Sounds stone_* / stone_cubelette in PixelGodSynth.
- Gauntlet: DeviceKind.Gauntlet 17 (vendorOnly, hold 1, costs in the Device list). The Wizard offers it (PixelWizardMinigame.ModifyWares) when all six stones are held and there is no Cubelette / Gauntlet. Right-click it -> PixelInfinity.Open: place a stone on each face of the flat golden cube, Forge uses up the Gauntlet and the stones, adds the Cubelette and plays its show (PixelGodFind.PlayAtScreen).
- The Cubelette is a trophy for now.

PixelHints

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelHints.cs

First-time tips: Inspector list of Hint (id, enabled, text; defaults added by EnsureDefaultHints), shown once each via PixelNotice (remembered in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Hint.<id>, queued one after another, showDelay, master hintsEnabled). PixelHints.Trigger(id); hooks events itself (potion drunk, device placed device_<kind>, crafted, first combo, minigame spawned minigame_<id>, PixelClicker.UltraGained); PixelShop.TryBuy triggers crafting/grab/timestop/bank, upgrade_interval/clicks/combo/bank, upgrade_ultra (Ultra boost) and pixel_<Type> for packs that unlock pixel types. The event_log intro tip is NOT queued as a box: Enqueue only announces it as a log line (ShortFor text) whose click opens the tip (ShowEventLogTip on tutorial replay does the same). Also owns the event log: a transparent box at the bottom left running from above the bottom black bar up to the top black bar (logWidth wide), newest line at the bottom (PixelHints.Announce(short, fullText)); lines in the upper part fade out towards the top (fadeUpperLines, fadeStartFraction 0.5, fadeTopAlpha; per-row CanvasGroup set in UpdateRowFade, called from ApplyScroll, so it follows scrolling); a new event shows only the newest line (fullView false: RebuildRows builds one row) and the whole list only when the player opens the log (the EventLog key: DisplayOverlay(true); pressing it while only the newest line shows opens the full list, pressing it again hides it); the whole box fades after overlayVisibleSeconds unless the mouse is over it; mouse wheel over it scrolls; left-click a line to show its full tip, Enter shows the box when hidden and fades it away when open, right-click anywhere also fades it away (logWidth, logFontSize); the log (historyMax) is saved with the save file (its own save part eventLog via PixelSaveRegistry, ExportHistory/ImportHistory) and SuppressFor mutes events while a save loads; each Hint has a shortText; also announces new pixel types via onTierUnlocked, the auto clicker and the first black hole; ignored for startupQuietSeconds after start). Every shop purchase / upgrade level / Ultra boost (not Value upgrades: too frequent) also announces itself on the event line (PixelShop.AnnouncePurchase; first-time tips replace it with their own line). Extra bank/Time Stop tips: bank_hose/_suck/_spit/_full, timestop_first/_empty. Also runs the new-game intro (after the title screen; marked seen only once both tips were confirmed): once, on a fresh game, opens the Log with a tip, then (after "Got it") the Inventory with a tip, then a third tip on how currency is earned (introEarnText: clicking is the main way, upgrades / minigames / husk management open other ways); the tier guide appears after that (showIntro, introLogMessage, introInventoryText, introEarnText; uses PixelNotice.Show(..., onClosed)). Added by PixelClicker.Awake.

Intro tip 4 (right click): the new-game intro now has a fourth Got-it box right after the one about clicking (introRightClickText): the right mouse button (hold a block, Block Hold + its stake). Showing it marks the stake_intro tip as seen; saves that already finished the intro get stake_intro once after 25 manual clicks instead (PixelBlockHold).

Intro fixes (2026-10-16): the cube cannot be clicked while the new-game intro is pending or running (PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock includes PixelHints.IntroHoldsGuide), so the tier guide and clicking start after the last box; a game counts as new while fewer than introFreshClicks (60) pixels were collected, so a few early clicks never skip the intro; tip 4 (right click) only shows when Block Hold is unlocked (Standard tree) - otherwise stake_intro stays unseen and shows later via PixelBlockHold.

New game decided at Play (2026-10-16): PixelTitleScreen.NewGameStarted is set in OnPlay (true when nothing was loaded). The intro plays and clicks stay blocked from Play until its last box whenever it is true, whatever the player clicked or which tips were seen in earlier games; the old 'nothing collected' check is only the fallback when there is no title screen.

PixelLog

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelLog.cs

Bottom-left "Log" toggle with two tabs (the husks / Clicks tab was removed; per-pixel click counts are now in Settings > Stats, 'Clicks per pixel', with PixelStats.RecentClicks +N indicators; the code for the old tab is still there but unreachable) - formerly three tabs (the window has ONE fixed size, FixedPanelHeight, on every tab; each tab's list scrolls inside it): Pixels (unlocked tiers with how many times each was clicked (PixelTier.timesCollected, multipliers don't count; overall total; +1 per click indicators; no vacuum deltas)) and Achievements (scrolling list, one badge-style row per achievement (BuildAchievementRow: a panel per achievement with a gap between rows, the pixel picture in a framed box, big title, wide progress bar with its '60 / 100' in the middle; a search box (achievementSearchField, searchPlaceholder) sits between the total bar and the first heading, same width: it filters by title / description / section and shows matches expanded; hovering a row shows a tooltip (UpdateAchievementTip / AchievementTipText) saying explicitly whether it counts CLICKING (clicks only) or COLLECTING (everything earned), with progress and tier; the old click-to-switch grouping is gone, BuildAchievementGroups makes one group per achievement)) and Goals (RefreshGoalsTab: progress bars - the next pixel unlock, before Red / Green / Blue an 'earn the Black that brings Cubie's second visit' goal (PixelGuideVendor.BlackUntilVisitTwo; Cubie unlocks RGB and opens the shop), then once Red is unlocked and the Shop button shows a 'Purchase the Auto Clicker' goal (PixelShop.ShopAvailable / TryGetAutoClickerGoal: bar = price lines saved up, each capped at its own price; buyRgbGoalFormat/Description) and, once it is bought and the Toggles tutorial is done, a Lumberjack's Pack goal (TryGetWoodcutterGoal; both share TryGetPackGoal; the old RGB Pack / Combo Meter goals are gone), the PixelGoals table goals and Cubie's bill (PixelCubieBill.NextGoal), plus every PixelMinigame tracker that is running or has progress, e.g. Ghosts Caught, Singularity, Stardust, Meteor Chunks; goalsTabText, goalRowHeight; goal rows are badge-style like the achievements (BuildGoalRow: framed live 3D picture of the goal's item via SkinIcon (Goal.skinTier, GoalSkinTier(minigameId): ghost -> Ghost, blackhole -> Singularity, meteor -> Meteor, stardust -> Solar, next unlock -> that pixel, buy-RGB -> Red; no item = a spinning gold cube), title, wide bar with the count in the middle; the list is as wide as the tab row, the scroll bar sits in the margin; hovering shows the explanation + exact numbers through the shared tooltip, GoalTipText / hoverGoal); tab content groups are built by the shared BuildScrollGroup). Achievement icons show each pixel's real look (SkinIcon: rendered once per type with CreateDisplayPixel in a hidden studio into a cached RenderTexture; PixelAchievements.GetSkinTier; pet achievements too; other achievements keep the spinning PixelCubeIcon); the 'click to switch' mode tag is a single non-wrapping line. Has its own local FormatAmount.

Goals table + the early goals ARE the unlocks (2026-10-18): the Goals tab also lists the rows of PixelGoals (AddTableGoals, right after the next-unlock goal; data table in the Inspector, header of PixelGoals.cs). The built-in rows run the early game: Click 50 blocks -> Cubie's visit 1 (the Recycler), Crush 10 husks (appears at 20 husks on the floor) -> Gray unlocks, Recycle 20 husks (after visit 1) -> Black unlocks, Get a crush streak of 5 (minute 3) -> 60 Standard XP, Earn 120 Black (after Recycle 20) -> Cubie's visit 2 (RGB + the shop). Gray / Black use TierUnlockMode.ByGoal (no threshold unlock while a goal unlocks them; the threshold is the fallback), so the next-unlock row skips them and the 'Unlock Red, Green and Blue' row is hidden while a goal grants visit 2 (PixelGuideVendor.BlackUntilVisitTwo = -1). The on-screen guide line (PixelUI.BuildGuideText) follows the current goal: 'Crush 10 husks  (3 / 10)  -  Gray Pixels unlock' (goalGuideFormat).
--- docs/systems/PixelLooks.md ---
PixelLooks

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelLooks.cs

Editing looks in game: the Look Studio (PixelLookStudio.cs, Dev Hub tab 'Looks') edits any type's PixelLook at run time and exports it as a new PixelLook { ... } line. To make an exported look official: put the line in PixelLooks.CreateDefaults (replacing the type's old entry) and add a looksVersion step in PixelClickerLookFixups.cs that replaces that type's saved look; apply the export's Block values to the tier's defaults. A new PixelLook field shows up in the studio by itself; add its name to a section in PixelLookStudio.Sections.

Per-pixel-type 3D look, separate from the tier data (the UI keeps the tier colour): PixelLook list on PixelClicker (looks, useLooks; defaults from PixelLooks.CreateDefaults()): colour override + alpha, the basic pixels (White, Gray, Black, Red, Green, Blue) have a subtle drawn surface (PixelLook.basicSurface: Grain / Brushed / Sheen / Mosaic via PixelLooks.BasicTexture, near-white 128 px textures that multiply the colour) (Obsidian and Seed have the same bevel rim, shade taken from their tier colour via outlineShadeOfTier: Obsidian -0.55 (dark, looksVersion 24), Seed -0.4; looksVersion 20) and a thin rim (PixelLook.outlineBevel: a flat rim on every face along its four borders, mitred at the corners, solid at the cube's edge and fading inwards (AddBevelRims; no overlapping boxes, nothing sticks out) - no neon halo / white core - EdgeMesh(..., bevel)) that is a different SHADE of the pixel's own colour (PixelLook.outlineShade: above 0 lightens towards white, below 0 darkens towards black; White -0.3, Gray / Red / Green / Blue +0.4, Black +0.3; thickness ~0.027, strength ~0.5); Black is drawn in a slightly lighter near-black so the sheen shows; looksVersion 21 gives them a material feel (White matte 0.35, Gray brushed metal 0.65 / 0.55, Black glossy 0.85, RGB glossy 0.6 + faint emission 0.12; revertible as one commit) ; looksVersion 17 adds / updates them in saved lists (18 switches the rim to the shade, 19 to the flat bevel) (a Red/Green/Blue look you already edited is left alone), metallic/smoothness, plain emission, neon edge outline (no pixel type uses it by default now; runtime-built mesh of 24 thin boxes with vertex colours, Sprites/Default material), surface texture (streakTexture white streaks on black, damageCracks: cracks that spread with every hit on a tough pixel, drawn by PixelLooks.SurfaceTexture and set per hit via PixelClicker.SetLiveDamage; used by Obsidian; the live cube's property block is cleared in ApplyTierLook), jelly wobble (live cube scale in AnimatePixel; falling copies draw on a child Wobble Visual), husk physics per look (customPhysics: bounce/friction/gravityMultiplier; ignoreViewBounds read by PixelViewBounds via PixelClicker.IgnoresViewBounds; floatAway -> OldPixelFloat: after N ground bounces it stops colliding, drifts up/sideways and is removed off-screen; used by Ghost; a floating ghost is kept above the floor in OldPixelFloat.FixedUpdate so a gravity well can't drag it through the ground), gravity well (gravityWell, wellRadius, wellPullSpeed, wellGrip, wellRingOpacity: OldPixelGravityWell drags other husks toward it at a constant speed (overrides floor friction; works while carried with Pixel Grabbing) while it is at full size, plus a very faint camera-facing ring sprite; used by Singularity, whose spawn weight is 0.08; each well holds at most wellMaxPixels (20) pixels at a time: it keeps the nearest unclaimed husks in its radius (a pixel is claimed by one well only, OldPixelGravityWell.claims), other Singularity pixels inside the radius count towards the 20 so wells packed close together starve each other and have to be spaced out; the well pixel itself lasts wellLifetime = 300 s (5 min, set in the well's Start via OldPixelDespawn.SetLifetime) and every husk inside its pull radius is kept alive by OldPixelDespawn.KeepAlive() each physics step, so it doesn't age while the well works; a flying meteor pixel within wellFlyReach (4) pull radii is steered towards the well (turn rate 5 rad/s, slowed) and, once inside the ring, captured: PixelClicker.LandFlyingPixel + its speed cut to 20%, then it is dragged in like the others), face circles (flat black discs on the 6 faces, used by Vacuum; faceRing + faceRingColor draw each as a vortex: black centre, a glowing violet ring that fades outward (PixelLooks.VortexMesh), plus a violet bevel rim and faint emission, looksVersion 22), dark-matter core (counter-spinning cube with a runtime swirl texture), lightning (Electric: PixelLookLightning builds a dynamic mesh of jagged crossed-ribbon bolts along the 12 edges + a few arcs through the inside, re-rolled ~12x/s on unscaled time, a wide soft glow pass + a thin bright core pass, simpler when more than 6 exist; unlit vertex colours via OverlayMaterial; lightningCoreColor/GlowColor/Jitter), forceTranslucent, and shatter (glass: OldPixelShatter breaks the husk into OldPixelShards when it hits the ground; the pixel is removed without payout; sound glass_shatter). Live-cube extras are managed by PixelClicker.UpdateLiveExtras; husk copies get them in SpawnFallingCopy.

PixelMeteorSolar

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelMeteorSolar.cs

Meteor and Solar rework. Meteor (PixelLook.meteorTexture + damageCracks + cometTail; tier now clicksToCollect 3, pays 45, glow off, packDataVersion 12 migrates saved packs / tiers, looksVersion 35 replaces the saved looks): a dark cratered rock (PixelStarTextures.Meteor(level, max), 64 px, lava cracks that spread / brighten per hit, dull embers at level 0), a few rising embers on the cube (PixelCometFx.Attach), sparks + chips on every non-final hit (PixelCometFx.Hit, disableMeteorFx) and a fiery world-space wake on the husk that streaks away (PixelCometFx.AttachWake, added in the fly branch of SpawnFallingCopy); it still launches in a straight tapered trail and FlyAwayLaunched still knocks stars down. Solar (PixelLook.sunTexture + sunFx + custom physics with gravity 0): churning gold with a white-hot face centre (PixelStarTextures.Sun), a turning corona + rays billboard behind the cube (PixelSunFx / PixelSunRays, swells with daylight; also on its husks), and the husk HOVERS (OldPixelSolarHover: spring to ~1.7 husk widths above the floor with a bob, drifts to a stop) and pays a trickle (TrickleFraction 0.08 of its value every TrickleSeconds 4 via AddCurrency, xPayMultiplier). Daylight (PixelDaylight, added by PixelClicker.Awake, not saved): every Solar click (OnSolarClick, in CollectInternal) adds perClick (0.1), it fades decayPerSecond (0.012); at level L seeds / Seed pets grow faster (GrowthBonus = L x seedGrowthBonus 1.5, added in SolarSeedBoostAt) and hover pay x (1 + L x hoverPayBonus 2); a small sun + bar at the top right (canvas 105) shows while L > 0 and Solar is unlocked (hideBar, disableDaylight). Old Solar pixels last solarLifetimeFactor (0 = 1.6) times as long as other husks (LifetimeFactor, in the two normal AddDespawn calls of SpawnFallingCopy); old Solar clusters (5 touching never despawn) show a faint yellow aura: UpdateSolarClusters calls OldPixelSolarAura.Keep() on every member of a complete group and each draws a soft camera-facing glow that fades in / out (overlapping glows read as one aura). Not built: Stardust raising daylight, Water drying faster in daylight.

PixelNotice

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelNotice.cs

PixelNotice.Show(message, seconds, onClosed, compact, small, aboveCombo, nearLog) (nearLog = small box at the bottom left beside the event log, used by the event_log tip shown at the first tier unlock; aboveCombo = a small box at the bottom just above the combo meter, used for the first-combo tip) / Hide() (small = a narrow small-text box, used for the first-time minigame tips minigame_<id> and the first black hole): a one-off tip box (message + "Got it") at the top of the screen under the black bar; built lazily, one at a time; Escape closes it like Got it and drops the queued tips (PixelWindows priority 140, PixelHints.ClearQueue). Used for first-time hints (first black hole, PixelHints); PixelNotice.IsShowing. Typewriter + back: untimed boxes type their text out (TypeCharsPerSecond 80); the first Got it press completes the text, the second closes (OnOk). Optional onBack parameter of Show adds a small back arrow at the top left (hold 0.6 s via PixelHoldButton): it closes the box WITHOUT running onClosed and calls onBack (the shop and skills tutorials pass the previous step). Cubie's pages and the visitor greeting / aside boxes type the same way (visitors: StartType / TickType / FinishType), Cubie's panel and the aside box have a hold-to-go-back arrow (Cubie: one page back, the gift is only handed over once; aside: back to the greeting). nearScreen (a Func<Vector2?> screen point the small box follows, beside it) + freezeTime (Time.timeScale 0 until the box is closed, restored after): used by the first Ghost Hunt tip, which PixelHints shows next to the ghost (PixelGhostMinigame.GhostScreenPosition) with no auto-close. With PixelShop.tipBoxMatchesShop (default on) the big purchase / unlock tip boxes sit to the right of the open shop window (PixelNotice.SideAnchor/SideWidth/SideGap, set by PixelShop.BuildPanel: as tall as the shop, as wide as the currency panel, mirroring it on the left); with the shop closed they fall back to a centred shop-sized box. beside (a window's RectTransform, e.g. PixelLog.WindowRect / PixelUI.WindowRect) puts a 640-wide box just to the right of that window, top- or bottom-aligned (besideBottom); the new-game intro tips use it.

Skip button
PixelNotice.Show(..., onSkip): a small Skip button at the box's top right (hold 0.9 s; like the back arrow). It closes the box without running onClosed and calls onSkip. The Shop, Skills and Toggles tutorials pass an onSkip that jumps to their own end step (marks only that tutorial seen), so skipping one never skips another.
--- docs/systems/PixelOrthoView.md ---
PixelOrthoView

PixelOrthoView.cs

Flat view (added by PixelClicker.Awake; Settings > Display > 'Flat view' = PixelOrthoView.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.FlatView, off by default, synced per save in PixelSettingsSync): switches the main camera to orthographic so there is no depth shrinking. The size is depth x tan(fov/2) x sizeFactor at the cube's distance (CubeBasePosition), recomputed every frame while flat, so the cube keeps its on-screen size and the zoom key / orbit camera (which move the camera) still zoom. Blended in and out over blendSeconds (0 = 0.6) by mixing the perspective and orthographic projection matrices (PixelOrthoView.Blend, 0..1). It stays perspective while the title screen shows, the camera intro moves or the camera swings back to the main menu (those lerp the field of view); the blend starts when that ends. [DefaultExecutionOrder(200)] so it runs after PixelZoom / PixelOrbitCamera, which also write the camera in LateUpdate. A camera that is already orthographic in the scene is left alone.

Things that assume a perspective camera and were adapted: PixelViewBounds.EdgePlane (orthographic rays are parallel, so each edge plane is built from the ray origins) and its plane cache (ortho flag / size), PixelFloor.FitToView cache (ortho size), and PixelUIKit.ViewHeightAt(cam, depth) (use it instead of computing the view height from fieldOfView; the Bomb and Ghost minigames and KeepOnScreen use it). Not adapted: the other minigames' depth placement (they still work: positions come from ViewportToWorldPoint), the horizon / fog look (there is no vanishing horizon).
--- docs/systems/PixelOvercharge.md ---
PixelOvercharge

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelOvercharge.cs / PixelElectricLinks.cs / PixelBolts.cs

Electric pixel abilities (both components added by PixelClicker.Awake). Overcharge (PixelOvercharge): a click that harvests an Electric pixel (manual, or the auto clicker's own with triggerOnAutoClicks) gives the auto clicker a split-second burst (burstClicks 8 clicker.AutoCollect() calls over burstSeconds 0.6; needs the Auto Clicker bought - toggled off still counts, needsAutoClicker; the burst's own clicks never start another burst) while PixelOverchargeRing draws jagged bolts circling the cube on tilted orbits (world space, follows the cube, fades) and a flashing, shaking, popping "OVERCHARGE!" rises above it (UI canvas 640; sound overcharge, a code-made PixelSynth.Charge sweep while its clip list is empty; stat overcharge.bursts). Device links (PixelElectricLinks): every scan (linkRange = 3 main-pixel widths, only pixels in the camera view) the Electric husks on the floor are linked into a network growing out from the placed devices (PixelPlacedDevice.All): from each device DAISY CHAINS run pixel to pixel (Attach / NearestUnlinked: every pixel links to the nearest unlinked one in reach, then that one to the next; new chains start from the device while pixels remain in reach; pixels keep their old partner via stickiness); Electric pixels out of reach of any device still daisy-chain to each other (a chain grows from both ends), Battery model: pixels linked to a timed device POWER it (PixelPlacedDevice.HoldPower / Powered: its countdown pauses, the timer text shows POWERED; uses-mode devices just get arcs). Every pixel that arcs to another (to a device or only to each other, Register) gets pixelCharge (100) charge and never ages while linked (a free chain's first pixel is tracked in chainSeeds); only device groups drain. The group of a device is the pool and DrainAndHold drains drainPerSecond (25) from ONE pixel at a time, the one FURTHEST along its daisy chain from the device (ChainDepth, ties -> older), so chains burn from the far end and the pixel touching the machine goes last; it flickers / sparks below fizzleFraction and Dissipates (OldPixelDespawn.Begin) at 0; new pixels add to the pool without interrupting; linked pixels never age (KeepAlive) and stay when the device ends. At most maxLinkedTotal (30) pixels are linked in TOTAL across all groups (3 + 10 = 13 used): EvictOldest despawns the oldest beyond that (out with the old, new ones are safe until they are the oldest). The old +1 s per pixel / +10 s chain life / vanish-with-device rules are gone. the Electric pet is a rechargeable battery bank (PixelPets.TryGetActivePetBody: owned, switched on, shown, not shattered; its Rigidbody joins the daisy-chain network as one more node, never despawned, not counted in the 30-cap, never eaten by a Charge Booster): while it is part of a device's group the group drains the pet's own charge (petCapacity 600, petCharge, same drainPerSecond) BEFORE any pixel, and once it is empty the pixels drain as usual; it recharges petRechargePerSecond (2 = about 5 minutes) only while it powers nothing, and a tiny camera-facing meter floats over it (showPetMeter, DrawPetMeter; the charge is not saved, it starts full); the pet stands still while it is linked (PixelElectricLinks.PetConnected); The arcs are one dynamic mesh redrawn every redrawInterval. PixelBolts = shared mesh builder for jagged crossed-ribbon bolts (Jagged, Strokes; glow + core passes; vertex colours for PixelLooks.OverlayMaterial).
--- docs/systems/PixelPadMinigame.md ---
PixelPadMinigame

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelPadMinigame.cs

Ultra Holes (the reworked Ultra Pad; id pad, pack unlocksMinigame = "pad", Minigames tab, needs the RGB pack; NOT a takeover minigame): every minInterval..maxInterval s (and AllowAnother) FOUR round holes open in the floor, one near each corner of the screen (CornerSpot: the viewport corner cornerInset inside the black bars, nudged inwards until the whole hole is on screen and AreaFree; at least two are needed; one ClaimArea per hole via claimOwners), each a dark rim + black void + deeper black disc (BuildHole, radius holeRadiusPixels 1.3 main-pixel widths). They stay open roundSeconds (45); a timer line shows at the top of the screen (hudFormat). One hole is the correct one, marked by a spinning CreateDisplayPixel model of the wanted pixel type (random unlocked, floor-lying type) floating in its centre (AnimateModels; the other holes show nothing). A husk whose middle comes inside captureFraction (0.85) of a hole's radius falls in (TakePixels: ReleaseOldPixel + OldPixelDespawn.Begin, which makes it a trigger so it tumbles down through the floor while shrinking): the WANTED type in the CORRECT hole pays its normal value (ReleaseOldPixel(body, true)) and has ultraChance (10%) of an Ultra pixel; anything else is simply taken away (no payout, no penalty). The marker jumps to another hole after every correct drop and after swapIdleSeconds (12) without one (NextHole; changeTypeOnSwap also changes the wanted type, off by default). A very gentle pull acts only in a thin band outside each hole's edge (pullAcceleration 5, pullBandFraction 0.5; nothing drawn). An event-log line sums up a round. Sounds pad_spawn/_click/_wrong/_ultra. Ultra pixels are ONE general currency (PixelClicker.TotalUltra held / UltraEarned lifetime, AddUltra(amount), RollUltra(chance), saved in the GameState as ClickerState.ultraHeld / ultraEarned; shown as an animated row at the very top of the Inventory's Currency tab with a line under it - PixelUltraSwatch: rainbow-cycling chip, glow, shine sweep, sparkles) for future upgrades; the minigame's tracker shows Ultra pixels ever earned (PixelClicker.UltraEarned, saved via the tracker count; spending Ultra on boosts does not lower it).  Ultra is now a general minigame reward: PixelClicker.RollUltra(chance) + PixelMinigame.UltraRoll(clicker, chance, ...) (per-round cap ultraRoundCount, event-log line, and PixelUltraFx.Play at the pixel's position: a rising gold "+1 Ultra Pixel" text, a sparkle burst and the sound ultra_gain; the Ultra Pad keeps its own text/sound and only adds the sparkles) are called by Snake (each eaten pixel of a won round + a guaranteed one), Sorting Race (each pixel of a win, chance x (1 + time left fraction)), Breakout (each broken brick) and Black Hole (each swallowed pixel); each has ultraChance, ultraMaxPerRound, disableUltra in the Inspector (0 = the coded default 4% / 6% / 3% / 1%, caps 3 / 3 / 3 / 2). Extends PixelScheduledMinigame (the shared round schedule; id pad); sounds pad_spawn/_click/_wrong/_ultra.
--- docs/systems/PixelPauseMenu.md ---
PixelPauseMenu

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelPauseMenu.cs (Settings screen)

The Settings screen is a scrolling list with section headers (Gameplay, Interface, Accessibility, Display, Audio, Controls - the Saving section was folded into Gameplay). Row helpers: AddSettingToggle (re-reads its value whenever the screen opens via settingsRefreshers), AddSliderRow (0..1, or a min / max / whole-number range with a value read-out and a reread getter, e.g. Display > 'Orbit field of view' = PixelOrbitCamera.FieldOfView), AddDropdownRow (every multi-choice setting is a drop-down now; the old cycle-button choice row was deleted), AddLookRow (floor / sky picker = the shared PixelUIKit.CreateLookDropdown with big rows; the floor's row also holds its on/off tick box), AddEyedropperButton + AddCycleButton (small buttons beside a colour tick box), AddWideButtonRow, AddSettingsSectionHeader. SettingsCatalog() lists every BUILT row (section, label, kind, drop-down choices, current value, look source) for the Game Database's Settings tab, so a new row shows up there with no extra wiring.
Current layout: Gameplay = replay tutorial, abbreviate numbers, hide purchased, run in background, pause stops game, day / night cycle + length of a day, autosave message + autosave interval, floor (style + tick box), when the floor is off, sky. Interface = tips, event log, popups, block tips, next-up window, drop guide, fancy pause menu, pixel-assemble buttons, camera intro, menu buttons, neon bar glow (+ eyedropper + a small rainbow 'cycle colour' button), custom UI colour (+ eyedropper), custom bar colour (+ eyedropper). Accessibility = cube rotation, pulsing, colour-blind marks, popup size, event log size, UI scale, reset achievement popup position (last). Display = screen filter + strength, VSync, fullscreen, resolution. Audio = mute, UI click sounds (PixelAudio.UiClicks, gates sound id ui_click), the three volumes. Removed: Click effects (row only), Flat view (the View window has Orthographic), Fancy buttons / Fancy windows (always on now), Auto-contrast outline and Cube spotlight (PixelContrast.cs deleted). Accessibility section (collapsible header after Interface): cube rotation (switching it off restores the cube's scene pose, PixelClicker.startRotation), pulsing, colour-blind marks, click effects level, popup size, event log size, UI scale. Interface: first-time tips (PixelHints.TipsEnabled) + Replay tutorial (PixelHints.ReplayTutorial), event log on/off + text size (PixelHints.EventLogEnabled/LogSizeChoice), number popups on/off + size (PixelUI.PopupsEnabled/PopupSizeChoice), UI scale, colour-blind marks. Saving: autosave interval + message (PixelSaveGame.AutoSaveChoice/AutoSaveMessageChoice). Menu search (top right of the main screen, 'Paused' is left-aligned: menuSearchField, OnMenuSearchChanged): hides the button grid and lists matching menu entries plus up to 6 matching settings rows (settingsItems labels); a settings result calls OpenFilteredSettings (settingsFilter: LayoutSettings then shows only matching rows and their headings, folded or not); the search clears each time the menu opens. A Key Bindings screen (BuildKeysPanel, click a key, press the new one, Escape cancels) opens from Controls. Row widths: every row under a section header (toggles, choice rows, sliders, wide buttons) is SettingsRowInset (112) narrower than the list, exactly the Replay tutorial button's width. Per-save settings sync: PixelSettingsSync.Known_ lists each PlayerPrefs key with its TYPE (0 int, 1 float, 2 string) - a key registered with the wrong type is read as 0 and overwritten on load (that wiped the eyedropped glow colour, the hues and the skill pack); an entry stored with another kind than the key's is ignored.

PixelPauseMenu How To Play (tabs)

The pause menu has one Game Guide button (between Settings and Give feedback; it was called How To Play) that opens a screen with three TABS: Controls, How To Play (the written guide) and Database (opens the game database window) - the tabs: Controls (Controls.txt, formerly the pause menu's Controls button) and Game Guide (HowToPlay.txt); each tab is its own GuideScreen panel and the tab buttons switch between the panels (BuildGuideScreen(..., isControlsTab)). Settings ends with a 'Keybinds' header with one 'Change keybinds' button that opens the key-binding screen (titled 'Keybinds').

PixelPauseMenu Game Guide text tabs (Controls and How To Play are two panels; the third tab, Database, opens the database window)

Two more screens that show Assets/Resources/HowToPlay.txt and Assets/Resources/Controls.txt (the user edits these files; GuideScreen, BuildGuideScreen, RefreshGuideScreen - it reads the file and lays out the rows - in PixelPauseMenuGuide.cs). File format: lines starting // are hidden notes, # Heading = coloured heading, Key | Action = two columns, anything else is shown as written (TMP rich text allowed). When a keybind or control changes, update Controls.txt; when a major system is added, update HowToPlay.txt.

Deleting a save slot
Hold the RIGHT mouse button on a slot that has a save in the Save / Load window (PixelPauseMenu.UpdateSlotDeleteHold, deleteSlotHoldSeconds 0 = 1.4 s, a red PixelHoldMeter ring on the pointer): when the ring is full PixelSaveGame.DeleteSlot removes the file, its screenshot / meta and the pending name. The hover box shows 'Hold right click to delete'. Works from the main menu's Save / Load window too. The autosave list is not affected.

Look search (2026-10-16): the Floor and Sky rows are found by the name of ANY of their looks (lookRows, RowMatches) in the Settings search, and the pause menu's search lists up to 8 matching floors / skies as results ('Floor: Grass', '(in use)' highlighted) that switch to that look at once.

Title plate (2026-10-18): every section screen built with BuildSectionPanel (Settings, Stats, Save / Load, Guide, Quit, Restart, Key bindings, Playlist) has a sunken rounded title plate across its top (titlePlateColor, StyleBox inset); the X (AddCloseX) sits inside its right end with equal gaps (PlatePad), a three-pixel ornament mirrors it on the left, and the title is centred between them (auto-shrinks if long). Geometry: PlateMargin / PlatePad / PlateHeight (titleFontSize x 1.45). The MAIN pause panel has the same plate (BuildTitlePlate): 'Paused' on the left after the ornament, the menu search box in its right end (where the X sits elsewhere). No blue line under the plates (removed 2026-10-18). Small cubes with light trails fall from behind every plate now and then (PixelMenuFx.AddCubeFall, fancy menu only).

PixelPets

Progress (2026-10-24): the owned pets (type, off, outfit, name) live in the GameState (PixelGameState.Current.pets, PetsState): each live pet keeps a reference to its entry (Pet.saved), and OnLoaded rebuilds the bodies. Overrides any older save wording below.

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelPets.cs

Pets (added by PixelClicker.Awake, singleton PixelPets.Instance): every click (PixelCollected, manual or auto at autoClickChanceFactor) rolls chancePerClick (0.0003) for a pet of that pixel type (one pet per PixelType; owned types don't roll). A hit freezes the game (PixelPets.PopupOpen, Time.timeScale = 0, restored on close; PixelTimeStop doesn't undo it) behind a modal popup (canvas 750, spinning PixelCubeIcon, "Awesome!"; Escape closes it via PixelWindows priority 150; popups queue) and the pet then spawns: a rigidbody + box collider the size of the main pixel (PixelClicker.PixelBaseSize) wearing CreateDisplayPixel of its type, bouncy PhysicMaterial, that every hopInterval seconds (only from the ground) hops with a sideways + upward impulse and a rolling torque, heads back to the cube when beyond roamRadius or near the screen edge, is respawned above the cube if it falls out of the world, and is hidden while a takeover minigame runs (TakeoverActive). Mouse: hovering a pet stops it (hoverStopDamping; PixelPets.HoveringPet, sets PixelClicker.ExternalClickBlock so the cube ignores the click and PixelGrab skips it), (a pet that is behind the clickable cube from the camera never counts as hovered, so the cube still gets the click: PetUnderPointer compares ray distances) left-click picks it up (drag on the camera-facing plane, followSharpness, collides like Pixel Grabbing) and letting go throws it (throwStrength, maxThrowSpeed). The Ghost pet only hovers (RoamGhost: no gravity, trigger collider, drifts between random spots ghostHoverHeight pixel-widths above the floor with a bob and a slow spin; a thrown ghost's speed fades into the drift). The Glass Pet (MaybeBreak / BreakAndReform) shatters at random while it moves (glassBreakChance per second at glassBreakSpeed, faster = likelier; not while held or standing still): the pet turns invisible but stays solid in place, PetReformShards fly about for glassHoldSeconds, then reverse back into it over glassReformSeconds and it reappears (glassCooldown); sounds glass_shatter / glass_shatter_reform. (Glass husks are unchanged: they still shatter on landing and are gone.) The Seed Pet (RoamSeed) is a big sprout (seedPetScale 3x the main pixel, PixelLooks.CreateSproutObject + a tall box collider) that never hops: wherever it lands or is put down it stands up, pushes up a dirt mound, goes kinematic and plants itself (PlantPet, only after a short settle time and when SolidBelow finds ground other than its own collider or the clicker cube; it sprouts up out of the floor at a spot beside the cube (SeedPetSpawnPoint + FindGround: starts hidden below the floor, rises over seedPetRiseSeconds (1.6) with the dirt mound staying at floor level, Pet.rise/groundY, planted at once; if no floor is found below, it drops from the spot as before) and ignores the cube's colliders, so it can't land and plant on top of the cube; picking it up uproots it; picking it up uproots it, Uproot), and while planted it sows a seed every seedPetInterval (10-20) s (SowSeed: a random spot 1.5..seedPetRadius (5) main-pixel widths around it, on screen, PixelClicker.PlantSeedSproutAt = a SeedSprout planted at once that grows a random pixel, capped by seedSproutCap; hyper pets sow faster). The Vacuum Pet (SuckAround) sucks up the nearest husk within vacuumRadius pixel-widths every vacuumInterval s (ReleaseOldPixel(body, false): no payout, nothing else counts; they fly in and shrink) and a small camera-facing number above it (counterText, LateUpdate) shows the total (stat counter pet.vacuum, PixelStats.Total(key)). The Stats screen has a Pets section (found X / total, every pixel type's pet or ???, the vacuum count). Slower despawn: while a pet is out its pixel type's husks age despawnSlowFactor (2) times slower (PixelPets.DespawnRate, read by OldPixelDespawn every 0.5 s). Spawn bonus: while a pet is active (owned and switched on) its pixel type's spawn weight is multiplied by spawnWeightMultiplier (2) in PixelClicker.PickSpawnTier (SpawnWeightOf / PixelPets.SpawnMultiplier; potions and dev spawn selections still override). Toggles window has a fourth Pets tab (tick box per owned pet = SetOn; the Toggles button also shows once a pet exists). Saved as SaveData.pets ("type:off" strings; Export/Import). Stat pets.found, Stats row "Pets found". Dev tools: "Give pet (selected pixel)" button (popup; "All pixels" gives every pet quietly) and a separate "Give all pets" button (all dev gives pass Award(type, popup, startOff: true): the pets arrive switched OFF), and "Give a Dragon Cube" (the lowest-numbered one you still lack).

Which pixels have pets
PixelPets.CanHavePet(type): every pixel type except the seven Dragon Cubes and the hazards (Pest, Gas Pocket, Hornet Nest, Toadstool, Jellyfish, Mold). It used to skip all standalone pixels (Fire, the ores, the skill blocks...); they have pets now. The pet find roll (OnCollected), the 'Pet' achievements (PixelAchievements.RegisterPetAchievements, one per pet type) and the Game Database's Pet entries all use it. The Collect / Click achievements follow the same idea (PixelAchievements.NoPixelAchievements: only Dragon Cubes and hazards are left out).

Right-click menu (2026-10-16, PixelPetsMenu.cs): right-click a pet for a small menu at the pointer with its name and 'Pet away' (SetOn(type, false), turned back on in Toggles > Pets). Clicking elsewhere / Escape closes it.

PixelPetsCustomize.cs

Part of partial class PixelPets: names and outfits. Hovering a pet shows a name tag above it (UpdateTag, EnsureTag: screen-space canvas, the pet's name + a procedurally drawn pencil button; stays tagGraceSeconds after the mouse leaves and while the mouse is over it, and the pet stands still while its tag is up). The pencil opens the customization window (OpenCustomize; freezes the game like the found popup via PopupOpen, Escape closes it through PixelWindows priority 160): a name box (maxNameLength, empty = default "{Pixel} Pet"; Pet.customName), a 3D view (a hidden preview studio far below the scene StudioHeight with a camera rendering into a RenderTexture shown by a RawImage; click-drag turns the model via an EventTrigger, it spins by itself otherwise; no extra light - it uses the scene's directional light) and < > arrows that change the outfit (Pet.outfit, applied to the live pet at once by ApplyOutfit). Outfits (OutfitList) are generated from outfitSeed (outfitCount = 12 + "Plain"), followed by the named special outfits (Specials(): Super Saiyan / Blue / God / Ultra Instinct / Legendary Spikes (Acc.SuperSaiyanHair = a cone-spike mane + Acc.Aura = see-through spheres and flames), Pirate Captain, Viking, Chef, Kitty, Bunny, Santa, Flower Child, Gentleman, Explorer, Punk; new accessories are appended to the Acc enum so saved outfit numbers don't change): 2-3 of four slots (head / face / neck / back) each with one accessory (Acc: Top Hat, Party Hat, Wizard Hat, Crown, Halo, Headphones, Antennae, Bandana, Sunglasses, Mustache, Bow Tie, Scarf, Cape, Wings) in palette colours, built from primitives + a runtime cone mesh in unit-cube space under an "Outfit" child (BuildOutfit, colliders removed) so most of the pet stays visible. PixelRobotWorker
