PixelRobotWorker.cs

UFO Helper (class and shop pack keep the old name 'Robot Worker' so saves keep working; shop pack unlocksRobot, Upgrades tab, 1M each; PixelShop.robot found/added like the bank; Activate/Deactivate): a small code-built flying saucer (hull, rim, glass dome with a green alien, spinning rim lights; fixed size robotScale) that idly hovers about the map (always higher than the cube - world height at least 1.4 pixel widths over the cube's centre and higher up the screen than it; no text label over it; UserDisabled switch in Toggles > Upgrades, saved in the GameState as UfoState.userDisabled; hoverHeight pixel widths above the floor height floorY, PickWander: random spots round the cube between minDistanceFromCube and maxRoamDistance, on screen, and at least minScreenGapFraction of the screen height from the cube ON SCREEN so it is never above it; new spot every wanderMinSeconds - wanderMaxSeconds; smooth velocity flySpeed, bob, leans into its flight; stops while hovered so it can be clicked) with a speech bubble showing an alien face floating over it (BuildBubble, clamped to the screen, also a button that opens the window). Click-through: while the mouse is over the saucer or its bubble both fade to hoverAlpha (parts swap to transparent materials, UpdateFade), left clicks pass through (PixelRobotWorker.Hovering stays false, the bubble is not a raycast target) and a RIGHT-click opens the window (BuildWindow, canvas 520, PixelWindows priority 55) listing the placeable devices (PixelConsumables.IsPlaceableKind: Vacuum, Fan, Charge Booster, Lightning Rod, Sprinkler) with the counts you hold. Picking one starts a normal ghost placement (PixelConsumables.BeginRobotPlacement, nothing is used up) and the chosen spot becomes the job (SetJob, a persistent see-through ghost via PixelConsumables.CreateGhost). Every checkInterval it looks whether a non-ending placed device of that index stands there (IsOccupied) and, when not and you hold one, flies to workHeight above the spot, switches on its abduction beam (BuildBeam / UpdateBeam: a see-through cone with vertex colours, beamColor, flicker, from the saucer's underside to the spot) for placeDelay s, then PixelConsumables.RobotPlace puts the device down already armed (uses one up; stat robot.placed) and DropDevice slides it down the beam over dropSeconds. Out of stock -> red label, it goes back to wandering. The window (780 x 780, a scroll list of AddRow rows: name left, count right ('Unlimited' for infinite, red when 0), hover highlight; two pages windowPage: devices and, with the Pixel Bank bought, 'Abduct a pixel into the bank' -> a list of unlocked pixel types with how many are banked; footer Back / Stop working). Bank job (bankJob / bankType, UpdateBankJob): it flies over the nearest loose husk of that type (FindBankTarget: not kinematic, not held), holds the beam on it for abductSeconds, then PixelBank.StoreOldPixel(body, undersideOfSaucer) / IsFull pulls it into the saucer and banks it; idles (wanders) when none are left or the bank is full (labels 'Bank full' / 'Needs the Pixel Bank'); stat robot.banked. The job is saved as a string (Export/Import, SaveData.robot, device by name). Renamed text fields (ufoTitle, ufoJobSetFormat, ufoBankJobFormat) so old serialized texts are dropped. Screen saver (PixelRobotSaver.cs, same partial class; disableScreenSaver, saverIdleMinutes 0 = 3, saverCheckInMin/Max 0 = 2-5 min, saverRoamSeconds 0 = 20, saverSpeedFactor 0 = 1.6): with no input (PixelInput.AnyInputHeld, pointer move, wheel) and no job / open window for the idle time it flies up out of view above the camera (Leaving -> Away); each check-in does a FlyBy across the top of the screen or a Roam visit (normal wandering) then leaves again; any input drops it back to normal wandering; the speech bubble hides while it is off screen; UserDisabled set (off or on) calls ResetSaver so toggling it off and on respawns it on screen. Ride along (PixelRobotCockpit.cs, same partial class; first row of the UFO window, boardText; PixelRobotWorker.Piloting makes ExternalClickBlock true and blocks Pixel Grabbing / first person): the glass dome holds a small cabin built in BuildUfo (floor + ring, a 16-panel ring console with 32 blinking buttons consoleButtons, a console screen, a round glass WINDOW in the cabin floor (hull / underside are lathe meshes MeshPrim / LatheMesh with a hole of WindowRadius, a dark shaft between, a transparent floor disc) so you can look down through the saucer, a tiny alien at a station, a cabinLamp that is only on while riding). Choosing the row puts you inside as a passenger: the camera (UFO Passenger root object, CopyFrom main camera, main cullingMask 0) sits at bodyT.TransformPoint(walkPos, CabinFloorY + eyeHeight) so it leans and flies with the saucer, which keeps wandering and doing its job on its own (Update runs the AI while Piloting, only hover / click / bubble are skipped). The mouse always looks around (cursor locked while riding, cockpitLookSpeed 0.35), WASD walk (walkSpeed, kept within walkRadius and off the alien); Escape (PixelWindows 125) climbs out (no Leave button: the cursor is locked). The old piloting (flying it yourself, the console screen camera, abducting from the cockpit) was removed.
--- docs/systems/PixelRules.md ---
Pixel rules: value, body and use (design, not built yet)

Status: agreed rule; step 1 built (husks stop paying currency, 2026-10-15). Switch: PixelClicker.husksPayCurrency (off = the rules; on = the old behaviour, for comparing). Still to build: the Recycler, charges from holding utility blocks, the drained look. This is a game-wide rule; every skill, machine and block should follow it. Woodcutting's design (WoodcuttingProgression.md) is the first place it is applied.

The problem

Today one block can pay twice: the click gives currency, and the husk it drops can be turned into currency again (the Vacuum and Vacuum Device pay out its stored value, the Pixel Bank stores it, a hovering Solar husk pays a trickle). It is not clear which of the two is the "real" reward, and it does not make sense in the game's world: you should get the money or the thing, not both.

The rule

A click splits a block into its VALUE and its BODY.

| What you get | What it is | What it is for |
|---|---|---|
| Currency (from the click) | the block's value | spending in the shop, selling to vendors |
| Husk (the body) | the drained, physical leftover - material | machines, crafting, charges, XP; never currency by itself (only the Recycler machine turns husks into currency, at a loss) |
| Charges (from holding a utility block) | the block's use | Pesticide, Whetstone and other tools |

Why it makes sense in the world: extraction

The clicker does not simply "break" blocks: a click extracts the block's essence (its colour, its value) into your counter, and what drops out is the drained husk. That is why you get both: the currency *is* the essence, and the husk is what is left once the essence is gone. The husk is still a real thing - wood is still wood - so it is useful as material, but it has no value of its own to cash in.

Idea for the look: husks could be slightly duller than the block on the cube (a little desaturated, a faint "drained" shimmer as they drop), so the player can see that the value went into the counter and the husk is what is left.

The three ways to take a block

| Action | You get | You give up |
|---|---|---|
| Left click (break) | its currency + its husk | nothing - this is the staple |
| Hold (Block Hold) on a utility block | its charges (e.g. Pesticide charges) + its husk | its currency |
| Right click on a hazard (clear) | the hazard as currency + its husk | the time the right clicks take |

- Clicking is the reliable way to earn currency, at every stage of the game; going back to farm currency is a real choice.
- Holding a plain block keeps today's Block Hold behaviour (pull it out of the rotation, the stake); holding a utility block converts it to charges.

The rule, word for word

> Husks are never currency by themselves. Only a machine can turn them into something - and the Recycler is the machine that turns them into currency, at a loss.

This keeps the world consistent (a machine is processing husks, like every other machine) while clicking stays the real way to earn currency.

What husks are for

Machines exist to turn husks into something useful; despawning hurts because unused material is lost, not money.

| Machine / system | Turns husks into |
|---|---|
| Recycler (built: PixelRecycler.cs) | currency, at 50% of each husk's value |
| Husk Crush (built 2026-10-17: PixelHuskCrush.cs, free from the start) | right-click a floor husk: half a click's XP to its own skill (bonuses for air / last-gasp / streak crushes, capped at a click's XP), never currency |
| Wood Chipper | skill XP (and Wood Chips) |
| Kiln | Charcoal (fuel / material) |
| Storage Crate | stored material for later |
| Pesticide Sprayer (planned) | used up to clear hives automatically |
| Crafting (planned) | recipe ingredients |
| Conveyor belts, sorters, hoses | moving husks to the right machine |

Husks wait for you (Early game feel 1, agreed 2026-10-18 in the Design Discussion chat, built 2026-10-18)

Clicks and husks must not compete: husks wait long enough that the player clicks a while, builds a pile, then clears it (docs/GameDesign.md 'Early game feel: click, pile, crush').
- Lifetime 35 s (PixelClicker.oldPixelLifetime, was 15 s). Standard's Lingering pixels perk still adds 5 s per level above 1 (level 2 = 40 s, level 7 = 65 s).
- Floor limit 120 (PixelClicker.baseOldPixelCap, was 50): at ~3.5 blocks a second 50 husks filled the floor in ~14 s and the oldest were culled, so a longer lifetime alone did nothing. When the floor is full the OLDEST husk still goes first. Standard's Roomy floor perk now adds 10 per level from level 4 (was 5 on the 50 floor; level 7 = 160).
- Big piles stay cheap: resting husks sleep (see 'Husks settle into small heaps' below) and vanished husks are reused (PixelHuskPool). Dev Hub > Widgets > 'Husk pile' > Fill floor drops husks until the limit is reached, for profiling a full floor.
- Fixed-second husk timings (Husk Crush's last-gasp 2 s, Clean Break's 2 s back, a gentle set-down's +12 s) were left as they are; they matter less against a 35 s life.

Husks settle into small heaps (agreed 2026-10-17 in the Design Discussion chat, built 2026-10-17)

A husk lands, maybe rolls or tips once, and stays put - no more bouncing about.
- Where they land: the burst (PixelBurstPop.TryBurstVelocity) still throws each husk out to a ring round the cube, so the floor fills with a ring of small heaps, never one tower right under the cube. A Recycler parked under the cube must not catch everything: aiming (Aimed Clicks) and the Recycler's suction are what route husks (docs/GameDesign.md 'Husk routing rework').
- How they feel: the default husk material is barely bouncy and a little grippier (PixelClicker bounciness 0.08, friction 0.65; were 0.3 / 0.5). Types whose look sets its own physics (look.customPhysics: Water's jelly, Sap, Leaf / Herb / Seaweed drifting...) keep their own values.
- Heaps are free: a resting husk falls asleep quickly (huskSleepThreshold 0.03, Rigidbody.sleepThreshold; Unity's default 0.005) and stays asleep - the script gravity (ScaledGravity) no longer pushes sleeping husks, which used to keep every husk awake for ever. Anything that touches one wakes it as usual (a grab, a crush taking a husk out from under it, Recycler suction, Magnet, Fan, belts, another husk landing on it).
- Throws still tumble: a thrown husk gets its spin and brief extra grip (PixelGrab, 'Tumbling when thrown'); its throw grip (thrownGrip 0.85) stays above the resting friction 0.65 so corners catch and tip it over.

Currency, husks and PRODUCTS (agreed 2026-10-19, not built)

Currency comes from the cube, husks come from clicks, products come from machines. A machine chain never makes a new husk (a husk
always has a block and its currency behind it). What machines make is a PRODUCT: it has a body (rides belts / tubes / chutes, sits in
crates and the Workbench, falls like a husk) but it is not a pixel type - no block, no click currency. Its value is in USE: crafting
ingredients, vendor orders, upgrades, cosmetics; vendors may BUY products (the exit to currency). Chains stay short (2 steps, sometimes
3): ore husk -> ingot -> tool; leaf / moss / seaweed husks -> cloth -> pet outfit. Wood Chips and Charcoal (pixel types today, no use yet)
are really products and should move over.

Discovery: products unlock new blocks (agreed 2026-10-21, not built). A product never turns into a husk, but making enough of a product
DISCOVERS a new pixel type: from then on the cube spawns it like any block (its own currency, husks, Value upgrades). Example: the Kiln burns
wood husks into Charcoal PRODUCTS; make enough of them and the Charcoal PIXEL is discovered (a Firemaking block), which then drops real
Charcoal husks. This is how the MAKING skills (Smithing, Firemaking, ...) get their blocks, while gathering skills keep theirs from shop
packs - the factory becomes the gateway to new skills. Rules: discovery is ONE-TIME (no feeding a machine to keep a block); NO LOOPS (a
discovered block's husks process into something new or higher, never back into the product that discovered it); watch the currency count
(group the Inventory by skill before there are dozens).

Crafting uses husks (agreed 2026-10-18, not built)

Currency buys access (shop); husks build things: crafting consumes HUSKS stored in the Workbench (one placeable bench, keeps its stock when moved), not pixel currency. Design: docs/GameDesign.md section 8 'Crafting uses husks: the Workbench'.

The Recycler (built 2026-10-15, PixelRecycler.cs)

Reworked 2026-10-17 (docs/GameDesign.md section 6 'Husk routing rework'): the Recycler is CURRENCY ONLY - each husk pays 50% of a click's value times the same-type streak (cap 80%, never above 95% of a click), and NO XP; XP from husks comes from Husk Crush and the skill machines. A modest suction ring round the rim draws nearby loose husks in (Operation: Suction), several Recyclers can stand at once at a rising price (x2 each), and whoever captures a husk first owns it (PixelHuskHolder). Edge-click aiming is now Standard's tree node 'Aimed Clicks' (Standard 3).

Built version: an open bin (DeviceKind.Recycler, first on the shop's Utilities card, 75 White + 50 Green and rising; Cubie gives one on his first visit). Every husk that lands in it (or is drawn in) is recycled half a second later for 50% of its value as currency of its type, raised by a same-colour streak. (The first version filled up and was emptied with the red button - still there for anything left inside.) The notes below are the original plan; the live numbers are on the component.

- What it does: takes husks in and pays out currency of that block's type at 15% of the block's click value per husk (start value, to be tuned). A Wood husk from a Wood block that pays 3 gives 0.45 Wood.
- Takes time and setup: it processes one husk every so often (proposed: one every 1.5 seconds), so throughput is a real limit; husks are fed by belt, Funnel, hose or by hand (through a coupling on its socket, like the Kiln).
- Upgrades through other skills: Electrician power raises its speed; a later tier raises its rate (e.g. to 25%). Another cross-skill link.
- It competes with the other uses: the same husk can go to the Recycler (a little currency), the Wood Chipper (XP), crafting (material) or the Pesticide Sprayer (charges). Choosing where husks go is the builder's puzzle.
- A home for the old systems: the Vacuum can collect husks and hand them to a Recycler instead of paying currency out directly. (The Pixel Bank is storage, not a cash-in: see the table below.)
- Never better than clicking: whatever its upgrades, the rate stays well below 100%, so a block never pays twice in full.

Hands first, machines later (first person)

First person is not a second clicker mode. You can still click the cube high above you, but the mode exists for handling husks by hand: breaking husks for XP, picking them up and carrying them, and feeding machines yourself (e.g. carrying wood to the Wood Chipper).

The progression:

1. The cube makes currency and husks.
2. Hands (first person): you save husks from despawning, break them for XP and carry them to machines. Free but slow.
3. Machines take the carrying and processing over (belts, Funnel, hoses, sorters, Chipper, Kiln, Recycler). Faster, but they cost something to buy and place.
4. Machines break (see below), and your hands are needed again. That sends you back to fix the layout properly.

Rules that keep it balanced:

- Hand-breaking a husk never beats clicking. It gives a fraction of the XP the click on that block gave, so clicking stays the main source and camping in first person is never the best play.
- Hands are free but slow, machines are fast but cost something. Otherwise machines lose their point, or hands do.
- Its first moment: before the player owns any machine, first person is the only way to save husks from despawning. Cubie can point at it the first time husks start to vanish.
- Jobs only hands can do keep it alive later: repairing machines (below), grabbing a rare husk before a sorter takes it, carrying a Hive to the Pesticide Sprayer, clearing a jam.

Machine breakdowns and repair (design)

Some machines break, and the player goes into first person to repair them.

Wrong input (the main cause). A machine that gets something it should not take (e.g. a Gas Pocket husk in the Kiln) bangs, smokes and stops.
- This makes sorting matter: a Belt Sorter or Junction Box protects the machine, not just tidies.
- The player caused it and can fix it for good with a better layout; it teaches what each machine takes.
- Each machine lists what it accepts (shown in its tooltip / the Database), so a breakdown is never a surprise rule.

Wear over time (optional, gentle). If used at all:
- a worn machine slows down, it does not stop;
- nothing wears while offline;
- upgrades / perks (Electrician) reduce or remove wear.

What a breakdown does. The machine stops and keeps its contents; nothing is lost. It holds things up instead of destroying them, so being busy elsewhere never costs material.

Seeing it. Smoke, sparks and a warning icon over the machine, visible from the normal clicker view, so the player chooses *when* to go down and fix it instead of checking all the time.

Repairing.
- Quick: a few hits or a short hold on the machine in first person (a small bonus for doing it well is optional). The real cost is the walk, not a long minigame.
- Repairs use husks from other skills: metal husks from Mining, Wire from Electrician. This gives Mining a link the connection map says it is missing, and gives those husks another use.

Open questions: which machines can break, which inputs break each one, whether wear exists at all, and which husks repair which machines.

Systems that needed a new job under this rule

Step 1 (built): with husksPayCurrency off none of these pay a husk's value any more. Consumed husks give husk XP instead (PixelClicker.GiveHuskXp -> PixelSkills.AddHuskXp: a share of a manual click's XP, huskXpShare 0 = 0.5; pure husks train Standard, a skill's husks train that skill).

| System | Today | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| Vacuum pixel / Vacuum Device | paid the stored value of every husk it sucked up | built: gives husk XP per husk sucked up (no '+N' popup any more). Later it may hand husks to a Recycler / crate instead |
| Pixel Bank (hose) | stores husks with their payout, which comes back when they are spat out | decided: husk storage for their EFFECT, never to cash in: keep husks you cannot use yet (e.g. Electric husks before Electrician) and spit them out when a machine wants them. Stored husks carry no payout; capacity is one shared total (already how PixelBank.IsFull works) |
| Solar husk | hovered and paid a small currency trickle | built: each trickle tick adds a little daylight instead (PixelDaylight.hoverDaylight, 0 = 0.02 per tick), so a hovering Solar husk keeps the day going |
| PixelClicker.ReleaseOldPixel(body, credit: true) | re-added the husk's reward (black hole option, Ultra Holes right hole) | built: the credit only pays with husksPayCurrency on; Ultra Holes gives husk XP for a correctly sorted husk; the black hole already gives its minigame XP |
| First-person axe | paid 1.5x a block's value for chopping a wood husk | built: Woodcutting XP only (swinging the axe at a wood block on the cube still adds its tool bonus: that is a click) |

Not changed (open question for the developer): the minigames that use husks as play pieces (Snake, Sorting Race, Breakout) still pay currency per husk as their minigame reward. The Pixel Bank's stored payout is kept in the data but nothing pays it any more.

Open questions

- Recycler: is 15% right, how fast does it process, what does it cost, and is there one per save or many?

- Should vendors buy only currency counts (as now), or also husks (e.g. the Lumberjack buys a pile of wood husks for a little)?
- The "drained" look for husks: worth doing, and how subtle?
- Which blocks become utility blocks with charges, besides Pesticide (and the Whetstone once it moves to Mining)?
--- docs/systems/PixelSaveGame.md ---
PixelSaveGame

GameState (2026-10-23, Phase 1; overrides the wording below for the systems it names). The player's progress is moving into one plain object, GameState (owner PixelGameState, see docs/systems/PixelGameState.md). It is saved whole as SaveData.state; a save calls PixelGameState.RaiseSaving() first (world objects copy themselves in); a load calls PixelGameState.Replace(data.state) FIRST and PixelGameState.RaiseLoaded() once the husks and the IPixelSaved parts are back, BEFORE the tester machines. Moved so far: the Bank's stored husks, the Stats, the Auto Clicker, the Focus potion, the Time Stop / Pixel Grabbing / Combo switches, the Bait Bucket, Cubie's bill, the event log, the UFO Helper, the pets, the achievements the potion / device inventory, the skill choices, the minigame switches / counters, the shop packs and the currencies (every IPixelSaved part: the registry has no users left; PixelSaveGame still writes the core - husks, settings, tester machines, plus the slot summary: name, date, play time, totalPixels - itself). Every other system still uses the registry below.

Save registry (2026-10-18 - RETIRED 2026-10-24: every system's progress is in the GameState; PixelSaveRegistry / IPixelSaved and SaveData.parts are gone). Systems save themselves: they implement IPixelSaved (SaveId, SaveOrder, Export, Import) and register with PixelSaveRegistry in Awake (see its header for the load-order bands). The save file keeps one list SaveData.parts (id + JSON). It held the auto clicker, the Pixel Grabbing / Time Stop / Combo switches, pets, the UFO job + switch, the event log, potions + devices held, placed devices + running potion and achievements (stats and the Bank moved to the GameState 2026-10-23). PixelSaveGame itself still writes the core: tiers, Ultra, shop packs, minigame counters, husks, settings, the Dev Hub tester machines, plus playSeconds as a summary for the slot list. (Now a new thing to save goes in the GameState: see the paragraph above and docs/systems/PixelGameState.md.)

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelSaveGame.cs

Save/load to JSON in Application.persistentDataPath (tiers, shop packs by name, auto clicker, potions owned). Also saves the running potion (type, combo second type, seconds left; restored after FinishLoad via PixelConsumables.RestoreActive) and the placed devices (PixelConsumables.PlacedState: device name, position, yaw, seconds left, a Charge Booster's charge (aim), device extras; GetPlacedDevices / ClearPlacedDevices / RestorePlacedDevice; every PixelPlacedDevice knows its DeviceIndex). Time away does not run their timers down. Loads on start (1 frame delayed), autosaves (shows a small pulsing "Autosaving..." note at the bottom right above the black bar: ShowAutoNote, autoSaveNote* fields; manual saves keep the centred message), saves on quit. Save slots: slotCount slots (slot 1 = the old saveFileName, others ..._slotN.json); CurrentSlot (PlayerPrefs, the slot autosave / quit-save / startup load use; set by SaveToSlot / LoadSlot); SlotHasSave, TryGetSlotInfo. The pause menu's single "Save / Load" entry opens a window (BuildSaveLoadPanel): scrolling slot list of compact rows (name + a Rename button that opens a text box via OpenRename / ConfirmRename; names live in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.SlotName.N, empty = 'Slot N'; date / time played / pixels show in a hover box, UpdateSlotTip), select one, Save / Load buttons, an overwrite warning (BuildOverwriteOverlay) when saving over an existing slot. After a save or load in that window a blue result window the size of the whole menu (BuildResultOverlay / ShowResult, resultColor, texts savedResultFormat etc.) says "Saved to slot N" / "Loaded slot N" (or the failure) with a "Got it" button (Escape closes it too); those calls pass message: false to SaveToSlot / LoadSlot so the centred toast doesn't also show. Restart deletes the current slot's save. Uses PixelClicker.LoadTierState/FinishLoad and PixelShop.SetPackState. Husk positions are saved (SaveData.oldPixels = PixelClicker.OldPixelState: tier, payout, position, rotation, velocities, OldPixelDespawn.Remaining; not flying / vanishing ones; skipped while a takeover minigame has moved them) and restored by PixelClicker.RestoreOldPixels after FinishLoad (older saves leave the pixels alone). Settings per save file (SaveData.settings, PixelSettingsSync.cs): Collect() copies a fixed list of PlayerPrefs keys (PixelClicker.Setting.* except display hardware, PixelAudio.*, PixelClicker.Key.*, PixelClicker.Dev.*) into every save, Apply() writes them back on load (keys not in the file are deleted = defaults; an older save without the list changes nothing) and Reload() calls each cache's reload (PixelClicker.ReloadSettingsFromPrefs, PixelShop, PixelKeys, PixelAudio, PixelDisplaySettings, PixelPauseMenu, PixelHints.RefreshLog, PixelDevTools.ReloadFromPrefs). Resolution / fullscreen / VSync / quality / UI scale and the 'seen tip' flags stay global. A new PlayerPrefs setting that should follow the save file must be added to the list in PixelSettingsSync.Known_ (and its cache reloaded in Reload).

Autosave list + screenshots (current). Every TIMER autosave (not manual / quit saves) is also copied into <persistentDataPath>/Autosaves/autosave_<ticks>.json (+ .meta info, + .jpg), at most autosaveHistorySize (100, Inspector; keepAutosaveHistory) - when full the OLDEST are deleted (PixelAutosaves.Add / Trim). The Save / Load window has a small 'Autosaves' button under Save | Load that opens PixelAutosaveWindow (PixelAutosaves.cs): newest first, a Load button per row (PixelSaveGame.LoadFile, the current slot does not change; the game resumes), hover = screenshot + details. EVERY save writes a screenshot next to it (<save file>.jpg, 320 px wide, PixelSaveShots: taken at the end of the frame while the game is visible; a rolling copy every 4 s is used when saving from the pause menu) and the slot rows' hover box shows it above the details (PixelPauseMenu.UpdateSlotTip, SlotThumbPath). Deleting a save deletes its screenshot. With the autosave interval set to Off there are no autosaves to list.

Save rework (current; overrides the slot / autosave wording above). Autosaves (timer, quit, focus loss, Quit > Main menu - Save(bool)) go ONLY into the autosave list (PixelAutosaves.Add, always kept, at least 1); the numbered slots are written ONLY by a manual save (SaveToSlot). Quit / focus-loss saves within 5 s of the last autosave are skipped; no autosave happens on the main menu, and the timer pauses (not resets) on the title screen and while the pause menu is open. TryGetLatest(out slot, out path) = newest file among manual slots and the newest autosave (slot 0); Continue and the startup load use it (ContinueMostRecent). The save's name is SaveData.slotName (GetSlotName / SetSlotName; a name for an empty slot waits in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.SlotName.N until the first save). Settings follow the profile: while the title shows, the newest save's settings are applied (ApplyProfileSettings) and Continue of that same file does not re-apply them; a new game / Restart calls PixelSettingsSync.ResetToDefaults (keys and volumes stay) and sets AutoStartNewGame. Restart deletes the autosaves only (PixelAutosaves.DeleteAll).

Look settings are NOT per save
PixelSettingsSync stores per-save settings in the save file and puts them back on load / start. The look of the interface (UI colour + hue / saturation, button colour, neon bar glow + hue / cycle / picked colour, bar colour, clicker aura + colour / mode) is deliberately left out of its Known_ list: those PlayerPrefs belong to the player's machine. Kept per save they were overwritten on every start by the newest save's copy, so anything changed after the last save was lost.

PixelShop

Progress (2026-10-24): bought / level of every pack live in the GameState (PixelGameState.Current.shop, by display name); ShopPack.purchased / level are properties over it. On load PixelSaveGame calls ApplyLoadedPackStates() at the old fixed step. Overrides older save wording below.

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelShop.cs / PixelShopUI.cs / PixelShopDefaults.cs

One partial class PixelShop in three files: PixelShop.cs = data types, ALL [SerializeField] settings, lifecycle, buying/requirement logic; PixelShopUI.cs = building and refreshing the UI (button, panel, tabs, scroll list, upgrades window, trackers); PixelShopDefaults.cs = the built-in pack definitions (Create…Pack) plus the BuiltInPacks table (how to recognise each one, which pack it requires, how to build it) - to add a built-in pack: write its Create…Pack and add one line to the table. Keep new serialized fields in PixelShop.cs so the Inspector order stays stable. Shop button (appears once requiredTier = Black is unlocked) + panel. (Vacuum spawn weight 0.28 and Electric 0.2 since packDataVersion 11, was 0.2 / 0.15) NOTE: some entries below are outdated - the Seed, Water, Electric, Obsidian, Wizard / Tinkerer / Entrepreneur and Pixel Grabbing packs are no longer separate shop packs (their blocks now come with the Farmer's, Electrician's and Prospector's packs, the vendors are started by Cubie, and Pixel Grabbing is unlocked from the start with a Toggles switch); the descriptions still explain how those blocks and systems behave. ShopPack list: RGB Pack, Auto Clicker, Glass Pixel, Vacuum Pixel, Obsidian Pixel, Luminescent Pixel (Pixels tab), Faster Clicking, Multi-Click, Combo Meter, Husk Capacity (REMOVED - code deleted 2026-10-24, to come back as a Standard reward; was UpgradeEffect.OldPixelCap, 5 levels 75/100/150/200/300; base cap baseOldPixelCap = 50, the Time Stop stockpile never drops below the cap), Crafting, Pixel Grabbing (Upgrades tab), Ghost Hunt, Black Hole, Bomb Defusal, Ultra Pad, Snake, Sorting Race, Breakout (Minigames tab), Singularity Pixel (Pixels tab, gated by the singularity tracker), Ghost Pixel (Pixels tab, gated by ghosts caught), Solar Pixel (Pixels tab, needs Meteor Pixel + stardust), Time Slow (Upgrades tab, needs Time Stop), Meteor Strike (Minigames tab, needs the Prospector's Pack, which replaced the Obsidian pack), Meteor Pixel (Pixels tab, gated by meteor chunks), Seed Pixel (Pixels tab, listed right BEFORE Electric, needs the RGB pack, 4K each of Red / Green / Blue, PixelType.Seed, spawn weight 0.3; a brown shell you dig open: clicksToCollect 8, pays 15 on the last click, look = white tint with a brown grain texture (PixelLook.shellTexture + damageCracks: dark cracks spread per hit; looksVersion 12-13), packDataVersion 7 moves it before Electric, 8 / 9 update old packs (shell, then 8 clicks / 15). Every hit plays seed_dig (code-synthesized PixelSynth.Dig while its clip list is empty) and SeedDigFx.Play (PixelSeed.cs: two code-built particle systems, dark-brown dirt chunks and pale drifting dust, that fall from the cube's underside; stronger as the shell nears cracking: PixelClicker.SeedDigEffect). The cracked shell does NOT spawn a husk: CollectInternal calls PixelClicker.SpawnSeedSprout a random seedlingsMin - seedlingsMax (0 = 1 - 5) times (all sprouts together capped by seedSproutCap 36; each seed has its OWN despawn timer seedSproutLifetime (0 = 45 s from dropping, SeedSprout.SetLifetime: it withers if its pixel hasn't popped yet) and the pixel it grows is an ordinary husk with its own normal timer) -> SeedSprout (PixelSeed.cs): a big green sprout (seedSproutScale 2.2x a husk, PixelLooks.CreateSproutObject) tumbles down (it never collides with the live cube and is a trigger for popNoCollisionSeconds after it is born, SeedSprout.SetGhost, so a burst of sprouts doesn't pile up inside the cube), plants itself upright on the first flat contact (dirt puff, a pushed-up dirt mound and a soft green point light make it easy to spot), then (seed_plant: a small code-made crunch, PixelSynth.Crunch, played by every SeedSprout.Plant and when the Seed pet plants itself) over seedGrowSeconds (6) a display model of a RANDOM unlocked pixel type grows from nothing on its tip (CreateDisplayPixel), holds seedHoldSeconds (1) wobbling, and pops off as an ordinary husk (SpawnStoredPixel, payout RollOldPixelAmount); the sprout then shrinks away. Rarer types are likelier (RandomSeedPixelTier: chance ~ 1 / spawnWeight^seedRarityBias (1); not rare drops / fly-away / Seed / Singularity), never a Vacuum or Singularity pixel (those only come from bought seeds). Old Solar pixels within seedSolarReach (6 husk widths) speed growth (SolarSeedBoostAt: +seedSolarBoostEach (0.75) each, capped at seedSolarBoostMax (3); the sprout's light turns warm yellow while boosted). Solar clusters: old Solar pixels that touch each other (centres within solarClusterDistance 1.5 husk widths, connected groups) in a group of solarClusterSize (5) or more never despawn (PixelClicker.UpdateSolarClusters, every 0.15 s OldPixelDespawn.KeepAlive(); the husk cap still removes the oldest). Seeds never sit on top of each other: PixelClicker.SeedSpotFree(pos) (spacing seedSpacing, 0 = 0.7 x a sprout's width; SeedSprout.AnyNear, PixelPets.PlanterNear) is checked when you plant (message), when the Seed pet sows (it tries another spot) and PlantSeedSproutAt refuses; a shell sprout that lands too close slides away (SeedSprout.MakeRoom). Sprouts are not saved; stored Seed pixels (Bank, saves) are plain shells), Water Pixel (Pixels tab, listed right after Seed / before Electric, PixelType.Water, TESTING price: 10 White, no requirements so it is available at once - raise it later; pays 3, spawn weight 0.4; look = see-through wobbling blue jelly (wobble, forceTranslucent, looksVersion 15); packDataVersion 10 moves it into place; has a spawn switch in Toggles): old Water pixels are used up by splashing: OldPixelSplash (PixelSeed.cs, added in SpawnFallingCopy when the look has splash) splashes the pixel the first time it lands on the ground (not on other pixels, not while held; or after lying still 1.5 s), whether or not anything needed water: ReleaseOldPixel(body, false) (no payout), WaterSplashFx (blue droplets + mist particles), then WaterSplashPiece.Spawn (PixelSeed.cs): the pixel BREAKS INTO 4 smaller Water cubes (waterPieceCount 0 = 4, waterPieceSize 0.4 of the pixel, waterPieceSpeed 2.2, waterPieceSeconds 1.2; PixelClicker 'Water splash pieces', disableWaterPieces) that are flung out and up and break completely into a small droplet puff the moment they land or time out (WaterSplashFx.PlayMini, ONE particle system from a pool of 24; the pieces are plain rigidbodies, not husks, OldPixelImpact ignores them), sound water_splash (PixelSynth.Splash) and PixelClicker.WaterSplashAt gives every growing SeedSprout and planted Seed pet within seedWaterReach (5 husk widths) an INSTANT jump of seedWaterGrowSeconds (1.5 s of growth / sowing time), but each target accepts water only every seedWaterCooldown (0.75 s) so auto clickers can't make it instant (a splash flashes the sprout's light blue); Solar pixels make them 'sun soaked': any old Solar pixel near a sprout / pet sets it soaked for seedSunSoakSeconds (8) at the boost it gave, so the speed-up outlasts the Solar pixel. A soft ray of sunlight (SunRayFx in PixelSeed.cs: two crossed vertical ribbons fading upward, unlit vertex colours, shimmering, fades in/out) stands over every sun soaked sprout / planted Seed pet. (a wet sprout's light is blue (looksVersion 16 switches splash on in saved Water looks). Mirror Pixel (Pixels tab, listed before Vacuum, needs the Electrician's Pack (it used to need the Electric pack), 15K each of Red / Green / Blue, pays 4, spawn weight 0.2, PixelType.Mirror, chrome look (PixelLook.chromeTexture: a code-drawn sky / horizon / ground texture so it reads as reflective in any render pipeline, plus emission, and shine: PixelLookShine sweeps a SOFT diagonal band of light across each face in turn (the overlay mesh is an 8x8 grid per face, PixelLooks.ShineGrid, and the vertex alpha follows a moving band so it fades at the edges - the old single quad could only blink as a white rectangle)) added by looksVersion 11 and replaced by 14, moved into place by packDataVersion 6; a click on a Mirror pixel also pays the last other pixel type's last click amount again (PixelClicker.lastReflectTier/Amount in CollectInternal, raises PixelCollected for it too, so Overcharge / pets react)), Electric Pixel (no longer its own pack: it comes with the Electrician's Pack, random 1-15 payout, spawn weight 0.2; older saved lists were migrated by packDataVersion 4-5, MovePack reorders a pack and renumbers requirement indexes). The shop's tracker rows (one per minigame with a goal counter) are off by default (showMinigameTrackersInShop): they moved to the Log's Goals tab. When the shop closes it brings back the Inventory (or Log) it closed on opening (reopenInventory/reopenLog), unless the player opened another window meanwhile. Handles buying, requirements, affordability colouring, levels/upgrades, Inspector-ticked purchased. [RequireComponent(typeof(PixelAutoClicker))].

Packs kept out of the shop (ShopPack.hideInShop)

hideInShop leaves a pack out of every shop list (PixelShopUI.HiddenAsPurchased returns true for it) while it keeps existing and working. Set (by the builders and packDataVersion 33, PixelShop.IsDevOnlyPack) on the testing packs: Fire Pixel, Nuclear Pixel, the Farmer's Pack and the Prospector's Pack (packDataVersion 34; so Farming and Mining are reachable only through Dev Tools for now), and on Block Hold (free from the start). Untick it in the Inspector to sell a pack again. Unlock / relock them with the Dev Tools Shop unlocks widget (PixelShop.DevUnlockPack / DevRelockPack / DevUnlockAllPacks / DevRelockAllPacks; relocking also locks the pack's reward pixels with PixelClicker.LockTier).
- Upgrades tab order: the Pixel Bank is shown right above Time Stop (PixelShopUI display sort only; the pack list keeps its indexes because requirements point at them).

RGB is an unlock (2026-10-16): the RGB Pack is hideInShop and given for free by Cubie's second visit (GrantPackRewarding(PixelType.Red)) at 100 Black earned; the Shop button appears with Red (requiredTier Red, packDataVersion 40); the Auto Clicker costs 150 of each pure pixel and is the first Log goal after RGB (the RGB and Combo Meter goals are gone); the Lumberjack's Pack (250 each) follows.

Shop rebalance (2026-10-16, shopBalanceVersion 1 = ResetBuiltInPackPrices once + minigame packs lose their requirements): prices follow the early-game path from the session reports (about 40 of each pure colour per minute early, more with Value / potions / auto clicking). Tiers (each = of all six pure pixels unless noted): ~5-10 min Auto Clicker 150; ~10-20 min Lumberjack 250, Combo Meter 400, Faster Clicking 500, Husk Capacity 600, Forager 500 (W/G/B), Angler 800 (W/R/B), Snake 1.2K, Prospector 1.2K (W/G/B), Seed 1.5K RGB, Multi-Click 1.5K; ~20-60 min Cube Dash 2K, Electric / Electrician 2.5K RGB, Bomb 2.5K RGB, Farmer 2K (W/G/R/Gr), Glass 3K, Cube Blink 3.5K, Bank 5K, Sorting Race / Evasion 5K, Mirror 6K RGB, Red Light 7K, Treasure 8K W + 4K B; ~1-3 h Breakout 12K, Vacuum 2K Glass + 12K RGB, Time Stop 15K, Ultra Holes 15K, Obsidian 20K B + 5K Glass, Time Slow 50K, Black Hole 60K B + 8K Vacuum; later UFO Helper 150K, Luminescent 20K Glass + 60K Obsidian, Meteor Strike 150K B + 200K Obsidian, and the special pixels (Ghost / Meteor / Solar / Singularity) about a tenth of their old prices. Leveled upgrades (Combo, Faster Clicking, Husk Capacity, Multi-Click, Bank Storage) got a gentler first level. MINIGAMES need only their price now (no pack requirements; ones priced in Glass / Obsidian / Vacuum / Wood still come after those pixels). A first pass - check it against session reports.

Shop text file (2026-10-18): every pack's and built-in device's name, description and price can be edited in Assets/Resources/ShopText.txt (PixelShopText.cs; PixelShop.ApplyShopText in PixelShopDefaults.cs, PixelConsumables.ApplyShopText). The file wins over the Inspector at play; blocks are keyed by the coded default name; the Editor writes missing blocks on play. Potions and seeds are priced at run time and are not in it.

Skill pack price ladder (2026-10-24, GameDesign section 6 'Tier 0'): the gathering packs (Lumberjack's, Forager's, Angler's, Prospector's, Farmer's; ShopPack.skillLadder) are priced by PixelShop.LadderCosts, not by their typed costs: skillLadderStarterPrice (250) of EACH pure colour x skillLadderGrowth (2.5) ^ rung, two significant figures (250 / 630 / 1.6K / 3.9K / 9.8K). By purchase order (skillLadderByPurchaseOrder, on): the Nth pack you buy costs rung N-1, whichever it is; skillLadderStarterFixed (on) keeps the Lumberjack's Pack at rung 0 and every other pack at rung 1 or more. Saved packs took the flag through shopBalanceVersion 2. The Electrician's Pack is not on the ladder (a making / operation skill: input-skill levels will open it). ShopText.txt prices are ignored for ladder packs.
--- docs/systems/PixelSkills.md ---
PixelSkills

Progress (2026-10-24): the pack in play, the guest blocks switched on and the offline task live in the GameState (PixelGameState.Current.skills, SkillsState; no longer per-save settings). Skill XP, nodes, the bonus bank and Mastery are StatsState counters. Overrides older wording below.

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelSkills.cs / PixelSkillsWindow.cs

Skill system SHELL (added by PixelClicker.Awake, PixelSkills.Instance, disableSkills): skill packs swap the basic cubes (monochrome, RGB, Glass, Luminescent) for another set and you train the skill of the pack in play. SkillPack list (id, name, description, accent, replaces / spawns / xpFrom texts, baseXp + xpGrowth, Perk list with implemented flag; built-ins Standard and Mining are added by id in EnsureDefaults). Mining IS Prospector mode (ActiveId reads PixelProspector.Setting, SetActive writes it; the change applies from the next spawn, the current cube stays), the Skills window is the only place that chooses it; the old Toggles 'Mining mode' switch is gone; a pack is available when its pixels are unlocked (IsAvailable). XP = stat counter skill.<id>.xp (saved generically, Xp, LevelOf(pack, out into, out need)); OnCollected adds 1 XP per manual / autoXpFactor (0.25) per auto click of the active pack's pixels (every pack's baseXp is multiplied by xpCurveScale (2) - BaseXpOf, used by LevelOf, Mastery and the balance report - so Standard's 380 means level 2 after 760 blocks; Standard is different: every collected block gives it a flat standardXpPerBlock 1 XP, manual or auto, whatever skill is trained, no multipliers - AddXpFlat); a level-up posts an event-log line. Window (PixelSkillsWindow.cs): a docked Skills tab on the left edge directly under the Bank + Hose pair (PixelHud.DockInGroup, width PixelBank.DockGroupWidth, appears when SkillsTabUnlocked: a skill pack is owned, or Standard is level 2 and its level-2 popup - which opens the window - is no longer waiting; Cubie no longer introduces skills: the Toggles tutorial lost its skills step and the skills walk-through only auto-starts with autoStartTutorial, off), a window that opens from the left edge like the Bank window (closes / is closed by the Inventory, Log and Bank, CloseWindowIfOpen; PixelWindows priority 14, PixelPop): header (training line + XP bar), pack cards on the left (Standard is a card, locked packs show ???), the selected pack's detail + perk list + Train button on the right; the first availability auto-opens it with a one-off explanation (introText, tip id skills_intro). Pixel packs are generic now: SkillPack.types lists a pack's pixel types (the FIRST one being unlocked = the pack is available), PixelSkills.PackSetting (PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.SkillPack, per save; old Prospector = Mining) is the one chosen pack, PixelProspector.Setting just reads / writes it, and PixelSkills.LeftOutOfSpawns(type) (used by PickSpawnTier) keeps a pack's pixels out of the spawns unless the pack is in play OR the type is a guest; the basic pixels step aside while any pack is in play. Guests (cross-breeding): a TreeNode.guestType node, once bought, makes that block a switch in Toggles > Pixels ('Seed (Farming)'; UnlockedGuests, GuestOn, SetGuest, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.SkillGuests) so it also spawns in other rotations. (The Toggles window no longer has '<Pack> mode' switches: the Skills window is the only place that chooses the pack.) Farming (id farming, bought with the Farmer's Pack): see 'Farming pixels' in docs/systems/SkillBlocks.md. Electrician (id electrician, PixelSkills.ElectricianId, bought as the shop's Electrician's Pack - a ShopPack with skillId = 'electrician', needs the Auto Clicker, 8K each Red / Green / Blue, listed under Skill packs; PixelShop.IsSkillPackBought; the separate Electric Pixel pack is gone - Electric is the pack's first pixel, no longer a spawn toggle (IsSpecialType lost Electric), and MigrateOldPackData removes a saved Electric pack, upgrades a saved Electrician pack and makes the Mirror pixel need it): its eight pixels are Electric plus the pack's own Resistor (45, 2 clicks, pays 5, weight 0.6, a conductor), Transistor (46, pays 5; its click makes the NEXT collected pixel pay transistorMultiplier (2) x), Fuse (47, pays 5; xfuseBonus (3) if collected within fuseSeconds (3) of appearing, 0.3x after) and Bulb (48, pays 6; x(1 + bulbPerConductor (0.1) x old conductor pixels, max 30, CountConductors)), looksVersion 33, and Wire (PixelType.Wire 42, 1 click, pays 2, weight 1.0, copper), Battery (43, 3 clicks, pays 12, weight 0.4, green) and Capacitor (44, pays 6, weight 0.35, blue) - all IsStandalone; they spawn only while the Electrician pack is in play or as guests (tree node 'Static charge' covers Electric); Electric / Wire / Battery / Resistor husks carry the device links (PixelClicker.IsConductorType, PixelElectricLinks.IsConductor), a Battery click sets off an Overcharge too, and a Capacitor pays 1 + stored charge x capacitorPayPerCharge (0.15; every other click stores one charge, cap capacitorChargeCap 40, PixelClicker.CapacitorCharge) then resets; looksVersion 32; CheckFarmBundle also unlocks all of them once the pack is bought, MigrateOldPackData gives an older saved pack its pixels; XP from clicks of any of them; its other perks / nodes (Overcharge+, Wired up, Power bank, Surge) are 'coming soon'. Offline Progression skill (id offline, PixelSkills.OfflineId, available once the Auto Clicker runs): it is passive now (see 'Offline Progression is passive' below: it is never chosen to train, SetActive ignores it, and its only XP comes from offline progress via AddOfflineXp); ActiveId stays the pixel pack in play. Its effects are NOT built yet (perks and tree nodes say 'coming soon'). Skill tree per skill (SkillPack.nodes = TreeNode id / name / description / requires / row / cost / implemented, defaults by DefaultNodes for Mining, Offline and Standard (Standard's tree: first point on Block Hold or Power Click, which are locked until then; branches Lucky stake -> High roller and Quick charge -> Aftershock; HasStandardNode), also filled into older serialized packs): one point per level above 1 (PointsAvailable = level - 1 - spent), unlocked nodes are the counters skill.<pack>.node.<id> (NodeUnlocked, CanUnlock, UnlockNode); the detail panel's 'Skill tree' button opens a separate skill tree window (BuildTreeWindow, TreeOpen, PixelWindows 15) to the right of the Skills window that stretches to the right screen edge and is as tall as the Skills window: nodes by depth x row with connector lines (RefreshTree), points label, node text and an Unlock button; it closes with the Skills window. The perk list is collapsible: one header button per level ('+ Level 2  COMING SOON'), the text under it when open (perkOpen, perkHeads/perkBodies, scroll view). A skill's description / replaces / spawns / XP-from text is a tooltip on an orange '!' box at the top left of its card (CreateInfoBox, PackTip, UpdateTip). Not built yet: real effects for perks / nodes, more packs, the XP bar on the main screen, a cycle-skill key, a Stats / Log summary, a HowToPlay paragraph (see Todo.txt).

Perk guests (Woodcutting level 5): Perk.guestType works like TreeNode.guestType: from that level the block is listed in the Toggles window (UnlockedGuests / GuestUnlocked also read perks) and can be switched on to spawn in every rotation (off until the player ticks it). Woodcutting level 5 = Wood ("Lumber stock"), added to saved packs by the Woodcutting perk migration.

Offline Progression is passive (2026-10-16): it is never the skill in training (the old offline switch is gone, the Train button reads 'Earns while away', no pixel-pack buttons); its only XP is PixelOfflineProgress.Grant's offline clicks x offlineXpPerClick (0.25) via PixelSkills.AddOfflineXp, listed in the Welcome back window. An old save's 'offline chosen' switch is ignored. Offline task: the pack buttons on the Offline card pick what your character does while away (PixelSkills.OfflineTask, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.SkillOfflineTask, per save; OfflineTaskPack falls back to Standard); that skill also gets offline clicks x offlineTaskXpPerClick (0.1) via AddOfflineTaskXp. What's Next (2026-10-16): Standard tree node nextup needs both hold and power (TreeNode.requires may list several comma-separated ids; ParentsOf, MissingParents, depth = longest parent chain) and unlocks the Next up window (PixelNextUp.requireTreeNode); nextup2 'Look Further' and nextup3 'Foresight' each add one block ahead (pre-roll queue PixelClicker.PeekTierAhead). Level-up popup (PixelSkillLevelPopup.cs): every skill level-up (from AddXpFlat, except Standard's level 2 which has its own big popup) queues a round badge in the skill's accent colour with its first block spinning inside, 'SKILL  LEVEL N', '+1 skill point' and three tiny firework bursts.

Skill guides (2026-10-16, PixelSkillsGuide.cs): first time the Woodcutting card is selected, a Yes / No box asks about a tutorial (stat guide.woodcutting.asked); Yes plays woodGuideSteps (Got it boxes beside the window, dimmed + paused), No points at the guide book button after the skill's name (gold PixelHighlight), which replays the guide any time.

Aimed Clicks (2026-10-17, design 'Husk routing rework'): Standard tree node aimed (PixelSkills.AimedClicksNode, row 1 column 0 between Block Hold and Power Click, minLevel 3, cost 1) = the alternative to the second move at Standard 3. Edge-click aiming needs it: PixelBurstPop.AimingOwned is checked in PixelClicker.TryGetAim, the one gate for both the throw (AimedDirection) and the aim cursor (PixelAimCursor); without it every husk scatters (the burst) and the normal cursor shows. Level 3's perk now has a popup (StandardLevel3Notice, beside the Skills window) about the choice; level 2's popup mentions it. UnlockNode triggers the tip node_<pack>_<node> for any node (PixelHints; unknown ids do nothing) - node_standard_aimed is the first.

Husk Crush nodes + node levels (2026-10-17): TreeNode.minLevel (0 = none) holds a node back until the skill reaches that level (NeedsLevel, checked by CanUnlock; the node shows 'Level N', the button 'Needs <Skill> level N', the Game Database entry says so) and TreeNode.column places a node at least that far along (Depth = max(column, longest parent chain); its children follow). Trees may use a 4th row (row 3): the window spreads up to four rows over the same height and has 20 node buttons / 75 connector lines. Standard's bottom rows are the husk branch (PixelHuskCrush.cs): Chain Crush (chaincrush, level 4, column 1 beside Lucky stake / Quick charge) -> Clean Break (cleanbreak, level 6, 2 pts) -> Hard Hands (hardhands, level 9, 2 pts) on row 3, and Chain Crush -> Charge Crush (chargecrush, level 7, 2 pts) on row 4 (2026-10-17: trees may use rows 0-4, MaxTreeRow; with more than 3 rows the nodes get shorter so all rows fit the tree area). AddHuskXp(tier, xp, crushed, popupText, noPopup) (a crush always shows its popup, with decimals; a crush bonus shows its text by the mouse instead - ShowMouseText), ClickXpOf(tier), HuskSkillName(type).

Cancelling a charge (2026-10-18): a LEFT click while a Charge Crush charges (PixelHuskCrush) and a RIGHT click while a Power Click charges (PixelPowerClick) cancel it: nothing fires, the charge is dropped and NO cooldown starts, so a new charge can begin at once. The cancelling click is swallowed - Charge Crush claims the pointer as a button while charging (the cube, Pixel Grabbing and Power Click ignore the left click), and PixelRightClick gives the right press to a charging Power Click (Owner.Charge, kept while the button stays down) so Block Hold and hazard clearing ignore it.

Mystery bar (2026-10-18, design 'Early game feel' point 3): until Standard level 2 its name is hidden: PixelSkills.ShownName(pack) answers mysteryName ('???') for Standard while StandardHidden, and every player-facing skill name goes through it (the early tracker shows just '???', XP / crush / goal-reward popups '+1 ???', the XP hover box mysteryHoverText 'Something is growing...', the Skills window's cards / header / detail / description tip / tree title, block tooltips via TryGetOwnerLevel, Husk Crush's guide note via HuskSkillName, the hazard penalty line). The Game Database marks every entry naming 'Standard' undiscovered and Did You Know skips tips naming it until then. Level 2: RevealStandard (from AddXpFlat) plays a fanfare (achievement + ready sounds, a gold punch on the tracker - UpdateReveal -, an event log line '??? is Standard!'), then the level-2 popup, which now opens on the mystery ('That ??? bar was a skill all along: STANDARD'). showStandardNameEarly turns it off. AddRewardXp(skillId, xp) = flat reward XP (PixelGoals).

Crush refund perks (2026-10-18): three Standard level perks make every crushed husk give a little back (PixelHuskCrush.CrushRefunds, levels and amounts on that component): level 7 'Crush recharge' - 0.5 s off Power Click's cooldown (PixelPowerClick.ShortenCooldown), level 8 'Crush grip' - 2% of the Block Hold meter (PixelBlockHold.Refill), level 9 'Crush rhythm' - 0.25 s off Charge Crush's cooldown (not from the husks a Charge Crush breaks itself). Added to a saved Standard pack's perk list by the EnsureDefaults migration.

Double floor (2026-10-18): Standard level 10 perk - the floor AREA is twice as wide (PixelSkills.StandardFloorScale, doubleFloorLevel 10 / doubleFloorFactor 2), read by PixelFloor.AreaScale exactly like the Dev Hub's 'Double floor area' tick box: the floor picture, the wireframe rim and the husk / first person limits all grow with it. The husk floor LIMIT is unchanged.

Skill pace (2026-10-24): every skill pack (Woodcutting, Foraging, Fishing, Farming, Mining, Electrician, Firemaking) now uses Standard's curve - baseXp 600, xpGrowth 1.5 (x xpCurveScale 2 = 1,200 XP to level 2, about 90K to level 10). They used to be far quicker (Woodcutting / Foraging / Fishing 60 x 1.45 = 120 XP to level 2). Saved packs are moved over once (skillPaceVersion 1). Offline Progression keeps its own curve (150 x 1.6; its XP only comes from offline clicks). A pack still earns 1 XP per block of its own while it is the one in play, and only autoXpFactor (0.25) per auto click, while Standard counts auto clicks in full.
--- docs/systems/PixelSkybox.md ---
PixelSkybox

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelSkybox.cs

Sky styles (added by PixelClicker.Awake, implements IPixelLookSource): panoramas drawn in code (lat-long 2:1, same mapping as Unity's Skybox/Panoramic shader: u = 0.25 is +z) and set as RenderSettings.skybox (camera clearFlags -> Skybox; Classic restores the scene's skybox + camera background). Inspector list of SkyStyle (colours, sun elevation/azimuth/size, clouds, stars, width, pixelated, exposure, rotateSpeed, flashesPerMinute, seed; defaults by name): Classic, Clear Day, Sunset, Starry Night, Nebula, Aurora, Synthwave, Pixel Clouds, Storm (lightning). Removed skies (Synthwave, Alien World, Blocky Void, Black Hole, Spiral Galaxy, Ringed Planet: sky objects look wrong mirrored; RemovedStyleNames drops them from a saved scene list at Awake, their pattern code is dead). Panoramas and previews are cached per style (first pick of a big sky can hitch briefly). panoramicShader is filled in by OnValidate so builds keep the shader (else falls back to a solid camera colour + warning). updateAmbientLight (off) re-lights the scene from the sky. Everything below the horizon is the sky mirrored (Shade flips dir.y), so every sky fills the whole view (the menu shows no floor). More skies (PixelSkyboxExtra.cs, partial class, ShadeExtra / ExtraStyles, patterns appended to SkyPattern): weather (Rainy Day, Thunderstorm = Rain + flashes, Snowfall, Foggy Morning, Sandstorm, Rainbow Sky), space (Ringed Planet, Spiral Galaxy, Black Hole, Binary Suns) and cube world (Blocky Day / Night / Ember / Void: stepped gradients, square suns / moons, BlockClouds). Watcher (PixelSkyboxEye.cs, SkyPattern.EyeVoid, pitch black): in LateUpdate an eye (three code-textured quads far in front of the camera, so scene objects hide it) opens at a random spot in the upper sky, its slit pupil follows the cursor and it drifts a little towards it for eyeWatchMin..Max (6-14) s, blinks now and then, closes and stays away eyeHiddenMin..Max (10-28) s (eyeSize 0 = 0.16 of the screen height, disableEye; 0 = default). More skies: space variants without objects (6 Nebula colourings: Crimson / Emerald / Cobalt / Violet Haze / Golden Dust / Teal Void; SkyPattern.Starfield = Deep Space / Star Dust / Cold Stars, dense + coloured stars and a dusty band), Violet / Ice / Crimson Aurora, and Underwater / Deep Ocean (SkyPattern.Underwater, UnderwaterColor: caustic web overhead, swaying sun shafts, drifting specks, NOT mirrored; animated: IsAnimated styles are redrawn into their small (384 px) panorama about 12x a second from Update with shadeTime = skyClock, unscaled time, frozen in Time Stop). Rain (PixelSkyboxRain.cs, UpdateRain, disableRain, rainPerSecond 0 = 650 (x2 with lightning)): while a Rain pattern sky (Rainy Day, Thunderstorm) is on, a world-space stretched-billboard particle system falls through the scene, sized from the camera's distance to the cube. The Watcher's eye is fancier (PixelSkyboxEye.cs: heartbeat glow halo, bloodshot veins, fibrous iris with limbal ring / collarette / glints, a separate slit pupil that breathes and narrows when it stares at the cursor, lid line with tapering lashes). Test Grid (SkyPattern.TestGrid, TestGridColor): a NOT-mirrored black sky with a line every 15 degrees (thick every 45 azimuth / 30 elevation), the eight 45-degree azimuths in different colours (0 = +Z green, 90 = +X red), a white horizon, tinted below-horizon half, an orange disc (az 0 / el 30) and a white disc (az 90 / el 15) - for screenshots that diagnose orientation. Sky icons are their own little wide-angle pictures (DrawPreview: a 110-degree view towards the sun, tilted up, sampled through the same Shade; PreviewRect is the whole picture) instead of a crop of the panorama that missed most details. Picked in Settings (Display, "Sky" row) or the picker left of Play; saved in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.Skybox.

Shark (2026-10-16): the Underwater / Deep Ocean skies draw a dark, hazy shark silhouette circling far below (SharkShade: directions below about 30 degrees down projected onto a flat sea-floor plane, a slow wobbly lap in about 26 s, swaying tail, fins) - only in the bottom of the sky, faded in from dir.y -0.5 to -0.72, never on the sides. It is part of the animated redraw (no extra objects).

Glowing jellyfish (2026-10-16): Deep Ocean only (by style name): a bioluminescent jellyfish (JellyGlow) drifts slowly round the view a little below the horizon; every 7 s it lights up - the bell's rim brightens, pulses of cyan / pink light run down its wavy tentacles - then fades back to a faint shape.

Not mirrored (2026-10-16): Clear Day and Sunset no longer mirror below the horizon (there was a second sun and upside-down clouds): LowerHaze fades from the horizon colour (plus the sunset's warm band towards the sun) into a deeper haze of ground and top colour.
--- docs/systems/PixelTitleScreen.md ---
PixelTitleScreen

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelTitleScreen.cs

Start screen (added by PixelClicker.Awake): freezes the game (Time.timeScale = 0, PixelTitleScreen.Showing; auto clicker and the intro wait) behind a live blurred picture of the scene (each LateUpdate the main camera is rendered into a small RT and blitted down a chain: liveDivisor, lightBlurDivisor, strongBlurDivisor, no post-processing; the cube keeps spinning because PixelClicker.AnimDelta is unscaled while the title shows) with a runtime-drawn pixel-art "PIXEL CLICKER" logo (5x7 bitmap font, bevelled cells + shadow, logoColors, bobs), a black bar across the middle with a Play button and, in ONE row under it (together as wide as Play), three icon buttons drawn in code by PixelMenuIcons (32 px pixel art): Load = a floppy disk, Settings = a cog, Quit = an open door (red); MakeIconButton, hovering one shows its name in the small tip (hoveredText). Load / Settings call PixelPauseMenu.OpenLoadFromTitle/OpenSettingsFromTitle (the pause menu opens its view with openedFromTitle; Back / Escape return to the title; the title canvas drops to sorting 400 and hides its Foreground while the pause menu is open; the pause menu doesn't touch timeScale while the title shows). Play: buttons shrink + logo fades, bar thins while the strong then light blur fade out, then after startDelay time resumes. The component now stays after Play (canvas hidden, textures released): PixelTitleScreen.ReturnToMenu() (pause menu Quit > Main menu) closes open windows, runs PixelCameraIntro.PlayReverse, then Open(true) fades the title back in by running the Play transition (ApplyTransition(t)) backwards. Escape does nothing while it shows (PixelWindows entry). It shows again after Restart (scene reload = fresh launch); showTitleScreen turns it off. The hint system's startup quiet period uses Time.timeSinceLevelLoad (frozen while the title shows), and the intro tips start introDelay seconds after Play. Sound game_start. Bar glow (shared helper PixelBarGlow.cs; the HUD's top / bottom bars get the same glow on their inner edges, fields in PixelHud; player switch Settings > 'Neon bar glow' = PixelHud.BarGlow, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.BarGlow, synced per save, covers the title bar too) (barGlow, glowSize/InnerSize/Strength/ColorSpeed/EdgeOffset/Saturation: BuildBarGlow / UpdateBarGlow, soft gradient strips outside + inside both bar edges and a bright LED line, hue cycles slowly on unscaled time, fades as the bar thins). Small floor (right of Play) and sky (left of Play) pickers: drop-downs (a PixelUIKit.CreateDropdown listing every look, small caption above, fade in while the mouse is near or the list is open; showLookPickers, lookBoxHeight, lookDropdownWidth, ...; refreshed each frame when the look changes). Settings > Floor style and Sky each have a pop-out button (PixelPauseMenu.OpenLookPopout).

Background crossfade: UpdateBackgroundFade (called first in UpdateBlur) notices when 'Live background' or 'Blur background' is switched, keeps the picture that was showing as a snapshot (fadeTexture, shown by fadeImage above the blur layers) and fades it away over about 1.1 s, so the floor / sky comes in gradually instead of swapping at once. The in-game floor itself already fades with PixelFloor.FloorVisibility. The blur slider toggle ignores the click that comes when you let go of the slider (Toggle.sliderUsed + a pointer-over-slider check).

Studio intro (PixelStudioIntro.cs + PixelStudioSynth.cs)

The Galactic Arcade studio intro plays once per launch before the launch intro: PixelTitleIntro.IntroRoutine starts with yield return PixelStudioIntro.PlayAtLaunch() (from its black panel, so the fold-down carries on from black). ~11 s: Earth close-up (night side, Moon, sun glare, shooting star, starfield + nebulae) -> fast pull-back to a spiral galaxy -> blinking 'GAME OVER' with an RGB split, score line, INSERT COIN, scanlines, CRT vignette and glare -> the camera pulls back from the screen of an arcade cabinet floating in space (chasing marquee bulbs, neon frame, side art, two-player panel, tumbling pixel cubes) -> a little green cube alien slumps, perks up, turns to wink at the camera, drops a coin in (CREDIT 01 / PRESS START), presses START (screen flash, GET READY) and hops -> the camera rises to the marquee as 'presents' fades in -> fade to black. Any key / click skips to a quick fade. Its own world far below the game (worldOffset), two own cameras into RenderTextures, its own top canvas; everything built is destroyed at the end. Sounds studio_whoosh, studio_gameover, studio_hum (loop), studio_coin, studio_start are made by PixelStudioSynth. Settings > 'Studio intro (at launch)' (pref PixelClicker.Setting.StudioIntro); Dev Hub > Events > Launch > 'Studio intro' replays it. Every timing, colour and text is an Inspector value on the component.

Logo styles (PixelLogoView.cs)

The logo is no longer a texture: PixelLogoView builds it from ~200 little bevelled cells (body + shadow + glow image each) animated every frame. 20 styles (Classic, Rainbow Wave, Neon, Shatter, Matrix, Inferno, Frost, Glitch, Jelly, Flip, Blocks, Plasma, Synthwave, Gold, Ghost, Storm, Shockwave, Disco, Ocean, Hologram), each a static function plus an intro kind; invisible arrows at the far left / right of the logo fade in on hover and switch style (saved: PixelClicker.Setting.LogoStyle). PixelTitleScreen.BuildLogo only creates the container; it still bobs / scales (localScale) / fades (SetAlpha) it. Add a style = one function + one line in Styles.

Dev Record session button (2026-10-16): in dev builds (PixelDevTools.Available) a 'Record session' button sits at the top left of the start screen (showRecordButton, recordButtonSize, BuildRecordButton), hidden until the mouse comes near like the floor / sky pickers (it rides the same lookPickers fade with no look source). It starts the session recorder, then reads 'Stop & save m:ss'.

Launch intro (2026-10-16, PixelTitleIntro.cs, partial class): at launch the screen starts black, folds down into the middle bar once the blurred background is ready, the bar thumps, then the glow, the dropping logo and the buttons / widgets (popping in one after another) appear; any key or click skips it. playIntro and the timings are in the Inspector under 'Launch intro'. Not played when coming back from the game or when a New game auto-starts.

Menu cube show (2026-10-16, PixelTitleCubeShow.cs + PixelClickerTitlePreview.cs): while the menu shows, the cube becomes a random pixel type on every beat of the music playing (cubeShowOnMusicBeat, PixelAudio.TryGetMusicBeat: the song's tempo from PixelAudio's Music Tempos, 90 bpm when not listed; every cubeShowBeatsPerChange beats), or its own cubeShowBpm clock (90 bpm) while no music plays ( look only, PixelClicker.ShowLookPreview, no events). On Play the menu cube pops off as a husk (a mesh / material snapshot thrown out and shrunk away in huskPopSeconds, unscaled time, not a real husk) and the player's block materializes (EndLookPreview), then the camera intro plays. cubeShow off = the real block on the menu.
--- docs/systems/PixelTitleWanderers.md ---
PixelTitleWanderers

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelTitleWanderers.cs

Vendors wandering the main menu (component added to the title screen in PixelTitleScreen.Build, layer under foreground, behind the bar / logo / buttons): a small tick-box toggle at the bottom left that is invisible until the mouse is over it (a third, 'Blur background' (while it is ticked its text label is replaced by a blur-strength slider: PixelTitleWanderers.BlurAmount, global PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitleBlurAmount, 1 = full blur, read by PixelTitleScreen.BlurLevelsForAmount) (the 'Display floor' tick box is no longer on the menu: it is in Settings > Display right under 'Floor style', same global pref; the menu's 'Pets Wander' toggle sits directly above the vendors toggle; the same NPC never walks twice at once, Walker.key) (PixelTitleWanderers.BlurBackground, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitleBlurBackground, default ON), stacked above the live one: OFF = no blur - with the live scene the blur layers are simply hidden and the real scene shows sharp behind the title, without it the floor picture is blitted into a full-resolution RT (PixelTitleScreen.UpdateSharp); the wandering vendors and the toggles fade out the moment Play is pressed (PixelTitleWanderers.FadeOut, whatever the settings); a fourth, 'Display floor' (PixelTitleWanderers.DisplayFloor, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.DisplayFloor, default OFF (the floor is invisible unless ticked), global): OFF = PixelFloor never draws the floor in the game (FloorOff: visibility target 0, no flat base, props stay; the renderer is only forceRenderingOff, so the collider still catches pixels) and only the sky shows; a second one stacked above it, 'Live background' (PixelTitleWanderers.LiveBackground, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitleLiveBackground, default ON = the live blurred game scene behind the title as before; OFF = no camera render: PixelTitleScreen.UpdateBlur blits the floor style's picture (PixelFloor.TryGetTitlePicture: the active - animated - texture, tiled 6 across for repeating styles, 1x for fitted / stretched ones, a flat tint for Classic) into the same low-res live RT, so the usual blur chain turns it into a blurred full-screen floor behind the black bar); both appear while the mouse is over either) (PixelTitleWanderers.Enabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitleWanderers, global, default off). While on, every arriveEvery seconds (max maxAtOnce at once) a vendor walks in from the left or right edge along the bottom of the screen (feet feetHeight up, walkerHeight 230 tall, walkSpeed), makes stops stops at random spots (standing pauseRange s, sometimes turning to look round, walking bob / sway, mirrored to face his direction) and walks off the nearer-random side; with stayChance (25%) a vendor 'stays for a while' (3x the stops, 2x the pauses). Cast = Cubie (PixelGuideVendor.BuildWalker, which preserves the visit's own pupil / arm / gift references) and every PixelVisitorMinigame (BuildWalker: a holder + 'Art' child drawn by the vendor's own DrawVisitor); the Egg and Bean cats too (whether or not they were met). All of them vanish when the title screen ends. Unscaled time.

Pets & UFO (added later): a fifth stacked toggle 'Pets' (PixelTitleWanderers.PetsEnabled, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.TitlePets, default off, global) shows the CURRENT save's pets hopping along the bottom of the menu (UpdatePets: a random maxPets (6) of PixelPets.TitlePets(), rebuilt whenever the owned list / outfits change; each pet is a hidden studio - PixelPets.BuildTitleModel = the real-look pixel + its outfit under a pivot, an orthographic camera into a transparent RenderTexture shown by a RawImage, re-rendered every frame - hopping with squash and stretch every petRest s) The UFO is NOT behind that toggle: if the UFO Helper is bought in the save and not switched off in its own Toggles tab switch (PixelRobotWorker.Active), a block-drawn flying saucer drifts along the top (UpdateUfo, BuildUfoArt). The UFO abducts: every abductEvery (0 = 14-32) s, if a vendor is walking or a pet is hopping on the ground (on screen), it flies over one (UpdateAbduction: Ab.Fly at 1.8x speed, Ab.Beam), switches on a translucent green beam (BuildBeam / UpdateBeam, a child of the saucer) and pulls the victim up over abductSeconds (0 = 2.4) while it shrinks and spins (Walker.abducted / Hopper.abducted make Step / StepHopper skip it); it is then removed (FinishAbduction; abducted pets come back after 20 s via petRespawnAt, vendors just keep arriving). disableAbduction turns it off; clearing the UFO or pressing Play aborts it (AbortAbduction). Everything is removed when the menu ends; the Egg and Bean cats now walk in whether or not they were met.
