PixelToggles

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelToggles.cs

Toggles window: a button docked to the middle of the right edge (PixelHud.Dock with anchor (1, 0.5) = side docking, slides sideways) stays hidden until the Auto Clicker is bought (hideUntilAutoClicker) and opens a window with a group selector (Pixels / Upgrades / Minigames / Vendors / Pets (skill packs are never listed here - the Skills window chooses the pack in play; do not add skill-pack switches (Mining / Farming / Electrician mode) to Toggles) (Vendors = the visitor minigames PixelVisitorMinigame, filtered out of Minigames; tab names are tabNames): a drop-down while docked beside the open shop, where the window copies the currency panel's width and the shop's height (FitBesideShop), tab buttons when it stands alone; besideShop, ApplyGroupControl) over a scrolling list of tick-box rows (no visible scroll bar: PixelScrollBarFade.hideBar; the wheel and bottom fade still work). The tabs are pixel-art icons drawn in code (PixelTabIcons: three cubes in a triangle, a machine with a green up arrow, a joystick + Pac-Man, a market stall with a person, a cat + a dog), used both beside the shop and standing alone (the drop-down is no longer shown); hovering one shows its name in a small tip (UpdateTabTip). Pixels = SetSpawnEnabled for switchable unlocked tiers; Upgrades = PixelAutoClicker.UserDisabled, PixelGrab.UserDisabled, PixelTimeStop.UserDisabled and PixelCombo.UserDisabled (all saved by PixelSaveGame; Working = bought and not switched off); Minigames = PixelMinigame.UserDisabled. Rows are sorted alphabetically (Collect result sorted by label). Every tab with two or more switches starts with a 'Toggle all' row (toggleAllText; ticked while everything is on; skipAll entries are modes rather than on / off items; no skill pack is listed in Toggles any more). This replaced the tick boxes that used to sit on shop rows. Buying the Auto Clicker pack no longer switches it on: PixelShop.TryBuy sets PixelAutoClicker.UserDisabled = true and calls static PixelToggles.ShowHint() (a hint box next to the slid-out tab telling the player to tick Auto Clicker under Upgrades; closes on "Got it" or when the window opens; the tab stays out while it shows). Dev "Skip intro" / "Unlock all" still switch it on. The pause menu adds this component if missing.
--- docs/systems/PixelTreasureMap.md ---
PixelTreasureMap

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelTreasureMap.cs / PixelWhaleFight.cs / PixelOceanSynth.cs

Fishing's SPECIAL blocks (a rarer tier beside the base blocks; the same idea is on the to-do for every skill; both are in the Angler's Pack, packDataVersion 23 (24 Driftwood, 25 whale 15-50 clicks), looksVersion 48, PixelType.Bottle 73 / Whale 74, IsFishBlock, BlockSurface.Bottle/Whale drawn by PixelBlockTextures.BottleGlass(level,max) / WhalePixel). Message in a Bottle (4 clicks, pays 8, weight 0.05, cracks spread per hit): the break calls PixelTreasureMap.Begin (sound bottle_break; queued one at a time): glass bursts, a UI scroll (canvas 660) flies from the cube to the middle, unrolls between two rollers over a code-drawn parchment map (MakeMapTexture: stains, burnt edges, island, palms, compass, you-are-here cube, dotted path and a big red X placed from the real screen positions of the cube and the spot), is read for readSeconds (1.8), then shrinks into the spot, where an X (two red planks + dirt patch, Mark) appears on the ground (PickSpot: spotNearest..spotFarthest 3-7 pixel widths from the cube, in view, via PixelBait.FindGround; up to maxMarks 3, fade after markSeconds). Clicking the X (Hot blocks cube clicks via ExternalClickBlock) digs (dig_x, dirt FX, a mound grows, the X shrinks); after digClicks (6) clicks PixelClicker.GiveFishingHaul pays haulPixels (10 +-) good fishing pixels at once (bait most often, then seaweed / shells / fish / pearls) and fountains the same husks out for show (dig_done). Right-click the bottle = PixelClicker.DismissBottle (no map, no pay). disableMap. Whale (random 15-50 clicks via randomClicks, pays 20 x RewardMultiplier(need) = clicks x (1 + clicks x rewardGrowth 0.05), weight 0.015): the first MANUAL hit starts PixelWhaleFight (not while a takeover / Dash-type minigame is busy; the auto clicker pauses meanwhile, PixelAutoClicker): the whale stays almost still (drifts at driftSpeed 0.03 screen heights per second) for 2-3 clicks (restClicksMin/Max, the hooking click counts as the first), then dashes (runSpeed 1.3, up to runSeconds 0.9, to a spot at least minRunDistance 0.3 screen heights away, sound whale_yank) and rests again; it no longer flees the mouse or yanks on a timer, a thin fishing line (a PixelTube mesh, unlit vertex colours) is drawn from the cursor (on the whale's depth plane) to the whale with a gentle sag and ripple, fully invisible within lineHiddenWithin (1 pixel width) of the cursor and fading in over lineFadeLength (1.8); fightSeconds (80) without landing it = PixelClicker.WhaleGotAway; landing it plays whale_caught + rings (OnCaught), stats whales.hooked/caught, bottles.broken, treasure.dug. PixelOceanSynth makes all their sounds in code (pixel_bottle, pixel_whale, bottle_break, map_unroll, map_reveal, dig_x, dig_done, whale_hook, whale_yank, whale_caught, whale_escape; PixelAudio.SynthFor asks it first). The block tooltip also shows 'Clicks left'.
--- docs/systems/PixelUI.md ---
PixelUI

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelUI.cs

"Inventory" toggle box (top-left) (rows = swatch + plain light name + right-aligned amount (Currency tab: swatch + number only by default, the name in the hover tip, click a row to switch all rows to name + number: PixelUI.CurrencyShowNames, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.InventoryNames, PixelClickable) via SetRow / RowParts, 'Pixels' suffix stripped, ~95% opaque frame, ONE fixed height maxPanelHeight on every tab; infinite resources show an infinity sign via PixelConsumables.OwnedText) with Currency / Consumables tabs (Consumables has a half-width category dropdown with a search box beside it (consumableSearchField, SetCategoryRowActive; searching lists every held consumable whose name matches, ignoring the category); opening it closes the Log via PixelUI.SetInventoryOpen / PixelLog.SetLogOpen - they never show together) (right-click a potion to drink), active-potion timer and a tier guide ("Click X more Y pixels..." / "Y pixel unlocked") at top of screen; Currency tab: per-tier counts, cursor +N popups (manual) / cube popups (auto), vacuum +X total popup, per-entry vacuum +X indicators. The +N popups come from a pool (ShowPopup / TickPopups, at most maxPopups = 24 labels made once, oldest reused; tough-block payouts wait toughPayoutDelay inside the pool instead of a coroutine). Builds in Start() so shop-added tiers are included. The tier guide text ("Click X more...") stays hidden until the new-game intro tips have been closed (PixelHints.IntroHoldsGuide). The Inventory has a third Materials tab (RefreshMaterials): one line per PixelMinigame that overrides MaterialName / MaterialAmount / MaterialColor and has some (Stardust, Meteor Chunks, Bomb Parts, Ghost's Ectoplasm - 1-4 per caught ghost, ectoplasmMin/Max, saved via ExtraValue; no use yet, it is on the to-do). Tier lines auto-size down (no wrap) for huge numbers; the Log's rows do the same and FitAmount starts the amount box right after the name's text. Simplified (pinned) Inventory: a small toggle button at the window's top left (four-squares icon, green while on; it pins the tab you are on: PixelUI.CompactMode 1 = Currency, 2 = Consumables, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.InventoryCompact, global) drops the title / X / tabs and shows only swatch + number rows (currency, or every consumable you hold as swatch + xN; a plain left click uses it, hover shows the name) in a box as wide as its widest line (FitCompactWidth: swatch + the text at full size, measured with a hidden label; a compactWidth typed in the Inspector overrides it) and as tall as its lines; it ignores Escape (it is not a registered window). It is a movable window now (like the Skills tracker): drag it anywhere (PixelPopoutDrag on the box; the list's ScrollRect is disabled while it doesn't scroll so the drag gets through), two small buttons stacked at its top right - minimise (-/+, only the button row stays) and stick (padlock, can't be dragged); position / minimised / stuck are global PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Inventory.CompactX/Y/Small/Stuck (UpdatePinnedPlacement, KeepCompactOnScreen; the spot the player chose is kept as compactDesired and re-applied every frame before the on-screen clamp, so a window that was pushed up to fit when maximised returns to its spot when minimised again - the skill tracker does the same with trackerDesired); un-pinning puts the window back in its normal place. It no longer fades away when other windows open: it stays visible BENEATH them (its canvas sorts below the Shop / Log / Bank / Skills canvases; SetInventoryOpen(false) is ignored unless force; the Inventory dock button and the toggle still close it for good). The Inventory key still cycles currency <-> consumables before closing (InventoryKeyCycle).

Inventory currency groups (PixelUI.Refresh, CurrencyGroup)

The Currency tab lists the pixel rows in GROUPS under foldable headers (click a header, '- PURE' / '+ PURE'; folded keys saved in PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.InventoryFolded, global): Pure = White..Blue (the base colours, the shop's money), Composite: <Skill> = a skill pack's goods (PixelSkills.PackOfType; Wood belongs to Woodcutting), one group per skill, Exotic = everything else (Glass, Mirror, Vacuum, Obsidian, Luminescent, Ghost, Meteor, Solar, Singularity, Dragon Cubes...). The Ultra row stays above all of them. The pinned (compact) Inventory shows the same order with no headers. Naming rule: 'Pure' is reserved for the base colours; there is no 'Raw' / 'Refined' ladder (refined results would be crafted items in Materials, not currency tiers).

The Consumables tab opens on its Devices sub-tab by default (PixelUI.consumableSubTab starts at 1).
--- docs/systems/PixelUIKit.md ---
PixelUIKit

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelUIKit.cs

Shared static helpers for code-built UI: EnsureEventSystem, CreateCanvas, CreateText, CreateButton (EVERY button is made by it, directly or through a thin per-script wrapper (MakeButton / CreateButton / AddButton in the pause menu, title screen, Shop (+ press pulse and fancy-menu shine), Log, Inventory, Dev Inspector, Dev Mockup, Feedback, window chrome, PixelDevUI.Button, PixelTalk mute); it already plays the ui_click sound, so callers must NOT play it again; an icon button = CreateButton with an empty label plus drawn children (Skills Guide Book, skill-tree square). Things that are clickable but are NOT buttons - list rows (Bank, Crafting recipes / categories, the event log, Pocket log lines), pictures (Collection Log pages and camera, favourite star, pet pencils, Gauntlet faces / stones, Pocket + card), click catchers (visitor peek), the title wanderers' toggles - keep their own look and go through MakeClickable(go, onClick, sound, pressTint, target) (plays ui_click unless sound: false because the click makes its own sound; pressTint: false for scroll-list rows and plain pictures). Enforced: tools/compile-check/rules.sh fails the compile check on any AddComponent<Button> / typeof(Button) outside PixelUIKit), Stretch, CreateDropdown (arrow = a drawn triangle sprite; EVERY drop-down in the game is made by it - no hand-built popup lists; list entries are as tall as the box; escapeMasks: true = the opened list escapes a clipping scroll view, same as LetListEscapeMasks), CreateLookDropdown(..., source) (a drop-down of every look of an IPixelLookSource with preview pictures; its PixelLookDropdown component applies picks and follows look changes made elsewhere; used by the Settings floor / sky rows and the title screen pickers), CreateInputField (white 3 px blinking caret, placeholder hidden while focused), AddSearchIcon(field) (magnifier inside a search box; used by every search box), Caps(label|button) (UpperCase font style: every window title and tab label is shown in capitals), arrow buttons (UseArrowGlyph(button, pointLeft) swaps a "<" / ">" letter for a drawn triangle: the shop's Back button in the sub window (Pixel Bank / Auto Clicker upgrades), the floor / sky pickers, the pet outfit arrows), UI colour (PixelUIKit.UiColorOn / UiHue / UiSat, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.UiColor/UiHue/UiSat, Settings > Interface, synced per save; there is no slider: an eyedropper button beside the 'Custom UI colour' tick box picks the colour from the screen (SetUiColorFromColor takes its hue and saturation, switches the colour on): Tinted(colour) keeps a window colour's brightness but gives it the chosen hue; StyleWindow remembers every window image so RefreshWindows() recolours them live), bar glow colour (PixelHud.GlowHue / GlowCycle, PixelBarGlow.EdgeColor(baseHue, time, speed, offset, sat)), boxes (PixelUIKit.StyleBox(image, inset): rounded, softly shaded list rows / cards with a thin lit rim, inset = a sunken frame for 3D pictures; used by the Shop rows, currency rows, cards and picture frames), fancy windows (PixelUIKit.StyleWindow(image, colour) gives every window background a code-drawn rounded 9-slice frame with the colour baked in - gradient, bright cyan rim, inward glow; always on (the old flat-window switch and its Settings toggle were removed); new windows should call StyleWindow instead of setting Image.color), fancy buttons (always on, the flat-button switch was removed: CreateButton uses a code-drawn grey-scale 9-sliced ButtonSprite - rounded corners, dark outline, gradient, light top strip - tinted by the button colour; the shop's own button builder calls PixelUIKit.StyleButton too), close buttons (a button labelled "X" draws a cross sprite via UseCloseGlyph; the shop's own CreateButton does the same), TickSprite() (code-drawn check mark used as the fill of every tick box), CreateScrollView/UpdateScrollView (masked scroll list + auto-hiding scroll bar). Every scroll bar uses PixelUIKit.StyleScrollBar(track, handle) (slim ScrollBarWidth 10, rounded dark track + lighter rounded handle): new scroll bars must call it. Bottom fade: while a list scrolls and isn't at its end, PixelScrollBarFade fades the ROWS (direct children of the scroll content, each given its own CanvasGroup: alpha follows the row's centre height above the view's bottom edge, fadeHeight 44 x 1.6) plus a bobbing chevron below the list; nothing is drawn over the window and the mask is untouched (an earlier soft-mask version let rows ghost over the tabs at the top). Not for drop-down templates or views without a RectMask2D. StyleScrollBar also adds PixelScrollBarFade (CanvasGroup alpha: visible while scrolling / hovered / held, holdSeconds 0.45 after the last movement, then fades in fadeSeconds 0.18). UI scripts delegate to it instead of carrying their own copies.

Custom UI button colour
Settings > Interface > 'Custom UI button colour' (tick box + eyedropper, PixelUIKit.ButtonColorOn / SetButtonColorFromColor, PlayerPrefs PixelClicker.Setting.UiButtonColor / UiButtonHue / UiButtonSat, per save via PixelSettingsSync). PixelUIKit.StyleButton registers every button image and its base colour; RefreshButtons recolours them all at once (neutral buttons take the picked hue, strongly coloured ones such as red Quit keep theirs; a button whose colour something else changed, like a selected row, is left alone). Replaces the retired 'Pixel-assemble menu buttons' effect (PixelDockFx).

Button roles (UI unification step 3)
- Make a button by MEANING: PixelUIKit.CreateButton(font, parent, name, label, size, ButtonRole.X, labelSize); switch it later with PixelUIKit.SetRole(button or image, role) (a tab: SetRole(img, open ? ButtonRole.Selected : ButtonRole.Neutral); an on/off toggle: Success when on, Neutral when off). Never set a role button's Image.color by hand.
- Roles and their colours live in PixelUITheme (a system component, Inspector-editable): Neutral (ordinary, window X / header buttons, inactive tabs), Primary (main action, every '?' help button), Selected (open tab / chosen item), Success (green confirm), Danger (red quit / delete / reject). Neutral, Primary and Selected follow the custom button colour; Success and Danger keep their meaning colour (ticks in the Inspector).
- A plain colour (CreateButton(..., Color color, ...)) = role Custom: only for truly unique colours (a skill's accent, a vendor's theme colour, gold); weakly coloured Custom buttons still follow the custom colour (the old rule).
- Not a button but should look like one (a colour swatch, a progress trough, a list row): StyleButtonLook(image) - the shape only, never recoloured.

The theme (UI unification step 4)
PixelUITheme (a system component, Inspector) is the ONE place for the UI's colours:
- Button roles (above).
- Window backgrounds: windowColor (big windows, dialogs, the pause menu screens) and toolWindowColor (View, Pocket, Next up). Use PixelUIKit.CreateWindow(...) WITHOUT a colour, or StyleWindow(image, WindowShade.Window / ToolWindow) - they follow the theme live. A plain colour only for a window with a look of its own (the leather Collection Log, Cubie's teal, the inventory's see-through box, the Dev Hub's dev palette).
- Text: titleColor (every CreateWindow title, live), textColor and dimTextColor (PixelUITheme.TextColor / DimTextColor, read when a window is built - a change shows after a restart). Windows read them instead of their own textColor fields.
- highlightColor: PixelHighlight's pulsing frame.
- Window SIZES (header heights, title / X sizes) stay constants at the top of PixelUIKitWindows.cs (layout is built once).
- The player's own colours (Settings > Interface > Custom UI colour / Custom UI button colour) still tint on top of the theme.
--- docs/systems/PixelVisitorMinigame.md ---
PixelVisitorMinigame

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

PixelVisitorMinigame.cs / PixelTinkererMinigame.cs / PixelWizardMinigame.cs / PixelEntrepreneurMinigame.cs / PixelFarmerMinigame.cs

Visitor minigames (no shop packs any more - started by PixelGuideVendor; every minInterval..maxInterval s after firstVisitDelay (+ StartDelayOffset), AllowAnother; dev Spawn/Despawn work). PixelVisitorMinigame (abstract) holds everything: a code-drawn block-built visitor (DrawVisitor(art) in a 300x400 space via Block) waits for a calm moment (CalmForVisit: no Got it box showing or queued (PixelHints.TipsPending), no dragon show / wish / pet popup, not paused / Time Stop; the check runs every frame once the timer is up) and for the shared cooldown (sharedCooldownSeconds 180 after ANY vendor leaves, static nextAnyVisitTime / anyVisiting), visits every visitMinSeconds..visitMaxSeconds (480-900 s; renamed fields so old serialized 300-600 are dropped), then darkens the screen (visitDarkness) and stops time (Freeze, Time.timeScale 0, restored by Unfreeze; left alone if already stopped) from the moment they appear, slides in from the right edge (slideSeconds) and a speech box greets the player with an offer at discountFraction (0.8) off (Wares = items where IsWare(item), PriceOf = ItemCosts x (1 - discount), min 1 per line, paid like the shop incl. minigame currencies). Accept opens the wares window (name, discounted cost coloured by affordability, owned / capacity, Buy x1, Full at the hold limit; RefreshWares), Reject or Escape (PixelWindows priority 150) sends them away; time resumes as they slide out. Texts are Inspector fields (empty = the visitor's Default*). Every vendor is a CUBE character now (Cubie's shape): CubeBody(a, face, top, side, shoe) draws the legs / shoes / arms / shaded cube and CubeEyes the eyes, and each vendor adds their trade on top - Wizard (purple, pointy star hat, long beard, glowing staff), Tinkerer (copper, goggles, cap, gear backpack, wrench), Farmer (leaf green, straw hat, sideburns, wheat stalk, shotgun, seed sack), Entrepreneur (navy suit cube, tie, briefcase, coin cube), the Prospector (orange, hard hat, pickaxe), and the cats Egg (orange cube cat holding the egg) and Bean (grey tabby cube cat with a sun hat and bean pouch). A new visitor = a subclass overriding Id, IsWare, DrawVisitor, DefaultName/Greeting/WaresTitle + a "Minigames" line in PixelSystems.All (no shop pack: PixelGuideVendor.StartVendors starts it). Tinkerer sells consumable devices, devicesOffered (3) random ones per visit with stockMin..stockMax (1-3) each; Wizard sells potions (every potion whose pixel is unlocked or owned; combo potions are craft-only) but only potionsOffered (3) random ones per visit with stockMin..stockMax (1-3) bottles each (base hooks OfferedWares / StockMin / StockMax, per-visit stock, 'Sold out' button, 'Stock n' text); Farmer (PixelFarmerMinigame, id farmer, block-drawn: straw hat, overalls, pitchfork, seed sack) sells SEEDS (IsWare = seed items of unlocked pixel types) seedsOffered (5) random ones per visit; stock by rarity via the StockFor(item) hook (spawn weight >= 1: commonStock 10-30, 0.3-1: uncommonStock 4-10, rarer: rareStock 1-3); with dragonSeedChance (5%) per visit he also brings a Dragon Seed (ModifyWares hook, listed first, stock 1, only while the player lacks a Dragon Cube; Highlighted(item) hook -> PixelPulseGlow: the row pulses gold with a glowing outline and yellow name): a Seed device with seedType DragonCube1 as marker (PixelConsumables.IsDragonSeed, typed costs 500K White + 250K Black, hold 3, not in the shop's Seeds card); planting it picks PixelClicker.PickMissingDragonCube() (a Dragon Cube the player doesn't hold; refuses when all seven are held) and the sprout's pop-off gives that cube straight away (AddCurrency, first-find announcement included; the flying pixel is for show, amount 0). He is drawn with black hair, wispy facial hair, a shotgun held upright at his side and a wheat stalk in his mouth; when he brings a Dragon Seed a second speech box (dragonAsideText, base AsideText / asidePanel, 'Next' button) shows after you accept, and the Dev Tools tick box 'Farmer always has dragon seeds' (PixelFarmerMinigame.DevAlwaysDragonSeeds, pref Dev.FarmerSeeds) forces the seed on every visit; Pixel Entrepreneur (PixelEntrepreneurMinigame, id entrepreneur) overrides RollTrades() instead of IsWare: offerCount (3) pixel-for-pixel deals, each asks minPayFraction..maxPayFraction of a held pixel type (>= minHeld) and gives another unlocked type at a rarity-based rate (UnitValue = 1 / (spawnWeight x amountPerClick / clicksToCollect)) times a random luck minLuck..maxLuck; the base builds a trade row (Trade, DoTrade, button 'Trade' then 'Sold') when RollTrades returns a list. Counters <id>.bought, shop.items; sounds <id>_spawn (+ automatic <id>_click).

Peek (2026-10-15)

Every visitor (all PixelVisitorMinigame subclasses: Tinkerer, Wizard, Entrepreneur, Farmer, Lumberjack, Prospector, Egg, Bean; Cubie is separate and keeps his full visits) now PEEKS first (usePeek, on): they slide ~40% in from the right edge, leaning in, with a small speech bubble (peekText, '{0}' = name) and an X, while the game keeps running (no dim, no freeze, not registered with PixelWindows yet). Clicking the visitor or the bubble starts the normal full visit from there (screen darkens, time stops, greeting, wares). The X, or peekSeconds (20) without an answer (time in a menu doesn't count, PixelCalm.InMenu), sends them away: an event-log line (missedLog) and they come back after missedReturnFactor (0.5) of the normal wait (the shared cooldown is shortened the same way). The click catcher (a clear Image + Button on the visitor) is removed as soon as the peek ends.

2026-10-16: the Farmer only visits while Farming is the skill in training (CanVisit, like the Lumberjack with Woodcutting). Vendors can show an orange '!' with a tooltip on their Toggles row (PixelMinigame.ToggleNote: the Lumberjack and the Farmer say when they visit). The two rare cats (Egg, Bean) can't be switched off (PixelMinigame.CanSwitchOff false: no Toggles row; a saved 'off' is switched back on).
Shared training rule (2026-10-16 cleanup): a vendor that only comes while one skill trains overrides VisitSkillId (Lumberjack = Woodcutting, Farmer = Farming, Prospector = Mining); the base CanVisit checks PixelSkills.IsTraining(id) and the base ToggleNote writes '<name> only visits while you are training <skill>.' for its Toggles row.

Jabs at Cubie (2026-10-23, Cubie's bill)
Once Cubie's shop is open (visit 2 done), a visit's greeting ends with a jab at Cubie cubieJabChance (35%) of the time, in small italics
(GreetingWithJab). The built-in jabs are ONE table in PixelVisitorMinigame.CubieJabs(id) (Lumberjack, Prospector, Wizard, Tinkerer,
Entrepreneur, Farmer: two each); a visitor's own cubieJabs Inspector list wins. The vendors resent his monopoly, so the player ends up on their side.

Vendors buying goods - one shared rule (2026-10-24): every vendor that buys goods for pure currency prices them with PixelMarket (Quote / RecordSale / TrendText): the clicks the goods took x what a click on pure blocks earns x the route rate (raw skill currency 1.2x; products 2x and orders 3x when they exist), less as that vendor's market for the good fills up (1 / (1 + clicks-worth sold / 900), floor 25%, half recovers every 10 real minutes). The Lumberjack's Sell tab shows 'Market N% (recovering)' beside the amount; the Prospector's offer rows show it on their second line (base hooks OnTradeDone, TradeNote). The old per-vendor copies of the rarity formula (sell rate 0.8, the Lumberjack's bulk discount) are gone; the Entrepreneur's pixel-for-pixel deals use PixelMarket.RarityValue. Foraging, Fishing and Farming have no buying vendor yet.
--- docs/systems/SkillBlocks.md ---
SkillBlocks

Moved from CLAUDE.md. The script header comments and CLAUDE.md index point here.

Farming pixels / Wood / Rock

Wood is part of the Woodcutting skill now (see the Woodcutting row; the old opt-in switch is gone). Farmer's Pack (testing price 10 White; replaces the separate Seed and Water packs, which MigrateOldPackData drops; their tiers stay unlocked in old saves) rewards eight farm types (PixelClicker.IsFarm): Dirt (36, one click; old Dirt pixels near a sprout add growth speed, dirtSeedBoost, in SolarSeedBoostAt), Seed and Water (the existing pixels, no longer sold alone), Fertilizer (37; its click calls Fertilize = an instant growth jump for every sprout / Seed pet on the field), Pollen (38; floaty look physics; an old Pollen within pollenReachPixels of a popping sprout doubles its pixel's pay, PollenNear), Wheat (39; payout x (1 + wheatPerSprout x sprouts, max wheatMaxSprouts)), Weevil (40; while it is the pixel ON the cube, UpdateWeevil makes it eat one growing sprout every weevilBiteSeconds, SeedSprout.EatOne, until you click it) and Golden Fruit (41; a sprout grows one with fruitSproutChance 3% once unlocked, pays 40). The eight new types are IsStandalone (no potions / seeds / crafting / pets / achievements / Value upgrades); looksVersion 28 adds their looks; a sprout popping off gives Farming XP (PixelSkills.GrewSprout).

Seed shell rework

Seed block: a glossy ridged seed POD (PixelLook.blockSurface Seed + blob rounded mesh, PixelBlockTextures.SeedShell(level, max): ridges, dimples, a seam; cracks spread per hit with a green light leaking out; RoundedCubeMesh now has UVs per face; looksVersion 41, which also redoes Wood = rough bark on a rounded block and Stone = natural fractured rock with lichen). A cracked shell plants 1-5 seedlings (seedlingsMin/Max, 0 = 1 / 5; replaces the old seedsPerShell) that grow only plant pixels (PixelClicker.IsPlant = Wheat, Pollen, Golden Fruit, Wood; RandomPlantPixelTier by spawn weight, falls back to any growable type when none is unlocked; SeedSprout.PlantsOnly()). Bought seeds and the Seed pet are unchanged (any pixel).

Woodcutting (PixelSkills.WoodcuttingId, PixelClicker.IsWoodBlock)

Planned rework: randomly arriving trees with designed shapes, level gating, XP curve and cross-skill upgrades are designed in WoodcuttingProgression.md (not built yet).

The first, simple skill (listed right under Standard / Offline): bought as the cheap Lumberjack's Pack (the old Wood Pixel pack renamed by packDataVersion 17; 250 of each pure pixel since packDataVersion 38 (renamed from the Woodcutter's Pack; was 150 White, then 250 White); CreateWoodPixelPack). Blocks (enum 51-56 appended after GasPocket): Wood (34, 2 clicks, pays 3), Bark (51, rough dark jagged wood, 1 click, pays 2), Sap (52, translucent amber blob, pays 4; its husk is sticky: bounce 0, high friction), Leaf (53, floaty green flake, pays 1; husk drifts down slowly), Heartwood (54, rare, 4 clicks, pays 25, glowing red-brown), Twig (55, the FILLER, pays 1) and the Hornet Nest (56, the HAZARD, 5 clicks, pays 0: when it goes off, hornets (PixelPestSwarm.Scatter) chase off every husk within gasBlastWidths of the cube, no payout, and Woodcutting XP drops 10%; right-click defuse works like the other hazards). Looks reuse the Wood bark texture with tints (looksVersion 44), new FarmAmbient kinds Leaves / Sap / Hornets, break bursts in PixelFarmFx.Break, sounds wood_chop + pixel_<type> (PixelSynth.Wood(kind, seed)), Pixel Guide texts, bonus-bank 'good' pixels = every wood block but Twig (PixelClicker.IsPlant no longer includes Wood; the Inventory groups them as 'Composite: Woodcutting'). Perks: level 2 Wood Chipper (implemented: Device.shopSkillId / shopSkillLevel on the chipper open it in the shop and the shop search at Woodcutting 2 even though it is vendorOnly; PixelConsumables.ItemVendorOnly checks the level, ItemSkillGated keeps the Tinkerer from selling it) with a tutorial popup (Perk.noticeText, queued once per skill / level by PixelSkills.QueuePerkNotices, shown by UpdatePerkNotices when calm and tips are on; it points at Consumables and the shop search), level 3 Seasoned wood (old wood pixels last woodcuttingLifespanBonus 0 = 50% longer: PixelSkills.SkillLifetimeFactor read by PixelClicker.LifetimeFactor), level 4 Sap collector and level 6 Heart of the forest (coming soon). Saved packs are migrated (the old 'Sharp axe' level 2 perk is replaced). Skill tree: the first node is axe ('Unlock the axe to get the job done yourself', coming soon) - the axe itself is always available for now; the first person axe will be tied to this node later. baseXp 60, xpGrowth 1.45 (quicker than the others).

Hazard blocks (PixelType.Pest 49 = Farming, GasPocket 50 = Mining; PixelClicker.IsHazard, TriggerHazard, UpdateHazardExpiry, PixelSkills.TryGetHazard / ApplyHazardPenalty / PenaltyFactor)

Negative blocks in the skill packs: a tough block (Pest 6 hits, Gas Pocket 5, pays nothing) that makes up a small share of the spawns (SkillPack.hazardBaseShare 0 = 3% at level 1, hazardPerLevelShare 0.2% per level, hazardMaxShare 8%; weights worked out in PixelClicker.SpecialInfo together with the filler). A few taps are harmless; the hit that would break it sets it off instead of paying (CollectInternal -> TriggerHazard): the Pest kills EVERY sprout (SeedSprout.KillAll), the Gas Pocket explodes (FireBurstFx + husks within gasBlastWidths (4) husk widths vanish, no payout). Left alone it leaves after hazardSeconds (7). Right-click defuse: a RIGHT click on a hazard on the cube (PixelClicker.Update -> DefuseHazard, same minClickInterval) counts towards clearing it safely: it needs clicksToCollect + the hits already put in (5 to break, 2 hits in = 7 right clicks; shown as a rising '3/7', DefusePopup); done = no explosion / swarm, no penalty, no payout, the cube rolls a new pixel (hazards.defused). The auto clicker never clicks a hazard (CollectInternal returns for automatic unless the static HazardAutoClear is on - the hook for a future upgrade), so the cube waits on it until you act or it goes away (the auto clicker earns only a quarter of the skill XP, which is the trade-off). XP penalty: each hazard set off takes penaltyStep (10%) off ALL skill XP (PixelSkills.AddXp x PenaltyFactor), cumulative while one is running, the penaltySeconds (60) timer restarting each time (Time.time, so it holds while paused); an event-log line says so. The pinned skill tracker shows the running penalty as a red line under it (trackerPenalty), only for the skill it hit (the penalty is per skill: PenaltyFactor(id), the pack in play when the hazard went off). Part of the packs' pixel lists / reward tiers (packDataVersion 14, looksVersion 42), tip hazard, stat hazards.triggered. Electrician has none yet.

Foraging pixels / Forager's Pack

Foraging (PixelSkills.ForagingId, second base gathering skill after Woodcutting; bought as the Forager's Pack, 150 White, CreateForagerPack, packDataVersion 18): PixelType.Berry 57, Mushroom 58, Herb 59, Truffle 60 (rare, 3 hits, pays 30), Weeds 61 (filler) and Toadstool 62 (hazard; IsForageBlock = Berry..Weeds, IsHazard / IsStandalone cover the Toadstool). A Toadstool that goes off (TriggerHazard) wipes the husks within gasBlastWidths in a spore cloud. looksVersion 45; FX in PixelFarmFx (Hit/Break cases, sound forage_pick), sounds PixelSynth.Forage(kind, seed) (pixel_berry..pixel_toadstool); IsGood = forage blocks except Weeds; one shop tip pixel_Berry. Mold replaced the Toadstool in the pack (PixelType.Mold 75, hazard + standalone, 8 clicks, packDataVersion 26 adds it to saved Forager's Packs, the Skills pack lists it and hazardType = "Mold"; the Toadstool type, look and code stay but nothing spawns it): see PixelMold.cs (zone that grows per click, infects fallen pixels, shake held pixels clean, right click only stops the spreading; also the glowing Berry / Mushroom / Herb / Truffle husks that glow from half their lifespan). Not built yet: perks' effects, a foraging vendor / machines, the later link to Seed / Farming.

Fishing / ore rework

Fishing (PixelSkills.FishingId, third base gathering skill; bought as the Angler's Pack, 150 White, CreateAnglerPack, packDataVersion 19): PixelType.Fish 66 (2 hits, pays 4), Seaweed 67, Shell 68, Pearl 69 (rare, 4 hits, pays 35), Driftwood 70 (filler), Jellyfish 71 (hazard: stings away the husks near the cube like the Toadstool). IsFishBlock = Fish..Driftwood; sounds PixelSynth.Fishing(kind, seed) + fish_splash; looksVersion 46; tip pixel_Fish. Mining ores reworked (same migration 19): Copper, Tin, Iron, Silver 63, Gold 64 and Bedrock 65 (a random 20-30 clicks via randomClicks, pays 150; packDataVersion 20) plus Obsidian, which moved INTO the Prospector's Pack (15 clicks, pays 80; the separate Obsidian Pixel pack and its table entry are gone, packs that needed it now need the Prospector's Pack, saved lists are remapped). Lead / Zinc / Nickel are retired (IsRetiredOre, never spawn, dropped from saved Mining packs; their enum values stay for saves). Obsidian is not IsOre (it keeps its own FX and is not standalone) but counts as a good Mining block and sells to the Prospector vendor; it no longer has a Toggles spawn switch.

Termite Hive, filler family (Woodcutting)
- The Hornet Nest became the Termite Hive (enum PixelType.HornetNest kept for saves; renamed in EnsureTier, packDataVersion 31). Breaking it (PixelClicker.TriggerHazard) calls PixelTermiteSwarm.Release: one termite per husk on the floor (not held, capped by TermiteMax), each runs to its pixel and a billboard bar over it counts down (TermiteEatSeconds); a left click on the pixel saves it (stats termites.pixels_saved), otherwise it is eaten (ReleaseOldPixel(rb,false), termites.pixels_eaten). Pesticide Block Hold still clears the hive in one click (clearsHazard). Sound termite_chitter (PixelHazardSynth).
- Woodcutting's filler is a family: Twig (twigs tangle texture), Pinecone (enum 92) and Moss (93) share the filler's spawn share evenly (SkillPack.extraFillerTypes, PixelSkills.GetFillerFamily/IsFillerType, PixelClicker.SwapFiller; extras have spawn weight 0). The Shuffle bonus treats any family member as the filler. looksVersion 61 replaced the Twig / Leaf / Hive looks (new BlockSurface.Twigs/Pinecone/Moss/LeafBlock/Hive, PixelBlockTextures).

2026-10-15 changes

- Foraging is a TRULY RANDOM rotation (SkillPack.uniformRotation: every block of the pack in play, filler and hazards included, equally likely; PixelSkills.TryGetUniformPool, used by PixelClicker.PickSpawnTierBase and the spawn-share readout). Two hazards (hazardType may list several, "Mold,Toadstool", TryParseHazard picks one at random): Mold and Toadstool, both ONE left click to set off and ONE right click to clear (a reaction check). Mold now molds every husk on the field (PixelMold.wholeMap), and its timers are longer (zone hold 30 s, kill 22 s). The Toadstool gives a 12 s dizzy status (PixelToadstoolDaze): the cube jerks away from the cursor after each of your clicks.
- Firemaking (PixelSkills.FiremakingId, dev-only: the Firemaker's Pack, which is the old Fire Pixel test pack, hidden from the shop): Tinder, Kindling, Firewood, Flint, Ember, Fire, Charcoal, filler Ash, hazard Wildfire (5 hits: the fire burns up the husks around the cube; right-click 5 times to put it out). Tag PixelTag.Firemaking / PixelClicker.IsFireBlock. Mechanics still to come. Fire lost its Toggles spawn switch (it is a skill block now).
- Mining: Lead, Zinc and Nickel are back (no longer tagged RetiredOre; PixelShopDefaults.ProspectorOre), and the filler is called Rock everywhere (the enum was always Rock).
- The Prospector's and Farmer's Packs are in the shop with real prices (2,000 White + 1,000 Gray / 1,500 White + 750 Gray). Scene data is updated by packDataVersion 35.

Inconsistencies found
- Close X: 8 sizes (80 / 70 / 64 / 60 / 56 / 46 / 44 / 40), 7 offsets, grey in most but red in View, Collection Log, Dev Hub, UFO.
- Titles: 10 sizes (22-64), centred in most but left in Pocket, Next up, Dev Hub, Outfit Studio; four hard-code capitals, Pet Customize has none.
- Dragging: Dev Hub + Next up use PixelWindowChrome; Pocket has its own copy (drag + minimize + dock); View, tracker and pinned Inventory a bare PixelPopoutDrag. View's position is not saved.
- Minimize only in Pocket, Next up, Dev Hub, tracker, pinned Inventory; docking only in Pocket, Next up, Dev Hub.
- Bank (and Dev Hub, Studio, Customize, Offline) don't use PixelPop; Pocket, Next up and the tracker are not registered (Escape doesn't close them).
- PixelWindows priorities clash: Skills and Bank both 14; Toggles, Pixel Guide and Skill Tree all 15.
- Header heights set per window (90 / 100 / 60 / 56 / 52 / 36); Toggles and Pixel Guide borrow the Shop's.
- Top-left header slot used for different things (pin, book, hose, info, expand, search); a ? help button only in Shop (80) and Skills (70).
- Collection Log is the only main window without StyleWindow (on purpose: a leather book).

The standard (chosen by the user 2026-10-18)
Built by PixelUIKit.CreateWindow (PixelUIKitWindows.cs); the numbers are constants at the top of that file.
- Fixed windows (the big HUD windows: Inventory, Log, Shop + Upgrades, Skills, Skill Tree, Toggles, Crafting, Bank, Game Database, Pixel Guide, Collection Log, UFO helper): open in place, NOT draggable, no minimize. Header 90, title 48 centred in capitals, grey X 70 at (-14, -12).
- Tool windows (View, Pocket, skill tracker, Next up, Dev Hub; the pinned Inventory keeps its own drag): draggable by the header with the position saved, minimize, dock to a screen edge (PixelWindowChrome). Header 56, title 30 centred in capitals, grey X 44 at (-8, -6), minimize just left of it.
- Dialogs (Outfit Studio, Pet Customize, visitor greeting / wares, Cubie's speech, offline summary, pet found): NO X - their Done / Continue / Next button is the choice (CreateWindow closeButton: false); fixed in the middle.
- Close X is always grey (no red ones).

Progress (one window per commit)
- [x] View - Tool window: title 30 (was 32), grey X 44 (was red 46), NEW: minimize, dock to a screen edge, position remembered between sessions (it used to reset).
- [x] Inventory - X / pin buttons to the standard spots (2-6 px), Inspector header fields removed (it always has its header now). Pinned (compact) mode unchanged.

- [x] Shop - title 56 -> 48, X 80 -> 70 grey, ? 80 -> 70 in the right slot, header 100 -> 90 (tabs / list / currency panel up 10). Upgrades window: title 48, X / Back 70, now grows in.
- [x] Toggles - title 44 -> 48, X 64 -> 70, header 100 -> 90 (content up 10).

- [x] Skills - title 56 -> 48; pin / ? in the standard slots (? 2 px).
- [x] Skill Tree - title 52 -> 48; points + nodes 10 higher.
- [x] Crafting - X / book 6 px right-ish, title ~9 higher; Recipe Book panel X 60 -> standard 70.
- [x] Bank - title 44 -> 48, X / hose 64 -> 70, list 12 lower / shorter; NOW grows in / shrinks out like its neighbours.

- Not on the builder (widgets, by choice): the skill tracker, the pinned Inventory.

Known gaps (builder)
- The chrome's undock arrow uses the first right slot: a Tool window with an extra right header button must shift it while docked (Pocket does, LayoutHeaderButtons).
- Title colour is set after CreateWindow where a window keeps its own (pets yellow, visitors / Cubie their colour).
- Guarded: tools/compile-check/rules.sh fails when a script registers a window with PixelWindows without building it with CreateWindow (escape-only / window-built-in markers for the exceptions). The chrome raises StateChanged on minimize / dock.
--- docs/systems/WoodcuttingProgression.md ---
Woodcutting progression (design, not built yet)

Status: agreed direction; build step 1 TESTED IN UNITY and feels good (2026-10-16) - only timings and numbers still to tune. Step 1 was built as a TEST (2026-10-15): PixelTreeMode.cs (filler rotation + Oak trees + infested trees + tree bar + Timber), switched on by the hidden Oak Trees (test) shop pack (Dev Tools > Shop unlocks) while Woodcutting is in play. Data (fillers, species slot lists, infested chances) is on the PixelTreeMode component; details in its header. The "Open questions" section lists what is still a proposal.
Today's Woodcutting (blocks, perks, Termite Hive, filler family) is described in SkillBlocks.md; this doc is where it is going.

Goals

- The intro skill. Quick to start and to understand; the first ten levels take about an hour.
- A rhythm, not random noise. Each click should feel random, but the session should have a shape: a beginning, a build-up and a payoff that come round again and again.
- Hazards you can plan around. A Termite Hive should be a known risk, not bad luck.
- Unlocks you can see. Every early level adds one new thing; later levels make your trees visibly richer.
- Better through other skills. Woodcutting stays simple on its own; other skills supply its upgrades (the Whetstone comes from Mining, and so on).

The core idea: random trees, designed shapes

While Woodcutting is in play the cube alternates between two modes:

1. Filler rotation (random). The cube rolls randomly among the basic blocks: Twig, Leaf and plain Wood (later Moss and Sap too). Quick, cheap, the "waiting" part.
2. A tree arrives (random timing). After a random stretch of filler (see "When a tree arrives") a tree of a random species starts.
3. The tree plays out in a fixed shape (designed). Each species is an ordered list of slots: the blocks come in that order, with a few controlled random parts (see "Tree shapes"). The Termite Hive can only appear in a fixed slot, and an infested tree is warned from its first block.
4. "Timber!" When the last slot is chopped the tree falls: a felling show, sound and a small reward (see "Timber reward"). The cube goes back to the filler rotation.

So: when a tree comes is random, what is inside it is designed.

Why it works:

- Keeps the clicker's surprise. You never know when the next tree comes or which species it is.
- An authored rhythm. Inside a tree every click has a place: canopy, trunk, core, payoff. Each species can be tuned like a little level.
- Hazards you can plan around. The Hive only ever sits in the tree's last slot, and an infested tree shows it from the start (see "Infested trees"), so the player can prepare instead of being ambushed.
- The Next Up window becomes a tool. Once you know a species, you know what is coming; the window confirms it.
- Easy to expand. A species is data (a slot list). A new tree is a new list entry, not new code - and the same system fits other skills (see "The same system for other skills").

How the existing mechanics fit in

| Mechanic | With trees |
|---|---|
| Next Up window | Shows the next block: from the tree's slot list while a tree is up, otherwise the pre-rolled filler. |
| Block Hold | Holding pulls the live block out; letting go puts it back as the *next* block (it already works like this through nextInLine). Inside a tree the held block slots back in before the rest of the tree, so the tree's order is kept. |
| Shuffle bonus | Works on filler blocks during the filler rotation, as now. Inside a tree it is off (a tree's order is the point). |
| Hazard defuse / Pesticide | The Hive in an infested tree's last slot: right clicks (10) or a held Pesticide clear it, as now. |
| Great Oak | A rare species (see "Species"); acorns lying on the floor raise its chance when a tree arrives. |
| Auto clicker | Chops filler and trees like a manual click. It never touches a Hive (it waits for you), as now. |
| Focus potion | Lumberjack's Focus (made from collected Termite Hives): no tree is infested while it lasts. |

When a tree arrives

Built (2026-10-16, after testing): the filler run shrinks with the level - 12-30 filler blocks between trees at level 1, down to 3-10 by level 20 (PixelTreeMode minFillerAtLevel1 / maxFillerAtLevel1, minFillerAtTop / maxFillerAtTop, fillerLevelSpan). Trees came far too often at low levels with a fixed 3-10.

- During the filler rotation each click has a growing chance to start a tree: a few filler blocks always come first (minimum 3), then the chance climbs until a tree is guaranteed (by about 10). Inspector data: minFiller, maxFiller (or a chance curve).
- Levels shorten the filler stretch a little (more trees per hour), so the share of junk falls as you level - replacing today's filler fade (85% -> 25% over 20 levels; the start was raised from 75% on 2026-10-24).
- The species is a weighted pick among the unlocked ones; acorns on the floor add weight to the Great Oak.

Tree shapes

A species is an ordered slot list. A slot is one of:

| Slot kind | Example | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Fixed | Leaf | Always that block. |
| Run | Wood x3-6 | That block a random number of times: this is what makes trees different sizes. The range can grow with the level. |
| Wildcard | [Sap 30% / Wood] | A small weighted pick, so details change while the shape stays. |
| Optional | Acorn? (50%) | Sometimes there, sometimes skipped. |
| Hazard | (Hive) | Only filled when the tree is infested; always the last slot before Timber. |

Proposed species

| Species | Unlock | Shape | Character |
|---|---|---|---|
| Oak | 1 | Leaf, Leaf, Bark, Wood x3-6, [Sap 30% / Wood], Acorn?, [Heartwood 20% / Wood], (Hive) (Sap from level 2, Acorn from 3, Heartwood from 6; before that those slots are Wood) | The all-rounder; feeds the Great Oak through acorns. |
| Pine | 4 | Leaf, Pinecone, Wood x4-8, Pinecone, Wood x1-2, (Hive) | Tall: a long log run with cones. |
| Birch | 8 | Leaf, Bark, Wood x2-3, Bark, (Hive) | Short and quick: Timber after Timber. |
| Maple | 12 | Leaf, Wood x2-4, Sap, Sap, Wood x1-3, [Heartwood 25% / Sap], (Hive) | Sticky and well paid. |
| Great Oak (BUILT 2026-10-16 as Leaf, Leaf, Wood x2-4, GREAT OAK block, Heartwood) | 3, rare | Leaf, Leaf, Wood x6-10, Bark, Wood x2-4, Heartwood | Long, guaranteed heartwood core, never infested; its blocks stall the Block Hold meter. 1% when a tree arrives, up to 25% with acorns lying on the floor. |

Infested trees

- Chance per tree: about 25% at level 2 (no Hives before level 2), rising slowly to about 45% at level 20; 0 while the Focus potion lasts; the Great Oak is never infested.
- Warned from the first block: the tree bar shows a Hive icon, the tree's blocks get a darker, bored-through bark look, and a faint termite skitter plays. The player can then:
  - prepare: have a Pesticide ready to hold, or plan a Block Hold;
  - accept it: chop through and clear the Hive with right clicks at the end;
- The Hive keeps today's rules: 4 left clicks set the termites loose, 10 right clicks clear it safely (and collect it).

Dealing with Termite Hives: the toolkit

Pesticide used to be pure luck (a random block you could only use if it showed up at the right moment). Now every play style has its own answer, and preparing is a real choice:

| Approach | How it works | Suits |
|---|---|---|
| Effort | 10 right clicks clear the Hive and collect it (as now). | The clicker: free, but costs time and focus. |
| Preparation | Pesticide charges (see below): one left click on the Hive with charges clears and collects it, using one charge. | The planner: bank charges whenever Pesticide shows up. |
| Automation | A Pesticide Sprayer placed near the cube sprays any Hive that appears, using Pesticide husks fed in by hand or by belt. | The builder: a machine to set up and keep supplied. |
| Avoidance | A Focus potion, or the Infestation dial on Calm (below). | Anyone who wants a quiet stretch. |

Pesticide charges (hold to harvest). Pesticide stays a block in the rotation, but holding it with Block Hold converts it into Pesticide charges instead of paying currency (the game-wide "hold a utility block for its use" rule, see PixelRules.md). Its husk (husk) still drops, so it can be fed to a Pesticide Sprayer or used again. Charges are banked: every time Pesticide comes by you can hold it, so when a warned Hive arrives you already have answers stocked. When an infested tree is warned, the tree bar shows your charge count. (Later, Farming can produce charges too - the planned cross-skill link.)

When a Hive goes off anyway (the termites):
- Click threatened husks to save them (as now).
- Sap as bait: termites go for Sap husks first and get stuck in them, so sap left on the floor near valuable husks is a cheap defence (and gives Sap a real job).
- Husks riding a belt or stored in a Storage Crate are safe from termites: building already protects you.

So a Hive has a full arc: prevent it (Focus, Calm), prepare (charges), automate (Sprayer), respond (right clicks) and recover (sap bait, saving pixels).

Hives are a resource: Focus potions

- Clearing a Hive safely (10 right clicks, or one click with a held Pesticide) collects it as currency (this already works: right-click clearing collects hazards and drops them as a husk). Breaking it with left clicks gives nothing and lets the termites loose.
- Termite Hives are the ingredient for Lumberjack's Focus. The Lumberjack sells it for hives (and a little currency), or it is crafted from them. A Focus potion gives a stretch of trees with no Hives (proposed: the next 10 trees, or 60 seconds as today).
- So a hive costs you something in the moment but pays off later: dealing with it well matters, and avoiding every hive would mean having no Focus potions.

The Infestation dial

Unlocked by a perk or skill node (proposed: the Infestation node, from about level 12). A three-way setting in the Skills window for Woodcutting:

| Setting | Hives | Trade-off |
|---|---|---|
| Calm | about half the normal infested chance | about 10% less Woodcutting XP; for quick, quiet chopping |
| Normal | the normal infested chance | the default (and the only setting before the unlock) |
| Swarm | about double the infested chance | about 15% more XP and many more hives to collect: the way to farm Focus potions |

- A Focus potion still stops Hives completely while it lasts, on any setting (Calm only lowers them and costs XP, so potions keep their value).
- The same dial can later be given to every skill's hazard (Pest in Farming, Gas Pocket in Mining, Mold in Foraging, Jellyfish in Fishing), so each hazard has a reason to exist besides being annoying.

Rejected: skipping infested trees

A free or cheap "skip this tree" was considered and dropped: the warning would turn into a skip prompt and the decision (prepare, accept, clear) would disappear. With hives as a Focus potion ingredient, skipping would also throw away a resource. Players who want fewer hives use Calm or a Focus potion instead.

The same system for other skills

Filler rotation + randomly arriving shapes fit the other skills without new spawn code:

| Skill | Filler | "Trees" (shapes) |
|---|---|---|
| Mining | stone, gravel | ore veins (copper, iron...), a rich vein as the special; a Gas Pocket in a fixed slot |
| Foraging | weeds | bushes (berry bush, mushroom ring, herb patch), a truffle spot as the special; Mold in a fixed slot |
| Fishing | water | spots (shallows, reef), the whale as the special; a Jellyfish in a fixed slot |
| Farming | dirt | crop rows |

Lookahead grows with the skill

How far ahead you can see is itself a reward: early on you react, later you plan.

| Source | What you see |
|---|---|
| Level 1 | The next block (the Next Up window, as now) and, during a tree, the tree bar (Oak 7 / 12, with the Hive icon if infested). |
| Level 7 | Keen eye (perk): the next two blocks. |
| Level 20 | Woodsman's eye (perk): the tree bar shows what the tree's wildcard slots rolled (e.g. whether a heartwood is coming). |
| Skill tree: Read the grain | The next three blocks. |
| Level 30 | Master forester (perk): during the filler rotation a small hint shows the species of the next tree. |

- The Next Up window grows a slot per extra block (the next block on top, then the ones after it, smaller).
- Blocks bumped back by Block Hold show first, then the real next blocks, so the preview is always the true order.

Blocks and their roles

| Block | Where | Hits | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| Twig | filler | 1 | Basic filler. |
| Leaf | filler, tree tops | 1 | Basic filler and the start of most trees; floats down. |
| Wood | filler, tree trunks | 2 | Plain wood in the filler rotation; the log runs of every tree. |
| Moss | filler | 1 | Joins the filler later (level 2+). |
| Sap | filler (2+), oak / maple slots | 1 | Sticky husk, pays a bit more. |
| Pinecone | pine trees | 1 | Part of the Pine's shape. |
| Acorn | oak trees (3+) | 1 | Its husk on the floor raises the Great Oak chance. |
| Bark | birch, oak, Great Oak | 1 | Cheap trunk block. |
| Heartwood | cores (6+) | 4 | Rare payoff. |
| Termite Hive | last slot of an infested tree | 4 left / 10 right | The hazard. |
| Whetstone | - | - | Leaves Woodcutting: comes from Mining (see "Cross-skill upgrades"). |
| Pesticide | trunk (rare, any tree) | 3 | A utility block: hold it for Pesticide charges (no currency; its husk still drops). |

Level gating

| Level | What changes |
|---|---|
| 1 | Filler: Twig, Leaf, Wood. Trees: Oak only, with plain wood in its special slots. Small trees. |
| 2 | Sap (filler and oak slot), Moss in the filler; trees can be infested. Perk: Wood Chipper (as now). |
| 3 | Oaks drop Acorns; the Great Oak can arrive. Perk: wood pixels last longer (as now). |
| 4 | Pine trees. |
| 5 | Perk: Wood can spawn in any skill (as now). |
| 6 | Heartwood cores can roll. |
| 7 | Perk: Keen eye - see the next two blocks. |
| 8 | Birch trees. First skill point (see "Skill tree"). |
| 10 | Evil Tree minigame can appear (it needs the Lumberjack's Pack today; gate it at 10). |
| 12 | Maple trees. Lumberjack sells Lumberjack's Focus (for Termite Hives); the Infestation skill node can be bought. |
| 15, 20, 25, 30 | Mastery perks (see "Perks"). |

Special trees

- Great Oak. A rare species (above) with a guaranteed heartwood core and a bigger Timber reward.
- Later special trees (the to-do's "special-block tier" for Woodcutting): ideas to pick from - a *Beehive tree* (honey that sticks pixels together), a *Hollow Log* (a creature hides in it), a *Lightning-struck tree* (a quick-click event). Fishing's bottle -> map -> treasure is the model.

Timber reward

When a tree falls:

- A felling show: the cube "falls over" (a quick tilt and drop), chips burst out, a deep timber sound.
- Bonus XP of about 20% of the tree's own XP.
- A chance of an Acorn husk dropping (feeds the Great Oak chance).
- Counted in stats (woodcutting.trees_felled), which later achievements and the Lumberjack can use ("Fell 100 trees").

XP and pacing

Today: 60 XP for level 2, each level costs 1.45x the one before, 1 XP per block. That is about 1 hour to level 10 but about 43 hours to 20 and 1,770 hours to 30, so the curve hits a wall around level 15.

Proposed: a curve that flattens - 1.45x per level up to 10, 1.25x from 10 to 20, 1.15x after 20 (new fields on SkillPack, e.g. xpGrowthAfter10 / xpGrowthAfter20, or a small list of level bands).

At about one block per second of manual chopping:

| Level | Total XP | Time |
|---|---|---|
| 5 | 456 | ~8 min |
| 10 | 3,645 | ~1 h |
| 15 | 15,673 | ~4.5 h |
| 20 | 52,379 | ~15 h |
| 30 | 307,337 | ~85 h |

Also proposed: XP per block = its hits (Wood 2, Heartwood 4) instead of 1 per block, so tough blocks are worth their clicks; the Timber bonus comes on top.

Perks

| Level | Perk |
|---|---|
| 2 | Wood Chipper (built) |
| 3 | Seasoned wood: wood pixels last longer (built) |
| 5 | Wood can spawn in any skill (built) |
| 7 | Keen eye: see the next two blocks (see "Lookahead") |
| 15 | Clean cut: Wood sometimes breaks in one hit |
| 20 | Woodsman's eye: the tree bar shows what the tree's wildcard slots rolled |
| 25 | Old growth: Great Oak chance +5% |
| 30 | Master forester: every tree has a heartwood core, and a hint shows the species of the next tree |

The current placeholder perks at 4 ("Sap collector") and 6 ("Heart of the forest") are replaced by the gating unlocks above.

Skill tree

Points: 1 at level 8, then one every 4 levels (12, 16, 20...). Proposed nodes:

| Node | Effect |
|---|---|
| Axe | The first person axe (the existing, not yet built node). |
| Sharp edge | Wood takes 1 hit. |
| Tapper | Sap wildcards roll more often. |
| Acorn hoard | Acorns count double for the Great Oak chance. |
| Big timber | Log runs are 2 blocks longer (more XP per Timber). |
| Read the grain | See the next three blocks. |
| Infestation | Unlocks the Infestation dial (Calm / Normal / Swarm). |
| Chipper tune | The Wood Chipper gives more XP per wood pixel. |

Cross-skill upgrades

Woodcutting stays the simple intro; other skills make it better:

| From | Upgrade |
|---|---|
| Mining | Whetstone - moves to Mining as a block / item. Used in Woodcutting it gives the rapid clicks it gives today (Block Hold), or becomes a permanent "sharpened axe". |
| Farming / Foraging | Pesticide charges made in Farming (on top of holding the Pesticide block), and Saplings that grow better trees (from the existing to-do "Cross-skill links"). |
| Electrician | Chainsaw (idea): a powered tool for the trunk part of a tree. |
| Fishing | Idea: driftwood or a "log raft" that adds a bonus tree. |

Value, body and use (the game-wide rule)

Woodcutting follows PixelRules.md: a click extracts a block's value (currency) and drops its drained husk (the husk = material, never currency by itself); holding a utility block (Pesticide) gives its charges instead of currency. Wood husks are material for the Wood Chipper (XP), the Kiln (Charcoal), crates, crafting and the Pesticide Sprayer.

Economy and sinks

Wood goods today only sell to the Lumberjack. Proposed sinks, to decide later: Magic Wood (Evil Tree) and heartwood spent on permanent upgrades (a better first person axe, a stronger chipper), and wood as a crafting material.

Saves and migrations

- Tree state (the species, the position in its slot list and what its runs / wildcards rolled, whether it is infested) is saved with the skill state so a reload does not reroll a half-chopped tree; an old save simply starts in the filler rotation.
- Whetstone leaves the Lumberjack's Pack: a packDataVersion bump removes it from saved packs (the tier stays unlocked so counts are not lost) and adds it to Mining's side.
- The Hive's per-pack shares (hazardBaseShare / hazardPerLevelShare) and the filler fade (fillerStartShare / fillerEndShare) are no longer used while tree mode is on; they stay for the other skills until they move over.

Build plan (in steps)

1. Tree mode (shared system). Data per skill pack: a filler pool, the tree-arrival settings (minFiller / maxFiller) and a list of species (unlock level, weight, slot list with Fixed / Run / Wildcard / Optional / Hazard slots, infested chance). PixelClicker.RollTier serves the filler rotation or the current tree's next slot. Built generic so Mining (veins), Foraging (bushes) and Fishing (spots) can use it later.
2. Tree bar + Timber + infested warning + lookahead. The tree bar (Oak 7 / 12, Hive icon, near the Next Up window), the felling show, sound, Timber reward and stats; the infested look and sound; the Next Up window learns to show 1-3 blocks ahead (PixelClicker.PeekTierAhead(ahead) peeks N blocks ahead).
3. Woodcutting species + gating. Oak, Pine, Birch, Maple, Great Oak and the filler pool; remove the per-block filler / hazard shares for Woodcutting.
4. Hook the existing mechanics in. Block Hold keeps the tree's order; Shuffle only on filler; Great Oak becomes a species; Focus potion stops infestation; Focus made from collected hives (Lumberjack trade / recipe); holding Pesticide gives charges (and a left click on a Hive with charges clears it); Sap husks bait termites.
5. XP curve bands + XP per hit. Then retune with the Balance report (which will need a "per tree" view).
6. Perks and skill tree nodes, including the Infestation dial.
7. Cross-skill moves. Whetstone to Mining (migration); Farming-made Pesticide charges.
8. Pesticide Sprayer (a machine fed with Pesticide husks).
9. Special trees beyond the Great Oak.

Open questions

- Species shapes, run lengths, unlock levels and the infested chance are proposals.
- Filler stretch between trees (3-10 blocks proposed) and how much levels shorten it.
- Focus potion: how many hives per potion, and does it last N trees or N seconds?
- Infestation dial numbers (Calm half / Swarm double, XP -10% / +15%) are proposals.
- Should Pine bring its own block types later (e.g. Pine Log, Resin) or keep sharing Wood / Leaf?
- Lookahead steps: the levels above (7 / 20 / 30 and the tree node) are a proposal.
- Pesticide charges: how many per held block, and is there a cap on how many you can carry?
- Pesticide Sprayer: its cost, range and how many husks per Hive.
- XP per hit vs XP per block.
- Should the auto clicker be able to fell whole trees, or stop at the core so heartwood is always hand-chopped?

Tree mode is the normal Woodcutting (2026-10-16): PixelTreeMode.requireTestPack (off) - trees play whenever Woodcutting is in play, no hidden pack needed.

Bark fix (2026-10-24): Bark had no source once tree mode became how Woodcutting plays (it was only planned for the Birch at level 8), yet the Wood Chipper (150 Bark) and the Evil Tree pack (250 Bark) cost it. It now joins the filler rotation from level 1 and every Oak has a Bark block at the top of its trunk (PixelTreeMode speciesVersion 2). The pack's fillerStartShare (85% since 2026-10-24) only applies when tree mode is off; in tree mode the filler stretch between trees is minFillerAtLevel1 / maxFillerAtLevel1 (12-30 blocks).

=== 11. IDEAS - MAYBE SOMEDAY (Assets/Resources/Ideas.txt) ===
Ideas (maybe someday)
Loose ideas that are NOT on the to-do yet. Claude adds to this when an idea comes up; when you decide to build one, it moves to the to-do (Todo.txt). Older system notes that used to live here are in docs/systems/OlderNotes.md.

Big features
- Prestige: reset for a lasting bonus (wanted, from the balance plan).
- Passive production (things that make pixels without clicking).
- Offline progress for more than the auto clicker (minigames, devices).
- Formula-based auto clicker levels instead of a fixed list.
- Minigame goals measured in hours, for the idle-over-days pace.

Content
- More shop packs and pixel tiers.
- A balancing pass on spawn weights and costs.
- Particle polish and filling in missing sound clips.

Device connections beyond husks (sockets + couplings)
- The socket / coupling system (belts and hoses snap to a coupling on a device socket) starts with HUSK flow; its socket kind is an open list so more connection kinds can be added later, each with its own coupling look.
- Power (Electrician): electricity that speeds machines up or keeps them running. Today Wire / Battery / Resistor husks and Electric pixels arc to devices in their own hand-made code; it will be its OWN mechanic, NOT sockets / couplings (decided 2026-10-20: sockets carry husks only) - giving Electrician a real job in the factory (e.g. a powered Recycler works faster). Plan this one next.
- Signal (the planned Automation skill): on / off triggers between devices - a full Storage Crate starts a Recycler, a hopper opens when a colour arrives. Turns a pile of machines into a factory. Plan this one next too.
- Water (Farming / Fishing): Sprinkler, Water pixels and the Bait Bucket's water piped between devices for growing and cooling.
- Heat (Firemaking, later Smithing): fuel and heat for the Kiln, Charcoal and burning.
- Link (late game): wireless pairs that pass a husk or effect between two devices, like the Quantum Organizer's teleport.

More transports (later, after belt / tube / chute / robot arm / launcher)
- Water flume (Fishing / Farming): husks float along a water channel; floaty husks (Seaweed, Leaf) ride it best.
- Mag-rail (Electrician): husks hover along a powered rail - the fastest ground route.
- Teleport pads (late game, Quantum Organizer tech): a pair; husks entering one come out of the other, any distance.
- Drone courier (UFO Helper theme): flies batches between two pads every few seconds, over everything.

Cubie
- Take over the shop (late endgame, after enough prestiges): you end up running the only shop and Cubie has to visit YOU as a vendor - his 'maybe one day you'll run things around here' line paid off. Mundane, transactional, no story twist.

Code clean-ups worth doing some day
- Split PixelShop further into separate logic and UI classes.
- One shared game object that owns the references, instead of scene searches.
- PixelLog keeps its own number formatter (FormatAmount) next to PixelClicker.FormatNumber - could be one.
- Pool the husk objects themselves (only if a profile with ~150 husks shows it matters).

[ ] First-hour husk machine: one cheap machine reachable in the first 15-20 minutes (before the Auto Clicker) that uses the basic husks (White..Blue, Glass), fed by hand with Pixel Grabbing and later by the Funnel / belts, following the pixel rules (husks never become full currency), so the first tip's promise about husks pays off inside the first hour. Candidates: Husk Engine (burns husks to click the cube by itself, a loop that slowly runs down), the Recycler moved early, a Press (husks into floor tiles). PLAN: husks teach XP, not money. (1) Standard XP popups and level-ups visible from the first click (today hidden until a skill pack). (2) The first husk machine pays Standard XP (same pattern as the Wood Chipper paying Woodcutting XP); intro candidate = a deposit box: fill it with pure husks, it pays a chunk of Standard XP when full (a goal, never a gamble, so it does not clash with the stake). (3) Standard's perks (lingering pixels, roomy floor) are the early unlocks, so husk XP makes husk handling easier. (4) Later the Husk Engine (burn husks for clicks) gives the first choice; much later the Recycler arrives, framed by Cubie as the lossy money exception.
